# Gymdesk changelog

One entry per ticket, written by the ticket's own PR at merge time and never edited afterwards. Newest month first. The entries up to 2026-09-07 are the roadmap's original Status list (one item per ticket); everything after it was moved here from that section on 2026-10-09, when each heading was checked against the GitHub issue state — the entries that still read *In progress* are the ones whose issue is still open.

What the product does today and what is still open is `docs/roadmap.md`; this file is the record of how it got there. A ticket's entry goes under the month its issue was closed in, as a `- **Done**: #N (…)` bullet; a ticket shipped in stages keeps one bullet per stage.

## October 2026

- **Done**: #1381 (**The Admin sidebar's scrollbar is hidden.** `AppShell`'s stylesheet sets `scrollbar-width: none` and hides `::-webkit-scrollbar` on `.sidebar-panel > nav`, the sidebar's one scroll container, so wheel, trackpad and touch scrolling and every menu item stay reachable while the bar no longer shows; no other scrollbar is affected and no JavaScript was added. `mobile-sidebar-scroll.test.ts` asserts both rules and that the nav is still `overflow-y: auto`.)
- **Done**: #1321 stage 3 (**Section Cards have a Touch Effect.** A new Members App theme setting `sectionCardsTouchEffect` (none · press / scale · ripple · highlight · lift, default none, inherits from nothing and is never supplied by a Visual Style) is declared in the three mirrors and the en/es/ca labels. `apps/member/src/lib/cardTouch.ts` is the one place that decides what each word does (a stylesheet `ThemeProvider` mounts once, selected through `html[data-card-touch]`) and which cards it reaches (`isInteractiveCard()`: a button, a click handler or a button role), so `MembersSectionCard` adds the class and a ripple pointer handler to interactive cards only and a static card never looks pressable; click and navigation are untouched. Short 120ms transitions, a `:focus-visible` ring, and under `prefers-reduced-motion` no movement but a static brightness cue. The editor's preview draws a rest and a pressed mini-card from `CARD_TOUCH_PRESSED`, which a test keeps in step with the stylesheet. Closes #1321.)
- **Done**: #1360 stage 1 (**Exercise categories are a closed, normalized set.** `api/src/domain/exerciseCategories.ts` declares the seven supported categories (stored lowercase, trimmed), the category → result-type-slug table stage 2 will derive Recorded Metrics from, and `parseExerciseCategoryInput()` (absent keeps, `null` clears, an unsupported value is a 400, never coerced). `POST`/`PUT /exercises` and `/platform/exercises` now accept `category`; the shared Exercise editor has a Category select (a stored legacy value is shown disabled) and the read-only card shows it, in en/es/ca. Migration 257 lowercases and trims existing non-null categories and guesses nothing; the Free Exercise DB import lowercases too. `npm run exercises:audit-categories` is the read-only report of every missing, variant or unsupported category, to review before stage 2. Not run here: MySQL-backed tests (no database in the session). Stage 2 derives `exercise_allowed_result_types` on write/import, returns 400 for a contradicting `allowed_result_types`, makes the Recorded Metrics chips read-only and runs the backfill.)
- **Done**: #1325 PR 3d (**`billing_events.user_membership_id` is gone.** Migration 256 drops the column, its FK and the two indexes over it; a Billing Event belongs to a ProductSet version and its assignment is the chain's current projection, read through the one module `api/src/domain/billingEventOwnership.ts` (`eventAssignmentSql()` for an event's assignment, `eventsOfAssignmentSql()` for an assignment's events). `GET /billing-events?user_membership_id=`, the Assigned Plan card's Billing Events, the Payments list and detail (both still reporting a derived `user_membership_id`), Retry / Manual payment and `linkInitialPaymentToSet()` read through it; every writer stops naming the column. Still to come: the commercial columns of `user_memberships` and the projection, PR 3e.)
- **Done**: #1370 stage 1 (**My Training's backend rules.** A workout can no longer be marked done for a date after today in the gym's time zone: `POST /me/workout-block-logs` and `POST /me/exercise-logs` answer `400 { code: 'workout_in_future' }`. New `GET /me/training-progress` returns Day / Week / Month completed-over-scheduled workouts (a workout is done on a date when every block has a log that date; the month counts scheduled days from the 1st through today; 0% when the total is 0). The rules live in `api/src/domain/workoutProgress.ts`. Not run here: the MySQL-backed integration tests (no database in the session). Gate: `workout-progress.unit.test.ts`. The UI is stage 2.)
- **Done**: #1376 (**The Members list shows each Member's profile photo.** A first, untitled `avatar` column draws `components/MemberAvatar.tsx`: a 32px circle (`object-fit: cover`) over the `image_url` the list already returns (#1374), cache-busted on `modified_at` through `memberImagePreviewSrc()`, falling back to the Member's initial in a neutral circle when there is no image or it fails to load. No API, migration or extra request; the column is `mobile: 'keep'` so it stays beside the name on a phone.)
- **Done**: #1325 PR 3c (**`next_billing_date` and `last_billed_at` are gone.** Migration 255 drops the two columns of `user_memberships`; every reader takes the derived values (`withDerivedBilling()` / `loadDerivedBilling()`): the Billing Events list's `next_payment_date` and its projection of future rows (every future obligation is a persisted scheduled event now), the Payments dashboard's *scheduled this month* (the persisted `is_scheduled` rows), My Membership's `upcoming_payments`, the Close action's pending-billing check (a projected plan is retired through its set) and `cardRemovalBlock()` (`has_pending_obligation`). `currentCycleDate()` is the cycle containing today. #790's `domain/nextBillingDateStamp.ts` is removed: the webhook stamps no date and Reactivate rolls nothing forward, since a version's obligations are generated from today onwards. `ensureTestProductSet()` / `seedScheduledEvent()` in the test helpers are what a fixture uses in their place. Still to come: `billing_events.user_membership_id`, the commercial columns of `user_memberships` and the projection.)
- **Done**: #1325 PR 3b (**Every money row belongs to a ProductSet.** The Admin's Save & Pay now imports the assignment as a Pending Payment version with its initial Billing Event and lines, so a staff payment link, the member's Pay now and a cash `record-payment` are attempts on that one event and the commit activates the version; manual ledger appends resolve the member's set (400 with none), a Promotion adjustment on a set-less assignment writes nothing, a class-package purchase is a `product_purchase`, closing a pending assignment cancels its pending version, and `plan-checkout.ts` is gone. Migration 254 adds `chk_billing_events_product_set` (`product_set_id` NOT NULL except `product_purchase`, `card_verification`, the retired `status_changed`), refusing to run while a row would violate it. Still to come in 3c: `next_billing_date`/`last_billed_at`, `billing_events.user_membership_id` and the commercial columns of `user_memberships`.)
- **Done**: #1380 (**The Video URL input is gone from the Exercise editor.** The shared `ExerciseEditor` no longer renders it (it was shown in the creation card only), so the gym Exercises and Cordel → Base Exercises screens offer video through **Upload Video** alone; `toExerciseCreatePayload()` no longer submits `video_url`. Stored `video_url` values, playback and the upload routes are untouched. No migration, no API change. Tests: `exercises-inline-create.test.ts` and `base-exercise-videos.test.ts` updated; not run here, no installed dependencies.)

- **Done**: #1395 (**The global API rate limit is keyed on the signed-in person, not on the address.** `app.ts`'s `apiLimiter` (`API_RATE_LIMIT_MAX` per 15 min) keyed on `req.ip`, and both Next apps' `/api/proxy` routes open a server-to-server request with no `X-Forwarded-For` — so to the API every member was the Members App container and every staff user the admin container, and one person spending the budget answered *Too many requests* to everybody else on sign-in (two people in two houses, on dev, with the limit already at 5000). `apiRateLimitKey()` (`api/src/domain/apiRateLimit.ts`, pure) keys an authenticated request on the bearer token's `sub` — read **unverified**, since the limiter runs before `tenantContext` and a bucket key needs no trust: a forged token lands its caller in a bucket of their own, exactly as an unknown address does, and the request is then refused by the auth middleware as before — and falls back to `ipKeyGenerator(req.ip)` for a request with no usable token, with the two namespaces prefixed (`user:` / `ip:`) so a subject can never collide with an address. It is deliberately *not* a `trust proxy` change: `forwardedClient.ts`'s reason for refusing a global hop count while the API host is still public (#1087) stands, and the three per-member limiters in `me.ts` already key this way. No migration, no API change, no new variable. Tests: `api/src/test/api-rate-limit.unit.test.ts` (+7 — the subject read with and without a usable header, repeated headers, the key for two people behind one address, one person across two addresses, the unauthenticated fallback and the no-collision property) and `api/src/test/api-rate-limit.test.ts` (new, 5 integration against the real limiter with `API_RATE_LIMIT_MAX` pinned to 6 before the app loads: the pin itself, two people behind one proxy get two budgets, a person's budget and the address's are independent both ways, one person is one budget from any address, and the 429 carries the draft-7 `RateLimit` header only). Docs: `docs/rules.md` (relays section), `docs/architecture.md` and `docs/go-to-production.md` (the `apiLimiter` descriptions), `CLAUDE.md`.)

- **Done**: #1375 (**A Member uploads their own face photo from the Members App Profile.** `POST`/`DELETE /me/profile/image` (`api/src/api/me-profile-image.ts`, member role + `member_web.profile`, the caller resolved through `resolveMemberId()` and never named by the request) write the same column and the same key as #1374's staff routes through one shared storage module, `api/src/api/member-image-storage.ts`, which the staff routes now call too. The Members App gains `uploadFetch`, a browser-side centre-crop to 512 × 512 PNG (`lib/memberImageUpload.ts`), `MemberAvatar` as the one rendering of the avatar — the photo, or the initials — in the top bar and on the Profile page, and a Photo row on the Profile with upload, replace and remove (confirmed in `MemberDialog`); while impersonating, the avatar shows with no control. Gates: `me-profile-image.test.ts`, `member-profile-photo.unit.test.ts`.)
- **Done**: #1368 stage 1 (**The muscle catalogue is a table again, with no behaviour change.** Migration 253 creates the global `muscles` lookup (`slug` UNIQUE, English `name`, image pair and #799 actor pairs for stage 3), seeds every `MUSCLE_KEYS` entry plus any key already on a link that the list does not know, and adds a nullable `exercise_muscles.muscle_id` FK backfilled from the slug. The old `muscle` column and every reader/writer are untouched; stage 2 switches the consumers, stage 3 adds Cordel → Muscles, stage 4 the effective exercise image. Not run here: no installed dependencies or MySQL. Gate: `muscles-catalogue-migration.unit.test.ts`.)
- **Done**: #1374 (**A Member carries a profile image, stored in the gym's own Cloudflare folder.** Migration 253 adds `members.image_url`; `api/src/domain/memberImages.ts` builds the deterministic key `gyms/<prefix>/members/<member_id>-<sanitized name>.png` and validates the bytes as an exact 512 × 512 PNG; `POST`/`DELETE /members/:id/image` are the writers (MEMBERS write, tenant-scoped, audited), a rename in `PUT /members/:id` copies the object to the new key, re-points the row and only then deletes the old one, and `members/` is appended last to the gym's folder markers. Admin: the photo is a value in the read-only Profile and `components/MemberImageField.tsx` in Edit mode, where the browser centre-crops and scales any picked image to 512 × 512; the Add modal stages a file and uploads it after the Member is created, opening the row in Edit mode with a Retry when that upload fails. A soft-deleted Member keeps the object. The Member's own `/me` routes and the Members App avatar are #1375's.)
- **Done**: #1321 stage 2 (**Section Cards gain Glow, a Glow colour and a Visual Style.** Three more Members App theme settings in the Section Cards group, each with a live mini-card in the Theme editor: Glow (none / subtle / strong), Glow colour (inherits the Primary Button colour) and Visual Style (clean / outlined / glass). A style is a bundle of *defaults* (`CARD_STYLE_DEFAULTS`): it fills a setting only when the Theme has not overridden it, so it never overwrites an explicit choice; `clean` supplies nothing and is today's card. The drop shadow and the glow are composed once by `cardBoxShadow()` into `--gd-members-card-effects`, which `memberChrome.ts` reads. Validated by `validateMembersApp()`, mirrored in both apps, en/es/ca labels in both theme namespaces. Glass here is a shadow-plus-glow preset only; translucency and Touch Effect are stage 3.)
- **Done**: #1325 PR 3a (**ProductSet is the only billing path.** The `PRODUCT_SET_BILLING` flag is gone: every committed assignment is imported as a ProductSet version and every edit of a projected plan is a new version (the legacy routes keep their status codes and 409s). `POST /billing/run` is `executeDueScheduledEvents()` and nothing else — the assignment pass that priced the cycle `next_billing_date` named is removed — and #785's escalation moved onto the event's own attempts: the second failed UTC day pauses the projected assignment (`pauseAfterFailedDays()`), reported as `paused`. `user_memberships.failed_attempts` / `last_failed_at` and `domain/billingDunning.ts` are dropped (migration 252; the derived readers still report the pair from the ledger). A4: `recordStatusChange()` writes the audit log (`user_membership` / `status_change`) instead of a `status_changed` Billing Event, and the migration moves the existing ledger rows there; it refuses to run while legacy billing rows exist (the approved dev reset removes them). Still to come in 3b: `next_billing_date`/`last_billed_at`, `billing_events.user_membership_id`, the commercial columns of `user_memberships`, the `product_set_id` CHECK and the Admin Save & Pay creating its Pending Payment version up front.)
- **Done**: #1358 (**The Import Exercises modal has no Slug filter.** #1356 had already removed the field from the shared toolbar the modal renders; this ticket only corrects the modal's stale comments that still said it offered one. No behaviour change.)
- **Done**: #1357 (**Assigned Training Plans shows the member in its own column.** The Member column sits right after Name, sortable by the existing `member` sort and secondary on a phone; the member's name no longer appears under the plan name, which keeps only its description. UI only: no API, migration or locale change.)
- **Done**: #1246 stage 5 (**The Members App formats dates through the gym's settings.** `GET /me/localization` (read-only, member role) returns the gym's Time & Localization settings; `apps/member/src/lib/useGymFormatSettings.ts` reads it once per gym and the Calendar sheet (start, *Booked on*) and the Alerts page now use `formatGymDateTime()` instead of `toLocaleString`. Date-only UTC values (goals, billing dates, Clerk account lines) and server-currency amounts are unchanged. Gate: `member-gym-format.unit.test.ts`.)
- **Done**: #1325 PR 2, purchases (**A one-off purchase and a card verification each have a Billing Event of their own, and the purchase and service tables carry the ProductSet names.** Migration 251 renames `member_products` to `member_products_oneoff_snapshot`, `member_product_promotions` to `member_products_oneoff_promotion_snapshot` and `user_membership_services` to `member_products_recurrent_snapshot` (no data moves), drops the snapshot's `billing_event_id` (the event is reached through `payment_requests.billing_event_id`) and makes its `product_id` link `ON DELETE SET NULL`. `POST /me/products/:id/purchase` now writes a `product_purchase` event with the payment request, before the money moves, and the webhook settles that event instead of inserting a `payment_recorded` one; a card verification writes a `card_verification` event of amount 0 owned by the member's Active ProductSet when there is one. Both are receipt-aware (`product_purchase` is receiptable once paid) and are non-obligations: `isNonObligationEvent()` keeps them out of the Payments dashboard counts, the failed-payments attention queue and Retry/Manual payment, and the member's own ledger hides the verification. `billing_events.product_set_id` stays nullable here; the NOT NULL-except-`product_purchase` CHECK and the dropping of the legacy columns are PR 3.)
- **Done**: #1321 stage 1 (**Section Cards gain Shape, Border edges and Shadow settings.** Three Members App theme settings in the Section Cards group, each a closed set with a live mini-card beside the option in the Theme editor (`SectionCardOptionPreview`): Shape (rounded 12px / square 4px / none 0), Border edges (all / none / top / bottom / left / right — *where* the border is drawn; colour and width stay their own settings) and Shadow (none / soft / medium / strong). They inherit from nothing and default to today's card (rounded, all edges, soft), are validated by `validateMembersApp()` and written as `--gd-members-card-radius`, `--gd-members-card-shadow` and `--gd-members-card-edges-{top,right,bottom,left}` (0/1 flags scaling the border width per edge). `sectionCardStyle` and `MembersSectionCard` read them through `memberChrome.ts`, and the per-page radius/shadow overrides on cards were removed so every card follows the theme. En/es/ca labels in both theme namespaces. Glow and Visual Style are stage 2, Touch Effect stage 3.)
- **Done**: #1356 (**The exercise lists no longer search by slug.** The Slug field, its active-filter chip and its locale keys are gone from the shared exercise filter toolbar (Base Exercises, the Import modal), and `?q=` on the three exercise lists matches the name and stored translations only; `?slug=` is ignored. The `slug` column and its import-matching uses are untouched. Tests updated in `exercise-list-filters.unit.test.ts`, `gym-exercise-filters.test.ts` and the admin `exercise-filter-toolbar.test.ts`.)
- **Done**: #1325 stage 6 (**The readers of the four billing values read them off the ProductSet's ledger.** `user_memberships.next_billing_date`, `last_billed_at`, `failed_attempts` and `last_failed_at` are legacy storage of facts the Billing Events and payment attempts already hold, and a ProductSet-billed assignment carries none of them (the assignment pass selects `next_billing_date IS NOT NULL`, so the column must stay empty). `domain/derivedBilling.ts` (pure) says what the rows mean — next billing date = the earliest unsettled obligation, a scheduled event or a failed one awaiting its retry, an overdue date kept; last billed = the latest completed money-moving attempt, never a waiver; failed attempts = the failed provider attempts of the event being charged counted by **distinct UTC day** (#785's one step per run day); last failed = the latest of them — and `api/src/api/derived-billing.ts` supplies them in one batched read. `withDerivedBilling()` overlays them on any row an assignment-keyed reader returns, and **falls back to the row's own columns for an assignment no set projects**, so a reader switches once and is right for both. Switched: the Assigned Plans list and detail and `loadAssignmentRow`, the Member configuration read, `GET /me/membership` (so the upcoming payments are derived from the ledger), the Assigned Plan's Billing Events view (it now includes the chain's events, scheduled ones excluded), the Payments → Billing Events list (a persisted obligation is a real `scheduled` row dated by its own due date, with the plan name and the assignment through its set, and payable by staff once due), the Payments dashboard (persisted scheduled events counted in *scheduled this month* and kept out of last month's ledger totals) and the **card removal block** — which would otherwise have let a member remove the card under a live ProductSet, because the assignment carries no `next_billing_date`. Staff **Retry** and **Manual payment** now work on an event that has no assignment: a due, never-attempted obligation is payable like a failed one, a retry runs the charge guard over every earlier attempt's raw status first, the attempt records its `method` and the provider's raw status, and the event leaves `is_scheduled` with its type saying how it ended. `chargeGuard()` reads an attempt written before raw statuses were kept from the internal outcome it carries (a failed or expired one does not block). **Not done**: the assignment pass's own selection and the columns themselves — they stay for legacy assignments until the final stage. Tests: `product-set-derived-readers.test.ts` (8).)
- **Done**: #1325 stage 5 (**The assignment screens hand over to ProductSets behind `PRODUCT_SET_BILLING`.** With the flag on (a deployment variable, forwarded by `deploy.yml`, off by default), every screen that still creates or edits an assignment — the Assigned Plan card, *Assign New Plan*, the Plans page's bulk assign, the Memberships modal — feeds ProductSet billing without being rewritten, at the two moments that matter. **Commit**: `commitAssignment()` imports the just-activated assignment as the owner's next ProductSet version (`importAssignmentAsProductSet()`, `api/src/api/product-set-bridge.ts`): the frozen plan, benefit rows, services, applied Promotions and coverage are copied onto the version, the previous Active version is superseded and its future events replaced, the obligations are generated, `next_billing_date` is cleared so the assignment pass never sees it, and the assignment's own first payment is linked to the first period of the plan schedule so that period is never generated and charged twice (`linkInitialPaymentToSet()`, also called by the cash `record-payment` path, which writes its event after the commit). **Edit**: a configuration write on an assignment a set projects — Billing & Duration, the Personal Membership Fee Benefit, the three benefit sections, services, Promotions apply/revoke, coverage — is applied as a **new version** (`reconfigureProjected()`: clone the Active version into the next Draft, apply the same Draft writer the `/product-sets` API uses, activate, re-project), so a refused edit leaves the set and its events exactly as they were and the editing lock (an unresolved past obligation) answers `409 edit_locked`. **Cancel / close** of a projected plan is an empty, plan-less version, whose activation closes the assignment and deletes the future events. A Promotion revoked on a committed version is kept with `revoked_at`, never deleted. Decisions taken: an edit through the card does **not** raise Q8's immediate prorated charge for an added item (the legacy card never did; the item appears in the future events), a change of cadence or of the fee on a projected plan is refused and points at a new plan, and pause/resume of a projected plan is not bridged yet. In-transaction reads: the engine's input loader and the Promotion loaders now take the transaction a version was written in. Also fixes recurring plan benefits that were put on the plan schedule whatever their frequency: every recurring item (benefit or service) is allocated a schedule by the one rule (`allocateItemSchedules()`). Tests: `product-set-bridge.test.ts` (8).)

- **Done**: #1327 (**Member status chips moved into the Account section.** The Membership Plan Status, Payment Status and Access Rights chips no longer sit under the member's name in the expanded row; they are labelled `StatusBadge` chips in the Account section, above #1326's Clerk Status and Clerk Invitation chip lines, with the same values and locale keys. UI only: no API, migration or business-logic change.)

- **Done**: #1325 stage 4 (**A Draft ProductSet's configuration is editable through the API.** Mounted under `/product-sets/:id` (`product-set-draft-router.ts`, writers in `product-set-draft.ts`): `GET`/`PUT /benefits/:category` (replace-all, `{ product_id, quantity }` per line like the assignment editor's contract — a line already agreed keeps its frozen facts and only its quantity moves, a newly added Product is held to the catalogue's current state and to the section's category and is frozen at its present price), `PUT /billing-duration` (Free / Paid / Bonus / Pre-paid periods and auto renew, Pre-paid bounded by Paid), `PUT /fee` (a negotiated Membership Fee, the reason required), `PUT /fee-benefit` (the Personal Membership Fee Benefit), `POST`/`DELETE /services`, `POST`/`DELETE /promotions` (the version's own application with its own snapshot; a Draft has charged nothing, so removing deletes), `POST`/`DELETE /members` (coverage keyed by the chain, the plan's member limit counting the owner, #956's one-plan rule) and `GET /billing-event-simulation` (the Draft's Billing Event Forecast, the existing engine's). Every write locks the Draft, refuses a committed version (`409 not_a_draft`) or one idle past two hours (`410 draft_expired`), refreshes the two hours and is audited against the `product_set`. **Nothing in a screen calls these yet**: they are what the Assigned Plan card, *Assign New Plan*, the Plans page's bulk assign and the Memberships modal need before they can leave the assignment flow. Tests: `product-set-draft-api.test.ts` (9).)

- **Done**: #1349 (**Products carry a `one_time_purchase` flag, shown as a `One-time` badge.** Migration 250 adds `products.one_time_purchase` (NOT NULL DEFAULT 0) and backfills it to 1 for the System Premium Fitness App and Registration Fee of every gym (idempotent; keyed on charge type and `is_system`, nothing else touched). `defaultGymProducts.ts` declares it so new gyms get both flagged; `GET /products` returns it, Duplicate copies it. The Products list shows the badge from the flag alone, independent of Billing Frequency. The flag identifies only: no purchase enforcement. Tests: `default-gym-products.unit.test.ts`. Not run here: no installed dependencies or MySQL.)

- **Done**: #1325 stage 3b (**The Admin's "+ Add Membership Plan" creates a ProductSet version.** The Member card's add form posts to `POST /product-sets` and commits through the server's own answer (`apps/admin/src/lib/productSetCommit.ts`: activate; on `409 payment_required`, save-and-pay), instead of creating an assignment Draft. It sends no `confirm`: a ProductSet version never overwrites, and the server refuses a Member who already holds a plan — their own legacy assignment, or someone else's plan covering them — with `409 active_plan_exists` (#956), which the section shows as the server's sentence. The Member card lists the member's **in-flight versions** beside the plans (`GET /user-memberships/member/:id/configuration` now carries `product_sets`): a Pending Payment version offers *Get payment link* (a new attempt on its initial event, copied to the clipboard and shown), *Record cash payment* (`POST /product-sets/:id/record-payment`: a `cash` request on the initial event settles it and activates the version in the same transaction) and *Discard*; a Draft idle past two hours reads *Draft expired* and offers only Discard. The replacement confirmation dialog left this section (it stays on the three assignment paths that can replace a plan), and the contract tests were updated to match. **Not done**: *Assign New Plan* (replacing a plan), the Plans page's bulk assign, the Memberships page's create modal and the Assigned Plan card's section editing still use the assignment flow, because editing a Draft's configuration through `/product-sets` (sections, services, Promotions on the Draft) has no API yet — so the legacy columns and the assignment pass stay. **Verification limits, stated**: type-checked and covered by source-contract tests and the API integration tests; not exercised in a signed-in browser (the deployed admin does not carry this branch). The admin test suite has 11 failures in files this change does not touch (calendar, nutrition library, theme settings), which the CI does not run.)

- **Done**: #1325 stage 3a (**The Members App's plan flow runs on ProductSets, with the operational assignment projected from the Active version.** `POST /me/membership-plans/:id/assign` — the screen's endpoint, response shape unchanged — now creates a ProductSet Draft with its plan frozen (`snapshotProductSetFromPlan()`), the member's declined benefits and each chosen Promotion applied with its own snapshot (`applyPromotionToProductSet()`, the version-keyed counterpart of `applyPromotionToMembership()` with the same validations), then activates at once when the first cycle owes nothing or saves it for payment with its initial event and the hosted checkout. `GET /me/membership` reports a Pending Payment version as `pending_membership`, and `POST /me/payment-requests` (Pay now) takes a new attempt on that initial event; the webhook that settles it activates the version. **Activation projects the operational assignment** (`api/src/api/product-set-projection.ts`, migration 249's `product_sets.user_membership_id`): the one writer of a `user_memberships` row for a ProductSet — same plan, a new version updates it in place; a different plan closes the previous and opens a new one; a plan-less version has none — together with the benefit rows, applied Promotions, services and family coverage the legacy readers (access, eligibility, `GET /me/membership`, the Assigned Plan card) still price from. `next_billing_date` is left NULL, so the assignment pass never charges a projected assignment; the persisted events do. The link is ProductSet → assignment only (`user_memberships` carries no ProductSet reference) and the projection is removed with the legacy commercial columns. Tests: `product-set-projection.test.ts` (5), the member flow end to end in `me-membership-plans.test.ts` (assign → Pay now → webhook → current plan); full API suite 6782 passing. **Not done**: the Admin app's assignment screens (Assigned Plan card, Assign editor, Plans page assign) still create legacy assignments, so the legacy columns and the assignment pass stay.)

- **Done**: #1325 stage 2d (**The ProductSet-keyed commercial API, and the webhook that activates a paid version.** `/product-sets` (`api/src/api/product-sets-router.ts`, behind the PAYMENTS module and `payments.transactions`): `GET ?member_id=` lists a member's versions, `GET /edit-check?member_id=` answers what blocks editing, `GET /:id` returns a version with what it froze and its events, `POST /` starts a Draft from a Membership Plan (or none — a plan-less set), `POST /:id/save-and-pay` makes it Pending Payment and returns the hosted checkout, `POST /:id/activate` activates it when nothing is owed, `DELETE /:id` cancels an in-flight version; `GET /me/product-sets` is the member's own read, the member never named by the request (#1036). The **editing lock** (`domain/billingEventEditLock.ts`, read through `loadEditLock()`) is evaluated server-side on creating a Draft, on Save & Pay and on activation: an unresolved past obligation of the active chain answers `409 edit_locked` naming the events and their raw provider status, while REFUNDED, PARTIALLY_REFUNDED, PAID_OUT, SUCCEEDED and waived/cash-settled obligations do not block. Save & Pay writes the version's **initial `payment_recorded` event with its lines before the money moves** (`product-set-checkout.ts`, the ProductSet counterpart of `plan-checkout.ts`) and the payment webhook settles that event — and, through `activateWithEvents()` in the same transaction, activates the version, supersedes the previous one and replaces its obsolete future events; the hosted-page return activates nothing, and a duplicate delivery changes nothing. The webhook now also stores the provider's raw status in `payment_requests.provider_status` on completed, failed and expired. **Still no existing flow creates a ProductSet**: the Admin and Members App keep using assignments; wiring their screens to these routes, the `member_products` / services replacement, the derived `next_billing_date` / `last_billed_at` readers and dropping the legacy columns are what remains of #1325 (PR 3 cannot drop a column the live Assigned Plan and Members App flows still read). Tests: `product-sets-api.test.ts` (6) through the real webhook.)

- **Done**: #1325 stage 2c (**The nightly run executes a ProductSet's persisted Billing Events — a second, separate pass.** `POST /billing/run` now ends with `executeDueScheduledEvents()` (`api/src/api/scheduled-event-execution.ts`) before the run is closed as completed, and reports its counters beside the legacy ones as `scheduled_events` (the legacy counters keep their names, `.github/workflows/billing-run.yml` parses them, #778). The pass takes `is_scheduled` events due today or earlier, and failed ones awaiting a retry, of an **Active** ProductSet; the event's own persisted amount is what is charged, and `user_memberships.next_billing_date` is never read. Per event, under its row lock: `chargeGuard()` over the raw provider status of every earlier attempt (a settled, in-flight, unknown-outcome or refunded one refuses the charge and is logged for reconciliation); one attempt per UTC day and at most two failed days (#785's cadence — the retry is the next run day); a zero amount is waived as a `payment_requests` row of method `waive` with no provider call; no stored card attempts nothing and leaves the event scheduled and past due (it blocks a ProductSet edit and never counts towards a pause); otherwise the attempt row is written **before** the provider is called, so a crash or timeout leaves an attempt with no provider status — an unknown outcome — and is closed with the raw status afterwards (`PENDING_PROCESSING` stays pending and unresolved, never a failure). A settled charge gets its receipt number after the charge commits, exactly as the assignment pass does. `POST /billing/cleanup` also deletes ProductSet Drafts idle for more than two hours (`drafts_expired`, beside the totals). **Inert until a ProductSet is Active**: nothing creates one from the assignment flows yet, so no live charge changes; the assignment pass is untouched and cannot double-charge (an event belongs to a ProductSet, an assignment charge to a `user_memberships` row). Not done here, and stated so nobody assumes it: pausing a membership after two failed days (a ProductSet owner may hold no assignment; the events simply stop retrying and keep blocking edits), `status_changed` to the audit log and the derived `next_billing_date` / `last_billed_at` readers are 2d. Tests: `scheduled-event-execution.test.ts` (8).)

- **Done**: #1325 stage 2b (**A ProductSet version's obligations are generated and persisted, still not charged.** Migration 248 makes `user_membership_id` NULLable on the five re-keyed configuration tables (a plan-less set has no assignment) and gives ProductSet-keyed rows their own uniqueness — one line per Product per version, one open service per Product per version, one standing application per Promotion per version — as generated keys that never constrain a legacy row. `api/src/api/product-set-configuration.ts` freezes a Membership Plan onto a version (`snapshotProductSetFromPlan()`: plan snapshot, the `plan` schedule, and the benefit lines keyed to the version), adds a recurring service on its own schedule, and reads a version back as the **existing engine's own input** (`loadProductSetSimulationAssignment()`, built from the version's rows only, so a catalogue edit moves nothing frozen). `materialiseScheduledEvents()` runs `computeBillingSimulation()` over it and persists `is_scheduled` Billing Events with their lines — grouped by the schedule that owns each line, at most two cycles per schedule, lines summing to the event to the cent, idempotent through `UNIQUE (schedule_id, period_start)`. `activateWithEvents()` is the commit: it supersedes the previous Active version, deletes that version's obsolete future Scheduled events (strictly after today, with no payment attempt of any kind; past, attempted, paid, refunded and waived obligations and every payment request are untouched) and generates the new version's, in one transaction. `loadPromotionApplicationsFor()` was refactored onto a keyed helper so a version reads its own applications. **No live billing behaviour changed**: the nightly run still reads assignments; executing the persisted events is 2c. Tests: `product-set-events.test.ts` (6) against the real engine and constraints.)

- **Done**: #1325 stage 2a (**Billing foundations: the ledger learns its ProductSet, its schedule and its lines — nothing reads them yet.** Migration 247 is additive: `billing_events.product_set_id` / `schedule_id` / `period_start` / `period_end` / `billing_date` / `is_scheduled` with `UNIQUE (schedule_id, period_start)` (one obligation per schedule and period across ProductSet versions; a NULL schedule — a one-off — never collides; `schedule_id` is SET NULL so removing a schedule never deletes an obligation); `billing_event_lines` (the priced components of an event, from the engine's own output); `payment_requests.provider_status` (the provider's own status, verbatim, **no CHECK**) and `method` (`provider`/`cash`/`waive`) (the `UNIQUE (billing_event_id, attempt)` is deliberately deferred to the writer that numbers attempts: today's Pay-now requests share the default `attempt = 1` on one event); and `product_purchase` / `card_verification` in `billing_events_event_type_check`. Pure, tested modules: `providerPaymentStatus.ts` (what each provider status means; an unknown one is `unknown`, never FAILED), `billingEventEditLock.ts` (past-due and unresolved blocks a ProductSet edit; refunds do not), `chargeGuard.ts` (refuse a new attempt when an earlier one settled, is in flight, has an unknown outcome or was refunded), `scheduleAllocation.ts` (plan schedule → existing schedule → new independent one; cadence mapping), `proration.ts` (monthly price ÷ 31 × days, decision D) and `billingEventLines.ts` (lines restate the engine and sum to the event to the cent). The MONEI adapter and webhook now also expose the **raw** `providerStatus` beside the legacy mapping, which still collapses `PENDING_PROCESSING`, `CANCELED` and `REFUNDED` to `failed` — the reason the raw one exists. **No live billing behaviour changed**: no writer fills the new columns and no reader reads them; generation, execution and the readers are 2b–2d. Tests: `billing-foundations.unit.test.ts` (18), `billing-event-schema.test.ts` (6, against the real constraints).)

- **Done**: #1325 stage 1 (**ProductSet: the model, its lifecycle and the guarded development reset.** First of three PRs for the versioned commercial configuration. Migration 246 adds `product_sets` (one row per version, chain = `root_product_set_id`, statuses `draft` → `pending_payment` → `active` → `superseded`), `product_set_plan_snapshots`, `product_set_schedules` and `product_set_members`, plus a nullable `product_set_id` on the five assignment snapshot tables and `schedule_id` on the two recurring item tables, and the `modified_*` / `deleted_*` actor snapshots `user_memberships` lacked. The database guarantees one `active` and one in-flight (`draft` or `pending_payment`) version per owner through two UNIQUE generated columns; a Draft is persisted section by section and expires after two hours of inactivity (`last_activity_at`), lazily when its owner starts a new one and through `expireDrafts()`. `api/src/domain/productSet.ts` (pure) decides transitions, Draft expiry and whether a Pending Payment checkout may be cancelled; `api/src/api/product-sets.ts` is the one writer (create Draft, touch, Save & Pay, idempotent activation that supersedes the previous Active version in one transaction, cancel, family coverage keyed by the chain root). **Nothing reads the new tables yet and no billing behaviour changed**: persisted Billing Events, `billing_event_lines`, the schedule allocation, the nightly generation/execution split, the read-path switch of the billing run and the removal of the legacy columns are PR 2 and PR 3. Also ships the **development reset**: `npm run billing:reset-dev` (dry run by default; `--execute` + `RESET_CONFIRM`) over `api/src/domain/devBillingReset.ts` deletes the assigned Membership Plans, Billing Events, payments, one-off purchases and the audit rows of those entities in one transaction, with per-table counts and a post-check, and `.github/workflows/billing-reset-dev.yml` runs it from the VPS in the `dev` environment only. Executed on the local development database on 2026-10-10 (42 billing events, 7 assignments, 8 coverage rows, 20 audit rows). Tests: `product-set-domain.unit.test.ts`, `product-sets.test.ts` against the real constraints.)

- **Done**: #1246 stage 4 (**More audit timestamps follow the gym's Time & Localization settings.** The Staff Details dates, the Member Professional Services history, the Member payments modal's created/last-billed dates and the Payments → Transactions list now go through `formatGymDateTime()` / `formatGymDate()` with `useGymFormatSettings()` instead of `toLocale*String()`. Date-only values (hire date, next billing date), locale month names, the Cordel/platform screens (no active gym) and the Members App remain, so the issue stays open; `gym-format-sweep.test.ts` pins the converted files. Not run here: admin dependencies are not installed.)

- **Done**: #1227 stage 2 (**Plan Session Benefit allowances renew.** A Membership Plan Session Benefit with a renewing Frequency (`2 | Weekly`, #918) now adds its quantity again on each renewal. Each renewal is one append-only `plan_allowance_renewals` row (migration 244, `UNIQUE (line, renewal_date)`): the plan-session grant is the Assigned Plan snapshot line's quantity plus its renewal rows, and the same row is the *Plan allowance renewed* entry in the Member's Professional Service history. The nightly `POST /plan-allowance-renewals/run` is a step of `billing-run.yml` (shares `BILLING_INTERNAL_SECRET`, relayed through the admin allowlist, no run-log slot since it is idempotent); `domain/planAllowanceRenewals.ts` decides what is owed. A line's first run catches up one week only, so existing assignments are not granted months of sessions retroactively; afterwards a run continues from the last renewal written. Tests: `api/src/test/plan-allowance-renewals.unit.test.ts`. Not run here: no installed dependencies or MySQL.)

- **Done**: #1326 (**Clerk Status and Clerk Invitation are chips, and an invitation change refreshes them.** The Account section of an open Member row read `/members/:id/clerk-status` once, so Invite, Re-invite and Revoke invitation (menu actions outside the row) left the Invitation line stale until the row was reopened; those three now bump an `accountVersion` that re-reads only that status. The two values render with the list's `listNameBadgeStyle` / `listNameBadgeAccentStyle` chip, the stored date beside it as text. No API, migration or locale change; gate: `api/src/test/member-account-chips.unit.test.ts`.)

- **Done**: #1329 (**Log out from the Members App's Support bar, and while impersonating.** A superadmin who is not impersonating is linked to no member, so `TopBar` and its avatar menu rendered nothing and there was no way to log out; the Support bar (`AdminBar`) now carries a *Log out* button and the avatar menu offers *Log out* while impersonating (it was hidden by #1282). Both go through the new `LogoutConfirmDialog`, which asks first: from the Support bar it signs the superadmin out, and while impersonating it ends and audits the impersonation (`lib/endImpersonation.ts`, shared with the banner's *Stop Impersonating*) and returns to Support mode, keeping the superadmin signed in. The *Support mode* label was hardcoded English and is now `impersonation.home_prompt_title`. No migration, no API change. Tests: the updated `api/src/test/member-user-menu.unit.test.ts`.)

- **Done**: #1323 (**`docs/rules.md`: the product's business rules stated for a person.** The second half of the application-status review's item 13 (#1322 was the first). One file, 16 sections in the roadmap's area order, 188 entries — each the rule in one sentence, the ticket that decided it, one to three sentences on the behaviour, and the module or route that enforces it, every path verified against `main`. It is a view of the Hard constraints in `CLAUDE.md`, which keeps the reasoning and the implementation detail; constraints that are purely about code structure are deliberately not restated. The rules that lived only in `docs/payments.md` and `docs/mobile-app.md` are included in the same shape. `CLAUDE.md`'s read-first list and Finishing-a-task checklist, `.claude/commands/plan.md`, the `gh pr create` hook, the README and `docs/architecture.md`'s TL;DR point at it, and a ticket that adds or changes a behavioural constraint now adds or changes its entry.)

- **Done**: #916 (**One column grid for a Membership Plan's Product sections, with the original and final price.** The three Benefit sections (One-off / Session / Period) were three independent tables sized by their own content, so `QUANTITY`, `FREQUENCY` and `BENEFIT` landed at a different horizontal position in each one and the Products could not be read as one data set — and none of them said what an item costs. The columns are now declared **once**, as `PRODUCT_BENEFIT_COLUMNS` in `apps/admin/src/components/ProductBenefits.tsx`, in the order the ticket fixes: Product, Quantity, Frequency, **Benefit**, Original Price, Final Price — Benefit between Frequency and the prices, never after them. `productBenefitColumns()` takes the flags from the *page*, not from the section, so every section of one card gets the same grid; the read-only view renders a `table-layout: fixed` table from that declaration with one `<colgroup>`, so a long item name can no longer widen its column, and the table scrolls (`overflow-x: auto`, #637's answer) instead of squashing. `showFrequency` is true for all three Plan sections now: a One-off item's Frequency cell holds a `—` rather than disappearing and shifting everything after it. The two prices are **the server's**: a new pure module `api/src/domain/planBenefitPrices.ts` reports the item's gross unit price, the same price after the row's own `(action, value)` treatment, and the two line totals beside them — every figure from `applyLineBenefit()` (#896), the very function the Billing Simulation's charge builders call, so the Benefit sections and #915's Billing Event Simulation on the same card cannot quote one line two ways (an integration test asserts they agree to the cent). The gross-up is `computePriceFields()`'s, extracted as `grossBenefitUnitPrice()` and shared with #915's own loader, so no page does tax arithmetic (#817) and an item with no price reads `—` rather than €0.00. `enrichPlan` and the three section `GET`/`PUT`s all report the pair, and a Mandatory item the Plan has no stored row for yet (#893's `implicit: true`) is priced exactly like a configured one — `loadMandatoryProducts()` carries the price columns for it. Nothing is stored and nothing is charged: like `example_timeline` and `billing_event_simulation`, the amounts are computed on every read. No migration. The Promotions card is deliberately untouched — a Promotion's grants are priced against the assignment they are applied to, which is #919/#920's question. Tests: `api/src/test/plan-benefit-prices.unit.test.ts` (9), a `#916` block in `api/src/test/membership-plan-benefits.test.ts` (10 integration) and `apps/admin/src/test/plan-benefit-columns.test.ts` (27).)

- **Done**: #919/#920 (**One column grid for a Promotion's Product sections, with the regular and final price, and a Membership Fee Promotion duration that stops claiming to be months.** #920 is #919 plus the two price columns, so one PR answers both. The three Promotion sections (Session / One-off / Periodical) were the mirror image of the defect #916 fixed on the Membership Plan card: `showFrequency` was true for the Periodical section alone, so `QUANTITY` and `PROMOTION` sat at a different horizontal position in each section and the three could not be read as one table. They now render from the very same `PRODUCT_BENEFIT_COLUMNS` declaration with the same flags for every section — Product, Quantity, Frequency, **Promotion**, Regular Price, Final Price, Promotion between Frequency and the prices — so a Session or One-off item whose Product carries no frequency keeps its cell with a `—` instead of shifting the columns after it. No second declaration and no second grid: the shared component already took the flags from the page. The two prices are **the server's** and are not a second pricing implementation either — #916's wiring moved out of `membership-plans.ts` into `api/src/api/product-benefit-pricing.ts` (`grossBenefitUnitPrice()` over `computePriceFields()`, `withProductBenefitPrices()` over `domain/planBenefitPrices.ts` and `applyLineBenefit()`), and `promotion-details.ts` is now its second caller with `context: 'promotion'`, which is what lets a Promotion quote the two amount-taking actions (`fixed_discount`, `fixed_price`) a Plan may not configure (#896 section 16). A Promotion section therefore cannot price an item differently from the Plan section beside it: same gross-up, same treatment arithmetic, VAT-inclusive, `—` rather than EUR 0.00 for an item with no price, and the line total shown beneath the unit price whenever the quantity makes the two differ. The price columns come from the benefit row's own join, so a Promotion still prices an item that has since been deactivated. This narrows #916's "the Promotions card is deliberately untouched" note, which named these two tickets as the place to decide it; what is **not** narrowed is that an *applied* Promotion is still priced against the assignment it is applied to and from that application's own snapshot (#635 section 16) — these two columns are a catalogue-side preview of what the Promotion grants, nothing an assignment reads. Nothing is stored and nothing is charged; no migration. The Membership Fee Promotion keeps its own five-column grid and its own data model, with `DURATION (MONTHS)` renamed to `DURATION` in both halves of the section (the already-present `promotions.col_duration` key; the two `(months)` keys are gone). Terminology only — the stored `duration_months` is still a count of calendar months bounded by the Promotion's own timeline (#625, #892, #899), and no value was converted. Tests: `api/src/test/promotion-benefit-prices.test.ts` (10 integration) and `apps/admin/src/test/promotion-benefit-columns.test.ts` (11), plus the updated Promotions block in `apps/admin/src/test/plan-benefit-columns.test.ts`.)

- **Done**: #922 (**Billing Event Simulation on Promotions.** The Promotion card gains the third projection #915 gave the Membership Plan card, over the items a Promotion affects: one group per **billing date**, each line showing the Regular Price beside the Final Price the Promotion's own `(action, value)` pair gives it (#896), with a per-date total and VAT-inclusive amounts. It is the *same* projection rather than a second one — the ticket's load-bearing clause is "do not create a second pricing engine [...] the only difference should be the input/context" — so #915's shared parts (the result shape, the `SIMULATED_CYCLES` horizon floor, the group-by-date re-grouping) moved into the new pure `api/src/domain/billingEventSimulation.ts` and each entity keeps only its adapter: `promotionBillingEventSimulation.ts` expresses the Promotion as the hypothetical assignment of a member given it today and hands it to `computeBillingSimulation()`, so every date, covered unit/period and amount is the engine's — the code the nightly run prices an applied Promotion with. A `Once`/`Per Session` grant appears once for its whole quantity; a periodical grant recurs at its own frequency, discounted for the periods its quantity covers and at the regular price afterwards; frequencies stay independent and only coincide when their dates do. **No Membership Fee line**: a Promotion has no fee of its own (it belongs to the Membership Plan the Promotion is applied alongside), so inventing one would advertise a price no member is promised — what a Promotion does to a fee is the Example Timeline beside it, and the disclaimer says so. Served by `GET /promotions/:id/billing-event-simulation`, loaded with the card's other sub-resources and refreshed whenever a Benefit section is saved; the grant prices come from #919/#920's own `grossBenefitUnitPrice()`, so the two halves of one card cannot quote an item two ways. Read-only, persisted nowhere, charges nothing, no migration. Admin: #915's component moved to the shared `apps/admin/src/components/BillingEventSimulation.tsx` and both cards render it, with the wire shape and price-label mapping in the JSX-free `lib/billingEventSimulation.ts` and every label resolved in the calling page's namespace.)

- **Done**: #926 (**`Applies To` on a Promotion — Membership Plan or Product.** A Promotion was implicitly always about a Membership Plan: it carried a Membership Fee Benefit and a set of Suitable Membership Plans, and both sections were shown whatever the gym was actually configuring. `promotions.applies_to` (migration 204, `VARCHAR(32) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'membership_plan'` + `chk_promotions_applies_to`) makes the target explicit, and the editor's new `APPLIES TO` radio group hides the two Membership-Plan-specific sections — **Membership Fee Promotion** and **Suitable Membership Plans** — while the target is `Sellable Item`. The accepted set is declared once in `api/src/domain/promotionTarget.ts` and mirrored for the radio group in `apps/admin/src/lib/promotionTargets.ts` (a unit test asserts the two lists, the default and the locale keys agree), so a new target goes in those two places plus the CHECK. **Configuration only**: nothing reads the column — no apply, pricing, snapshot or eligibility path — so every existing Promotion keeps behaving exactly as it did and the default backfills every row to `membership_plan` in one statement. Switching the target **migrates and reinterprets nothing** (the ticket's §4): the `promotion_membership_fee_benefits` row and the `promotion_membership_plans` rows are left exactly as stored, so switching back shows what was there before — which is why `PUT /promotions/:id` writes this column alone and why the admin's main save *skips* the replace-all `PUT /:id/plans` while the target is `sellable_item` instead of sending the hidden draft (a `[]` there would silently discard the gym's selection). Both halves of the card ask one predicate (`targetsMembershipPlan()`), so a hidden section cannot be visible in the read-only view and missing from the form; the radio updates the card at once and closes the Membership Fee editor it hides; `⋮ → Details` reports the target too; `POST /:id/duplicate` copies it. The Example Timeline, Billing & Duration, the three Product Benefit sections and the Billing Event Simulation are **not** hidden: the first two are the Promotion's own Free/Paid/Bonus timeline (which also bounds a grant's coverage) and the last carries no Membership Fee line by design (#922). Out of scope per the ticket: multiple Products, bundles, a per-item benefit for the target item, a Billing Event Simulation for a Product Promotion and any pricing change. Tests: `api/src/test/promotion-target.unit.test.ts` (9) and an `applies_to` block in `api/src/test/promotions.test.ts` (9 integration).)

- **Done**: #928 (**The Members list is the same list as Products.** Members was the last staff list still rendered by `DataTable`: its column widths were per-cell hints with nothing underneath them, and its five filters sat unlabelled in a page-level toolbar above the table, so the screen read as a different design system from Products and Training Plans beside it. It now wears the shared shape and nothing else changed: the column titles and every collapsed row are laid out from one `LIST_COLUMNS` declaration (Name, Email, NIF/NIE/Passport, Payment, Enrollment, Actions — the same columns, in the same order, showing the same values), header band and rows share that grid inside one `overflow-x: auto` wrapper so a long name or email can no longer push a row's values out of line with its header (#637), and the surface, band, cell insets and dividers come from `listChrome.ts` — the ones `DataTable` itself is built from (#724) — rather than a copy, so the list cannot drift from the tables that still use it. The five filters move into `FilterBar`/`FilterField` with `filterControlStyle`, each one carrying its label instead of relying on a placeholder (two new keys, `members.filter_search` and `members.filter_document`, in en/es/ca; the other three labels already existed); what each filter *does* — the query params, the URL sync, the Center filter appearing only for a gym with more than one center — is untouched. The whole collapsed row is the expand control, as on Products, and stays keyboard-operable the way the chevron button it replaces was (`role="button"`, `tabIndex`, `aria-expanded`, Enter/Space); the chevron is decorative beside the ⋮ menu in the Actions cell, since a second control nested inside a `role="button"` row is not one. `+ Add Member` drops the page's own copy of `btnStyle` for the shared helper at the same colour the `+ Add Sellable Item` button uses. Everything else is deliberately as it was: the expanded body is still the recessed `listExpandedStyle` surface holding the read-only Profile and, behind `⋮ → Edit`, the inline form (#797/#882); creation is still the modal it was (inline creation is #805's shape for other pages, not this ticket's); the ⋮ menu, its order and every action are unchanged. Frontend only: no migration, no API change, no new component. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/members-list-ui.test.ts` (19), which pins both halves — the new structure, and the columns, values, filters, actions and expand/collapse that must not have moved with it.)

- **Done**: #929 (**The Member card wears the application's own chrome, in both modes.** #928 brought the Members *list* onto the shared card list; inside an expanded Member the card was still its own design system. Nine files — the read-only body, the inline Edit form, the Profile layout and the six sections — had each grown a private copy of the same objects, with the numbers drifting between them: two spellings of the uppercase section header, inputs at 13px on a 6px radius in one section and 14px on a 4px radius in the next, three different hairline greys, and Save buttons in a hardcoded lilac, a black box and a green no Theme could reach. `apps/admin/src/components/formChrome.ts` is now the one declaration of all of it — the two heading levels, a section's spacing and the rule that separates it from the section above, the card a section's contents sit in, the inline editor box, the muted/hint text, the field label, the single control box an `<input>`/`<select>`/`<textarea>` all wear, the read-only value box, the checkbox row, the help/field-error/form-error lines, the two action rows, the neutral half of a button pair, the dashed `+ Add` and the in-card text link — and every Member card file spreads it, so the card cannot drift from itself or from Products, whose inline form's numbers it takes. Three visible consequences: a read-only value now sits exactly where its input sits (`formValueStyle` carries `formControlStyle`'s padding, type size and transparent border of the same width — every value used to jump 10px sideways as `⋮ → Edit` opened); the sections are separated by the card's own themed hairline, with the first carrying none, so the card reads as one structure rather than a stack of independent fields (§4); and the Profile's two modes render the same section header and the same card around the same layout (`MemberProfileLayout`, #882), with the Edit form's Save moved onto the Theme's primary-button colours (#912) and its Cancel onto the matching secondary — as were the Extend expiration and Book pairs. Every border, background and link colour that had a Theme variable now reads it, with the old literal as the `var()` fallback, so nothing looks different until a gym themes it. Frontend only, and deliberately nothing else: no data, no API, no validation rule, no permission, no new field and no change to the Members list or its filters — the ticket's `New Member` badge is #927's field and is not invented here. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/member-card-chrome.test.ts` (8), which lists every file the card is built from and fails if the directory grows one that is not listed, asserts none of them restates a section header, an input border, a card border or a primary colour, and pins the shared objects' own relationships; #882's layout test keeps passing with its PROFILE-section locator relaxed to match the opening tag now that the first section carries `divider={false}`.)

- **Done**: #930 (**Center selection is the Calendar's, not the application header's.** `CenterSelector` sat in `TopHeader`, so every section of the admin app showed a gym-wide `All centers` dropdown — which read as a selection they all obeyed, and the Members list never did: it has had its own Center filter in its own filter bar all along, and the header control changed nothing on that page. The dropdown is deleted from the header (the component with it) and its one control is now `[locale]/calendar/CalendarCenterFilter.tsx`, in the Calendar's filter bar, wearing `FilterField` + `filterControlStyle` like any other page filter rather than the header band's translucent box. The *state* is deliberately unchanged: it is still the app's one `CenterContext`, because that is what `apiClient` sends as `x-center-id` and what `resolveCenterId()` defaults a write's center from (class sessions, spaces, rooms, trainer availability) and what `ThemeProvider` paints a Center's own theme from — forking it into page-local state would have cost a multi-center gym both. What is new is that the selection now narrows the calendar itself, which the header dropdown never did: the page passes `center_id` to `GET /class-sessions` (which already accepted it) and to `GET /calendar-events`, which gains the filter — a manual event whose `center_id IS NULL` is gym-wide and stays visible under every center, mirroring #478's rule on the member side, while a session's center is mandatory so that filter is strict. Nothing renders for a single-center gym, as before. Out of scope and untouched: the Members list and all five of its filters, member↔center assignment, every permission and `centerContext`'s own `allowedCenterIds` rules. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/calendar-center-filter.test.ts` (19) pins both halves — no header selector and no second copy of the control, the Calendar's filter and its query param, and the Members-side filter that must not have moved with it — plus a `GET /calendar-events?center_id` block in `api/src/test/calendar-events.test.ts` (5) covering the narrowing, the gym-wide row, combination with the other filters and tenant isolation.)

- **Done**: #931 (**Promotions removed from Members.** A Promotion applies to a target entity — a **Membership Plan** or a **Product** — so #634 §13's Member-level `PROMOTIONS` section was the wrong place for it: a Member benefits from a Promotion because of what they are assigned or buy, never because one was attached to them directly. The section is gone from the Member card (`MemberPromotions.tsx` deleted, with its `+ Add Promotion` button, its `No promotions applied` message, its Plan→Promotion picker and the seventeen `members.promotions_*`/`section_promotions` keys in en/es/ca), and `GET /user-memberships/member/:memberId/configuration` no longer reports a `promotions` array — which also drops one `fetchAppliedPromotions()` round trip per live assignment from every Member card expansion. The Member-level read is now `{plans, services}`; `new_member_eligible` stays on each plan, since it is a property of the Member and the apply paths are its enforcement point either way. Deliberately unchanged, because this is where the Promotion still lives: `promotions` and every `promotion_*` table, `POST`/`DELETE /user-memberships/:id/promotions`, `applyPromotionToMembership()`, `validatePromotionSelection()`, the #628 Promotions section of the **Assign New Plan** editor (the Promotions a plan is assigned *with* — the indirect path the ticket describes), `POST /membership-plans/:id/assign`'s auto-apply, the Assigned Plans card's own applied-Promotion cards (`AssignedPlanPromotions.tsx`, read from each application's own snapshot, #635 §16) and the Member's Billing Simulation, which still shows the discounts an applied Promotion produces. No migration and no data touched: every `user_membership_promotions` row and snapshot stays exactly as it was. Tests: new `apps/admin/src/test/member-promotions-removed.test.ts` (9) pins the absence — the deleted component, the dropped keys in all three locales, no apply call from any Member surface — alongside what must survive (the Assign New Plan selection, the Assigned Plans card, the Promotions page); `api/src/test/member-membership-configuration.test.ts` keeps applying promotions in its fixtures and asserts the payload has no `promotions` key while the rows are still in the database; `member-membership-sections.test.ts` and `member-card-chrome.test.ts` drop to the three remaining sections.)

- **Done**: #942 (**A session package's Price reads as the package total, not a per-session price.** A Product of Type `Sessions` stores the total price of the whole package, but the list put `Units: 5` and `Price: €50.00` in adjacent columns with nothing saying which of the two readings was right — €50.00 for the five, or €50.00 each and €250.00 the lot. Every surface that shows such a price now carries the sentence under it: *Total price for 5 sessions*, pluralised in ICU off the item's own `units` (`1 session` / `5 sessions`, AC 5), with *Total price for the complete package* as the wording for a session item whose nullable `units` is unset. Five call sites — the collapsed row's Price cell, the read-only expanded card, the Details modal and the Price input of **both** the inline create card and the inline editor (#805) — and one rule behind them: the pure `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/financials/products/productPriceNotes.ts`, so no page spells out `type === 'sessions'` beside a price for itself. The two form hints read the *draft's* live Type and Units rather than the saved row, so the sentence appears the moment `Sessions` is chosen and follows the count as it is typed — the person entering the figure is the one who most needs to know what it buys. The read-only card and the Details modal also gained the `(tax included)` / `(tax excluded)` suffix the list has always had (`taxNoteKey()`, which answers `null` for an item with no tax rate rather than claiming a tax that is not configured — the same condition the list already uses to choose between `amount_incl_tax` and the raw amount). **Labels only**: not one displayed figure moves, nothing multiplies a price by a unit count, both forms still submit `amount` unscaled, and there is no migration and no API change (AC 1, AC 8). Fee-based items are untouched — `sessionPackageNote()` answers `null` for every other type (AC 7). The Price column widens from 150px to 200px, since every cell on the #637 grid is nowrap-and-ellipsis and the sentence has to fit in en/es/ca. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/products-session-package-price.test.ts` (30) covers the pure rule's boundaries (1 / 5 / 10 units, null / 0 / negative / NaN / fractional, every other type), the tax suffix including the zero-rate and no-rate cases, the ICU plural in all three locales, a guard that no wording in any locale reads as a per-session price, and the five render sites.)

- **Done**: #918 (**Frequency on Session Benefits.** A Membership Plan's Session Benefits could say *5 Personal Training Classes* but not *2 Personal Training Classes per week*, so a recurring session allowance was inexpressible. Each Session Benefit now carries a Frequency beside its quantity — `frequency` on `membership_plan_session` and on the assignment-side `user_membership_session` (migration 205), nullable with no default and no backfill, because `NULL` is the dropdown's `—` and is exactly what every benefit stored before the ticket means: a one-time allowance granted on the start date. It is **its own accepted set**, declared once in `api/src/domain/sessionBenefitFrequency.ts` (`once, week, four_weeks, month, year`), mirrored by `chk_<table>_frequency` and by `apps/admin/src/lib/sessionBenefitFrequency.ts` — three places, asserted by tests on both sides. `Weekly` is offered here although #821 retired `week` from `gym_charges.billing_frequency`: how often an allowance *renews* and how often an item is *priced* are different questions, which is the thread's Q2 answer ("It is the way to cover 1 session per week or 2 sessions per week. Products cannot be purchased per weeks"), and `per_session` is not a period so it is not offered. The replace-all `PUT` follows #896's three-way rule — a request that names no Frequency **keeps** the stored one, since a quantity-only save is what every client written before this ticket sends, and clearing it is the explicit `frequency: null` — and the value is **part of the agreement**: `snapshotAssignedPlan()` copies it onto the assignment and `writeAssignedPlanBenefitSection()` keeps it on a kept line, so a Plan switched from Weekly to Monthly afterwards cannot move what an existing member was agreed (#635 §13–§17). In the **simulation** the allowance is summarised onto the billing events that already exist rather than generating weekly ones (the thread's Q1 answer: "I don't expect having one per every week, instead I'd like to summarize it based on billing event frequency"): `buildSessionAllowanceStream()` runs the item at the *assignment's own* cadence and reports `quantity ×` the renewals falling in each cycle, so a 4-weekly Plan with `2 | Weekly | 50% discount` reads **8 sessions, €400 regular, €200 charged** on every billing date — the ticket's own worked example — while a monthly Plan reports the 5 renewals a 31-day cycle really contains and the 4 of the next, never a fractional 4.35. `once`, `—` and an assignment with no cadence keep the single start-date charge unchanged; a cycle with no renewal shows no line rather than "0 sessions"; and a Promotion's session grant still grants its units **in total** rather than per cycle (`allocateSessionUnits()` allocates the line's units as intervals with an offset for what earlier cycles consumed, which for a single charge is the pre-ticket arithmetic to the cent). `sessionFrequency` is a *required* field of `SimulationPlanBenefit` for the reason `benefit` and `personalFeeBenefit` are: a loader that forgot it would quietly show a member 2 sessions where their contract entitles them to 8. Admin: **one** column, not a second — the shared `PRODUCT_BENEFIT_COLUMNS` Frequency cell takes a `frequencyColumn` prop (`'item'` by default, `'benefit'` only for the Plan's Session section), so the three sections stay the one aligned table #916 made them, and the Promotion sections are deliberately untouched. Still out of scope and still nobody's behaviour: a consumable allowance ledger — nothing tracks how many renewed sessions a member has used, and booking eligibility is unchanged. Tests: `api/src/test/session-benefit-frequency.unit.test.ts` (21, including the migration/domain list agreement and the renewal arithmetic), a `#918` block in `billing-simulation.test.ts` (6) and in `plan-billing-event-simulation.test.ts` (2), 10 integration cases in `membership-plan-benefits.test.ts`, 4 in `assigned-plan-snapshot.test.ts` (freeze, no drift, keep-on-edit, end-to-end simulation) and `apps/admin/src/test/session-benefit-frequency-ui.test.ts` (14).)

- **Done**: #927 (**The Member's automatically calculated `New Member` status.** A Member is a New Member when they have not had an active Membership Plan during the previous **six** months. The calculation already existed — `domain/newMemberEligibility.ts`, #634 §3 — so the ticket added no second rule: it shortened that one window from twelve months to six (the answer on the thread was *"6 months here and in promotions"*, so the badge a gym reads and the window the four Promotion apply paths enforce are the same constant, which is §5's point in both directions) and exposed it per Member. `GET /members` now projects `is_new_member` for the whole page in **one** query (`newMemberStatusByMember()`, never one round trip per row and never a second SQL copy of the rule) and `GET /members/:id` reports the same field through `isNewMemberStatus()`; neither excludes anything, because a Member-level answer has no assignment being configured, so a Member holding a live plan reads `☐` and a Member with no assignment at all reads `☑`. Nothing is stored: no column, no migration, no writer — the status tracks the Membership history and the passing of the window on its own, and `PUT /members/:id`'s explicit field list cannot write it. On the admin side it is a **calculated field of the Profile**: `MEMBER_PROFILE_FIELDS` carries it with `calculated: true` and `MemberProfileLayout` renders such a field through its own `renderCalculated` callback in both modes, so the Edit form has no way to grow an input under that label, the key is absent from `MemberEditFormValues` and from the `PUT` payload, and reading and editing draw the same cell (`NewMemberValue`, a ticked or unticked box with an `aria-label` rather than a disabled checkbox). The member header carries the same value as an accent name-cell badge (#913's voice, `listNameBadgeAccentStyle`), so a new Member is identifiable without expanding the row. **This changes Promotion eligibility**: every Member who left between six and twelve months ago is now eligible for a new-members-only Promotion — the plain reading of the ticket, confirmed on the thread. Out of scope and untouched: Promotion configuration and its eligibility UI, Product and bundle Promotions, and any manual override. Tests: `api/src/test/new-member-eligibility.test.ts` grows the six-month window, the cutoff day itself and the Member-level "nothing excluded" case (22); an `is_new_member` block in `api/src/test/members.test.ts` (8) covers the ticket's four examples, both reads agreeing, per-Member answers in one page, that a `PUT` naming the field changes nothing, and that another gym's assignment never reaches it; and `apps/admin/src/test/member-new-member-status.test.ts` (12) pins the declaration, that there is nothing to type into or submit, the one value in both places and that the frontend derives no window of its own.)

- **Done**: #945 (**Per Session removed from the Product Billing Frequency.** A session package's size is its **Units** and its Billing Frequency is when the whole package is billed — `Sessions / 10 units / €500 / Once` means €500 for the ten (#942) — so *Per Session* named a usage-based model the Product does not have. The selector now offers `—`, *Once*, *4 Weeks*, *Month*, *Year*. This is #821's retirement pattern applied a second time, which is the whole change: `per_session` moves from `OFFERED_PRODUCT_FREQUENCIES` to `LEGACY_…` in `api/src/domain/productFrequency.ts` and in its form mirror, so `POST /sellable-items` refuses it, `PUT /sellable-items/:id` accepts it **only** on a row that already stores it (editing another field of a legacy item neither 400s nor rewrites its frequency), nothing may be moved onto it — `week` included — and `POST /:id/duplicate` copies it verbatim, because Duplicate is a copy and not the dropdown. **No migration and no backfill**: `gym_charges_billing_frequency_check` (migration 123) still permits all six values, so every existing row stays valid, reads back, classifies into the same benefit section (`per_session` is non-recurring, exactly as `once` is) and bills as before. The backfill §3 invites "where the intended behaviour is known" was declined although `cadenceForProduct()` gives `per_session` and `once` the same (absent) schedule: what a gym *meant* by the value is not knowable from the row, so the ticket's own fallback applies and the editor is where the correction happens — the retired value renders as a **disabled** option while the form holds it, with the notice beside it now **naming** that value (`frequency_legacy_notice` interpolates it in en/es/ca, since there are two retired values now; the key is resolved before `t()` is called, never through a `defaultValue` next-intl does not have). §2's "configuring a Product within other entities" needed nothing: the offered list has exactly one consumer and every other surface only *displays* a stored frequency, so a legacy row still reads *Per Session* where it is stored — pinned by a test. `week` stays **offered** by a Session Benefit's own Frequency (#918), which is a different question and a different set. §4: the Sessions model is untouched — no column, no API shape and no charge changes. Tests: `api/src/test/product-frequency.unit.test.ts` (13, both retired values in both directions), a `#945` block in `gym-charges.test.ts` (10 integration: the refused create, the ticket's own 10-session package on *Once*, a stored `per_session` item read, edited, duplicated and corrected, the one-way move, and the 400 naming the four offered values) and `apps/admin/src/test/product-frequency.test.ts` (15).)

- **Done**: #946 (**The Pre-paid Membership Fee is charged, not waived.** A Plan sold as *€70/month, Paid Duration 3, Pre-paid Duration 3* means the member pays €210 up front and is then charged nothing until the fourth period, but every prepaid period priced at 0 with a `waive` benefit: the Billing Event Simulation read `Membership Fee · Waived · €0.00` where the gym had sold €210, and — because `POST /payment-requests` refuses a cycle that owes nothing — a brand-new prepaid assignment could not raise its first payment at all, never stored a card and was skipped by the nightly run for ever. The whole Pre-paid Duration is now owed **once, on the first of its periods**, and the periods it pays for generate no Membership Fee event. One pure rule decides it, `prepaidPeriodsDueOn()` in `api/src/domain/planDuration.ts`, keyed on the *period* and never on the exact billing date (a first payment raised a day late still owes the same €210), clamped by the Paid Duration and counted in the assignment's own Billing Frequency periods (#892 — a 4-weekly Plan's two pre-paid periods are 56 days, not two months). It is read in exactly one place, `resolveMembershipFee()`, which is why no surface could be fixed on its own: the nightly run charges the lump on that cycle (`recurring_payment`, the amount in minor units at the provider boundary) and still records `waived_billing · prepaid_plan` for the cycles it covered, a staff- or member-raised payment request asks for it, and the Payments dashboard, My Membership, the Example Timeline and the Billing Event Simulation all quote it.
