Three properties keep the rest of the engine intact: the charge carries **no benefit line** (it is the regular price of the periods it pays for — it reports a `prepaid_periods` count instead, which is what the admin's *3 periods prepaid* note renders), a covered period answers `quantity: 0` and so reuses #918's existing "not a billing event" path rather than a new branch, and the charge stays `promotional` in the horizon's sense so a projection runs on to the first ordinary charge instead of stopping at the lump. A governing Promotion still decides the fee alone (#635 Q2) and the Personal Membership Fee Benefit still discounts every period the lump covers (#772). Admin: the Example Timeline's first Pre-paid row charges (`waived: false`, `€210.00 VAT included · 3 periods prepaid`, amber rather than the green "no charge" tone), the Billing Event Simulation's first group carries one fee line of `quantity: 3 / €210.00 / Regular price` and the Member card's own simulation notes the same count — every figure the server's, no arithmetic in any page (#817). No migration, no new endpoint, and nothing else about a billing event changed: Products, billing frequencies, benefits, waives, discounts, mandatory items, one-off and periodic items all behave exactly as before (§4). Tests: `api/src/test/plan-prepaid-first-charge.unit.test.ts` (19 — the rule's boundaries including the 4-weekly case and the Free Period offset, the fee resolver's lump / covered / resumed cycles, the Promotion precedence and the personal discount, and the ticket's own €70 × 3 example end to end through the Plan projection), updated prepaid expectations in `plan-prepaid-duration.test.ts`, `plan-example-timeline.test.ts`, `membership-plans.test.ts` and `billing-run-waived.test.ts` (where the prepaid cycle is now a 2 × 29.99 charge with the covered cycle waived beside it), and `apps/admin/src/test/prepaid-membership-fee-note.test.ts` (5).)

- **Done**: #947 (**The Nutrition Library is three catalogues: Foods, Personal Goals, Nutrition Goals.** Both libraries — a gym's `nutrition/nutrition-library` and Cordel's `cordel/nutrition-library` — are now three tabs over the same declaration, each tab an independent library with its own search, list, `+ Add` and row actions (§1, §2, §6, §7). Migration 206 adds `personal_goals` and `nutrition_goals`: **two tables, not one with a `kind` column**, because §8 keeps the concepts apart in the data model as well as the UI and §9 already has a Nutrition Goal eventually carrying a target value a Personal Goal never will. Ownership is the Foods library's, unchanged (§5): `gym_id IS NULL` is a platform-owned **System** row, read-only to every gym and administered from Cordel; a set `gym_id` is that gym's own, editable and soft-deletable (`status = 'deleted'`, with `deleted_at` and #799's three actor pairs). Because the two tables are identical in shape, there is **one router factory per side** — `goal-library.ts` mounted at `/personal-goals` and `/nutrition-goals`, `platform-goal-library.ts` at the `/platform` pair — over one declaration, `api/src/domain/goalLibrary.ts`, which owns the kinds, their tables, their audit entity types and the seeded rows; a third goal catalogue would be a line there plus a table, not a third router. Two details are load-bearing. Uniqueness is among **live** rows (a `live_name_key` VIRTUAL generated column non-NULL only while the row is not deleted, carrying the UNIQUE index — migration 183's device), so the index agrees with the routers' `status != 'deleted'` duplicate check and a name a gym deleted is re-usable rather than reserved for ever. And `slug` is a **System** row's label handle and only a System row's (`chk_<prefix>_slug_system_only`): the seven seeded rows of each kind are translated through `goal_library.<kind>_goal_<slug>` with the row's own `name` as the fallback — the `result_types` rule — which is why these catalogues need no per-locale junction table, and why neither router ever writes one. The slugs are the Nutrition Plan routers' own `NUTRITION_GOALS` vocabulary partitioned between the two concepts, plus **Fasting** (§4), so a later ticket can link a plan's goal to its catalogue row without a rename. **Nothing reads them yet**: assigning goals to plans or members and defining target values are explicitly out of scope, so `member_nutrition_plan_goals`, `nutrition_plan_template_goals` and the closed vocabulary they validate against are untouched, and Foods behaves exactly as before. Admin: `apps/admin/src/components/goalLibrary/` is the one declaration both pages import — the tab list, the four router roots, the row shape and payloads, the System-slug mirror, the tab strip, the Details modal and one `GoalLibrarySection` serving all four screens, which names no endpoint and decides no permission (#806) and keeps #797–#800's read-only-expanded / edit-behind-`⋮` model with inline create (#805), the shared filter bar, name badge and primary-button chrome (#724, #912, #929). New `goal_library` namespace in en/es/ca. Tests: `api/src/test/goal-library.test.ts` (72) and `platform-goal-library.test.ts` (54) — tenant isolation, auth and the role matrix, the System read-only 403s, the live-name uniqueness including re-creating a deleted name, the actor masking, validation and the `?status=deleted` listing; `goal-library-domain.unit.test.ts` (14) pins the declaration against the migration; `apps/admin/src/test/goal-library-ui.test.ts` (30) pins the tabs, the four roots against `app.ts`, the slug mirror, the label fallback, the read-only body and the locale keys.)

- **Done**: #954 (**Every primary action in the Admin app follows the Theme's Primary Button colour.** #901 made the subsection `Edit` button the first consumer of the Buttons group and #912 extended it to the two Theme screens — but the rest of the app still passed the legacy lilac literal to `btnStyle()`/`btnSmall()` at **56 call sites across 27 files**, so a gym that set *Primary Button = red* saw red on `Save changes` in the Theme editor and lilac on every other Save, Add, Create, New, Assign and Apply button in the product — including `CrudModal`'s shared Save, which every modal in the app renders. All of them now call `primaryBtnStyle()`/`primaryBtnSmall()`, and the two Calendar detail panels' full-width actions — which carry their own geometry rather than a helper's — spread `primaryActionColors` exactly where their `background: '#6c63ff', color: '#fff'` pair sat, so the trailing `opacity`/`cursor` overrides still win. **No new Theme token, no new helper, no geometry change** (§2, §4): the spread replaces two colour properties and nothing else, a `readOnlyStyle(primaryBtnStyle(), !canWrite)` dims the themed pair exactly as it dimmed the lilac, and no label, handler, `disabled` decision, permission gate, translation or locale key moved. Deliberately **not** converted (§5): the three file-picker controls — `ImageUploadField`, `ExerciseImageField`, `ExerciseVideoField` — because a picker is not its form's primary action, its Save is, and CLAUDE.md already rules that file-picker, secondary and destructive buttons keep their own colours; plus text links (whose token is the Links group's `--gd-link`, a separate decision), selected filter chips and selection/accent borders, which carry **state** rather than an action, and every `--brand` consumer, which is the sidebar's colour by design (§3). The enforcement is a scan rather than a list: `apps/admin/src/test/theme-primary-buttons.test.ts` walks every `.ts`/`.tsx` under `apps/admin/src` outside `src/test` and fails on a `btnStyle('#6c63ff')`, a `btnSmall('#6c63ff')` or a flat `background: '#6c63ff'` outside the three named pickers, and on any module but `ui.tsx` (and `themeTokens.ts`, which writes them) naming `--gd-primary-btn` for itself — so the next page with a Save button is told rather than trusted. It also pins the live-preview link §6 asks for: `applyTokens()` writes the edited Buttons group into exactly the two variables the helpers read, which is why the Theme editor needs no reload. Frontend only: no migration, no API change, no member app. Tests: 9 added to `theme-primary-buttons.test.ts` (19 total); `members-list-ui.test.ts`'s `+ Add` assertion moved with the helper it pins. Admin suite green at 1,571.)

- **Done**: #955 (**The Billing Event Simulation is collapsible billing-period cards.** The section was a vertically stacked list of every billing event with a `Total` row under each date's lines, which on a Plan carrying four Products filled a screen before the second billing date appeared. It is now one **collapsible card per billing date**: the header carries the date, how many lines fall on it and that date's total — so the per-date `Total` row is gone, the total moved into the header — the lines appear underneath when the card is open, and a single *Expand all* / *Collapse all* control sits above them, rendered only when there is more than one period and always reflecting whether every card is currently open. The first period is expanded and the rest collapsed, each card still toggles on its own, and the whole header is the control (a `<button>` carrying `aria-expanded`, the shape the list rows use, with the chevron decorative beside it). The detail table is deliberately **not** a new design: `ExampleTimeline.tsx` now exports its header and body cells (`timelineThStyle`/`timelineTdStyle`) and its three tones (`TIMELINE_TONE_BACKGROUND`/`TIMELINE_TONE_TEXT`) and the simulation imports them, so Date / Billing Event / Status / Amount reads in the same row density, borders, typography and colour semantics as the Example Timeline above it on the same card, and no hex carrying a billing meaning is declared twice (a test asserts the component contains none of the five). Which tone a line reads in is the new pure `simulationLineTone()` beside the wire shape — green for a `waive`d or `included` line, amber for any other action and for #946's `prepaid_periods` lump, grey for an ordinary charge — taken from the treatment the server reported and never from the amount, since €0.00 is also what an item with no price costs. The open/closed state is pure too (`initialExpandedPeriods()`, `allExpandedPeriods()`, `everyPeriodExpanded()`), which is what makes the ticket's interaction rules testable in an app with no component-test infra, and it is re-seeded only when the set of billing dates itself changes, so the Promotion card's late-arriving projection cannot close a card the viewer has just opened. **One presentation, every consumer**: both cards already rendered the one shared `apps/admin/src/components/BillingEventSimulation.tsx` (#922), so the Membership Plan and Promotion sections are converted by converting it and neither page gained a card, a toggle or a table of its own. The ticket's third context, an **Assigned Membership Plan**, has no Billing Event Simulation on `main` to convert — #924 stage 3 is adding a *Membership Fee* Simulation (the Example Timeline) to that card, and the Member's own consolidated Billing Simulation (#629) is a different section with its own grouping, per-section totals and grand total — so it inherits these cards the moment it renders this component, which is what the shared component is for. Presentation only, as §"Preserve existing business logic" requires: no API, no migration, no new endpoint, nothing persisted, and every date, amount, treatment and total still the server's — expanding a card fetches nothing and recomputes nothing. Seven new keys per namespace in en/es/ca (`simulation_items_count` ICU-pluralised, the two control labels and the four column headers), none of them saying "Benefit" in the Promotions namespace (#896 §3). Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/billing-event-simulation-cards.test.ts` (20) — the tone rule including the waive-wins and €0-is-not-free cases, the three state helpers, one component for both consumers, the borrowed palette, the total in the header, the `aria-expanded` control, the single-period case and the labels in both namespaces — with the two existing simulation files' "no arithmetic in the page" assertions still passing unchanged.)

- **Done**: #957 (**“Add Product”: edit-only, themed, and named Product.** The Member card's ADDITIONAL SERVICES section offered its add action as a lilac text link that was present whether the card was being read or edited — so an expanded Member row carried a write affordance (#797 says expanding reads and `⋮ → Edit` writes), and a gym that set *Buttons → Primary Button* saw the colour everywhere except here. The action is now a real button wearing `primaryBtnSmall()` — the same Theme pair #912/#954 gave every other primary action, no new style, no new token and no hardcoded lilac — and the shared editor (`financials/assigned-plans/AdditionalPeriodicServices.tsx`, rendered by both the Assigned Plan card and the Member card) takes a `canAdd` prop, default `true`: the Member card passes its existing Edit-mode flag, so the button is **absent** in the read-only view rather than disabled, while the Assigned Plans card — which has no edit mode — passes nothing and keeps the action it always had. The copy is the **final Product terminology** the ticket's implementation note asks for (*Additional Products*, *Additional Periodic Products*, *+ Add Product*, *Select a product…*, and the needs-a-plan line) in en/es/ca, so no screen shows an intermediate "Add Service" state while #949 is still unanswered. Only the locale **values** moved — the `services_*` keys are identifiers and renaming those is #949 stage 2's, which is also why this does not pre-empt #924 stage 5's §11 rename of the same section. *Professional Services* (`pt_slots_no_services`) and the persisted audit action `add_service` are deliberately untouched, as is the `Sellable Item` column header, which names the entity #949 owns. Frontend only: no API, no migration, no payload, no billing rule, and the table, the removal action and the needs-a-plan line all still read in both modes (§"Preserve"). Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/add-product-edit-mode.test.ts` (10 — the render gate and its `true` default, absent-not-disabled, the themed helper and the three lilac forms it must not carry, the preserved read-only affordance, and every section string in all three locales saying "product" and not "servic"/"servei").

- **Done**: #960 (**`New Member` reads as a Yes/No chip on the label's own row.** #927 drew the Member Profile's calculated status as a ticked or unticked box (`☑` / `☐`) in the box an `<input>` occupies, so one boolean cost the card a label, a 14px gap and a full value box of height — and a glyph is a worse answer than a word for a field nobody can change. The value is now a compact chip beside its label: *New Member  Yes*. **No colour and no shape are declared for it**: `newMemberChipStyle()` spreads the list's own metadata pill (#724) — the accent voice (#913) for `Yes`, which is literally the pill the Members list already puts beside a new member's name, and the neutral one for `No` — overriding nothing but the left margin the name cell wanted, so the badge in the row and the value in the card below it read as one statement and a test pins both halves to `listChrome.ts`. The layout change is the shared `MemberProfileLayout`'s, not either half's: a `calculated` field renders label-and-value inline while an editable one keeps the stacked shape, because that stacked box is where its `<input>` sits in the other mode (#929) — so the read-only Profile and the Edit form still place the field identically, still walk `MEMBER_PROFILE_FIELDS` once, and the Edit form still has no way to grow a control under it (#927 §4). Accessibility is the chip's `aria-label`, which keeps #927's full sentence (*New Member: yes*) rather than leaving a screen reader with a bare "Yes" next to a label that is not programmatically tied to it; the two keys are resolved by `newMemberValueKey()` / `newMemberAnnounceKey()` in `memberProfile.ts` so neither half picks its own wording. Presentation only, as the ticket requires: `is_new_member` is still the server's one calculation (`NEW_MEMBER_WINDOW_MONTHS`, #927), nothing is submitted, no API, no migration, no member app, and `members.yes` / `members.no` are the only new keys (en/es/ca, the per-namespace convention `plans`, `promotions` and `products` already follow). Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/member-new-member-chip.test.ts` (10 — no glyph left anywhere, the chip is read-only and declares no hex, both pills come from `listChrome`, the inline row vs the stacked editable field, one field set in both modes, and the labels in all three locales), with #927's own file updated to the chip and its label check extended to the two new keys. Admin suite green at 1,616.)

- **Done**: #924 (**Assigned Plans match Membership Plans — staged, 5 PRs, all landed.** The Assigned Plan card had a visual system of its own, so the ticket's §1 ("do not create a separate visual system for Assigned Plans") was answered in five independently useful stages, agreed on the issue thread: **1** the three snapshot Benefit sections adopt the shared column grid with an Agreed/Final price pair; **2** the applied-Promotion sections adopt it too, from each application's own snapshot (§6); **3** a Membership Fee Simulation — the Plan card's Example Timeline for this assignment, over its own cadence and durations (§7); **4** a Billing Event Simulation, a third adapter over `domain/billingEventSimulation.ts` expressing *this* assignment rather than a hypothetical one (§8–§10); **5** the card's own chrome, section order and read-only/Edit split, with Additional Periodic Services kept and renamed *Additional Products* (§11). **Stage 1 landed**: `AssignedPlanConfiguration`'s hand-rolled `<table>` is replaced by the shared `ProductBenefitView` over `PRODUCT_BENEFIT_COLUMNS` (#916, #919/#920) — the same six columns at the same horizontal positions as the Plan and Promotion cards, `showFrequency` true for all three sections so a One-off line keeps its cell with a `—`, the Session section's Frequency cell showing the **agreed renewal Frequency** (#918) and the Benefit column reporting the line's frozen `(action, value)` pair in the Plan's option set. The prices are the server's and are not a third implementation: `loadAssignedPlanSnapshot()` / `loadAssignedPlanBenefitSection()` report `original_price_incl_tax` / `final_price_incl_tax` plus the two line totals through `productBenefitPrices()`, the single-row entry point extracted from `withProductBenefitPrices()` (`api/src/api/product-benefit-pricing.ts` over `applyLineBenefit()`), so a waived €100 package reads €100 → €0.00 and cannot disagree with what the nightly run charges. What makes this side different is *which* amount is priced: the line's **frozen** `unit_price`, never `gym_charges.amount`, so a Product repriced or a Plan re-configured afterwards moves nothing (#635 §17). The one live column read is the Product's tax treatment — a statutory rate the snapshot never captured and the only way to answer §4's "tax included" — LEFT JOINed, so a deleted item leaves the frozen amount as the honest gross. The **editor** is deliberately unchanged: the assignment's section `PUT` takes quantity alone and keeps a kept line's pair and Frequency (#896 stage 4, #918), so it offers no Benefit dropdown and no Frequency control — a read-only card may report a column its editor does not configure, but it must not render a control the save cannot carry. Nothing stored, nothing charged, no migration. Tests: `api/src/test/assigned-plan-benefit-prices.test.ts` (11 integration — the three actions, the unit/line pair, the VAT gross-up, the frozen price after a reprice and after a Plan re-configuration, a €0.00-agreed line, GET/PUT agreement, a retired item and tenant isolation) and `apps/admin/src/test/assigned-plan-benefit-columns.test.ts` (9). **Stage 2 landed**: the applied-Promotion grant sections (`AssignedPlanPromotions.tsx`) adopt the same grid, which retires the card's last hand-rolled `<table>` and its single `Snapshot price` column. `fetchAppliedPromotions()` now reports each grant's frozen `(action, value)` pair — read in the **Promotion**'s option set, so a `fixed_discount`/`fixed_price` grant is not normalized away as a Plan's three would make it (#896 §16) — plus `original_price_incl_tax` / `final_price_incl_tax` and the two line totals, through the same `productBenefitPrices()` the other three callers use; the amounts come from the line's **frozen** `unit_price` and the item's live tax treatment alone (one lookup per card, LEFT JOINed through `tax_rates`), so a Product repriced or a Promotion re-configured afterwards moves nothing (#635 §16/§17) and a deleted item still prices from its frozen amount. `showFrequency` is on for all three sections and `frequencyColumn` stays the item's own, since #918's renewal Frequency belongs to a Membership Plan's Session Benefits. The labels are the page's: both halves of this card share one namespace, so the Plan sections keep `item_action_*` (*Benefit*) while the grant sections resolve `promo_item_action_*` (*Promotion* / *No promotion*, #896 §3/§4) through a `grantT` wrapper. Nothing stored, nothing charged, no migration. Tests: `api/src/test/applied-promotion-grant-prices.test.ts` (9 integration — the five actions, the unit/line pair, the VAT gross-up, the frozen price after a reprice and a re-configuration, a deleted item, the no-snapshot legacy path and tenant isolation) and `apps/admin/src/test/assigned-plan-promotion-columns.test.ts` (9). **Stage 3 landed**: the card gains a **MEMBERSHIP FEE SIMULATION** between the applied Promotions and the Additional Periodic Services (§7/§11) — the Plan card's Example Timeline (#818) for a contract that really exists. §7's load-bearing clause is *"the simulation should not implement a separate calculation engine"*, so the walk itself moved into a shared `api/src/domain/exampleTimeline.ts` (period stepping, the `TRAILING_REGULAR_PERIODS` horizon, the `MAX_TIMELINE_PERIODS` cap, the open-ended final row) with one adapter per entity, exactly as #922 did for the Billing Event Simulation: `planExampleTimeline.ts` keeps the Plan's single price, and `assignmentExampleTimeline.ts` prices every row through `resolveMembershipFee()` — the one implementation the nightly run, My Membership and every staff surface already read (#635 stage 12) — so the table cannot advertise a charge the run does not make, in either direction. It is handed the very `MembershipFeeContext` `priceMembershipFeeOn()` builds (`membershipFeeContextFor()`, extracted beside `negotiatedFeeLapsed()` and `standingFeePromotions()`), so the assignment's own frozen Billing & Duration, its standing Promotions and its Personal Membership Fee Benefit (#772) are all in the numbers, counted in its own cadence (`ASSIGNMENT_CADENCE`, #892) and anchored on its own `starts_at` — rows start at the period containing today, since elapsed cycles answer nothing, while the period *numbers* still count from the contract's start. The Status column reports whichever agreement actually decided the cycle: `resolveMembershipFee()` now returns a `periodStatus`, because the precedence between an applied Promotion and the Plan's own durations is its decision alone (#635's Q2 answer) and a caller re-classifying the period would label a cycle `free_plan` that the governing Promotion is charging for. Served embedded as `example_timeline` on `GET /user-memberships/:id` and alone on `GET /user-memberships/:id/example-timeline`, the counterpart of the Plan's own route. The table is the shared `components/ExampleTimeline.tsx` with the formatting rules shared too (`apps/admin/src/lib/exampleTimeline.ts`, which `planProfile.ts` now delegates to); the page contributes only labels, and unlike the Plan card's they may say *(promotion)*. Nothing stored, nothing charged, no migration. Tests: `api/src/test/assignment-example-timeline.unit.test.ts` (15 unit — the durations, the #892 cadence, the #946 pre-paid lump, a governing Promotion, the personal benefit stacking and never extending the horizon, a lapsing negotiated fee, the start-at-today rule and the row cap), `api/src/test/assigned-plan-fee-timeline.test.ts` (12 integration — both entry points, the frozen snapshot after a Plan edit, a reprice, an applied Promotion, the no-cadence reason, auth and tenancy) and `apps/admin/src/test/assigned-plan-fee-simulation.test.ts` (10). **Stage 4 landed**: the card gains a **BILLING EVENT FORECAST** below the Membership Fee Simulation and above the Additional Periodic Services (§8–§10) — one group per billing *date*, listing every line that falls on it (the Membership Fee plus each Product and Additional Periodic Service this contract carries) with a per-date total, where the Simulation above it is one row per billing *period* about the fee alone. §8's load-bearing clause is *"do not implement a separate simulation engine for Assigned Plans"*, so it is the **third adapter** over `domain/billingEventSimulation.ts` beside the Plan's (#915) and the Promotion's (#922), and the one that does not invent its input: `api/src/domain/assignmentBillingEventSimulation.ts` is handed the real `SimulationAssignment` the Member-level Billing Simulation (#629) is built from — `loadSimulationAssignments()`, extracted from `computeMemberBillingSimulation()` and scoped to one assignment by the new `SimulationScope` — so every snapshot rule (#635 §13–§17), every standing Promotion application and every attached service is already in it. The adapter adds exactly two things, both about where the projection runs: the horizon is anchored on **today** (`horizonFrom` on `BillingSimulationInput`, counted from each stream's first occurrence at or after it, which for the Plan and Promotion previews is the same date their streams already start on, so neither moves) — without it both the two-cycle floor and the engine's 36-month cap are measured from a 2023 signup and the forecast is empty — and the groups already behind us are dropped (`from` on `groupBillingEventsByDate()`), because those are the dates the assignment has been charged on and the Billing Events ledger two sections below is the record of them. The engine still walks the contract from its own `starts_at`, since that is what decides which period a future date falls in; the amounts are therefore the ones actually billed (the frozen fee `priceMembershipFeeOn()` prices each cycle from, and each line's frozen `unit_price`), the same figures the Membership Fee Simulation quotes and the nightly run charges, with no tax arithmetic in the projection or the page (#817). No line is flagged *Mandatory* — that is a Membership Plan's question (#832/#893) and an assignment bills what it was agreed with. Served embedded as `billing_event_simulation` on `GET /user-memberships/:id` and alone on `GET /user-memberships/:id/billing-event-simulation`, the counterpart of the Plan's and Promotion's own routes; rendered by the shared `components/BillingEventSimulation.tsx` with the labels resolved in the Assigned Plans namespace. Nothing stored, nothing charged, no migration. Tests: `api/src/test/assignment-billing-event-simulation.unit.test.ts` (16 unit — the years-old contract, the `starts_at`-anchored dates, the two-cycle span, the dropped past one-off, same-date grouping, a free period, a Promotion grant, the personal benefit, an attached service, the two unavailable cases), `api/src/test/assigned-plan-billing-forecast.test.ts` (13 integration — both entry points, the frozen line after a reprice, the already-charged one-off, a waived grant, a free period, an attached service, the no-cadence and cancelled reasons, auth and tenancy) and `apps/admin/src/test/assigned-plan-billing-forecast.test.ts` (5). **Stage 5 landed**, which completes the ticket: the card itself. Its four files had each grown their own chrome — three copies of the `Label: Value` row at a 140px label where the Membership Plan card uses 200px, two section headers with no hairline at all, a `#111` Save button no Theme could reach and a page-local lilac text link for the subsection `Edit` action — so the section heading, its hairline and its action slot are now one shared `components/CardSection.tsx` and the label/value pair one shared `components/CardDetailRow.tsx`, both over `formChrome.ts` (#929, which gains `cardDetailRowStyle` and its pair) and both presentational in `BillingDurationSummary`'s sense: no locale key, no endpoint, no permission. The Save/Cancel pairs are `primaryBtnSmall()` + `secondaryBtnSmall` (#912/#954) and the five section `Edit` buttons are the shared `SectionEditButton` (#901). The **order** is a declaration beside the page rather than a property of the JSX, exactly as `PLAN_SECTION_ORDER` is: `assignedPlanProfile.ts` lists the Plan card's own order with the three things only an assignment has — the assigned person where GENERAL sits (§2), the applied Promotions after the benefit sections (§3/§6), and after the two simulations the Additional Products (§11) and the Billing Events ledger that answers "what happened" where they answer "what will happen" (the thread's Q4 answer). `AssignedPlanConfiguration` renders a *contiguous slice* of that list, which is what let the nested `MEMBERSHIP PLAN CONFIGURATION` heading go: the five snapshot sections are the card's own sections now, at one level, since a level of structure the Plan card has no counterpart for is the separate visual system §1 forbids (the key is gone from the namespace with it). And the card is finally on the #797/#897 split: expanding it *reads* the assignment — no input, no checkbox, no section `Edit` button — while `⋮ → Edit` is the single entry point into Edit mode and the same item (*Done Editing*) leaves it, since an Assigned Plan has no main form whose Cancel could, every one of its own fields being derived or frozen. Leaving the mode closes whichever section editor was open and discards a half-typed service. `AdditionalPeriodicServices` is shared with the Member card, so its gate is an `editing` prop that defaults to true and that page passes only the `canAdd` flag #957 gave it — the Member's own ADDITIONAL SERVICES section is editable in place and is not this ticket's, and the two gates are independent: the add action needs both. Frontend only: no migration, no API change, no endpoint, nothing stored and nothing charged; the one locale change is the §11 rename (*Additional Products* / *Productos Adicionales* / *Productes Addicionals*) plus `action_done_editing`, with `section_configuration` dropped. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/assigned-plan-card-chrome.test.ts` (26) — the order declaration and its contiguous slice, the JSX rendering in that order, no section header / 140px pair / control box / card border / `#6c63ff` / `#111` restated in any of the four files, the first section's missing hairline, the two shared components' presentational-ness, the menu as the single way in and out of Edit mode, every write control absent outside it, both editors closing when it is left, the Member page's default, and the rename in all three locales with the namespace still in parity.)

- **Done**: #962 (**“Example Timeline” reads “Membership Fee Simulation”.** The section on the Membership Plan card and the Promotion card projects what the *Membership Fee* does over the periods of a contract, which is not what "Example Timeline" says — and the Assigned Plan card has called the very same projection **Membership Fee Simulation** since #924 stage 3, so one screen's name for it disagreed with another's. Both headings now read *Membership Fee Simulation* / *Simulación de la Cuota de Membresía* / *Simulació de la Quota de Membresia*, word for word the strings `assigned_plans_page.section_fee_simulation` already held, and the key is `section_fee_simulation` in all three namespaces: `plans.section_example_timeline` and `promotions.section_timeline` are **retired** rather than left holding the new label, so a grep for the old wording finds nothing. The two empty states that told a gym to configure something "to preview an example timeline" name the simulation instead (`plans.timeline_unavailable`, `promotions.timeline_empty`); every other string of the section — the row statuses, the `Example: a member starting on {date}` note, the disclaimers — is untouched, because "Example" there qualifies the *dates*, which really are illustrative. A label rename and nothing else, as the ticket requires: no API change, no migration, no new endpoint, no locale key added, and not one date, amount, period status or billing calculation moved. The projection keeps its own names on purpose — `api/src/domain/planExampleTimeline.ts`, the `example_timeline` wire field, `GET /membership-plans/:id/example-timeline` and the shared `components/ExampleTimeline.tsx` are what `CLAUDE.md` pins by name, and renaming a module to rename a heading is how a rename becomes a refactor. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/membership-fee-simulation-label.test.ts` (7 — one key and one wording per locale across the three namespaces, both old keys gone, no "example timeline" wording left in *any* admin locale string in any of the three languages, the three pages rendering that key, the declared section slot unchanged, and the domain names still in place), with `plan-example-timeline`, `plans-expanded-read-only` and `plan-billing-event-simulation` updated to the new key.)

- **Done**: #963 (**A subsection's actions sit beside its title.** The Membership Plan and Promotion cards both laid their section header out with `justifyContent: 'space-between'`, so on a wide screen a card's width of empty space separated `BILLING & DURATION` from the `[ Edit ]` that opens it — the action read as the card's rather than the section's and took a horizontal scan to find — and each section editor's Save/Cancel sat at the far right *under* its fields, leaving the open section's header looking as if it had no actions at all. One shared `apps/admin/src/components/CardSectionHeader.tsx` is the header row for both cards now: the title is a **child**, not a slot, so `[ ACTION ] SECTION TITLE` is unexpressible, the actions follow it immediately, and `flexWrap: 'wrap'` drops a two-button pair under the title at phone width instead of widening the card. `Edit` and `Save`/`Cancel` are the two states of that one slot (Plans' PRICING, BILLING & DURATION, the three Benefit sections and CENTERS; the Promotion card's three Benefit sections and its Membership Fee Benefit), with the **error line** staying in the body under the fields it belongs to. Every other action of a subsection moved with them: Plans' *Apply new price to assigned plans*, which is a PRICING action and is still gated on Edit mode (#816), and the Promotion card's *Retry* for Suitable Membership Plans. What deliberately did **not** move is the **card's own form pair** — Plans' GENERAL and the Promotion card's main configuration are the card's form rather than a subsection with a contextual action, so their Save/Cancel stays under the fields it commits, the app's `formActionsRowStyle` convention, one pair per card. Layout only, as the ticket requires: not one handler, label, `disabled`, `title`, `saving` flag, permission gate, validation rule, API call, locale key or billing calculation changed, no new button style or colour was introduced (the heading itself is `formChrome.ts`'s `cardSectionLabelStyle`, which also retires both pages' local `sectionLabelSt` copies — #929), and **Assigned Membership Plans are untouched** (§"no changes"), which a test pins across all four of its files. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/section-action-placement.test.ts` (21 — the row's layout and its wrap, the title-before-actions guarantee, the heading's provenance, the four Plan sections and the four Promotion ones carrying their pair in the header, no pair left at `flex-end` under a section, the two main-form pairs that stay, the gates and labels unchanged, and the scoped-out card), with the three assertions in `section-edit-button.test.ts`, `promotions-expanded-read-only.test.ts` and `plans-expanded-read-only.test.ts` that pinned the old placement updated to the new one. Admin suite green at 1,647.)

- **Done**: #959 (**Mandatory / Optional per Product inside a Promotion.** A Promotion's configured items were all alike, so nothing could say that the member must take the discounted Gym Membership Fee but may decline the discounted Locker Fee. Each line of a Promotion's three Benefit sections now carries a **Requirement** — `mandatory` or `optional` — beside its quantity and its #896 `(action, value)` pair, stored on the relationship and never on `gym_charges`, whose own `mandatory` flag (#832/#893) answers a different question about the catalogue item. Migration 207 puts the column on `promotion_{session,oneoff,periodical}` **and** the three `user_membership_promotion_*_snapshot` tables, because an application reports from its own snapshot and never from the live row (#635 §16) — so the screen that will later offer the member the choice reads what *that* application was agreed with. It is NOT NULL, defaulted and backfilled to `mandatory`: that is what every existing row means, since an applied Promotion grants everything it configures and nothing offers a choice today; announcing `optional` for stored rows would promise behaviour that does not exist. The accepted set is declared once (`api/src/domain/promotionItemRequirement.ts`), mirrored by `chk_<table>_requirement` and by the admin's `lib/promotionItemRequirement.ts` — three places — and `parsePromotionItemRequirementInput()` gives the replace-all `PUT`s #896's *keep / set / 400* rule, so a quantity-only save cannot reset an optional item to mandatory and a typo is never coerced into the opposite promise. Duplicate and `snapshotPromotionGrants()` copy it verbatim. The admin side is one more entry in the shared `PRODUCT_BENEFIT_COLUMNS` behind a `showRequirement` opt-in the Promotions page alone passes, rendered with the grid's existing inline select — no new column declaration, control or colour. **Scope, per the issue thread's answers**: Promotions only (Membership Plans excluded), no Requirement on the Membership Fee Benefit (it has no item to carry one), and the member's own enabled/disabled choice with its billing effect belongs to the promotion assignment process, which the thread puts out of scope — so nothing reads the column yet and an optional grant is granted and priced exactly as a mandatory one.)

- **Done**: #965 (**The Base Exercise card: the expanded view is the Edit view read-only, and Details carries the metadata.** Expanding a Base Exercise showed a flat list of `Label: Value` rows that matched neither the Edit form's sections nor the gym Exercises page's own expanded card — three presentations of one entity — and it ended with Created At, Modified At and a `View Audit Log` link, while `⋮ → Details` merely toggled the row open. The expanded body is now `components/exercises/ExerciseReadOnlyView.tsx`, the read-only counterpart of `ExerciseEditor`: the same five sections in the same order (GENERAL · CONFIGURATION · ALLOWED RESULT TYPES · MUSCLES · MEDIA) from the same `exerciseForm.ts` declaration, with the controls replaced by values in the box the input occupies (`formValueStyle`, #929) and the grids, labels and hairlines shared through a new `exerciseFieldChrome.ts` that both halves import — so a field added to the form appears in both, and neither can be restyled alone (§14, §15). It holds no `<input>`, `<select>`, checkbox, `onChange`, Save, Cancel or Edit affordance, and Allowed Result Types renders spans rather than disabled checkboxes (§3, §6, #799's rule). Both Exercise pages render it, for the reason #806 gave the editor: the gym page's own `renderViewSection` is gone with the Base page's `renderReadOnly`, and the two media previews — one a pair of thumbnails beside the raw `video_url`, the other a framed poster that doubles as a play control — are one `ExerciseMediaPreview`, still drawing the 512×512 thumbnail rather than the master (#716 §4) and mounting no `<video>` until asked (#717 §9), with `playing`/`onPlay` the page's so a second clip cannot start over the first. `⋮ → Details` is a real modal now (`components/exercises/ExerciseDetailModal.tsx`, shared, `scope` the page's), and it carries **only** the technical metadata — the internal id, Created/Modified At & By and `ViewAuditLogButton` — because the configuration it used to restate is what the card below it already shows (§12); it renders the list row it was handed rather than fetching a detail endpoint, so there is no second Exercise model (#799). Which needed a column to read: a Base Exercise is a `gym_id IS NULL` row written by a superadmin with no `gym_memberships` row for `created_by` to point at, so **migration 208** adds the `created_by_name`/`created_by_type` + `modified_by_name`/`modified_by_type` snapshot pairs to `exercises` with their CHECKs, exactly as 126/178/196 do, and the platform router stamps them on create, on `PUT /:id` and on all four media routes — a media change is a modification, and bumping `modified_at` alone would attribute an upload to whoever last saved the form. No backfill: `/platform/*` runs behind `requireSuperadmin` with no `tenantContext`, so `recordAudit()` wrote nothing to reconstruct from, and a row written before 208 reads as the em dash. The **gym-facing** read masks it: `GET /exercises/:id` serves base rows too, and a shared catalogue's rows are not the reading gym's to attribute — the same rule `maskPlatformActors` already applies to the Nutrition Library. The collapsed header gains Created By beside Created At, in the gym list's own column order. Deploy note: run the migration before the API restarts — the new SELECT names the columns. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/exercise-read-only-expansion.test.ts` (25 — no write control, no metadata, no audit link, the section order against the declaration, the shared chrome, the header's columns, the Details modal's fields and what it must no longer restate, both pages on one view, and the formatters), `api/src/test/exercise-actor-snapshot.unit.test.ts` (13 — the migration's guards and CHECKs, every `modified_at` bump carrying the pair, the masking, `actorSnapshot`), plus integration assertions in `platform-training.test.ts` and `exercises.test.ts`.)

- **Done**: #966 (**Expanding a Training Plan Template works again, and the API stops quoting SQL.** `GET /training-plan-templates/:id/hierarchy` still projected `b.result_type` — a column migration 074 (#154) dropped when it moved the result type from the block down to the exercise instance — so **every** expand answered `500 Unknown column 'b.result_type' in 'field list'`, the row sat on `Loading…` for ever and the gym read a raw driver message in a toast. The block object no longer carries it; each exercise now carries `result_type_id` + `result_type_slug`/`result_type_name` (LEFT JOIN `result_types`, so an exercise configured with none stays in the tree) + `target_value`/`min_value`/`max_value`/`unit`, which is `workout-templates.ts` `GET /:id`'s own projection and exactly what the shared `summaries.ts` `HierExercise`/`exerciseSummary()` both trees render through have expected since #154 — so the ticket's "do not simply delete the column from the SELECT" is answered by reading the level the data actually lives at, and a duration/distance/weight exercise reads correctly in this tree for the first time. Two defects behind the symptom went with it. The **global error handler** answered `err.message` verbatim, so any driver failure reached the browser as a database implementation detail; `api/src/domain/httpErrorResponse.ts` is now the one place that decides what a client is told — an error carrying an explicit HTTP `status` (the `Object.assign(new Error(m), { status })` shape, body-parser's own 400/413) keeps its message, anything else is a generic `500 Internal server error` with the real error logged server-side, which is what `docs/feature-patterns.md` has claimed all along. And the **expanded body had two states for three outcomes** (`loading ? spinner : tree`), which is what made a failed fetch permanent: the page keeps the failure per template (`hierError`), renders an application-level sentence of its own with a **Retry** that can bypass the cache guard, and the three-state shape is written up as a pattern. No migration, no new endpoint, no schema change. Tests: `api/src/test/training-plan-templates.test.ts` (new, 7 integration — one workout, multiple workouts in order, a block with no exercises and a template with none, the block carrying no `result_type`, each exercise's own result type with the LEFT JOIN keeping an unconfigured one, tenant isolation 404 and auth 401), `api/src/test/http-error-response.unit.test.ts` (new, 12 — the driver message generalised, a deliberate 4xx/5xx message kept, body-parser's 413, a non-HTTP status ignored, a thrown non-error, plus source scans of the handler's wiring and the query's projection) and `apps/admin/src/test/training-plan-template-expansion.test.ts` (new, 9 — the per-row failure state, the retry bypassing the cache guard, the three render branches with the old two-branch shape gone, the typed props and the two labels in all three locales). `cleanupTestGyms()` gained the `training_plan_template_workouts` → `training_plan_templates` pair before `workout_templates`, since `tptw_workout_template_id` is the one FK into that table that is `ON DELETE RESTRICT`.)

- **Done**: #968 (**The Base Exercise form's actions wear the platform's chrome.** The one Exercise editor (`components/exercises/ExerciseEditor.tsx`, which both Exercise screens render since #806) declared its own `{ display: 'flex', gap: 8, justifyContent: 'flex-end' }` action row and styled Cancel as `btnSmall('#888')`, so its Save/Cancel pair sat at the right edge where no other inline editor's does and the error line above it spelled its own colour. The row is `inlineActionsRowStyle` now — the left-aligned section/inline pair `formChrome.ts` already declares, at the fields' own content margin and with no rule above it — Cancel is `secondaryBtnSmall`, Save stays `primaryBtnSmall()` and the error line is `formErrorStyle`, so the form holds no justification, padding or colour of its own and both screens move together. The lilac went with it, in three places the view still spelled it: `Upload Image` and `Upload Video` (`ExerciseImageField` / `ExerciseVideoField`) take `primaryBtnSmall()`, which is #954's file-picker exception decided the other way for these two only — the Base Exercise form's Save already followed the Theme while its pickers did not, and the generic `ImageUploadField`, which this form does not render, is unchanged and still named by `theme-primary-buttons.test.ts`; the Base Exercises page's own `+ New Exercise` was `btnStyle()` with no argument, which resolves to `--brand` (the sidebar's colour, not an action's — CLAUDE.md's #912 rule), while the gym page's `+ Add Exercise` already used `primaryBtnStyle()`; and the poster-less clip's `▶` overlay reads `var(--gd-link, #6c63ff)` like its sibling in `ExerciseReadOnlyView`. Nothing else moved: every button keeps its handler, label, `disabled`, `title` and `saving` state, each picker keeps its position, its hidden `<input>` gating (#823) and its neutral grey `Remove`, and no new token, helper or Theme setting was introduced — a gym editing Buttons → Primary Button now sees it on Save Changes, Upload Image, Upload Video and + New Exercise at once. Ordering note: the pair is `[Cancel] [Save Changes]`, the order every other inline editor in the app uses (`GoalLibrarySection`, the Member card's section editors), rather than the ticket's sketch — §2 defers to "the exact ordering already established by the platform's standard form actions" and §5 asks that these buttons be indistinguishable from the equivalent actions elsewhere. Frontend only: no migration, no API change, no new endpoint. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/exercise-form-actions.test.ts` (new, 12 — the shared row and its left-alignment against `formActionsRowStyle`'s right-aligned counterpart, the one primary and one secondary button, the preserved Save behaviour, both pickers themed with their gating and Remove intact, and the Base page's primary action not resolving through `--brand`), plus `theme-primary-buttons.test.ts` updated so the two Exercise pickers are now asserted as *themed* and `ImageUploadField` alone carries the documented exception.)

- **Done**: #1009 (**Marking a workout block complete works again, and the block-level result snapshot is gone.** `POST /me/workout-block-logs` read `wb.result_type` — a column migration 074 (#154) dropped when it moved the result type from the block down to the exercise instance — so **every** member who marked a block complete got a `500`, and the endpoint had no test, which is why it went unnoticed. It was not a one-line fix, because the value was written into `workout_block_logs.result_type`, NOT NULL under migration 042's `wbl_result_type_check` with the **pre-#154 block-level vocabulary** (`None`/`Time`/`Rounds`/`Repetitions`/`Distance`/`Calories`/`Weight`/`Score`) — a vocabulary stored nowhere else after #154, while four of the current `result_types` slugs (`rpe`, `rest_time`, `pace`, `speed`) have no member of it and two legacy values (`Rounds`, `Score`) have no slug. **Migration 209 drops the column and its CHECK**, rather than re-vocabularying or coercing it, and the sweep is what decided that: *nothing reads it* — every reader is a blanket projection (`SELECT *` in `me.ts`'s three queries, `SELECT wbl.*` in `exercise-logs.ts`), there is no caller in `apps/admin` for `GET /members/:memberId/workout-block-logs`, none for `GET /me/workout-block-logs`, and no report. Keeping it would have meant inventing what a block's result type means when its exercises disagree and backfilling the `Rounds`/`Score` rows with that invention, i.e. writing a guess into a member's training history. What survives is `result_value`: **one optional, untyped free-text value** the member typed for the block (`21:04`, `7 rounds + 3`), an empty input being `null`; typed per-set results stay the exercise instance's, in `exercise_logs` + `exercise_log_sets`. The pre-insert `SELECT` keeps its other job and becomes `SELECT 1` — the ownership probe behind the 403. **A fourth instance of the same defect went with it, client-side**: `apps/member/.../training/page.tsx` still declared `result_type` on its `Block` type and read it three times, so with the server not sending it since #154 `undefined !== 'None'` passed and `undefined.toLowerCase()` threw **while rendering any block** — My Training was down ahead of the button whose 500 was reported. The typed caption is gone, the result input is offered unconditionally behind one neutral `training.block_result` placeholder, and the eight `training.result_type_*` keys that encoded the retired vocabulary are retired in en/es/ca. Three queries had now been found one ticket at a time (#966's tree, this endpoint, this page), so the ticket adds the **gate** rather than a fourth point fix: `api/src/test/migration-074-dropped-columns.unit.test.ts` scans every production `.ts`/`.tsx` under `api/src` **and** `apps/member/src` — with no per-file exemption, in particular none for the two routers and the page that shipped it, and a test asserting all three are in the scan so it cannot pass vacuously — for `result_type`, `exercise_type`, `distance_value` or `distance_unit`, none of which any table has after 209. `duration_seconds` is deliberately outside that set: 074 dropped it from `workout_template_exercises` only and it is still a real column of `workout_blocks`, `workout_template_blocks` and `exercise_logs`. It scans the Members App from the API suite because **CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only** (the admin job type-checks and builds). Tests: `api/src/test/me-workout-block-logs.test.ts` (new, 14 integration — the endpoint had none: happy path, no `result_type` on the response or the row, a log with no result value, the four slugs no legacy vocabulary could express, the 400s, 401 unauthenticated, 403 for a non-member role, 403 for another member's block, 403 cross-gym, 403 for a soft-deleted block, the `PUT`'s edit and its 404 for someone else's log, and the history list's ordering and scoping), `api/src/test/migration-074-dropped-columns.unit.test.ts` (new, 5 unit — the two scans, the scan-coverage assertion, the current exercise-level spellings staying allowed, and all three shipped spellings being caught; verified to fail on each real defect before the fix) and `apps/member/src/test/training-block-result.test.ts` (new, 5 — the page naming no dropped column, the unconditional input, the `null` payload, exercise-level logging untouched, and the locale keys in all three locales). `cleanupTestGyms()` gained a `workout_block_logs` line **before** `members`: its `workout_block_id` is `ON DELETE RESTRICT` while deleting a member cascades `training_plans` → `workouts` → `workout_blocks` in unspecified order, so the block could go while its log still pointed at it — a pre-existing hazard from 042 that only a suite writing a block log reaches. Migration reviewed by `db-reviewer`: no BLOCKERs; its WARNING is fixed (the `down`'s `ADD CONSTRAINT` is guarded on the constraint's own existence, not the column's, so a half-finished rollback re-run restores the CHECK instead of silently no-op'ing — verified from that state), and it confirmed the `up` is `ALGORITHM=INSTANT` while the `down` rebuilds under `ALGORITHM=COPY`.)

- **Done**: #967 (**Exercise names in all three supported languages.** An exercise's name — the only field a member reads in a workout — existed in one language, for a Base Exercise and a gym's own alike. `exercise_translations` (migration 210) adds one row per `(exercise_id, locale)` in the shape `CLAUDE.md` pins for translated *data* and #643 built for the Nutrition Library: `exercises.name` stays the base value, the routers' duplicate check and the fallback a locale with no row resolves to, so nothing renders blank and an edit form still submits the English original back. The rules are **one module** now rather than a second copy of #643's `COALESCE`: `domain/nameTranslations.ts` holds the builders and the writes over a `TranslatedNameConfig`, `domain/exerciseTranslations.ts` and `domain/nutritionLibrary.ts` are the two thin wrappers (the Nutrition Library's exported helpers kept their names, so its ~30 call sites are untouched). Both exercise routers accept `translations` on `POST`/`PUT` — validated once (base locale rejected, unknown locale rejected, bounded by `exercises.name`'s own 200 characters), replace-all when the field is sent, **untouched when it is absent**, which is what stops a client written before the ticket from wiping a gym's translations — and every read returns `name` + `display_name` + the stored map, list reads included, because `⋮ → Edit` seeds its form from the row the page already holds (#800). Search matches the base name **or any** stored translation rather than only the locale on screen (§7: a gym typing `Press de Banca` means the exercise), ordering follows the displayed name, and the three exercise pickers filter client-side on the same three things (`lib/exerciseNames.ts`). Every consumer surface is localized in place — `planTreeSelect(locale)` (the staff, gym-template and member reads of a training plan), workout templates and their platform twin, training plans, training plan templates, exercise logs, `GET /me/exercise-logs` and the Recycle Bin — so the Members app needed no change at all: it already sends `x-locale`. Duplicate, Clone and `POST /exercises/import` copy the rows, which is §4 (an imported Base Exercise reads in a member's own language from day one); a **re-import** deliberately does not, because it exists to restore System media and must not overwrite a translation the gym corrected. The editor is the shared one (#806): the GENERAL section renders one Name input per language, required for the base locale and optional for the rest (§5), with the language list arriving as a prop from `GET /exercises/locales` (gym) or the existing `/lookups` (platform) — no component names a locale, and `lib/localeLabels.ts` is the one place a locale gets a label, resolved before `t()` so an unlabelled tag reads `FR` rather than `languages.fr`; the Nutrition Library's own translation inputs now use it too, and are translated rather than English in every language. The **expanded card** is where every stored translation is read: `ExerciseReadOnlyView` is the editor with the controls replaced by values (#965), so the per-language Name inputs have per-language Name rows beside them, and `⋮ → Details` stays the technical metadata alone (#965 §12). A list row and a workout still show one name (§6). **Nothing is seeded and nothing is backfilled**: there is no base-exercise catalogue in the repo to translate and the Free Exercise DB (#964) is English-only, so an existing exercise keeps its one name and §9's "do not silently invent translations" is satisfied by doing nothing. Tests: `api/src/test/exercise-translations.unit.test.ts` (23 unit — the builders, the allowlist data-flow argument, the omitted-vs-empty payload rule, the env-driven configuration, migration 210's shape, and a source scan that fails if any router projects a raw `e.name` as an exercise name or queries the junction table itself), `api/src/test/exercise-translations.test.ts` (integration — both routers, the write rules, locale resolution and its fallback, search in either language, locale-aware ordering, Duplicate/Clone/import and the re-import rule, auth and tenant isolation) and `apps/admin/src/test/exercise-name-translations.test.ts` (20).)

- **Done**: #970 (**The member's plans read *Assigned Training Plans*.** “Training Plans” named two different things in the admin app — the reusable, gym-wide definitions (**Training Plan Templates**) and the plans a member has actually been assigned — so the sidebar item, the page heading at `/{locale}/training-plans` and the Member card's section all read exactly what the templates list beside them reads, and which one a screen was showing had to be inferred from where the user was. The member-side half is **Assigned Training Plans** / *Planes de entrenamiento asignados* / *Plans d'entrenament assignats* now: `nav.training_plans`, `training_plans.title`, `members.section_training_plans`, `members.training_plans` and `member_training_plans.title`, plus the three empty states and the editor's back link that named the section (`training_plans.empty`, `members.no_training_plans`, `training_plans.editor_back`) — in en/es/ca, since next-intl has no locale fallback. The template side keeps its name, as the ticket requires, with one label corrected rather than renamed: Cordel's nav item read **Base Training Plans** while the page it opens has always been headed *Base Training Plan Templates*, so the nav label now says what the page is (`nav.base_training_plan_templates`, all three locales) — that is the distinction the last acceptance criterion asks for, not a rename of the concept. Entity-level singulars are deliberately untouched: `+ New Training Plan`, *From a Training Plan Template*, the confirmations and the Details dialog title all name one plan rather than the section. **Locale values only**: not one locale *key*, route (`/{locale}/training-plans`), feature-flag key (`training.training_plans`), component, API field, endpoint, permission or behaviour moved, and the Members App is out of scope — its own page has always read *My Training Plan*, which already says whose plan it is. No migration, no API change. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/assigned-training-plans-label.test.ts` (new, 4 — every label naming the section says “assigned” in each locale's own word, every label naming the template concept still says “templates” and says nothing about “assigned”, the navigation's route/label/feature keys unchanged, and both headings still reading from the keys they already read from). Admin suite green at 1,750.)

- **Done**: #971 (**The Training Plan editor follows the Theme, and expanding a plan reads.** Two defects on the Assigned Training Plans card (`[locale]/training-plans`) and the shared `WorkoutBlockBuilder` it renders. First, nothing in the tree followed a gym's Theme: `+ Block` and `+ Exercise` were a dashed lilac link (`#b9b5ee` / `#6c63ff`), the day selector a lilac pill (`#eef0ff` / `#4b45c6`) declared twice — here and in the Training Plan Templates tree — the exercise picker highlighted on a lilac tint (`#f0eeff`), and `+ Add Workout`, `+ New Training Plan` and both Save buttons were `btnStyle()` with no argument, which resolves through `--brand` (`sidebarSelectedItemBackground`, the *sidebar's* colour) and is what §3 sees as red on a themed gym. The two controls of the hierarchy live in one place now — `apps/admin/src/components/workoutChrome.ts`, the `listChrome.ts`/`formChrome.ts` rule for the Training Plan → Workout → Day → Block → Exercises tree the three screens share: `weekdayChipStyle`/`weekdaySelectStyle` wear the Theme's input pair (`--gd-input-border` / `--gd-input-bg`) at a pill radius, because a weekday selector is a select, and `treeAddBtnStyle` is `formChrome`'s dashed `+ Add …` affordance at the tree's denser size, so §2's "lightweight, but the platform's language" holds without a filled button. Every filled action is `primaryBtnStyle()` (the existing Buttons group, #912/#954 — no new token and no new Theme setting), the card's own form takes `formFieldLabelStyle`/`formControlStyle`/`formFieldErrorStyle`/`formActionsRowStyle`/`secondaryBtnStyle` instead of the five copies the page had declared, a workout card is `cardSurfaceStyle`, the builder's compact controls share one `treeControlBox`, and the page's two remaining accents — a sorted column title and the inset `being edited` bar — read `var(--gd-link, …)` and `var(--brand, …)`, keeping the old literal as the `var()` fallback for the frames before `applyTokens()` has run. `dashedAddBtnStyle`'s own hairline became `var(--gd-input-border, #c8c8d0)` in the same spirit, which changes nothing until a gym themes its controls. Second, **expanding a plan was the editor**: a reader with write access saw `+ Add Workout`, editable workout names, the weekday `<select>`, drag handles and a `⋮` per workout — the opposite of CLAUDE.md's "Expanding a list card reads; `⋮ → Edit` writes". One expression fixes it, because every control in the tree already keyed off the `canWrite` it is handed: `canWrite && editing && !isCompleted`. So read-only shows the whole structure — the weekday as the same chip, the workout name and each block as values, the exercise table with its sets, targets and media — and nothing that changes it, while `⋮ → Edit` (which already expands the row, so Cancel reveals the read-only view rather than collapsing it) exposes the existing editing exactly as it was. The data model, the hierarchy, every endpoint and every handler are untouched (§6): frontend only, no migration, no API change, no locale key added or moved. Scope note: the Training Plan Templates tree shares the weekday chip and its `+ Add Workout` so neither diverges, but its own read-only/Edit split is not this ticket's. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/training-plan-editor-theme.test.ts` (new, 17 — the shared chrome asserted directly against the shipped Theme defaults, the one-chip-both-modes guarantee, the four themed primary actions, the retired page-local chrome, no lilac left in any of the three files, the Edit-mode gate and the read-only tree still rendering the structure, `⋮ → Edit` still expanding, and `applyTokens` writing the very variables the controls read), with the two assertions in `training-plans-list-header.test.ts` that pinned the old button helper and the old accent updated. Admin suite green at 1,788.)

- **Done**: #974 (**The Product card reads in the Edit form's own shape.** Expanding a Product showed a dense `Label: value` list on a 160px label column while `⋮ → Edit` showed a two-column form over five sections, so the two halves agreed on the section names (`GENERAL`, `BILLING CONFIGURATION`, `PROFESSIONAL SERVICES`, `PACKAGE INFORMATION`, `NOTES`) and on nothing else: the read-only view omitted **Name** entirely, joined the linked Professional Services into a comma sentence where the editor shows chips, and every field added since had to be added twice in two shapes. Both halves render one layout now — `[locale]/financials/products/productProfile.ts` holds the five sections in order with each section's fields, the `type`/`status`/`enrollment_status` option sets the three forms share, and the persisted-row → form-values mapping; `ProductLayout.tsx` renders them over the shared `CardSectionHeader` (#963) and `formChrome`'s section, control, value, textarea and checkbox styles (#929), reflowing at `minmax(240px, 1fr)` instead of the editor's fixed `1fr 1fr` and spanning the grid with the four free-text fields. `visibleProductSections({ isSystem, isSessionType })` is the one function both halves ask: it drops the session-only section for a non-session item — off the **draft's** Type while the editor is open, so choosing *Sessions* still reveals it before the row is saved — drops Package information and Validity for a System row, and returns `editable: false` for a System row's frozen name/type/units, which the layout renders as **values in both modes** (the mechanism #927 added for a calculated field): `PUT /sellable-items/:id` writes those three only inside its `is_system` guard, so the form must not offer a control the route would ignore, while the card still has to report them — which the old read-only list did and a shared layout that merely hid them would have lost. Mandatory stays editable on a System item (#832). §4/§5: the editor's Save/Cancel left its own `justifyContent: 'flex-end'` row and its `btnSmall('#888')` Cancel for the shared, left-aligned `inlineActionsRowStyle` + `secondaryBtnSmall`/`primaryBtnSmall()` (#929/#968), with the create card's pair moved with it so creating and editing an item do not put Save in two places; the page's own `SectionHeader`, `DetailRow`, `inlineInputStyle`, `inlineSelectStyle`, `inlineLabelStyle`, `checkboxLabelStyle` and `errorStyle` are deleted, and the list's search box and Type filter take `filterControlStyle` (#724), so the last two hardcoded `#ccc` borders are gone and every control on the screen follows the Theme's input pair. §4's literal `[ Edit ] SECTION TITLE` is **not** implemented and deliberately so: this card has no section-level Edit button (Edit is card-level, through `⋮`), and #963 makes an action before its section's title unexpressible by construction — the intent, actions left-aligned and never pushed to the far edge, is what the shared header already gives. The read-only Professional Services are spans wearing the editor's own selected chip (#799), never disabled checkboxes. **Presentation only** (§6): no migration, no endpoint, no payload, no column, no locale key added or moved, and no `Sellable Item` → `Product` rename — that is #949, still unanswered, so the ticket's own “now displayed as Products” is not pre-empted here. The Details modal keeps its own field list and stays the home of the audit metadata (§7). Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/products-expanded-read-only.test.ts` (new, 38 — the declaration's section order and field set, the locale coverage of every title and label in en/es/ca, the row → form mapping including every null column, the four `isSystem × isSessionType` combinations, the frozen-field rule, the single layout call site, the retired sub-components, no control anywhere in the read-only renderer, the chips, the themed left-aligned action rows and the unchanged `PUT` payload), with six existing source-scans in `products-price-label`, `product-frequency`, `products-session-package-price` and `products-mandatory` updated to the new wiring. Admin suite green at 1,826; `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` clean.)

- **Done**: #976 (**A Members App calendar event is the event's colour; the member's status is text.** The member calendar painted each event from `availability_state`, so the same class was yellow for a member on its waitlist, orange for one who could join it, green for one who was booked and red when it filled up — eight colours (`STATE_COLORS`) that said nothing about the event and everything about whoever was looking at it, while the colour the gym actually configured on the event was read by nobody. The box takes that configured colour now: `GET /me/schedule` reports `COALESCE(calendar_events.color, activity_types.color)` as `color` (the Activity Type is the fallback because a schedule-rule-materialized occurrence never went through the admin event form that pre-fills it), and the page sets `backgroundColor`/`borderColor`/`textColor` inline **only** when that is a usable hex — `null`, blank or a non-hex value sets no inline style at all, which is the one way to let the theme's own `calendarEventBackground`/`Border`/`Text` paint it (#559 stage 2; FullCalendar writes an inline style, which would beat the tokens). The member's own relationship with the event is the text inside it: `Booked`, `Waitlist · Position #2 · 4 waiting`, `Completed`, and — §2/§6's actual requirement — **nothing at all** for an event they never booked, so a past class they skipped reads as an ordinary past class with no `Not used`, no `Cancelled` and no `Past due` (none of which the app had, and none of which was added). `Cancelled` is reported only to a member who holds a booking on it, which is the one cancellation that is theirs to know (§7) and is unreachable today anyway, since `/me/schedule` returns `status = 'scheduled'` rows only. The waitlist total rides along beside `Booked` while the waitlist can still move and is dropped once the class has happened, because a total on a finished class is noise. One module decides all of it — `apps/member/src/lib/calendarEventDisplay.ts`: the colour resolution, the foreground (both candidates scored with the WCAG 2.1 contrast formula `apps/admin/src/lib/calendarContrast.ts` uses and the better taken, rather than a luminance threshold derived for pure black, since the dark candidate is the app's own `#111827`), the status line as a locale **key** plus its values so the page resolves `t()`, and a chip that declares no hue (`color: inherit` over a neutral 22% black, §11's "no new purple/lilac/orange/yellow for booking states"). **The Admin calendar is untouched**, per the ticket's own scope note: its events still take the theme's event tokens and carry their status in `CalendarStatusBadge` (#559 stage 3). Whether an admin event should take a per-event colour — which would reverse that stage — is the open `Q1` on **#975**, along with the waitlist count `GET /class-sessions` does not yet project. No migration, no schema change, no new endpoint, and no booking or waitlist rule moved: one additive `COALESCE` on an existing SELECT, eight locale keys in en/es/ca, and a presentation change. Tests: `apps/member/src/test/calendar-event-display.test.ts` (new, 26 — the colour resolution including the three "fall through to the theme" cases, the colour's independence from the member's state, the contrast choice and that only `#111827`/`#ffffff` are ever returned, every branch of the status line including the four `null` ones and the negative/fractional/NaN waitlist totals, the chip's hue-freedom and a scan of the module for one, the page no longer naming `STATE_COLORS`, the conditional inline spread, and locale parity with matching placeholders across en/es/ca) plus three integration cases in `api/src/test/member-calendar.test.ts` for the `color` projection. Member suite green at 255.)

- **Done**: #977 (**An Admin event says what happened to it: Scheduled · Not used · Completed · Cancelled.** The admin calendar could tell a future class from a cancelled one and nothing else: a slot that passed with nobody in it read exactly like one starting tomorrow, so a gym whose calendar is mostly open slots for private classes had no way to see which hours went unsold — and the alternative, a confirmation task per empty hour, is the thing §3 exists to avoid. The four statuses are one axis now (§10 — the member's *booking* status stays a separate one), **derived on every read and stored nowhere**: `eventExecutionStatus()` (`api/src/domain/eventExecutionStatus.ts`) answers `not_used` for a `scheduled` event that has ended with no `booked` bookings, `scheduled` while it has not ended *or* it ended with bookings and nobody has confirmed it (§4/§13), and carries the two values a person chooses — `completed`, `cancelled` — through from the `status` column that already held them. There is no column and no sweep, because `not_used` is a function of the clock: a stored copy would need a nightly writer, and a night it missed would report yesterday's empty slots as still Scheduled. An explicit decision outranks the clock in both directions, which is what keeps **`Completed · 0 attendees`** (§5 — the teacher held the session with nobody there) a different fact from an unused slot, and a cancelled event cancelled however empty it was. A `draft` manual entry has no execution status at all (`null`) rather than a fifth value: it was never put on the calendar, so it neither ran nor went unused. `withEventExecutionStatus()` wraps **every** read of both routers — list, single row and the rows the mutations return — so the field is reported and never re-derived: the Calendar page and `ClassSessionDetailPanel` read it, and the badge's `full` (a scheduled session at capacity, still the UI's own derivation) is gated on it, so a past empty session can no longer read *Full* instead of *Not used*. `EVENT_SELECT` gained `booked_count`/`waitlist_count`, so a manual entry — as bookable as a session since #503 stage 1 — is classified by the same rule, and §9's `Waitlist: N` line (per occurrence, never per activity type) has something to read; `SESSION_SELECT` gained the waitlist count it never projected. The panel gains **Mark as completed** (§4/§11) behind its own confirmation, offered only once the event has ended and only while it is awaiting one — a future event asks nothing of the teacher (§2) and an empty past slot needs no action (§3), though it may still be completed deliberately, which is how §5 is reached — and it posts to the existing `POST /class-sessions/:id/complete`, so bookings, attendance and `modified_by_membership_id` are untouched and the route's two guards (pending attendance, no trainer) now render as an actionable line instead of a generic toast. Both transitions record **previous → new** status in the audit log (§14), which is also where "who confirmed it and when" lives — no `completed_by` column was added. `Not used` stays Admin-only (§15) structurally rather than by filtering: both routers sit behind `requireModuleAccess('CALENDAR')` and a `member` has `NONE`, the member-facing `computeCalendarEventStatus()` (#503 stage 5) keeps its own four-value vocabulary, and a test scans the Members app for either name. Status never becomes colour (§8): it rides in `CalendarStatusBadge` (#559 stage 3) with `not_used` taking the palette's **existing** neutral tone (shared with `draft`, not the yellow the ticket rules out), and the event box keeps the theme's Calendar tokens — whether an admin event should instead take `COALESCE(calendar_events.color, activity_types.color)` is **#975's open `Q1`** and `CLAUDE.md` forbids pre-empting it, so that one acceptance criterion ("the configured event/activity colour") is deliberately left to #975. No migration, no new endpoint, no stored column; eight locale keys in en/es/ca. Tests: `api/src/test/event-execution-status.unit.test.ts` (new, 16 — every branch of the rule including the two "not evidence of an empty slot" cases where a missing count or end time must not read as `not_used`, the `Date`/string and numeric/string input shapes, the four declared values, the no-mutation contract of the wrapper, a scan for a migration or statement writing the field, and the §15 guards), `api/src/test/event-execution-status.test.ts` (new, 16 — the field over real rows on both routers, waitlisted and cancelled bookings not counting as participation, the waitlist count being this occurrence's, `Completed · 0 attendees`, cancel preserving the event and its bookings, the audit transition, a `draft` event's `null`, and tenant isolation) and `apps/admin/src/test/event-execution-status.test.ts` (new, 13 — the field being read rather than re-derived, `full`'s gate, no per-event colour, `not_used` reusing the neutral tone, the action's two conditions and its confirmation, and locale parity with the placeholders each key promises). API suite green at 4,968; admin at 1,839; `tsc --noEmit` clean on both.)

- **Done**: #949 stage 1 of 3 (**Sellable Item is called Product.** The thing a gym sells was named *Sellable Item* on screen while its table has been `gym_charges` since migration 102, its audit entity type `gym_charge` and its feature flag `financials.gym_charges` — so the ticket's §5 ("`sellable_items` → `products`") named a table that never existed, and the rename had to be measured before it could be planned: 2,179 occurrences of `sellable` across 174 files, 28 of them file names, beside 1,229 of `gym_charge`. The thread's `Q1 C` answer takes the rename all the way down to the schema, `Q2` rules out keeping `/sellable-items` as a compatibility alias, and `Q3` scopes *Product Type* to `gym_charges.type` alone, leaving the global `charge_types` lookup as *Charge Type*. This stage is the half a gym owner reads and nothing else: the 16 locale keys per language whose **value** named the entity — the nav label, the page title, the empty state, `+ Add Product`, *Product Details*, the delete confirmation, the retired-frequency notice, the *Product* column of the Membership Plan, Promotion and Assigned Plan benefit grids, the Additional Products column, the Mandatory-benefit hint, the Promotion requirement hint, the Promotion simulation's empty state and the Tax Rate impact list — in en/es/ca, using the translations the app had already settled on (*Productos* / *Productes*, from #957's ADDITIONAL PRODUCTS section, whose own item column still read *Sellable Item* beside the heading that did not). All four `docs/*.md` and `CLAUDE.md` move with it, in **prose only**: a backtick-delimited span is left exactly as it is, so every file path, type name, route and SQL identifier the docs quote still names what is really there — `api/src/domain/sellableItemFrequency.ts` was still that file until stage 2 and `PUT /sellable-items/:id` still that route until stage 3 — and `CLAUDE.md` gains the bullet that says so. One prose occurrence stays on purpose: `staff-sellable` in `architecture.md` is the adjective, not the entity. No identifier, locale key, route, column or migration is touched, so there is no behaviour to regress; the one new file is `api/src/test/product-terminology.unit.test.ts` (10 cases), which holds the line by scanning every locale value of both apps in all three languages for the retired English word and the retired Spanish and Catalan phrasings, while deliberately judging no key — `col_sellable_item` was correct until stage 2 renamed it — and bare *artículo* / *article* stays allowed, since it is ordinary Spanish and Catalan for an item of any kind. It sits in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only.
