Stage 2 is the identifiers, the 28 file names, the locale keys and the admin route folder; stage 3 is the `/products` API root and the `Q1 C` database tier — `RENAME TABLE gym_charges TO products`, `gym_charge_id` across twelve tables, the eight `gym_charges_*` CHECKs, the feature-flag key and the `audit_logs.entity_type` backfill — and carries the `Closes`. API unit suites green at 713; `tsc --noEmit` clean on `api/` and `apps/admin`.)

- **Done**: #949 stage 2 of 3 (**the code says Product too.** Stage 1 moved the copy; this stage moves everything a TypeScript reader chooses and nothing that crosses the wire or the schema, which is what keeps it a rename `tsc` and the suites can prove. Renamed: every identifier (`SellableItem*` → `Product*`, `sellableItem*` → `product*`, `SELLABLE_ITEM_*` → `PRODUCT_*`, `SellableBenefit*`/`SELLABLE_BENEFIT_*` → `ProductBenefit*`/`PRODUCT_BENEFIT_*`), the six API modules (`api/src/api/products.ts`, `product-benefit-pricing.ts`, `domain/product{BenefitActions,Classification,Frequency,ProfessionalServices}.ts`), the two shared admin modules (`components/ProductBenefits.tsx`, `lib/productBenefitActions.ts`), the admin route folder (`[locale]/financials/products/`, with `ProductLayout.tsx`, `productProfile.ts`, `productFrequency.ts`, `productPriceNotes.ts`), 16 test files, and the locale **keys** — the `products` namespace, the three shared `col_product` column labels, `applies_to_product` and `impact_products` — in en/es/ca. The old admin URL `[locale]/financials/sellable-items` stays as a `permanentRedirect` to the new one, exactly as `financials/gym-charges` already does for the name before that, so a bookmark still lands; its body names the retired term nowhere. **Deliberately untouched**, because stage 3 owns them with a migration and a `db-reviewer` pass: the `/sellable-items` API root (still mounted at that path, still called by the page), `gym_charges` with its `gym_charge_id` FK column and eight CHECKs, `sellable_item_professional_services`, every response field (`sellable_items` on an enriched Plan and on a tax rate's delete impact, `sellable_item_id`, `sellable_item_name`, `sellable_item_status`, `sellable_item_deleted_at`, `sellable_item_retired`), the stored `promotions.applies_to` value and Billing Simulation line `kind` (`'sellable_item'`), `financials.gym_charges` and the `gym_charge` audit entity type. That line is held by a second gate beside stage 1's: `api/src/test/product-identifiers.unit.test.ts` scans `api/src`, `apps/admin/src` and `apps/member/src` and fails on any retired identifier *shape* or the English prose — shapes rather than a name list, so it needs no allowlist, nothing stage 3 owns can match it, and only the two gates' own fixtures are exempt — while `product-terminology.unit.test.ts` now judges locale **keys** as well as values. No migration, no route change, no behaviour: API suite green at 230 files / 5071 tests, admin suite at 127 / 1892, `tsc --noEmit` clean on `api/`, `apps/admin` and `apps/member`. Stage 3 is the `/products` API root and the `Q1 C` database tier, and carries the `Closes`.)

- **Done**: #949 stage 3 of 3 (**the wire and the schema say Product.** The last stage of the `Q1 C` tier: a complete rename with no `/sellable-items` alias left behind. `app.ts` mounts the router at **`/products`** behind `requireFeatureEnabled('financials.products')`, and migration **214** moves the schema — `gym_charges` → `products`, `sellable_item_professional_services` → `product_professional_services`, `gym_charge_id` → `product_id` across the thirteen tables that key to it, `gym_charge_name` → `product_name` on the three promotion grant snapshots, `charge_types.is_gym_charge` → `is_product` (the **Charge Type** itself keeps its name, per `Q3`), the eight CHECKs to `products_*`/`chk_products_enrollment_status`, and every index and FK constraint whose name spelled the retired entity. Three stored values move with them: `promotions.applies_to` `'sellable_item'` → `'product'` with `chk_promotions_applies_to` rebuilt around the new set (which 214 exports, so `promotion-target.unit.test.ts` asserts against the constraint the database actually has), the feature-flag key `financials.gym_charges` → `financials.products` as one UPDATE so the gym's own choice travels with it, and `audit_logs.entity_type` `'gym_charge'` → `'product'` — the history's `entity_type` is the key `AUDIT_ENTITY_REGISTRY` and the Audit Log's filter are built from, while `previous_values`/`new_values` are left exactly as written. The response fields follow (`products`, `product_id`, `product_name`, `product_status`, `product_deleted_at`, `product_retired`), as do the Billing Simulation line `kind: 'product'` and the recycle bin's `product` entity type. Two things keep an older spelling on purpose: the legacy admin routes `financials/sellable-items` and `financials/gym-charges`, each a `permanentRedirect` to `financials/products`, and the old migrations, which are never edited. `product-identifiers.unit.test.ts` now bans the `snake_case`, `gym_charge*` and `gymCharge*` shapes stage 2 left to this stage — still a flat ban with no allowlist, its only carve-outs the two gates' fixtures and, by rule, a migration **file name** inside a `require()`. Verified by running migration 214 up → down → up with rows in place (the `user_membership_services.open_service_key` generated column and its UNIQUE index are rebuilt around the renamed column, since MySQL refuses to rename a column a generated column depends on) and by a second `up()` against the migrated schema, which is a no-op. API suite 233 files / 5083 tests, admin 127 / 1892, member 13 / 255, `tsc` clean in all three packages. `api/src/test/gym-charges.test.ts` is now `products.test.ts`.)

- **Done**: #956 stage 1 of 2 (**One Member, one Membership Plan — the backend invariant.** This reverses #634 §6/§14, which had widened the rule to "several plans in parallel, but only one of each type" (migration 172); the owner confirmed the reversal and every open question on the thread (Q1–Q5). Migration **213** restores migration 007's `user_memberships_one_active` UNIQUE index on `(active_member_key)` alone, and first **sweeps** the Members who own more than one live row — status-first (`active` before `paused`), then latest `starts_at`, then latest `id` survives; the rest are cancelled, dated like a replacement and given a `status_changed` ledger row carrying a `Migration 213 (#956): superseded by assignment #N` marker, the only thing that tells a swept row from one an admin closed. The index forbids two *active* rows, so those violators had to go one way or the other; closing a Member down to one *live* row is #956 Q2's product decision on top of that, not an index prerequisite. `ADD` comes before `DROP` and both assert `ALGORITHM=INPLACE, LOCK=NONE`, so a failed swap leaves the old constraint standing instead of none, and a server that could only do it by rebuilding the table says so; the swept row's `ends_at` is clamped with `GREATEST(…, starts_at)`, because the keeper is picked status-first and can have started before a newer `paused` row; and the migration **refuses to run** while any live assignment carries no `gym_id`, naming the ids, since `billing_events.gym_id` is NOT NULL. `npm run memberships:multi-active` (`scripts/report-multi-active-memberships.ts`) is the read-only report that identifies them *before* the migration runs, in two halves: the **owned** conflicts the sweep cancels (naming the keeper in advance) and the **covered** ones it deliberately leaves, where a Member is covered by two live plans but owns neither — cancelling a family plan because one of the people it covers has their own would take it away from everyone else on it, so that one is the gym's call. The rules are declared once in `domain/oneActivePlan.ts` (`LIVE_ASSIGNMENT_STATUSES` = `active` + `paused`, per Q2 — otherwise pausing would be a way around the rule, and a future-dated row is stored `active` so it counts too; `activePlanConflictBody()`; `supersedeStartsAtError()`) and the SQL half in `api/one-active-plan.ts` (`findLiveAssignmentsForMembers()` — `FOR UPDATE`, matching through `user_membership_members` so a **covered member of someone else's family plan already has a plan**, per Q4 — and `supersedeLiveAssignments()`). All three creation paths call them **inside their own insert transaction**: `POST /user-memberships`, `POST /user-memberships/:id/assign-new-plan` and `POST /membership-plans/:id/assign`. An unconfirmed caller gets **`409 { error: 'active_plan_exists', message, current_plan, conflicts }`** carrying both plan names and the current plan's dates, so stage 2's dialog needs no second read; `confirm: true` proceeds and the superseded row becomes **`cancelled`** with `closed_at` stamped now and `ends_at` set to the new plan's `starts_at` (Q3 — immediate, not scheduled; it was `expired` with no date at all before), so the two plans meet at one date and never overlap. A `starts_at` earlier than the plan being replaced is a 400. Assign New Plan needs no `confirm` (replacement is its contract) but does for a *second* live assignment it was not asked about; the Plans page's Assign is all-or-nothing across the selected Members. `POST /user-memberships/:id/members` is the fourth path the rule reaches and the only one with no `confirm` — covering a Member who already has a plan is a 409 naming it, since a replacement needs a new `starts_at` to end the old plan on and coverage has none. Concurrency is covered twice: two assignments of a Member who already has a plan serialise on its row lock, two for a Member who has none collide on the index. Knock-on: `POST /me/payment-requests`'s `409 multiple_active_memberships` is **removed** (the owner: "Remove, no need to preserve anything") — `user_membership_id` is still accepted and still narrows the read; the consolidated Member Billing Simulation is unchanged, as agreed. The four admin call sites now surface `body.message` through a new `apiErrorMessage()` so the interim error line reads a sentence rather than `active_plan_exists`. New `one-active-plan.unit.test.ts` (11), `one-active-membership-plan.test.ts` (19 — every path, both Q2 statuses, the covered-member case, cancel-the-dialog, the dates, the ledger row, tenant isolation and both concurrency shapes) and `one-active-plan-migration.test.ts` (9 — the sweep against the real migration, plus the report); the existing tests that asserted #634's parallel plans are inverted or rewritten against the shape #956 produces. **Stage 2** is the confirmation dialog, built from this 409.)

- **Done**: #956 stage 2 of 2 (**The replacement confirmation dialog.** Stage 1 made the backend the enforcement point and deliberately put both plan names and the current plan's dates in its `409 { error: 'active_plan_exists', message, current_plan, conflicts }`, so this stage needs no second read of what it is about to cancel. Two new modules carry it: `apps/admin/src/lib/activePlanConflict.ts` is the one place that recognises that body — by **shape** (`status === 409` *and* the code *and* a `current_plan`), so `/close`'s `unused_value_impacted` (#511) and a duplicate-key 409 still report themselves as an error line — and decides what the dialog says about it (`isSharedPlan()`, the `_many` wording keyed on how many plans Continue cancels, the `DD/MM/YYYY` that never goes through `Date`); `apps/admin/src/components/ReplacePlanDialog.tsx` is the one place that draws it, over the app's existing `ConfirmDialog` rather than a second overlay, modal or button pair (the ticket's UI section: "do not introduce a new visual system for this dialog"). `ConfirmDialog` gains one optional `details` slot for that, since the warning names two Plans and a date rather than being a longer sentence, and every existing caller is unchanged. All four admin entry points raise the same dialog — the Member card's `+ Add Membership Plan` and `Assign New Plan`, the Memberships page's create modal and the Plans page's Assign modal — because the rule is one rule and the words a gym owner reads about it must not differ per screen; the copy is one set of `common.replace_plan_*` keys in en/es/ca, resolved by the component (#806), never per page. Two properties are the point and are what `replace-plan-confirmation.test.ts` (28 tests) pins down: the first attempt **never** sends `confirm`, so a plan cannot be cancelled without the warning having been shown — which also means a click handler may not be passed by reference, since the `MouseEvent` would land in the `confirmReplacement` argument and confirm it (the Memberships page's `CrudModal` did exactly that) — and **Cancel clears the conflict and nothing else**: no request, no close, no reload, so the existing plan and its dates are untouched and the draft stays open. A family plan somebody else owns is named as such in the dialog (#956 Q4), because confirming takes it away from every member it covers. One defect fixed beside it: `POST /user-memberships/:id/members` has no `confirm` by design, and the Members modal read `err.message`, so the fourth path's conflict put the bare `active_plan_exists` code on screen instead of the sentence telling staff to close the member's own plan first — it goes through stage 1's `apiErrorMessage()` now. No API, schema or migration change; this stage closes #956.)

- **Done**: #969 stage 1 of 2 (**The exercise catalogue gets a compact filter toolbar — Base Exercises first.** With ~900 Base Exercises after #964's import, a single search box beside the title was the whole of the catalogue's filtering, and the ticket's §2 is explicit that the answer must not cost vertical space. Stage 1 lands the toolbar and the data behind it on **Base Exercises**: one row of `[ Search ] [ Slug ] [ Muscles ▾ ] [ Equipment ▾ ] [ Category ▾ ] [ Status ▾ ] [ Clear ]`, with `Showing N of M exercises` and the active-filter chips on the line under it and the list starting directly below. Three decisions came off the thread: `[ Exercise Type ▾ ]` **is the dataset's `equipment` axis** (the owner's `Q1` answer, and the only possible one — migration 074 dropped `exercises.exercise_type` and #1009 gates the name), #967 has merged so the name search is translation-aware on day one, and there is **no pagination** (`Q3`: "do not paginate, display all"), which leaves §14's count as the whole of §14/§15 and keeps the list response the plain array every caller reads. Server-side throughout (§16/§17): the vocabulary and the `WHERE` fragment #964 had inline became one pure module, `domain/exerciseListFilters.ts`, which is what makes stage 2's two screens the *same* filtering rather than a second reading of `?muscle=` (§19). It adds `slug` as its own field (partial, which is also how an exact slug matches — §4) and `muscle_match=any|all` (§6, default `any` = #964's existing behaviour, `all` becoming one `EXISTS` per muscle), keeping the role as the three parameters the endpoint already understood (§7); a closed set 400s an unknown value while a free-text metadata value is simply applied, since #964 preserves those verbatim and there is no set to compare against. `GET /platform/exercises/facets` answers `{ total, equipment, category, level, mechanic, source }` — the values **present** among the rows in scope, because declaring them would invent the taxonomy §8 forbids, and an empty facet is a control the page does not render (§9) — with `total` the count §14 quotes against. Frontend: one JSX-free declaration (`lib/exerciseFilters.ts`, each chip carrying the state that removing it produces) and one component (`components/exercises/ExerciseFilterBar.tsx`) over the app's existing chrome — `FilterBar`/`FilterField`/`filterControlStyle` (#724), the `MultiSelectFilter` popover (#350, which gained an optional pinned search box, a footer slot for the Match/Role radio groups and a trigger `style`), `StatusFilter` and `listNameBadgeStyle` for the chips — so no new colour, no new badge and no second filter-bar look; a free-text facet label is humanized from the stored value rather than looked up, because a value the dataset adds tomorrow has no locale key. 25 locale keys in en/es/ca. No migration. Tests: `api/src/test/exercise-list-filters.unit.test.ts` (new, 20 — the parse, the 400s, the fragment's placeholder/parameter parity, `any` vs `all`, the role arms, the slug-less context, the facets SQL and its grouping, and the absence of any Exercise Type filter), `api/src/test/base-exercise-source-filters.test.ts` (+10 integration — the slug field, `muscle_match` in both modes and in one role, the two 400s, the facets endpoint's auth, its sorted/de-duplicated values, a value only a gym's own exercise carries never being offered, and a soft-deleted base row out of both halves) and `apps/admin/src/test/exercise-filter-toolbar.test.ts` (new, 23 — the query every state produces, `muscle_match` sent only when it can change the answer, the chips' per-criterion removal and unique keys, the humanized facet label, the toolbar declaring no hex of its own, a facet-less control being absent, and locale parity with the placeholders each message promises). Admin suite green at 1,862; API unit suites green at 703; `tsc --noEmit` clean on both. **Stage 2** is the remaining half: the same toolbar on Custom Gym Exercises and the Import modal, where `GET /exercises` and `GET /exercises/base` gain the filters that apply to their rows — a gym's own exercises carry no slug and no source metadata, so there `withSlug: false` and an empty facet set are what §9/§19 look like.)

- **Done**: #969 stage 2 of 2 (**the same toolbar on a gym's own Exercises and on the Import modal.** Stage 1 lifted the filter vocabulary and the `WHERE` fragment into `api/src/domain/exerciseListFilters.ts` precisely so the other two screens would be *the same* filtering rather than a second reading of `?muscle=` (§19); this stage spends that. `GET /exercises` and `GET /exercises/base` now parse the identical parameters the platform list does — name-or-translation `?q=`, `?slug=`, `?status=`, the five metadata columns, and `?muscle=`/`?primary_muscle=`/`?secondary_muscle=` with `?muscle_match=any|all` — and each screen renders the one `ExerciseFilterBar` over the one `lib/exerciseFilters.ts` state: the gym list's own search box and Status dropdown and the modal's own name field and single-muscle `<select>` are gone, with five locale keys (`exercises.search_placeholder` and the four `import_filter_*`) retired in en/es/ca because nothing resolves them any more. Two context differences are the parameters, not forks: `withSlug: false` on the gym list, because a gym's own exercises carry no slug for `?q=` or `?slug=` to match (no editor writes one, and `POST /exercises/import` does not copy the Base Exercise's), so §4's field is not offered there; and the Status control stays off the Import modal, since the library is `status = 'active'` by definition. Each screen reads its **own** facets for §8/§9/§14 — `GET /exercises/facets` over the gym's rows and `GET /exercises/base/facets` over the importable library, both gym-facing for the reason `/base` is (a gym admin importing a System row is not a superadmin) and both registered before `/:id` — so one gym's values never reach another's dropdowns, a gym's own value never reaches the library's, and the Equipment and Category controls are simply absent on the gym screen (its rows carry no source metadata), leaving Search + Muscles + Status and the count. One behaviour gave way to the single vocabulary: `GET /exercises/base?muscle=<unknown>` answered `400` and now matches nothing, because only `status` and `muscle_match` are closed sets and #964 §8 lets the importer store a muscle key outside `MUSCLE_KEYS` rather than fail — refusing one would make a legitimately stored muscle unfilterable. `exerciseFacetsQuery(scopeSql, scopeParams)` is the one new piece of the domain module: the union repeats its scope once per column, so a parameterised scope's values are repeated there rather than in a router counting `EXERCISE_METADATA_COLUMNS` for itself. No migration, no new frontend concept, no pagination (`Q3` stands). Tests: `api/src/test/gym-exercise-filters.test.ts` (new, 20 integration — the muscle roles and both match modes on both routes, AND across groups, the two 400s, the slug neither searched nor filtered on the gym list, tenant isolation, and each facets route's auth, values, scope and total), `exercise-list-filters.unit.test.ts` (+2 for the scoped facets query), `exercises.test.ts` (the retired 400 becomes the documented "matches nothing"), `apps/admin/src/test/exercise-filter-toolbar.test.ts` (+10 — each screen renders the shared bar, passes the right context props, reads its own facets route and narrows nothing in the browser) and `import-exercises.test.ts` (the modal's filtering and its retired keys). Admin suite green at 1,901 / 127 files; API unit suites green at 755 / 51 files; `tsc --noEmit` clean on `api/` and `apps/admin`, `npm run build` clean on the admin app. The integration suites need MySQL, which this container has no Docker for — CI runs them.)

- **Done**: #958 (**The Member's Membership Plans as Assigned Membership Plan cards.** The section was a list of thin cards with a generic `+ Add Membership Plan`; #956 made a Member hold at most one plan, so the add is now named on the plan it replaces and each assignment gets a card of its own. All five questions on the thread were answered and the two dependencies (#956, #924 stage 5) had both landed. The card carries plan name, the fee resolved for its next cycle, **Created by**, **Created at**, Start date, End date and Status — the last five as shared `CardDetailRow`s — with `Assign New Plan` · `Cancel Plan` · `Details` in the `⋮` (Q2 `menu`: three buttons per card on a member with five past plans is chrome, not clarity) and `Details` alone on a historical card. Expanding it mounts the **shared** Assigned Plan card body (Q3 `share`), which moved out of the page directory into `apps/admin/src/components/assignedPlan/` and is rendered by both hosts — one section list, one set of `assigned_plans_page` keys, one `GET /user-memberships/:id` read, so `Membership Plan → Details` and `Member → Assigned plan → Expanded` really are the same shape (§"Snapshot structure"), and every later stage of #924 reaches the Member card for free. The Member card passes `embedded`, which drops that body's own header and `⋮` and therefore its Edit mode: `⋮ → Edit` is the single entry point into one (#797), so expanding a plan here reads the assignment and editing stays on the Assigned Plans page. `Details` reuses the existing `AssignedPlanDetailsModal` through a small loader (`AssignedPlanDetailsDialog`), since the Member card lists plans through the configuration read and has no detail in hand. **Q4 is the schema half**: key metadata is not to come from `audit_logs`, so migration **215** gives `user_memberships` the `created_by_name`/`created_by_type` snapshot pair (#799's shape, no CHECK — `ADD CONSTRAINT` rebuilds that table under `ALGORITHM=COPY`, as migrations 174/189/192/194 all noted), stamped by the three paths that insert a row and backfilled exactly from the audit rows it replaces — all three creation actions, `create`, `assign_new_plan` and the Plans page's `assign_plan`, which is also a defect found in `loadAuditMetadata()`'s mirror-image exclusion list: a bulk-assigned plan reported its creator as the last person to *modify* it, since the creation row was then the newest row not excluded. `created_by_type` stays NULL on a backfilled row, because `audit_logs` cannot say whether the actor was a gym admin or a superadmin acting on the gym and writing `staff` for all of them would mislabel every one of the latter. The *modified* pair stays derived, deliberately: the nightly run, the payment webhook and the dunning escalation all modify an assignment and none of them is a person. `GET /user-memberships/member/:id/configuration` reports `created_at` as a bare date beside the pair; the list endpoint carries them as the plain columns they are. New `assigned-plan-creation-actor.test.ts` (5) and `member-membership-plan-cards.test.ts` (16); the sixteen admin tests that scanned the moved files follow them, and `formChrome` gains the one expand affordance the applied-Promotion card and this one now share.)

- **Done**: #961 (**The Member card as five parallel tabs.** Expanding a Member rendered ten sections as one column several screens long; the owner's answers on the thread settled the shape — tabs **inside the expanded row** (`Q1`, "by default system should focus on the first one PROFILE but others should be clickable and rendered on click"), **all** the sections the ticket's own table left unassigned to **Products & Services** (`Q2`), the Personal Goals tab showing the assignments #948 §4 shipped rather than an empty state (`Q3`), and the work waiting for #956 and #958, which have both landed (`Q4`). One declaration carries it: `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/members/memberTabs.ts` names the five tabs in order (Profile · Products & Services · Nutrition · Personal Goals · Training Plan) and the `members.section_*` keys each one renders — Profile keeps Profile, Account and Membership Plans (§1); Products & Services takes Additional Products, the Billing Simulation, PT Class Slots, Session Packages and Billing Events, in the order the single column had them; the last three take the section they are named after. A section belongs to **exactly one** tab, which the new test asserts against the sections `MemberExpandedRow` actually renders, so none can be shown twice or lost in the move. The strip is the app's **one** tab component now: `components/Tabs.tsx`, promoted out of the Nutrition Library's `LibraryTabs` (which becomes a binding of it to `LIBRARY_TABS`) rather than drawn a second time, resolving no label (#901), declaring no colour — the active tab follows `var(--brand, …)`, so there is no custom active-tab hue and no lilac literal (#912/#954) — and scrolling rather than wrapping at phone width. The page owns the selected tab per expanded Member and mirrors it in the URL (`?member=<id>&tab=<tabId>`, `memberTabFromParam()` falling back to Profile for anything else, and collapsing the row clearing both), so a refresh, back/forward and a pasted link all land on the same work area; `MemberExpandedRow` is *handed* its tab and renders only that tab's sections, each tab opening with `divider={false}` on its first one so no tab starts under the card's hairline. The inline Edit form renders in the **Profile** pane, because Profile is the field set it writes and its Save/Cancel belongs with those fields (#929), and `⋮ → Edit` selects that tab before opening it so a form can never open behind a tab the user was left on; switching tabs discards nothing, since the draft is the page's state. **Layout only**: no migration, no API change, no endpoint, payload or permission moved, no section heading renamed — the only new locale keys are the five tab labels in en/es/ca, and the Member stays a row in the list rather than gaining a `/members/[id]` route (`Q1`). Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/member-tabs.test.ts` (new, 18 — the declaration and its order, the one-tab-per-section invariant against the rendered sections, the `?tab=` fallback, each pane rendering its own sections in declared order and no other tab's, no section outside a tab, the no-divider-first rule, the shared strip and its tablist/keyboard/scroll behaviour, the hex-only-as-a-var-fallback scan, the URL round trip and the Profile-tab Edit rule, plus the tab labels in all three locales), with the four source-scans that pinned the old single-column shape (`goal-library-ui`, `member-profile-layout`, `members-list-ui`) updated to the new wiring. Admin suite green at 1,963; `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` clean.)

- **Done**: #975 (**An Admin calendar event is the event's colour too; the status stays a badge.** #976 had already given the Members App this rule; the owner confirmed `Q1 event colour` on the thread — *"the event should always retain its configured event colour… the booking/status state should be communicated separately through the status chip"* — so the Admin half is now the same answer. It is the third design in this place in three tickets: #541 painted the event *by status*, #559 stage 3 overturned that and moved the status into `CalendarStatusBadge` with the box on the theme's event tokens, and in doing so left `calendar_events.color` editable and read by nothing (the #677 defect shape — the event editor's picker pre-fills from the Activity Type and the `PUT` persists it). This ticket restores the *configured* colour only: `apps/admin/src/lib/calendarEventPaint.ts` resolves `COALESCE(calendar_events.color, activity_types.color)`, answers `null` for a colour that is absent, blank or not a usable hex (so the theme's `calendarEventBackground`/`Border`/`Text` still paint that box — FullCalendar writes an inline style, which beats the tokens), and scores the foreground over `#111827`/`#ffffff` with the admin's own `calendarContrast.ts` `contrastRatio()` rather than a second WCAG copy. It cannot see a status, a booking count or a capacity, which is what keeps #977 §8 true by construction. Two CSS rules behind the `gd-event-colored` class restore what an inline background swallows: hover darkens the gym's colour instead of replacing it, and the focus ring becomes `currentColor`. No API or schema change — both reads already project the two columns — and §3's admin waitlist count had already landed with #977 §9. Frontend only: the paint module, the two mappings in the calendar page, `CalendarThemeStyles`, `apps/admin/src/test/calendar-event-paint.test.ts` (14), the two #559/#977 guards rewritten from "no inline colour" to "no colour the page spells itself", and the docs. Members App unchanged.)

- **Done**: #979 (**Reactivate a cancelled event.** A cancellation was one-way: the panel's Actions block was hidden entirely for a `cancelled` session, so an event cancelled by mistake could only be replaced by a new one — a new id, a new date, and every enrolled member and waitlist place gone. `POST /class-sessions/:id/reactivate` is the way back, and it writes `status = 'scheduled'` (clearing the `cancellation_reason` it undoes) and nothing else: `Completed` stays reserved for an event that actually took place, so the lifecycle is **Scheduled → Cancelled → Scheduled** and no fifth state appears. Anything not `cancelled` is a `400 not_cancelled`, which is also what the loser of two concurrent calls gets, since the `UPDATE` carries `status = 'cancelled'` in its own WHERE. The load-bearing discovery is that **nothing has to be restored**: `POST /:id/cancel` has only ever written the event's status and never touched `calendar_event_bookings`, so the enrolled members, the waitlist and its `waitlist_position` ordering survive a cancellation untouched — reactivation therefore inserts nothing at all, which is what makes §11's duplicate booking, duplicate waitlist entry and second event structurally impossible rather than merely avoided, and a booking the member cancelled themselves stays cancelled. The route's booking read exists only to *report* what came back. Two things it does guard. The cancellation freed the trainer/space slot — every conflict check in the router skips `cancelled` rows — so re-occupying it goes through `assertSlotAvailable()`, the same `FOR UPDATE` shared-slot rule `POST /` and `PUT /:id` use, which #979 extracted into one helper rather than copying a third time, and it answers the same four `409` codes when another session has taken the slot meanwhile. And the members who held a booking are alerted with the new `event_reactivated` (migration 216 — the `NotificationType` union *and* `chk_member_notifications_type`, per CLAUDE.md's two-places rule), deliberately **not** `booking_confirmed`, since the member booked nothing (§6/§11); a waitlisted member is not alerted, because they were never told the class was cancelled either and have no booking to have restored. The Alerts page reads *Class reactivated* plus one explanatory line (`detail_event_reactivated`), behind a declared type list rather than a `t()` fallback, since next-intl prints a missing key verbatim. A **past** cancelled event is reactivatable on purpose — "cancelled by mistake" is usually noticed after the class should have run — and #977's rule then classifies it (`not_used` when nobody is on it), which is the only interaction with that axis. In the admin, a cancelled session's Actions block offers exactly one action (completing, moving or re-cancelling all act on a slot nobody holds), behind the panel's own inline confirm card — the shape `Mark as completed` and `Cancel event` already use, so no second overlay style appears — with `primaryActionColors` and no colour of its own (#912/#954), and the panel reloads rather than closing so §8's status, capacity, enrolled list and waiting list read as restored immediately. §9's audit row records **previous → new** status through `recordAudit` (`action: 'reactivate'`, added to `AUDIT_ACTIONS` so it reaches the Audit Log's filter) and carries the cancellation reason the column no longer holds plus `restored_member_ids`/`restored_waitlist_member_ids`. A manual calendar entry has no reactivate route: its status is edited in `EventDetailsPanel`'s own select, which is also how it is cancelled, so the explicit action pairs with the explicit cancel only sessions have. Tests: `api/src/test/event-reactivation.test.ts` (new, 21 — the transition and the cleared reason, a past event, the `not_cancelled` refusals including a completed event and a second call, tenant isolation, 404 for a manual entry, 401 unauthenticated, the booking row keeping its own id, the waitlist's ordering, no duplicates, a self-cancelled booking left alone, the slot taken/free/cancelled-neighbour/shared cases, the alert set and the `booking_confirmed` that is not sent, the silence for an empty event, and the audit payload). Six locale keys in en/es/ca for the admin and two for the member app.)

- **Done**: #981 (**Trainer and space, readable on the calendar event itself.** An admin scanning the week saw a title and a booking count and had to open each event to learn who was teaching it or which room it was in; the Members App box named the trainer and never the space. Both apps now show both in the Day and Week views, and §3's requirement — the values are the **occurrence's** own — already held server-side: `GET /calendar-events`, `GET /class-sessions` and `GET /me/schedule` project `space_name`/`trainer_name` by joining `ce.space_id`/`ce.trainer_membership_id`, so a session retargeted away from its Activity Type's defaults reads as *Jane Smith · Studio 2* rather than the Activity's *John Smith · Studio 1*. **No migration, no API change, no new endpoint** — one pure module per app (the two share no frontend module, as #975/#976 established for the colour): `apps/admin/src/lib/calendarEventMeta.ts` and `memberEventMeta()`/`memberEventMetaLine()` in `apps/member/src/lib/calendarEventDisplay.ts`. Three of their answers are the rule rather than the implementation. A name that is absent, blank or whitespace answers `null` and **renders no line at all** (§6 — no `N/A`, no `Unknown`, no `Not assigned`), which is also why the module returns the *lines* rather than a joined string: a dangling `·` is the empty placeholder the ticket forbids. The **layout follows the view**, since a FullCalendar event cannot measure itself — `full` (a line each, §4's own mock) for the admin Day view, `compact` (one truncated `Trainer · Space`) for the admin Week view and both member time-grid views, because §5's example is compact on one line and the Members App is phone-first — and the admin Day view's occupancy counts keep a line of their own rather than being folded in, so nothing the box already carried was lost. The **trainer is the effective one where a read has one** (`effective_trainer_name ?? trainer_name`, who actually delivered the occurrence, #193), which is the choice the day view was already making inline and is now made once. Every other view is untouched per §1: the admin month cell keeps title, time and badge, and the member month cell keeps its trainer-alone line. It stays **text** (§8): the box is still painted by `calendarEventPaint()`/`eventBackgroundColor()` from the event's own colour, neither module declares a hue or resolves a locale key (a person's name and a room's name are data, not copy), and §10's dynamic update needed nothing — both pages already `refetch()` after a save. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/calendar-event-meta.test.ts` (new, 16) and `apps/member/src/test/calendar-event-meta.test.ts` (new, 10). Admin suite green at 2,006; `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` clean on both apps. Note one **pre-existing** failure left alone: `apps/member/src/test/calendar-theme-css.test.ts`'s byte-identical-stylesheet assertion already fails on `main` — the two apps' `CalendarThemeStyles` diverged when #975 added the `gd-event-colored` rules to the Admin's only — which is #975's drift to settle, not this ticket's, and CI does not run either app's suite.)

- **Done**: #783 (Internal run routes: the nginx GitHub Actions IP allowlist on `/billing/` is removed rather than automated — `corback.conf` loses `location /billing/`, the allowlist file and its refresh script are deleted; a per-route `internalRunLimiter` in `app.ts` (`domain/internalRunRateLimit.ts`, `INTERNAL_RUN_RATE_LIMIT_MAX`/`_WINDOW_MINUTES`, default 10 per 15 min per IP, only 401s count) now fronts `/billing/*` and `/recurring-bookings/*`; installing the conf on the server and rotating both internal secrets stay in `go-to-production.md`)

- **Done**: #784 (GitHub `production` environment — repo side landed: the billing, recurring booking and four deploy workflows take a `workflow_dispatch` `environment` input (`dev` | `production`, default `dev`) and the two scheduled ones read `vars.API_BASE_URL` instead of a literal host. Everything still targets `dev`; creating `production` and the one-line schedule switch are owner steps in `docs/go-to-production.md` §1.)

- **Done**: #982 (**A custom tile image replaces the Members App's default icon, at full opacity.** #728 painted each home surface with its Theme's artwork *under a scrim* — the theme's own `cardBackground` at 82 % — and left the default emoji sitting on top of it, so an uploaded Calendar picture read as a washed-out grey backdrop behind a 📅 rather than as the tile's visual: a black photograph came out mid-grey, and the one thing the gym configured was the one thing hardest to see. Frontend-only, no migration, no API change, no new asset. `cardBackgroundStyleValue()` now takes the URL **alone** — the scrim argument and `CARD_SCRIM_ALPHA` are gone rather than defaulted, so no caller can reintroduce an overlay — and `MembersSectionCard` paints the image at 100 % opacity with its own colours, contrast and background intact (§2). The page background keeps its 72 % scrim and that is deliberate, not an inconsistency: the general `background` slot is one surface under *every* page's text and controls at once, which is the "following its existing purpose" §Apply-to grants it, while a Section Card carries the single label its tile design already places. For §1 the component exports `useSectionImageUrl()`, the one place that answers "does this slot have artwork", and `NavTile` renders its emoji only when the answer is `null` — not over the picture, not under it, no placeholder and no `<img>` in its place — so clearing the image brings the default icon back with no further change (§Acceptance). The tile keeps the box the icon gave it (a `minHeight` applied *only* when there is artwork, so a tile without one keeps today's style object byte-for-byte) and the label stays in both states (§3). The default emojis themselves, the six slots, the Theme editor and every other surface are untouched. Note the consequence the ticket asks for explicitly: the My Membership card's text now sits on the uploaded image at full opacity too, so a dark photograph in that slot is a legibility choice the gym makes — §2 forbids the overlay that used to hide it. Tests: `apps/member/src/test/members-section-backgrounds.test.ts` rewritten around the new rule (24 cases — the full-opacity composition and the one-argument signature that keeps it, the page scrim surviving, the icon rendered only for an unconfigured slot, the emojis left intact, no placeholder or `<img>`, the preserved tile box, and #728's own "background, not a redesign" guards unchanged). Member suite green at 275 passing; `tsc --noEmit` clean. One pre-existing failure, `calendar-theme-css.test.ts`, is unrelated and reproduces on `main`.)

- **Done**: #983 (**The Members App renders with the Members App theme, from one declaration.** #833 added the 18 Members App settings and wired each to a CSS variable, but only four surfaces read them — the header, the Section Card border, the Calendar's event window and its inputs — so a gym that configured a dark brown header and orange accents still got white cards, near-black headings and a lilac-on-slate filter bar: every other screen spelled its colours as literals the theme cannot move, and the same heading level was `#18181b` on one page and `#71717a` on the next. **Frontend-only: no migration, no API change, no new endpoint, no new setting.** `apps/member/src/lib/memberChrome.ts` is now the one place this app spells a visual value — the rule `listChrome.ts` is for an Admin list and `formChrome.ts` for the inside of an Admin card — holding each role as the variable that carries it (`pageBackground`, `surface`, `text`/`textSecondary`/`textMuted`, `separator`, the three `title1..3`, the two Section Card border settings, the input pair, the primary/secondary/destructive buttons, the four status colours and the Calendar page's own button/modal/input surfaces), with the value `applyTokens()` writes as each `var()` fallback rather than whatever literal a page happened to carry. Every page and component spreads what it exports: `sectionCardStyle` gives §3's list (the dashboard, My Membership, My Training Plan, My Bookings, My Nutrition, Packages, Profile and the payment screens) the border `MembersSectionCard` already gave the navigation tiles, headings take §4's three Title settings, and §5's Calendar settings reach the filter bar and the event window — those controls read `--gd-sidebar-selected-bg`, the *Admin sidebar's* colour, until this ticket, which no Members App setting can move. Two defects fixed beyond the sweep: `MembersBackground` tinted its scrim with `colors.pageBackground` directly, so a Theme overriding the Members App background got the Admin colour over its photograph (§2/§6 — it resolves through `membersAppVarValue()` now), and the status pills were three copies of the same four-state map, so one tone is `statusPillStyle(statusTone(...))` and its tint is `color-mix()` of the theme's own status colour over the card surface rather than a stored pastel. Three files keep a literal and each is asserted rather than assumed: `layout.tsx`'s static `theme-color` meta (read from the document head before any gym resolves, and it tints the browser's chrome, not a surface), and `AdminBar`/`ImpersonationBanner`, the two bars telling a superadmin whose account they are looking at — the platform's, deliberately outside the Theme, because a gym able to repaint them could make them vanish into the page it designed. Tests: `api/src/test/members-app-theme-consumption.unit.test.ts` (new, 5) is the gate, in the API suite for #1009's reason — CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only — walking every `.tsx` under the member app's `app/` and `components/` and failing on a colour outside the chrome module once comments and `var()` fallbacks are stripped, on any surface reading a `--gd-sidebar-*` variable, and on the chrome module not reading one of the 21 variables it must; `apps/member/src/test/members-app-theme-vars.test.ts`'s painting block rewritten around the one-place rule (which object each surface spreads, the scrim's resolution, and #833's "no setting unpainted" check now counting through that module). Member suite green at 277 across 15 files — which includes clearing the **pre-existing** `calendar-theme-css.test.ts` failure #981's note flagged: the two apps' calendar stylesheets had drifted when #975 added its two `gd-event-colored` rules to the Admin's only, so the Member copy is byte-identical again and those rules are inert here (a member's events take their colour inline and nothing adds that class) — API unit suites green at 786 across 54 files, `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` clean. The API integration suites were not run here: this environment has no MySQL, and the ticket touches no API code.)

- **Done**: #984 (**The Calendar page background is the Members App's own, not the Calendar tile's.** #725 gave each of the six Members App images a page to paint and #728 gave five of them a home tile as well, so the `calendar` image was doing both jobs at once: a 512×512 tile, stretched to `cover` and anchored to the viewport, sat behind the Calendar's time grid and read as the dashboard tile leaking onto the page — which #982 made more visible still, since a tile is painted at full opacity now while the page keeps its 72 % scrim, so the same artwork appeared twice in two different treatments. **Frontend-only: no migration, no API change, no new slot, no asset touched.** The whole fix is that `slotForPathname()` no longer names `calendar`, so the Calendar page falls through to the general `background` slot exactly as `/profile`, `/packages` and the home page already do: the global Members App background image when one is configured, and — when it is not — nothing at all, which clears the property and lets `var(--gd-app-bg)` show through, the Members App background colour resolved through #983's per-setting inheritance (the ticket's three-step priority, in that order, with no second resolution anywhere). Two independences hold by construction rather than by a guard: the tile keeps its own image, because `MembersSectionCard`/`useSectionImageUrl()` ask for the `calendar` slot by name and never through the path map, so changing the tile cannot move the page and the slot stays one of the six; and the Calendar's own theme settings (header, buttons, time column, modal surfaces) are a different group of settings that this ticket does not read. The other four section pages are deliberately **unchanged** — the ticket's title, requirements and every acceptance criterion name the Calendar, and #725's per-section page artwork is a design decision, not a defect, so stripping it from `/training`, `/nutrition`, `/schedule` and `/membership` would be a product change nobody asked for. Flagged on the thread in case the same is wanted for them. Tests: `apps/member/src/test/members-background.test.ts` (20, four new cases — `/calendar` and a nested `/calendar/<date>` resolving to `background`, the slot still reachable for the tile, and the page taking the general image or the theme colour depending on what is configured). Member suite green at 281 across 15 files; `api/src/test/members-app-theme-consumption.unit.test.ts` and the CodeQL gate green; `tsc --noEmit` clean. The API integration suites were not run: this environment has no MySQL and the ticket touches no API code.)

- **Done**: #985 (**The Theme's Center assignments are an inline checkbox list, saved with the card.** Assigning a Theme to Centers meant opening a modal, searching, ticking, pressing *Assign* — and reading which Centers used the Theme meant opening that modal too, because the section listed only the ones already on it. The section is now the list: `All Centers` above a hairline, then **every** non-deleted Center of the gym, each box being that Center's own `centers.theme_id`, persisted by the card's existing `Save changes`. The `+ Assign Centers…` button, its modal, the modal's search field, its Assign/Cancel pair, the center search field, the show-all disclosure and the per-row `Restore Inheritance` button are all gone — unticking a box *is* Restore Inheritance, which is what makes the two halves of the old UI one control. Backend: `PUT /system/themes/:id/centers`, one **replace-all** write (an empty array is a legitimate save), with `api/src/domain/themeCenterAssignments.ts` deciding what moves — the requested Centers that do not already hold the Theme are assigned, the ones that hold it and were left out go back to NULL, and a Center pointing at *another* Theme and not requested is in neither list, because the submitted set is this Theme's assignments and never every Center's. Both UPDATEs run in one transaction, since a half-saved list would assign some Centers and leave others on a Theme the admin had unticked. It replaces the three routes the picker needed (`GET /:id/unassigned-centers`, `POST /:id/assign-centers`, `DELETE /:id/centers/:centerId`), all removed: a set edited as a set has one writer. Three decisions are the ticket's rather than the diff's. **The Active rule guards assigning, not the request** — a Theme taken out of service must still be removable from the Centers it was left on, so an empty set is accepted for a `draft`/`inactive` row and a non-empty one is still a 400. **An inherited Center's box stays unticked**: `GET /:id/assignments` reports `is_assigned` (its own column, null-safe `<=>`) beside `is_inherited` (no assignment of its own, and this Theme is the Gym Default), and the list renders the second as an `Inherited` tag beside the name — ticking the box is what would make the assignment explicit, so a checkbox that pre-ticked itself would silently end a Center's inheritance on the next unrelated save. And **a Base Theme now gets the Save pair too**: its configuration belongs to the platform and stays read-only on this screen (the settings `PUT`, which would 403, is not attempted), but its Center assignments are the gym's and there is no Assign action left to persist them. Frontend: `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/themes/centerAssignments.ts` holds the draft rules JSX-free — `All Centers` is **derived** (`allCentersChecked`), so selecting every Center by hand ticks it and unticking one clears it with no third piece of state to keep in step, and `centerSelectionChanged` puts the Centers in the card's own dirty state, so Save enables, Cancel discards and the unsaved-changes guard covers them like any other field. A failed assignment save keeps the draft *and* its baseline, so Save is the retry exactly as it is for a failed asset (#830), and its message is its own line above the asset report rather than replacing it. A gym with no Centers reads `No centers available.` (§7) and a non-active Theme gets disabled boxes with the reason. Seven retired locale keys removed from en/es/ca, five added. No migration. One thing the first CI run caught and is now a rule of the module: `centers.id` is an auto-increment **integer** (migration 043), so an id is a number on the wire from one client and a string from another — a strict string check 400d every real Center, in the browser as well as in the suite, so both sides compare through `centerKey()` and the response reports the stored form. Tests: `api/src/test/theme-center-assignments.unit.test.ts` (9 pure — the plan in both directions, idempotence, the other-Theme Center left alone, a repeated id, unknown ids) and `theme-assignments.test.ts` rewritten around the new route (the replace-all pair, idempotence, the 400s writing nothing, both halves of the Active rule, the two read flags, and the three retired routes answering 404); `apps/admin/src/test/theme-center-assignments.test.ts` (12) with `theme-primary-buttons.test.ts` updated — the gym screen's second `primaryBtnSmall()` and its last `#6c63ff` both went with the picker. Admin suite green at 2,040; API unit suites green (55 files, 794 tests); `tsc --noEmit` clean on both. No MySQL in the session container, so the integration file runs in CI.)

- **Done**: #986 (**The Default Trainer dropdown lists every active staff member.** `GET /trainers` filtered on the *role* of the login row — `role IN ('trainer_performance','trainer_perf_nutrition')`, which `PROFILE_ROLE_MAP` only ever gives the two *Personal Trainer* HR profiles — so a gym whose staff are Gym Managers, Front Desk, Accountants or Nutritionists had an **empty** Default Trainer dropdown on every Activity, while the Staff page listed those same people as Active. Eligibility is employment now, and nothing else: `api/src/domain/trainerAssignment.ts` is the one place that decides it (`staff.employment_status = 'active'` on a live row of the gym) and the one place the §5 ordering lives (first name then last name, from the staff row's own names rather than `gym_memberships.name`, which is a snapshot of them taken when the login was granted). **No migration and no new route**: the three readers are projections of one SQL fragment — `GET /trainers` (the Activity Type picker, the Calendar's two event panels and the Trainer Availability page), `GET /me/trainers` (the member calendar's filter, id + name only) and `isAssignableTrainer()`, which every writer that stores a trainer validates against — `POST`/`PUT /activity-types`, `validateSessionRefs()` on the class-session writes, `PUT /class-sessions/:id/effective-trainer` and `POST /trainer-availability`. The last three carried the retired role filter as their own validation, so without that the picker would have offered an active Front Desk member whom #980 stage 1's panel and the Trainer Availability page then refused. That validation is the ticket's second defect: `default_trainer_membership_id` was inserted **unvalidated**, so another gym's membership id satisfied the FK and crossed the tenant boundary, and a string `"5"` never equalled the stored `5`, so every save re-propagated the trainer onto future events. Three decisions are the ticket's rather than the diff's. `staff.current_status` (`on_vacation`, `suspended`, …) is deliberately **not** a filter — the ticket says only employment status may decide, and a trainer on holiday is still employed; migration 067 keeps the two columns apart for exactly that reason. §3's "existing value preserved" is `trainerWriteNeedsLookup(next, current)` — #945's `productFrequencyWriteError` shape — so a value identical to the stored one is not a *selection* and an edit of an activity's capacity cannot 400 because that trainer has since left, with the admin offering it as a **disabled** option (the #980 stage 1 device, over the `default_trainer_name` the read already returns) so it reads correctly instead of appearing to be none. And the one condition that is structural rather than a rule is `staff.gym_membership_id`: every table that stores a trainer keys to `gym_memberships.id`, so an active staff member whose login grant errored has no id to be stored under — repaired from the Staff card's App access section, not papered over here. Tests: `api/src/test/trainer-assignment.unit.test.ts` (new, 10 pure — the parser's three answers, the write rule in both directions, and that the shared SQL filters on employment and on nothing else, which is the gate against a role or center condition coming back), `api/src/test/trainers.test.ts` (new, 6 — a Front Desk employee offered, an inactive / soft-deleted / login-only / login-less one not, the staff record's own name, the §5 ordering, gym scoping and the two auth guards) and 7 cases appended to `activity-types.test.ts` (an active staff member of any profile stored and named, the foreign-gym and non-integer 400s, the explicit clear, an unrelated edit keeping a trainer who has since left, and the 400 on newly selecting one). `member-calendar.test.ts`'s `/me/trainers` fixture gains the employment record its trainer login lacked, plus the assertion that a login with no staff row behind it is not offered, and five other fixtures take the new `createTestStaffForMembership()` helper for the same reason — a `gym_memberships` row used as a trainer needs the employment record behind it now. API unit suites green at 805 across 56 files; `tsc --noEmit` clean in `api` and `apps/admin`. The integration files were not executed here — this environment has no MySQL and no Docker daemon — so CI is their first run.)

- **Done**: #1003 (**The desktop sidebar collapses to icons only.** The navigation was one fixed 220px column: nine groups, their subsections, and no way to give the page back the width on a laptop screen. It has two states now — expanded (labels and subsections, 220px) and collapsed (the group icons alone, 64px) — with the control in the panel's own top row on desktop, and `apps/admin/src/lib/sidebarCollapse.ts` is the one place that decides all of it: the two widths, the breakpoint (`(min-width: 769px)`, mirroring AppShell's own split rather than declaring a second one), the stored preference (`sidebarCollapsed` in `localStorage`, each access in a `try/catch` so a private window or blocked site data cannot break a render) and `navGroupContainsActivePath()`. That last one is the ticket's load-bearing part, because §2's rule is that **the active navigation item is always a subsection**: collapsed, the only thing left to highlight is its parent section's icon, so both modes ask one rule and the highlight cannot move when the sidebar collapses — asking it twice is exactly how the two would drift. The collapsed icon wears the treatment an active item already has (`--gd-sidebar-selected-bg`/`-text` plus the 3px `--brand` rail, no new colour, §4) and is still **not** an active item: clicking it navigates nowhere, it brings the sidebar back and opens that group (`expandIntoGroup()`), which is what keeps every option reachable without the flyout submenu §4's tooltip requirement rules out. The expanded header is byte-identical in behaviour — the highlight is gated on `collapsed && isAnyChildActive`, so expanded mode keeps the active subsection carrying it — and `NavGroup` renders the same single `<NavIcon>` at the same `NAV_ICON_SIZE` in both modes (#884's one-occurrence rule held, which is why the collapsed button is the group header with its chevron and label dropped rather than a second button). The width lives on `.sidebar-panel` with a 150ms transition, so `main` — the row's flexible item — reclaims the space by itself and **AppShell needed no change at all**, which is also what preserves the mobile drawer (§5): below the breakpoint the panel is the drawer, open at full width or off-screen, so the preference is ignored rather than shrinking it to a strip. The two collapse states are independent of which groups are open (§3): collapsing never writes `navGroupsExpanded` (`sessionStorage`, per tab) and expanding restores exactly what was open. One defect found on the way and fixed: the *Auto-expand group containing active route* effect compared `pathname` against the config's unresolved `/{{locale}}/…` hrefs, so it had never fired for any page — it goes through the locale-resolved items now, which is what makes §3's "the previously selected navigation context is restored" true on a cold load. Frontend-only — no migration, no API, no new Theme setting — plus two locale keys (`nav.collapse_sidebar`, `nav.expand_sidebar`) in en/es/ca. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/sidebar-collapse.test.ts` (new, 24 — the widths and the shared breakpoint, the two storage keys staying apart, `navGroupContainsActivePath()` including that a section's own path is not a prefix match, the control's desktop-only render and its naming in both states, no second scroll container (#883), the width coming from the helper, AppShell untouched, the collapsed icon's single occurrence and existing-treatment highlight, the gate that keeps expanded mode unhighlighted, the tooltip/`aria-label`, and locale parity). Admin suite green at 2,072 across 138 files; `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` clean.)

- **Done**: #1020 (**A thicker divider above the Cordel section.** The sidebar's nine groups ran together: the platform's own administration (Cordel, superadmin-only) read as the tenth gym section rather than as a different kind of thing, and the only divider the navigation had was the 1px hairline `NavItem.separatorAbove` draws *inside* a group. There are two now, and the ticket's "slightly thicker than the standard sidebar separators" is a relationship in code rather than two unrelated literals: `apps/admin/src/components/navChrome.ts` declares the one colour both draw in (`rgba(255,255,255,0.15)`, the white alpha the rest of the sidebar's chrome already uses — a divider is not a place to introduce a hue, and there is no `--gd-sidebar-*` separator token for a Theme to move) and the two widths beside each other, so a later change to one cannot silently make them equal; `NavGroup` reads both from it and spells neither. **Which** section carries one is navigation config, not a branch in the JSX: `NavGroup.separatorAbove` joins `icon` on the group declaration in `config/navigationGroups.ts` (#884's rule — nothing branches on a group's id or label), and `Sidebar` is the one place that switches it off for the **first visible** section, since a leading rule separates a section from nothing and the groups a role can see are its own to know. The rule survives the collapsed sidebar (#1003) with its inset narrowed from 16px to 12px and nothing else changed, because a 64px strip would otherwise show a stub of a line. The Cordel item itself — its position, its label, its icon, its `requiredRole` and every href under it — is untouched. Frontend-only: no migration, no API, no new locale key, no new Theme setting. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/sidebar-cordel-separator.test.ts` (new, 8 — the section rule being strictly thicker than the item hairline, both colours coming from the one constant, the collapsed variant changing only its margin, neither component spelling the colour again, `separatorAbove` declared on Cordel and on no other group, Cordel still last, the first-section guard, and the Cordel group's other fields unchanged). Admin suite green at 2,080 across 139 files; `tsc --noEmit` clean.)

The width is `SIDEBAR_EXPANDED_WIDTH` in `apps/admin/src/lib/sidebarCollapse.ts`, #1003's one place that decides it, so the panel and the `main` beside it follow the constant and there is no second value to keep in step. One literal did sit outside it: `AppShell`'s closed mobile drawer was parked at a hardcoded `left: -220px`, which at the new width would have left a 30px sliver of the drawer on screen — it reads the same constant now, so the two cannot drift apart again. The collapsed width (64px) and the 769px breakpoint are untouched, as are the navigation structure, the labels, the icons, the collapse behaviour and every Theme token. Frontend only: no migration, no API change, no locale key. Tests: the width assertion and the drawer's off-screen offset in `apps/admin/src/test/sidebar-collapse.test.ts`.)

Three of the eight carry **no header band** — **Themes** and the two Nutrition plan lists — and none was given one: a band is a desktop change §4 rules out, so there the declaration is the row's alone and the batch's own test says per page whether a band exists rather than assuming one. The drift was there to find in the five that do have a band: **Taxes**, **Gyms**, **Workout Templates** and **Payment Providers** folded the chevron and the `⋮` into one 68px header cell while the row rendered two, and **Training Plan Templates**' header spacer was 20px wide against the row's 14px chevron, so every title after it sat 6px off its own values. The three band-less rows had the other half of the same defect, sized per cell instead: **Nutrition Plan Templates**' name cell was `flexShrink: 0, maxWidth: 220` with the description `flex: 1`, so the description was the cell taken to zero; **Nutrition Plans** had the same pair one column over; and **Themes** sized its logo box, swatches and badge against a `flex: 1` name. Two decisions rather than mechanics: **Nutrition Plans** keeps the **Member** beside the plan name on a phone, which is `Q1`'s own exception for a member-related list (both cells truncate, and the name cell's `title` carries the pair), and **Themes** gives up its 56px logo box and its three colour swatches for the reason stage 3 gave up Staff's avatar — on a 390px row they are the difference between a readable name and a truncated one, and both are read in the expanded editor. One existing guard moved with the shape rather than being weakened: `assigned-nutrition-plans-header.test.ts` (#810) pinned the name and member cells by their inline styles alone and now matches them through `CELL_CLASS.name`/`CELL_CLASS.member` as well, the same claim one indirection later, plus the `mobile: 'keep'` that keeps the Member on the phone. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/list-mobile-columns.test.ts` grew a stage-4 half (49 more: the eight pages in one loop, with the header-band assertion parameterised, and the Member-beside-the-name rule), and `api/src/test/admin-list-mobile-columns.unit.test.ts`'s grid count moved from an exact 11 to an exact 19 and its total from 23 to 31 — still exact, because the adoption is finished and a page that went back to hand-sized cells is what would drop it. Admin suite green at 2,208 across 140 files, API unit suites at 832 across 59; `tsc --noEmit` clean. The API integration tests were not executed here — this environment has no MySQL — and this stage touches no API code, only the existing scan's counts.)

Which column is which is the ticket's ranking applied per list, and twice it is the two-badge decision stage 2 took on Members: on **Membership Plans** the lifecycle status is the Plan's own standing and rides beside the name while Enrollment is read in the expanded card, and on **Staff** the employment status says whether this person works here while Current status (available, on vacation) gives way — as does Staff's 36px avatar, which is the difference between a readable name and a truncated one on a 390px row. Two existing guards moved with the shapes rather than being weakened: `activity-colour-column.test.ts` (#676) matched the collapsed row and the name and description cells by their old inline styles and now matches them by `LIST_GRID_ROW_CLASS` and `CELL_CLASS.<key>`, reading the ticket's column order from the declaration instead of from the header JSX; and Promotions' inline-new row keeps its `#6c63ff` literal rather than reaching for `--gd-primary-btn`, which `theme-primary-buttons.test.ts` (#954 §2) reserves for `ui.tsx`. - **Done**: #1011 stage 2 of 4 (**The three `LIST_COLUMNS` pages onto the mobile mechanism.** Stage 1 moved the twelve `DataTable` lists, which cost one component; Products, Members and Training Plans lay their rows out from their own `LIST_COLUMNS` declaration (#637's shape) and write their cells by hand, so each adopts it itself. **Frontend-only — no migration, no API change, no new locale key, no new Theme setting.** The mechanism needed one addition, and it is the second axis of the same question: a `<table>` cell can simply be hidden, but a CSS grid declares its **tracks on the row**, so hiding a cell there would leave its track behind and slide every cell after it under the wrong title. `LIST_GRID_ROW_CLASS` is the answer — below the breakpoint the row stops being a grid and becomes a flex line of the cells that survive, the identity taking the leftover width (`flex: 1 1 auto; min-width: 0`) and the actions pushed to the end (`margin-left: auto`) — with `LIST_MIN_WIDTH_CLASS` releasing #637's track-sum minimum, since a row carrying only the columns a phone has room for has nothing to scroll (`Q3`). Both live in `components/listChrome.ts` beside stage 1's, and `listCellClasses(LIST_COLUMNS)` is how a hand-written cell reaches its column's declaration: `CELL_CLASS.status` on the header cell and on the row cell, so the two halves of a column cannot answer the question differently and no page spells a class. Which column is which is the ticket's ranking applied per list: on **Members** the Member's name is the identity and their **enrollment status** the badge that rides beside it — payment status is the state of a payment request rather than of the Member, and two badges plus a name leave a 390px row with nothing legible in it, so that is the one that gives way (the email is in `⋮ → Details`, the document in the Profile tab); on **Products** the item's name and its status, everything else being in the expanded card, which renders the same declaration in both modes (#974); on **Training Plans** the name cell already carries *whose* plan it is on its second line, so the phone keeps that cell, the status and the chevron, and reads the three dates below. Every name cell gained a `title`, so a truncated identity stays readable (§1). The `Q3` rewrite of the three alignment guards is this stage's too: `products-column-alignment.test.ts`, `members-list-ui.test.ts` and `training-plans-list-header.test.ts` pinned the exact JSX of the row, the header and the scrolling wrapper, and now pin the same structures *with* their mobile declaration — one cell per column still, each carrying its own column's class, the desktop `minWidth: LIST_MIN_WIDTH` still there and released only below the breakpoint. Two other guards that scanned the Products row by its opening tag (`products-mandatory-badge.test.ts`, `products-session-package-price.test.ts`) moved with it. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/list-mobile-columns.test.ts` grew a stage-2 half (15 more: the flex-line rule and its two pinned cells, the released minimum, `listCellClasses()`, and the three pages asserted in one loop — every column declaring a behaviour, exactly one identity and one actions cell, the class map derived from the declaration, no `gd-list-*` spelled in a page, the row class on both the header band and the row, and `collapse` rather than `scroll`, because these rows expand), and `api/src/test/admin-list-mobile-columns.unit.test.ts` now balances the brackets of `ListColumn[]` literals too, so a grid list cannot opt out either — its `title` rule reads the cell for those, since the title belongs on the cell rather than in the declaration. Admin suite green at 2,111 across 140 files, API unit suites at 832 across 59; `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` clean. The API integration tests were not executed here — this environment has no MySQL and no Docker daemon — and this stage touches no API code, only the existing scan.)

- **Done**: #1011 stage 1 of 4 (**A list row on a phone: the mechanism, and every `<table>` list.** The Promotions row in the ticket's screenshot is ~650px of cells that cannot shrink (five fixed metadata cells, the gaps and the row padding) against 358px of content width on a 390px phone, so flexbox took the two `minWidth: 0` cells — Name and Description — to **zero** and overflowed the rest by ~290px, carrying the `⋮` off the right edge: the name was not truncated, it was gone, and both symptoms are one cause. The owner's answers settled the rules: `Q1 three` (Name, the Member name where the list is member-related, the `StatusBadge` where there is one, and the actions), `Q2 scroll` (the five lists with no expanded row keep every column and scroll the block *inside the row*, pinned Name and actions, rather than making a value unreachable), and `Q3 yes` (below 769px a list does not overflow horizontally; at 769px and above #637's `LIST_MIN_WIDTH` and its scroll are untouched). This stage is the mechanism plus the twelve `DataTable` consumers; stages 2–4 carry the three `LIST_COLUMNS` pages and the sixteen flex-row pages onto it. **Frontend-only — no migration, no API change, no new locale key, no new Theme setting.** `components/listChrome.ts` is where it lives (#724's rule — a list's chrome is one place): `LIST_MOBILE_MEDIA_QUERY`, **mirroring** AppShell's own 768/769 split the way `lib/sidebarCollapse.ts` does (#1003) rather than declaring a third number; the `ListColumnMobile` vocabulary (`name` · `keep` · `actions` · `secondary`, the **default** — a column that says nothing about a phone is one the row has no room for, which is the shape that broke); `listCellClass()`/`listScrollerClass()`; and `LIST_RESPONSIVE_CSS`, which `components/ListResponsiveStyles.tsx` mounts once from `AppShell`. It has to be a global sheet rather than a style object because every cell in these lists is inline-styled and only `!important` in a sheet wins over an inline `display` — and the three surfaces it paints a pinned cell with are read from `LIST_SURFACE_BACKGROUND`/`LIST_HEADER_BACKGROUND`/`LIST_DIVIDER_COLOR`, extracted from the existing style objects so a pinned cell cannot end up a different white from the row it is part of. `DataTable` reads the declaration: each `<th>`/`<td>` carries its column's class, a `mobile: 'name'` cell wraps its value so it can be truncated and keeps the full value in the cell's `title` (§1), and the table sits in one wrapper whose mode is **derived, not configured** — `collapse` when `renderExpanded` is present (secondary columns hidden, read one tap below), `scroll` when it is not (every column kept, Name sticky-left and the actions sticky-right, the middle scrolling inside the wrapper, the table allowed to grow past it). The `⋮` needed nothing: `ContextMenu` has always used `position: fixed` off the trigger's own rect, so it escapes the new scroll container and stays inside the viewport (§2) once the trigger itself is on screen, which is what this fixes. Which column is which is the ticket's ranking applied per list: the **Member** is the name cell where it identifies the row (Memberships, Assigned Plans, Assigned Personal Goals), the email is on Orphaned Accounts (an orphaned Clerk account often has no name at all), and a `StatusBadge` is `keep` everywhere it exists. Two existing guards moved with the constants rather than being deleted: `members-list-ui.test.ts` and `training-plans-list-header.test.ts` pinned the *literal spelling* of the header band and the row divider inside `listChrome`, and now assert the named constants and the styles built from them — the same "one place only" claim, one indirection later. Tests: `api/src/test/admin-list-mobile-columns.unit.test.ts` (new, 7 — in the **API** suite for #1009's reason, since CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only: it balances the brackets of every `Column<T>[]` literal under `apps/admin/src` and fails on a column with no `mobile`, a list with none or two identity columns, a value outside the vocabulary, a name column with no `title` accessor, a breakpoint that stops mirroring AppShell's, and any page spelling a `gd-list-*` class of its own) and `apps/admin/src/test/list-mobile-columns.test.ts` (new, 16 — the class per behaviour and the `secondary` default, the two wrapper modes, every rule living inside the one media query, `display: none` reaching only secondary cells and only under the collapse wrapper, the name and actions never hidden, the truncation, the pinning, the surfaces coming from the shared constants, the sheet mounted exactly once and declaring nothing itself, and `DataTable` deriving the mode from `renderExpanded`). Admin suite green at 2,096 across 140 files, API unit suites at 832 across 59; `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` clean. The API integration tests were not executed here — this environment has no MySQL and no Docker daemon — and this stage touches no API code, only a new unit test file.)

- **Done**: #1028 (**A form's Save/Cancel pair is left-aligned, everywhere, from one declaration.** The app answered the alignment question three ways at once: `formChrome.ts`'s card-level `formActionsRowStyle` sat at `justifyContent: 'flex-end'`, its section-level `inlineActionsRowStyle` was left-aligned, and some thirty entity forms declared a `{ display: 'flex', gap: 8, justifyContent: 'flex-end' }` of their own — so Spaces' `[Cancel] [Save changes]` (the screenshot on the ticket) sat at the far right of a card whose fields start at the content margin, Products' pair sat at the left, and Staff's read `[Save] [Cancel]`. Per §"Prefer a shared implementation" the fix is the shared declarations rather than thirty CSS edits: **`formActionsRowStyle` loses its justification** (flex's own default is now the rule, so there is no second value a row can be set to by accident), `modalActionsRowStyle` is added for the Modal CRUD shape's footer, and every entity create/edit form spreads one of the three — the inline list forms (Spaces, Centers, Staff, Taxes, Plans, Promotions, Activity Types, Professional Services, Workout Templates, Training Plan Templates, Operating Hours, Payment Providers, Gyms, both Nutrition Libraries, the Nutrition Plan tree's meal editor, both Theme editors' footers, the Assigned Plan card's section editor), the card-level forms (the Member Profile, the Assigned Training Plan card) and the dialogs (`CrudModal`, the two `New <entity>` dialogs, the Add Member modal). Products and the Exercise editor already did (#968), which is why their pair was the one in the right place. **Layout only**: no label, handler, permission gate, `disabled` state, locale key, payload or endpoint moved, and the buttons keep their own colours — what a primary action is painted in is #912/#954's question. Two consequences are deliberate rather than drift: Staff's pair is reordered to the app's own `Cancel` → `Save`, which the ticket's own expected layout states three times; and the two Theme editor footers, in adopting the shared card-level row, take its `--gd-card-border` hairline in place of a local `--gd-border` one, which is #929's rule for a separator inside a card. Exactly three files keep a right-aligned footer, and none of them is an entity form: `ConfirmDialog` and `DependencyDialog` are a destructive confirmation's pair, and `CrudModal`'s `hideSave` branch is how that component renders a read-only Details view, whose single `Close` keeps the convention every standalone Details modal in the app already uses. Pagination rows, a card's `⋮` cell and the two Calendar panels' full-width stacked actions are untouched. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/form-actions-alignment.test.ts` (new, 9 — the three rows declaring no justification and no inset of their own, what still distinguishes them, a source scan for a `flex-end` within two lines of a Cancel/Save button that asserts the *set* of three remaining dialogs rather than consulting an allowlist, the Spaces form, Staff's order, the Assigned Plan override, and `CrudModal`'s branch and footer order), with `exercise-form-actions.test.ts` and `section-action-placement.test.ts` updated where they pinned the old right-aligned shape. The whole admin suite (141 files, 2217 tests) passes, as do `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build`.)
