- **Done**: #1029 (**An Activity's schedule is editable in the Activity's own Edit mode, and nowhere else.** The Schedule section held its controls in both halves of the expanded card: a per-rule `[Edit]` and `[Delete]` pair and an actionable `+ Add schedule rule` were rendered straight from the read-only view, gated on `canWrite` alone, so the card contradicted the app-wide rule that expanding a list card **reads** and `⋮ → Edit` **writes** (#797) — and the Add control was a lilac `#6c63ff` text link that followed no Theme setting (#912/#954). `renderScheduleSection(row, editing)` takes the mode as its one flag now, so the two halves cannot disagree: outside it the rules are values and every control is **absent** rather than disabled (a section's own actions already are — #897/#957), and inside it a rule row *is* the affordance that opens the existing inline editor — a real `<button>`, so it is reachable from the keyboard — which is what removes the per-rule `Edit` without introducing a second editing state. Deletion is one `✕` at the far right of each row (`rowRemoveBtnStyle`, declared once in `formChrome.ts` per #929 rather than respelled per row, and adopted by `ProductBenefits.tsx`'s byte-identical copy) carrying the row's action as its `aria-label`, since the glyph has no text of its own — which is why `activity_types.schedule.delete_rule` reads *Delete schedule rule* now and `edit_rule` is gone from all three locale files. `+ Add schedule rule` wears the shared `dashedAddBtnStyle`. **Frontend-only — no migration, no API change, no new route, no new permission** and no change to validation, saving, deletion or #482's booked-occurrence confirmation: the rule editor, its POST/PUT/DELETE routes and their 409 handling are the ones that were already there. One rule is new rather than moved: `resetScheduleEditing()` runs on every exit from the mode (Cancel, a successful Save, opening the mode again, deleting the Activity), because an open rule editor or a pending 409 confirmation left mounted would otherwise still be there the next time the card is expanded — in a mode that is supposed to hold no control at all. Note the schedule rows still persist through their own routes rather than the card's Save, so Cancel does not revert a rule already saved; that is the existing behaviour the ticket asks to preserve. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/activity-schedule-edit-mode.test.ts` (new, 7 — the section taking the mode as a parameter and both halves passing it, the per-rule `Edit` gone from the page and the locales, the one `openEditRule` caller inside the `editing` branch and its being a keyboard-reachable button, the `✕` with its shared style and accessible name beside the untouched delete route and 409 state, the Add control's shared dashed style and the absence of the lilac literal, the `✕` declared in `formChrome` and not respelled, and every exit from the mode going through the reset). Admin suite green at 2,224 across 142 files; `tsc --noEmit` clean. The API integration tests were not executed here — this environment has no MySQL — and this ticket touches no API code; the four API locale/identifier gates were run and pass.)

- **Done**: #1031 (**The Workout Templates editor is the application's own design system.** #971 put the workout hierarchy's chrome in `apps/admin/src/components/workoutChrome.ts` and swept two of the three screens that render it. It did not reach the third: `[locale]/workout-templates` draws the tree through `WorkoutTemplateTree.tsx`, a near-duplicate of the `WorkoutBlockBuilder.tsx` #971 fixed, and it kept its own copy of **every** control — `+ Block` and `+ Exercise` as a dashed lilac text link (`#b9b5ee` / `#6c63ff`), the block header's input and select on a hardcoded `#ddd` over `#fafafa`, the block card on a `#ececf0` border under a themed background, the exercise picker's selected option on `#f0eeff`, and the drop target that accepts a block dragged in from another template highlighting `#eef0ff` behind a `#6c63ff` dashed outline. So a gym that configured its Theme's Inputs, Cards and Buttons groups saw the result everywhere in the editor *except* on the screen the ticket's screenshot is of, which is also why the fix is the duplication rather than a second sweep of literals: `workoutChrome.ts` now declares `treeControlBox` and the four controls built from it (`treeHeaderInputStyle`, `treeHeaderSelectStyle`, `treeCellInputStyle`, `treeComboTriggerStyle`), the picker's panel/search/list/options with `TREE_COMBO_ITEM_SELECTED_BG` (the Theme's `--gd-app-bg`), the exercise table with its rule on `--gd-card-border`, both drag handles, `treeBlockCardStyle` (the app's own `cardSurfaceStyle`, so a themed Card Border and Radius reach a block), the tree's small text, the draft row's `✕` and `treeDropTargetStyle()` — and both tree files spread them, neither keeping a `---- Styles ----` block of its own (183 and 191 lines of near-identical declarations gone). Three decisions are the rule rather than the implementation. **The geometry stays the tree's** (`4px 8px` at 13.5px, where `formControlStyle` is `8px 10px` at 14px): §"preserve semantic hierarchy" — a block header is not a form field, so only the colours are shared, and a control needing a width spreads the shared object. **A drop affordance is not an action**: the highlight is `--gd-app-bg` behind a `--gd-input-border` dashed outline rather than borrowing the primary button's colour, and its idle and active forms share one geometry so the box does not move when a drag starts. And the draft row's `✕` stays the tree's neutral affordance rather than `formChrome`'s red `rowRemoveBtnStyle` (#1029), because it abandons a row nothing has been written for — it does gain the `aria-label` that rule asks of a bare glyph, in both trees. Outside the tree the page is now the ordinary card: its inline create and edit forms take `formFieldLabelStyle` / `formControlStyle` / `formErrorStyle` / `secondaryBtnSmall` instead of the five copies it had declared (its inputs were on `1px solid #ccc`, which no Theme reaches), its three sections and its read-only rows are the shared `CardSection` + `CardDetailRow` instead of a page-local `SectionHeader` / `DetailRow`, its two filters are `filterControlStyle`, and `+ Add` — like Base Workout Templates' `+ New Template` and its search box — is `primaryBtnStyle()` rather than a bare `btnStyle()`, whose `--brand` is `sidebarSelectedItemBackground`, the sidebar's colour and not an action's. §"Do not change functionality" is literal: no migration, no endpoint, no payload, no validation, no handler, no drag-and-drop behaviour and no locale key added or moved; the only non-visual change in the diff is that `aria-label`. Scope note: the Workout Templates card renders its tree with `canWrite` in **both** halves, so expanding it still edits — #971's Edit-mode gate was explicitly scoped to the Assigned Training Plans card and this is a styling ticket, so the split is left for a ticket that can decide it. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/workout-template-theme.test.ts` (new, 22 — the shared declarations asserted directly against the shipped Theme defaults, neither tree redeclaring or retaining any of them, no lilac left in either tree or in the chrome, the tree's denser geometry preserved against `formControlStyle`, the block card equal to `cardSurfaceStyle`, the drop target's two forms, the themed picker, the page's form chrome and its primary/secondary hierarchy, both halves rendering the same three `CardSection`s, the endpoints/guards/drag-and-drop unchanged, the `✕`'s accessible name, the Base screen, and `applyTokens` writing the five variables the chrome reads), with the one assertion in `training-plan-editor-theme.test.ts` that pinned `treeControlBox`'s old location rewritten to assert its new one. Admin suite green at 143 files / 2,246 tests; `tsc --noEmit` clean and `next build` clean.)

- **Done**: #1032 (**A workout block's name and its structure share one line in Training Plan Templates.** `TrainingPlanTree.tsx`'s `BlockRow` rendered the two as stacked `<div>`s — `Press-militar` over `Circuit · 3 rounds` — so every block of every workout cost two lines, and a plan with a handful of workouts was far taller than it needed to be. Both Workout Template trees (`WorkoutTemplateTree.tsx`, `WorkoutBlockBuilder.tsx`) already put them on one line, so the fix is the shape they use rather than a new one: the summary is an inline `<span>` nested inside the name element, carrying `workoutChrome`'s own `treeSummaryTextStyle` (400 weight, 12.5px, `#888`) instead of the page-local `{ color: '#888', fontSize: 12.5 }` literal it had declared — which is #971/#1031's rule applied to one more control, and what keeps the structure information visually secondary to the name. Inline spans rather than a flex row on purpose: a narrow viewport wraps the summary under the name by itself, so the acceptable fallback the ticket describes needs no media query and no horizontal scrolling is introduced. **Presentation only, in one file**: `blockSummary()` is unchanged, so the same information is displayed (the type, plus its one configuration value), and no data, hierarchy, ordering, API, schema, locale key or business rule moved — the exercise rows underneath are untouched, as §"Do not change" asks. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/training-plan-tree-compact-block.test.ts` (new, 4 — the nested one-line shape, the stacked summary block gone, the style taken from the shared chrome rather than a literal of its own, and the chrome's declaration still lighter and smaller than the 600/14 name), with the one assertion in `training-plan-editor-theme.test.ts` that pinned the tree's `workoutChrome` import as a single name relaxed to assert the chip is imported rather than that it is the only thing imported. Admin suite green at 144 files / 2,250 tests; `tsc --noEmit` clean. The API integration tests were not executed here — this environment has no MySQL — and this ticket touches no API code; the API locale/identifier gates that scan the admin app were run and pass.)

- **Done**: #1034 (**A Personal Goal is measurable, and `⋮ → Assign goal to member` assigns one.** The Personal Goals catalogue held a name and a description, so a goal said *what* a member wants and never *how much* — and the only way to assign one was the Assigned Personal Goals page or the Member card, never from the goal itself. **Migration 218** gives `personal_goals` a `target_value DECIMAL(10,2)` + `target_unit VARCHAR(20)` pair, carrying the very same two CHECKs `member_personal_goals` has had since migration 212 (a unit with no value is refused; a value with no unit is incomplete rather than contradictory, so it stays allowed — one direction only). `nutrition_goals` is deliberately untouched: the two catalogues are two tables precisely so they can diverge, and a Nutrition Goal's own target values (`2,000 kcal`) are still a later ticket's, so `MEASURABLE_GOAL_KINDS` in `api/src/domain/goalLibrary.ts` is the one place that says which kinds have the pair — asked once per router rather than branched on per route (that table has no such columns, and projecting them would answer ER_BAD_FIELD_ERROR the global handler turns into a bare 500, #966) and mirrored for the admin, so a second measurable kind goes in **three** places. What a target *is* moved to `api/src/domain/goalTarget.ts`, which the assignment module now re-exports rather than re-implementing — §1's *"do not introduce a second, incompatible unit system"* is a rule about exactly that, and the test asserts the two sides are the identical function. The unit stays **free text**: this codebase has no measurement-unit vocabulary to reuse (the only `*_UNITS` lists are billing periods), so declaring one here would *be* that second system, and a gym measuring in `lb`, `%` or `laps` is not an error. **§2's seeded defaults are a judgment call, stated plainly**: Weight Loss 3 kg, Weight Gain 3 kg, Muscle Gain 2 kg and Maintenance 0 kg (a change of zero *is* the goal, not a blank), and **none** on Performance, Recovery and Energy — neither carries a magnitude or a unit that follows from what it represents, and a number invented for a qualitative goal is the *"same generic target"* §2 forbids wearing a different value; §13 concedes the same by requiring a unit only *"for measurable goals"*, so `—` is a legitimate target and a gym may set one on its own copy or per assignment. The seed fills a row only while it has no target yet, so a value Cordel changed later is never overwritten and a re-run is a no-op. **§7/§12's snapshot is now real.** The assignment read `personal_goals.name` live, so renaming a Gym Goal moved every assignment of it; `member_personal_goals.goal_name` is written by the one `POST` that creates an assignment and read in its place (`COALESCE(mpg.goal_name, pg.name)` — the live name is the fallback for a row assigned before this migration, which has nothing else to read, and there is deliberately **no backfill**, because the catalogue's current name is not evidence of what it was called then: #635 §16's shape). The target inherits the same way, and **the snapshot is the server's, not the form's**: a field the request does not mention takes the catalogue's value, an explicit `null` is still a clear, the unit only inherits while the effective value is non-null (a unit qualifying nothing would be a 400 nobody asked for), and a submitted value is the per-member override §8 exists for — one Gym Goal, three members, three targets. Admin: the pair is two more fields of the one `GoalLibrarySection` (#806) behind that single `measurable` predicate — the form's row, the read-only value and the list's **Target** column all ask it, so they cannot disagree, and a non-measurable kind renders no column at all rather than a `—` for a field it does not have. **§4/§5's action is the page's, not the section's**: `onAssign` is an opt-in prop, so `⋮ → Assign goal to member` is **absent** from Cordel's Base Personal Goals, which has no gym whose members it could name. The dialog is `AssignGoalToMemberModal` over the app's own `CrudModal` and `formChrome` — the goal preselected as a **value** (§5: *"the user should not need to search for the goal again"*), the Gym Goal's current target shown beside the editable one, the unit editable because §5 says to follow the existing business rules and `target_unit` has been a staff-set column since migration 212, a required Member picker, and Cancel/Accept with the dialog staying open on a 400 or the 409 for a goal the member already holds. It submits through the **same** `toAssignedPersonalGoalCreatePayload` / `assignedPersonalGoalFormError` the inline assignment form uses, so the narrower field set is a narrower *view* of one create form and not a second one — the dates, the progress status and the notes stay at the create defaults the API would have stored anyway. §9 needs no work: `mpgoal_live_goal_key` already allows many goals per member and refuses the same goal twice while in progress. New keys in the `goal_library` namespace in en/es/ca. Tests: `api/src/test/goal-target.unit.test.ts` (new, 15 pure — the shared normalizer asserted identical on both sides, the column bounds against the migration, the single measurable kind, the rounding and the refusals, the pair rule's one direction, the seed map mirrored against migration 218 with the three unmeasurable goals asserted absent and every seeded pair proved acceptable to the normalizers, and the snapshot column's no-backfill shape); a `#1034` block in `goal-library.test.ts` (+7 integration, parameterised over both kinds so the non-measurable one asserts the field is absent) and in `platform-goal-library.test.ts` (+1); a `#1034` block in `member-personal-goals.test.ts` (+8 — inheritance, the per-member override, the explicit clear, the catalogue edited underneath an assignment, the pre-migration fallback, a goal with no target, a System goal, and the unit that cannot stand alone); `apps/admin/src/test/goal-library-ui.test.ts` (+5, and its context-menu gate count raised from two writes to three). Admin suite green at 144 files / 2,258 tests; API unit suites green (60 files, 847 tests); `tsc --noEmit` clean on both. No MySQL and no Docker daemon in this container, so the four integration files run in CI.)

- **Done**: #1038 (**The Members App images are one subsection of Members App, and My Goals is the seventh slot.** The six Members App background images (#725/#732) were a top-level Theme section labelled *Members App* in all three languages — the same label the Members App **settings** section (#833) carries — so the Theme editor showed two sections with one name and nothing said which configured what. They are now that section's first **subsection**, `Members App → Images`: both Theme screens hand the rendered `ThemeMembersImagesEditor` to `ThemeMembersAppEditor` as its new `images` prop, and the editor draws it with the one collapsible header its five setting groups already use (`renderSubsection()`, declared once, so Images is that control rather than a second accordion look beside it). The images editor still owns nothing — it takes a node, not a slot list, so the page keeps the draft, the previews, the storage gating (#823), the per-slot failure marks (#830) and both calls on Save — and the Base Themes screen still withholds the subsection for a theme that does not exist yet, since there is no id to upload to. `section_members_images` is gone from both namespaces in en/es/ca, replaced by `group_members_images` (*Images* / *Imágenes* / *Imatges*). **The seventh slot is `personal_goals`**, the My Goals artwork, labelled *My Goals* / *Mis objetivos* / *Els meus objectius* and sitting between My Membership and the general background in the editor's order. It is stored exactly like the other six — one `theme_member_images` row per configured slot, the object at `<prefix>/themes/<theme_id>-<name>/members_app/personal_goals.png`, the file name being the slot's own name, which is the ticket's requirement and also why it may never be re-spelled: R2 has no directories, so a stored key is the only way back to its object. **Migration 219** swaps `chk_theme_member_images_slot` for the seven (CLAUDE.md's "two places" rule — the list alone would upload the object and *then* fail the insert), guarded so a re-run is a no-op and a run that died between the DROP and the ADD repairs itself; its `down()` deletes the `personal_goals` rows first, because the narrower CHECK would refuse them, and the objects survive under their keys as a removed slot's always has (#725). No existing row, key or behaviour is touched, so every uploaded image keeps displaying — the ticket's own first requirement. The member side gains the slot in `MEMBER_BACKGROUND_SLOTS` and nothing else: `slotForPathname()` deliberately does **not** map a My Goals page, because that section is #1036's and a page joins that map when it exists (the `calendar` precedent, #984), so until then the artwork is the dashboard tile's alone. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/members-app-images-subsection.test.ts` (new, 9 — the subsection rendered inside Members App on both screens and exactly once, the top-level section and its locale key gone from both pages and all six namespaces, the shared header chrome asserted as one declaration, Images before the setting groups, the absent-not-empty branch, the editor still ignorant of uploads, the slot's position in the declaration, its labels in both namespaces in en/es/ca, and neither page spelling the slot itself), plus the new slot through the existing gates: `api/src/test/theme-member-image-keys.test.ts` (+3, including a gate asserting migration 219's CHECK mirrors `MEMBER_IMAGE_SLOTS` as a set), `theme-asset-paths.unit.test.ts`, `theme-members-images.test.ts`, `base-theme-members-images.test.ts` and `apps/member/src/test/members-background.test.ts` (+1 — no My Goals page in the map yet). Three pre-existing assertions that pinned the old section — two in `members-app-theme-settings.test.ts` matching the editor's call as one line, one in `theme-editor-unification.test.ts` naming `'members'` in the Base screen's `SectionKey` — were rewritten to assert the same intent against the new shape. Admin suite green at 146 files / 2,268 tests, member at 15 files / 282 tests, API unit suites at 59 files / 832 tests; `tsc --noEmit` clean in all three workspaces. The API integration tests were not executed here — this environment has no MySQL and no Docker daemon — so `theme-members-images.test.ts`'s slot loop, which is what exercises migration 219's CHECK end to end, runs first in CI.)

- **Done**: #1041 (**Cloning a Theme copies its assets, so the clone is independent rather than empty.** `POST /system/themes/clone/:sourceId` already checked the gym's bucket and wrote the new theme's folder tree (#827) — what it never did was copy anything into it: the clone started with no logo and no Members App backgrounds, which #725 had justified as independence (*“no shared object path”*) and this ticket reads as the defect it is, since a cloned Theme is supposed to *look like* its source. The clone now carries the source's logo and one copy of every configured slot, under its **own** keys. `api/src/domain/themeAssetClone.ts` is the one place that decides what moves (§18's reuse rule): `planThemeAssetCopies()` names no file name at all — a background is planned from the source's own `theme_member_images` row and keyed by `buildThemeMemberImageKey()`, the logo by `buildThemeLogoKey()` off the stored `logo_mime` — so §5 holds by construction (a seventh slot is copied with no change to this module, which matters because #1038 adds one) and §4's *“do not create an artificial empty file”* is what an unconfigured asset produces: no copy at all. Four properties are the rule rather than the implementation. **The copies run before the INSERT**, for the same reason #827's folders do: a failed copy sweeps the destination objects it had already written, answers 502 with `copy_logo`/`copy_members_image` as its `stage` and the destination `path`, and leaves **no** `themes` row — so §15's *“theme created + only 4 of 7 images copied + clone appears successful”* is unreachable rather than merely avoided. **Every destination key is the clone's**, built from its id and sanitized name, so the clone's `logo_object_key` and its slot rows are its own and never the source's URLs (§7/§9/§11) — which is also what makes the two independent (§12) and what keeps the source untouched (§13: nothing of the source's is written, moved or deleted, and the route sends no DELETE at all on the happy path). **The row and its references commit in one transaction**, with the slot rows carrying the clone's own `gym_id` even when the source is a Base Theme whose rows are the platform's (#732), and a DB failure sweeps the copies too (§16). And **the copy is server-side** — `copyStorageObject()` (`CopyObjectCommand`, the object's `Content-Type` travelling with it), so nothing is downloaded and re-uploaded — with exactly one exception: a legacy `themes.logo_bytes` logo (a Base Theme's from before #829) is not in the bucket at all, so its bytes are *uploaded* into the clone's key, which gives such a clone a real R2 logo while still adding no writer to `logo_bytes`. §14 is why the Base→Base `POST /platform/themes/clone/:sourceId` is untouched: cloning a Base Theme into a gym *reads* `cordel/` and writes only under the gym's prefix, and nothing initializes or copies into the platform root. §1's bucket check and its three translated messages are #827's and unchanged. Admin: the Clone modal's Save reads *Cloning theme…* while the request is in flight (`CrudModal` disables both buttons on `saving`, so a second click cannot start a second clone — §17) and a success raises a toast; the two new stages get `storage_stage_copy_logo`/`storage_stage_copy_members_image` in both theme namespaces in en/es/ca, because the admin interpolates the wire value into the key. **No migration** — the columns and the table are the ones #713/#824/#725 already defined. Tests: `api/src/test/theme-asset-clone.unit.test.ts` (new, 15 pure — the plan for each kind, the extension taken from the stored MIME and not from the source key, the blob branch, the object-over-blob precedence, the whole slot list driven by `MEMBER_IMAGE_SLOTS`, an unknown slot skipped, no destination key ever under the source folder or under `cordel/`, and the two stages asserted to be declared `STORAGE_FAILURE_STAGES` members); `api/src/test/theme-clone-assets.test.ts` (new, 11 integration — the copies and their `CopySource`, the clone's stored keys and URLs, the source byte-identical afterwards, the empty source, the folders written before the copies, the blob logo uploaded, a mid-sequence copy failure leaving no row and sweeping what it had copied, the logo-stage failure, the uninitialized bucket copying nothing, a Base Theme's `cordel/` objects landing under `gyms/`, and the duplicate name still refused before anything is copied); `apps/admin/src/test/theme-clone-feedback.test.ts` (new, 6); and the two existing assertions that pinned the old *“clone starts unconfigured”* behaviour rewritten to assert the copy. Merged with `origin/main` after #1098 landed the seventh slot (`personal_goals`), which is the ticket's §5 demonstrated rather than claimed: the planner needed no change and its slot-list test covers seven now. Admin suite green at 146 files / 2,278 tests; API unit suites green (61 files, 863 tests); member suite green (15 files, 282 tests); `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` clean. No MySQL and no Docker daemon in this container, so the three theme integration files run in CI.)

- **Done**: #1039 (**A Member's default language is a column, applied in one place.** The Members App's locale was entirely a property of the *request* — the URL's `/{locale}/…` segment, which next-intl resolves from its own `NEXT_LOCALE` cookie and `Accept-Language`, and which both apps send on as `x-locale` (#643). That renders one request correctly and cannot survive a session: a member who chose Català on a laptop was back in English on their phone, which is what §4's *"the preference must persist after logging out and logging back in"* rules out. **Migration 220** adds `members.preferred_locale VARCHAR(10) NULL`, and the NULL is load-bearing: it means **no preference**, which is what every existing member means, so §3/§11 hold with no backfill and no default seeded — writing today's default in would turn *follow the app's default* into a snapshot of it. There is **no CHECK**, deliberately: which locales exist is configuration (`SUPPORTED_LOCALES`, env, parsed at boot), so listing `en`/`es`/`ca` in the schema would make a deployment that adds `fr` store a locale the application accepts and the database refuses. The gate is the write path instead — `parseMemberPreferredLocaleInput()` (`api/src/domain/memberPreferredLocale.ts`) is the only place a request's value is judged, and it reuses `normalizeLocale()` rather than declaring a second list, so what is stored is **the allowlist's own string** (§10, as a matter of data flow rather than of an equality check having been done correctly) and `es-ES` folds onto `es`. Its three answers are the ones every partial write in this codebase draws, with one difference: a field the request does not **mention** keeps what is stored (every client written before this ticket patches the phone alone), an explicit `null` or `''` **clears** the preference, and anything else is a **400, never a coercion** — storing `en` for a member who asked for `fr` would tell them the choice was saved. `PATCH /me/profile` therefore writes `preferred_locale = IF(?, ?, preferred_locale)` keyed on whether the field was supplied; a `COALESCE` on the *value* would have made clearing it unpersistable, the one way a nullable column of this shape goes wrong. Both profile reads report the column through `toMemberPreferredLocale()`, so a locale the deployment no longer configures reads as *no preference* while the stored column is left alone. Ownership needed nothing new: both routes resolve the member from the authenticated context, so there is no member id on the wire to tamper with (§18). **The Members App applies it in exactly one place** (§5, §7): `components/MemberLocalePreference.tsx`, mounted once by the locale layout inside `AppProvider`, which `router.replace()`s onto the same page under the preferred locale — the locale of a page *is* its first URL segment, so that is what makes next-intl resolve the messages, `<html lang>` follow, the layout pick the matching Clerk localization and `apiClient` send the right `x-locale`. The decision itself is the pure `preferredLocalePath()` (`lib/memberLocale.ts`), including every case that answers *stay put* — no preference, a locale this app cannot render, or already being on it — so the effect cannot loop; and it is skipped while a superadmin is impersonating, because the preference is the member's and bouncing staff into a language they may not read is a surprise the ticket never asks for. That module is also the app's **one** language declaration: `middleware.ts` now hands next-intl `MEMBER_LOCALES` instead of its own `['en','es','ca']` literal (§2, §8). The selector itself is one `<select>` in My Profile's preferences card, directly below Default Center and in the same `styles.select` (§1, §12), persisting on change rather than behind the phone field's Edit/Save pair; a member with no stored preference sees the locale the app is already rendering in selected (§11), and the labels are `profile.language_<tag>` resolved **before** `t()` is called (next-intl has no `defaultValue` and would print the key) in en/es/ca — *English/Spanish/Catalan*, *Inglés/Español/Catalán*, *Anglès/Espanyol/Català*, the admin's own wording. `AppContext` carries the field and gains `updateMember` for the one thing the page must do after saving: refresh the cached profile, since leaving it stale would have the redirect bounce the member straight back to the language they just left. Tests: `api/src/test/member-preferred-locale.unit.test.ts` (new, 12 — the three write answers over every configured locale, the allowlist's own string, the regional fold, seven refusals, the read-time narrowing, and the Members App's side scanned from the API suite for #1009's reason: one declaration, the middleware reading it, the app's list never wider than the API's, `preferredLocalePath` called in exactly one component and in no page, and every language labelled in all three locale files) and `apps/member/src/test/member-locale.test.ts` (new, 9 — the path swap, the prefix case, and all four *stay put* branches); `api/src/test/member-preferred-locale.test.ts` (new, 11 integration — the default NULL, the round trip, `ca-ES` folded, `fr` and a number refused with the stored value intact, the field omitted while patching the phone, both clears, a dropped locale reported as no preference, and 401/403). API unit suites green (62 files, 875 tests), member suite green (16 files, 291 tests); `tsc --noEmit` clean in both workspaces and `next build` clean for the member app. No MySQL and no Docker daemon in this container, so the integration file runs first in CI.)

- **Done**: #1042 (**A failed Theme save says why it failed and offers the fix.** The diagnostic #824 built says *which step* broke (`stage`) and *what S3 answered* (`details`); what it never said is why, or what the administrator is supposed to do — the reported screenshot reads `Saving the theme failed. / Operation: Save theme configuration / Error: Theme not found (404)`, from which a gym owner cannot tell a missing Cloudflare bucket from a deleted Theme from a wrong credential, and the one action that fixes the commonest of those is three clicks away in a context menu they have no reason to open. Every storage answer of both Theme routers now carries a **`cause`** beside its `stage`, from the closed set in `api/src/domain/storageFailureCause.ts` — `not_configured` · `not_initialized` · `not_found` · `access_denied` · `invalid_file` · `unreachable` — and the admin renders it as the block's *Cause* and *What you can do* lines (§8's *what happened / why / what to do*, with the raw storage particulars left as the secondary section under them) and uses it to put the existing **`Initialize bucket`** action right beside the error. Three of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **The cause is read from what the storage layer answered, never from the step that was running** (`storageCauseFromDetails()`: the S3 error's own name or code first, its HTTP status second — `NoSuchBucket`/404 ⇒ `not_initialized`, `AccessDenied`/401/403 ⇒ `access_denied`, anything else ⇒ `unreachable`), because a missing bucket and a refused credential break at the same step and R2 has answered `AccessDenied` with a 400 as well as a 403. **The route states what the client could not derive**, and the admin's mirror (`apps/admin/src/lib/storageFailureCause.ts`) derives only the unambiguous statuses: a 409 is *also* `A theme with this name already exists` and a 400 is *also* a rejected status value, so both answer **`null`** unless the route said otherwise — and `null` renders #824's block exactly as it was rather than inventing an explanation. And **`storageCauseSuggestsInitialize()` is the one place that decides which cause may offer to initialize anything — `not_initialized` alone**, which is §4 (*do not incorrectly suggest bucket initialization unless the error indicates that initialization is the problem*) holding by construction rather than by each call site remembering it: a permission or network failure never sends an administrator to re-run a no-op while the real problem stands, and a row-level 404 offers to reload the list instead of to create folders for a Theme that does not exist. The button is the same `handleInitializeBucket` the `⋮` menu runs (§3's *reuse the existing initialization workflow*), wearing `primaryBtnSmall()` like every other filled primary action (#912/#954), and on the gym screen it still refuses with the reason while the **gym's** bucket is the thing that was never created (#823). **No migration and no new endpoint** — the shape is one more field on answers that already carried `stage`, `path` and `details`. Two pieces of drift went with it: the platform Members-image upload was the one storage answer in either Theme router that named neither the step nor the object (its gym-side twin has since #725), and its 503 named neither the step nor the cause; and §9's logging gap — every storage failure log now carries the diagnosed `cause` and the authenticated user beside the `stage`, `path` and `details` it already had. A new cause goes in **three** places: the declaration, its mirror, and a `storage_cause_<value>` + `storage_suggestion_<value>` pair in **both** theme namespaces in en/es/ca (the key is interpolated from the wire value and next-intl prints a missing key verbatim) — the one sentence that genuinely differs between the two screens being `not_initialized`'s suggestion, since a Custom Theme's assets live in the gym's bucket (which Gym Bucket Initialization owns, #735) and a Base Theme's under the platform root. Tests: `api/src/test/storage-failure-cause.unit.test.ts` (new, 11 — each mapping, the name-over-status precedence, the unrecognised error never offering Initialize, the admin mirror asserted to declare the same list, and both routers scanned so every 502 answers with a cause read off the storage error rather than a literal) and `apps/admin/src/test/storage-save-diagnostics.test.ts` (new, 24 — the status table, the two deliberately-ambiguous statuses answering `null`, the block's two new lines and their absence for an undiagnosed failure and for a caller that passes no labels, the one-line toast form, the per-failure diagnosis of a shared asset report, and the wiring of both screens: the cause resolved through each namespace, reaching all three formatters, the button gated on the cause and cleared wherever the failures are, and neither page deciding a cause for itself); plus `cause` assertions threaded through the existing `theme-logo-storage.test.ts` and `base-theme-members-images.test.ts`, which also gain the platform upload's new `stage`/`path`. Three pre-existing source-scan assertions were rewritten rather than deleted: two pinning `handleInitializeBucket(theme)` (it takes an id now, because the error block has only the id in scope) and the two `primaryBtnSmall()` counts, which are 2 per screen now. Full API suite green at **254 files / 5,419 tests** against MySQL 8.0.46 with all 235 migrations applied, admin suite at **146 files / 2,302 tests**, `tsc --noEmit` clean in both workspaces.)

- **Done**: #1051 (**The Member's assigned plans are Products & Services', drawn with the Assigned Plans page's own list.** The Member card rendered those assignments a second way: one `innerCardStyle` metadata card per plan — name and price in a header, six `CardDetailRow`s under them, a `▸/▾` toggle of its own — stacked under MEMBERSHIP PLANS in the **Profile** tab, while the Financials page listed the very same `user_memberships` rows as a `DataTable` with a chevron, five columns and a Status badge. Two renderings of one entity is what the ticket removes, and it removes it the way it asks to be removed: by extracting the Financials list rather than copying it. `apps/admin/src/components/assignedPlan/AssignedPlansTable.tsx` is that one source of truth — the columns, their headers, the chevron, the Status badge and the `AssignedPlanExpandedRow` they expand into — and both screens render it. Three of its properties are decisions rather than mechanics. The **scope** decides which column is the row's identity and nothing else: `gym` leaves the Assigned Plans page byte-identical (the Member names the row, #1011's one `mobile: 'name'`), while `member` makes the **Plan** name it, because repeating the person a list is already scoped to identifies nothing and, under #1011, would pin that repeated name on a phone while hiding the Plan behind it. The **actions are the caller's**: `rowActions` is how a screen puts its own `⋮` on a row, so the Financials page keeps the menu it already had — the one inside the non-embedded expanded body — and the Member card keeps `Assign New Plan` · `Cancel Plan` · `Details`, gated on each row's own `is_live` so a terminated assignment is offered `Details` alone whichever table lists it. And the **headers resolve in the `assigned_plans_page` namespace on both screens**, per §4's *"follow the terminology already used by the Financials UI"*, so a Member-card copy of a column name cannot drift. The move itself is one line of `memberTabs.ts` (#961's own rule: which tab holds a section is the declaration's, never the JSX) plus the `<Section>` block changing guards — `section_membership_plans` leaves Profile and opens **Products & Services**, which is what §2 asks for and what reverses #961 §1. Active and Past are two instances of the list (§4), split on `is_live` exactly as the two card groups were, under the headings `membership_plans_active` (new, en/es/ca) and the existing `membership_plans_history`. **Nothing the cards showed is lost**: the fee and the next billing date are the expanded body's PRICING section, and Created by / Created at are in `⋮ → Details` beside the audit link, which is where #799 puts an actor. Everything below the UI is untouched — the same `GET /user-memberships/member/:id/configuration` read, the same `POST /user-memberships`, the same `409 active_plan_exists` replacement dialog (#956), the same inline Assign New Plan editor (#628, now under the list it acts on rather than inside a card), the same `embedded` read-only expansion (#958 Q3), and the same `+ Add Membership Plan` gating on the Member holding no live plan. The one API change is a **projection, not a model change**: `LIFECYCLE_STATUS_SQL` is extracted from `user-memberships.ts`'s `LIST_SELECT` and reused by the configuration read, so the Status column shows the same date-aware value on both screens. Without it a future-dated assignment — stored `active`, `lifecycle_status` `pending` — would read `Active` on the Member card and `Pending` on the Assigned Plans page; the stored `status` is unchanged and still what every write path acts on, and `is_live` is still what splits Active from Past. No migration. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/member-assigned-plans-list.test.ts` (the renamed `member-membership-plan-cards.test.ts`, 21 — both screens rendering the shared table, neither keeping a column declaration, `DataTable`, date formatter or status badge of its own, the two scopes' name columns, the Active/Past split and its headings, the `⋮` set and its `is_live` gate, the menu staying the caller's, the embedded expansion, the retired expand affordance and snapshot label, and the two headings in en/es/ca) and `api/src/test/member-membership-configuration.test.ts` (+2 integration — `pending` beside a stored `active`, and `paused`/`cancelled`/`expired` carried through). `assigned-plan-card-chrome.test.ts`'s coverage gate excludes the list the card expands *into*, as it already excludes the modal and the dialog. Admin suite green at 146 files / 2,283 tests; API unit suites green at 62 files / 875 tests; `tsc --noEmit` clean in both workspaces and `next build` clean for the admin app. No MySQL and no Docker daemon in this container, so the configuration integration file runs first in CI.)

- **Done**: #1052 (**Website Integration is administered from Cordel → Gyms → [Gym].** It was an item of the gym's own *Configuration* group (`/{locale}/website-integration`), although what it configures — the gym's registration endpoint and its website API key — is platform-side setup a gym owner touches once, when its site is connected. It is now a section of the **expanded gym card** on Cordel → Gyms, beside Configuration and Storage, rendered by `apps/admin/src/components/gyms/GymWebsiteIntegrationSection.tsx`: the endpoint with its Copy action, the active key's prefix and date, Generate / Rotate / Revoke with both existing confirmations, the once-only plaintext key and the five connection steps — the same `website_integration` copy in en/es/ca, unchanged. **No API change and no migration**: the three routes (`GET /system/website-integration`, `POST`/`DELETE …/key`), their validation, their `website_api_key` audit rows and the public registration endpoint are untouched. What the relocation needed was one thing: a tenant-scoped route called from a screen whose *row* names the gym, so `apiFetch` takes an optional `gymId` that overrides the selected gym for that request (`apps/admin/src/lib/apiClient.ts`, the one place the `x-gym-id` header is assembled since #824) — and the permission is still `tenantContext`'s: a superadmin gets admin on any gym it is handed, anybody else needs a `gym_memberships` row for it, so the option can never widen what a caller may do. The section loads when a card is expanded rather than with the list, because the key state is not in the list's row and the endpoint URL is the API's to build (#645). Consequence worth stating: Cordel is a superadmin-only group, so a **gym admin no longer manages its own key from the UI** — the route and the `system.website_integration` flag are still there, and nothing a connected website does changes. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/website-integration-placement.test.ts` (new, 20 — the nav item and the standalone page gone, the dead `nav.website_integration` label dropped in all three locales, the rest of the Configuration group byte-identical, the section rendered on the expanded card between Storage and Notes under the feature's own title, all three routes unchanged and every call carrying the row's gym, the once-only key never persisted, the endpoint taken from the API rather than assembled in the browser, one ConfirmDialog, `primaryBtnSmall()` with no lilac, every string a key of the one namespace in en/es/ca, and `apiFetch`'s override with the header kept out of the page). Admin suite green at 148 files / 2,322 tests; `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` clean.)

- **Done**: #1070 (**The Feature Flags page says what the application says, and a Personal Trainer may manage Personal Goals.** Two unrelated halves of one screen. (1) **Names.** The page labelled every row with the last segment of its own key, so the section a gym owner knows as *Nutrition & Goals* read `nutrition` there. `apps/admin/src/lib/featureFlagLabels.ts` resolves a key to the **navigation's own** `labelKey` (`config/navigationGroups.ts`, where the key and the label are already declared side by side), so the page now reads *Nutrition & Goals*, *Personal Goals*, *Configuration* — the names the sidebar uses, in all three locales, with no copy of its own: #948 §1 renamed that group and this is the consequence. Display-only by construction — nothing writes a `feature_flags` row, the stored key is still printed in monospace beside the label, and a key the navigation does not gate (the `member_web.*` flags) keeps the short key it always showed. (2) **An override.** `nutrition.personal_goals` × *Personal Trainer* is **RW**, which the `NUTRITION` cell cannot express: that role has `R_ASSIGNED` there, and widening the cell would hand it the Nutrition Library, the plan templates and every member's nutrition plan. So `FEATURE_PERMISSION_OVERRIDES` (`api/src/infra/permissions.ts`, mirrored in `apps/admin/src/config/permissions.ts`) is a new, deliberately narrow concept: one entry per **feature key**, replacing the module's level for that feature alone. Three of its properties are the rule rather than the implementation. It matches the **exact** key and never an ancestor, so granting Personal Goals says nothing about the rest of Nutrition — which is the whole reason to override at the feature level. It **replaces** the inherited level in both directions, so a `NONE` override could equally take a feature away from a role the module admits. And it is read where the feature flag already is: `requireFeatureAccess(featureKey, module)` / `requireFeatureWrite(featureKey, module)` take the same key `requireFeatureEnabled` is given beside them (`/personal-goals`, `/nutrition-goals` and `/member-personal-goals`, with `GOAL_LIBRARY_FEATURE_KEYS` declaring which kind rides which key, so one factory still serves both catalogues and the trainer writes one of them and not the other), and `useModuleAccess(module, featureKey)` is what the two Personal Goals pages and the Member card's PERSONAL GOALS section ask — so the control a page offers and the route it calls read one answer (#611's lesson, which is why the declaration is mirrored and gated). (3) **§3's red.** `GET /platform/feature-flags/role-access` reports `overrides` keyed by the full feature key, **beside** the inherited `access` rather than folded into it, and the page draws only that cell in `formChrome`'s `alertTextColor` — the app's one red, exported now so the Feature Flags page, a failed save and `ContextMenu`'s `danger` item stop spelling the same hex three times — with a `title` naming the role and the level and a legend line under the description, because a distinction that rests on colour alone is not one. The row keeps its own colours (§3), every other role keeps the styling it had, and the editor stays read-only: a permission is declared in code, not toggled on this screen. **No migration.** Tests: `api/src/test/feature-permission-overrides.unit.test.ts` (new, 11 — resolution, the ancestor it must not match, the other roles left alone, every override reportable and genuinely different from what its module gives, and the admin mirror imported and compared), `api/src/test/goal-library.test.ts` and `member-personal-goals.test.ts` (+6 integration — the trainer creating, renaming and deleting a Personal Goal and assigning, editing and removing a member's, the same role still 403 on Nutrition Goals, and front desk still 403 on both), `api/src/test/platform-feature-flags.test.ts` (+3 — the matrix, the overrides reported apart from it, and the superadmin guard) and `apps/admin/src/test/feature-flag-labels.test.ts` (new, 10 — the label resolution including the two items that share one key, the `member_web` fallback, the fallback decided before `t()`, the key still printed, the override read from the full key and coloured with the shared constant, and the legend in en/es/ca). Three existing source-scan gates were rewritten rather than deleted: the two mount strings and the page's `useModuleAccess` call in `goal-library-ui.test.ts`, the Member-card prop in `assigned-personal-goals.test.ts`, and `ContextMenu`'s hex in `professional-services-context-menu.test.ts`. Admin suite green at 149 files / 2,338 tests; API unit suites green at 64 files / 895 tests; `tsc --noEmit` clean in both workspaces. No MySQL and no Docker daemon in this container, so the three integration files run first in CI.)

- **Done**: #1072 (**Mobile app WP1 — the API contract for push, and FCM delivery behind it.** The first work package of the mobile app plan (`docs/mobile-app.md`, decision #18), and the only one that is backend-only. **Migration 221** adds `member_device_tokens` — `gym_id` NOT NULL, `member_id`, `platform` (`chk_mdt_platform` = `ios`|`android`), `app_id` (default `com.cordel.fitness`), `token`, `last_seen_at`, `created_at`; `UNIQUE (platform, token)`; `KEY (gym_id, member_id)` for the one query shape it has; both FKs `ON DELETE CASCADE` — and `POST /me/devices` / `DELETE /me/devices/:token` sit on the `me` router behind `requireRole('member')`, with the member resolved from the session so there is no member id on the wire to tamper with (§18-style ownership, as every `/me` route has it). Two decisions are the design rather than the mechanics. The unique key is **global, not per gym**: an FCM registration token identifies an app *installation*, so the `POST` is an upsert that re-points `gym_id`/`member_id` and refreshes `last_seen_at` — re-registering on every sign-in (WP2) adds no rows, and a shared handset is taken over by whoever signed in last instead of pushing one member's alerts to a phone that is now someone else's. And `app_id` exists **before the second app does**, which is design rule 2 of the plan: credentials are resolved per app, so adding the column later would be a data migration with every existing token to attribute by guesswork. `parseDeviceRegistration()` (`api/src/domain/deviceTokens.ts`) is the only place a body is judged — an unknown platform, a blank token or an over-wide value is a 400 and never a coercion, because each of those would be a row that can never be delivered to. **Delivery** is `api/src/infra/push.ts` over the pure `api/src/domain/pushDelivery.ts`, called by all three `member_notifications` writers (`sendNotification`, `sendBulkNotification`, `recordNotifications`) *after* the insert and never awaited: the row is the durable fact the Members App reads and the push is a courtesy copy of it, so no credentials, an unreachable Firebase or a member with no device each cost the push and nothing else — and with no credentials the module does not even reach the database, which is what keeps it inert in tests and locally. No notification type was added, so `chk_member_notifications_type` is untouched. It speaks **FCM HTTP v1 with no SDK**: a service-account JWT signed with `node:crypto` is exchanged for an access token (cached per project) and one POST goes out per token — `firebase-admin` would be a large dependency tree for one signed HTTP call, and the legacy server-key API is decommissioned anyway. Credentials are **one** environment variable, `FCM_SERVICE_ACCOUNTS`: a JSON object keyed by app id, accepted raw or base64-encoded because `deploy.yml` writes the API's environment as inline quadlet `Environment=` lines, so a stage-2 per-gym app is a new key rather than a code change and a malformed entry is dropped and logged rather than thrown (push must not fail a booking). A token whose `app_id` has no entry is **skipped**, not failed — that is an unconfigured gym app, not an error on every alert. A failed send deletes the token **only** where FCM says it can never work again (`UNREGISTERED`, `SENDER_ID_MISMATCH`): `INVALID_ARGUMENT` is deliberately kept, since FCM answers it for a malformed *message* too and deleting on it would empty the table on the first bug in the message builder, while a 429 or a 5xx is an unknown outcome rather than evidence against the token (#785's `provider_error` judgment, one domain over). The message carries the payload's own `title` and a `data` block for routing and **no sentence**: the per-type copy a member reads is the Members App's locale keys, and composing it here would be a second copy of that copy in a module that has to pick a language — answerable now that #1039 stores a member's locale, but that is the ticket which decides where push copy lives. Two things the `db-reviewer` pass added are worth recording: the table pins `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` with `platform` and `token` on `utf8mb4_bin` (an inherited collation differing from `gyms`' fails the FK with errno 3780, and a case-insensitive unique key would keep a stored token the sender then pushes and FCM rejects, which `pushFailureAction()` would turn into a silent deletion), and the flip side of the global key is stated in the migration's header — a member who *learns* another member's token could re-point the row at themselves, which per-gym uniqueness would confine to one gym without removing while reintroducing the double delivery, so the key stays global and the token stays something the route never echoes, never logs and only the sender reads. Tests: `api/src/test/me-devices.test.ts` (new, 14 integration — 401/403, register, idempotent upsert refreshing `last_seen_at`, shared-device takeover, platform separation, five refusals, delete, another member's token and another gym's token both 404 with the row intact, and a failing send leaving both the notification row and the token alone), `device-tokens.unit.test.ts` (new, 18 — the vocabulary, every refusal, the configured default, and the migration's own `PLATFORMS`/`DEFAULT_APP_ID`/widths/unique key asserted against the module so the two halves cannot drift), `push-delivery.unit.test.ts` (new, 19 — the credential parser including base64 and partial failure, the message shape, the delete rule and FCM's error-code extraction) and `push.unit.test.ts` (new, 9 — the delivery loop with the database mocked and `fetch` stubbed: one message per token, one token exchange for several sends, an unconfigured app skipped, the gym-and-member scope of the read, the delete on `UNREGISTERED`, the keep on `INVALID_ARGUMENT`, and three ways of failing that must not throw). `cleanupTestGyms` extended. API unit suites green at 67 files / 942 tests; `tsc --noEmit` clean. No MySQL and no Docker daemon in this container, so the integration file runs first in CI. **Not verified anywhere yet:** a real delivery — nothing in the repository has FCM credentials, which is WP5's item in `docs/go-to-production.md` §6.)

- **Done**: #1036 (**My Goals — the member manages their own Personal Goals.** #948 §4 gave the gym the assignment model (`member_personal_goals`, migration 212) and two staff screens; this ticket gives the member the same goals from the Members App, and it is deliberately the *same* table, the same domain module and the same rules rather than a second surface with its own. The owner answered all four open questions on the thread: the member may add, remove **and update** a goal at any time (so the unit is editable, as it has been staff-side since migration 212); the flag is **global and enabled by default** (`member_web.my_goals`, migration 223 — seeded flat rather than from a predecessor the way migration 211 does, because the section is new, and the gym-side `nutrition.personal_goals` still gates the routes beside it, since a gym that hid Personal Goals did not mean "and let members assign them anyway"); the selector hides a retired goal **and** one the member already holds live (`Q3 live`, which is the predicate `mpgoal_live_goal_key` generates from, so the UI and the unique index agree by construction instead of the UI being stricter); and either side may remove an assignment, after which it reads as a **Past Goal** and re-assigning creates "a brand new one". API: `api/src/api/me-personal-goals.ts`, five routes mounted at `/me/personal-goals` (registered **before** `/me`, or Express answers `meRouter`'s 404). Four of its properties are the ticket's rather than the file's — the member is **never named by a request** (`resolveMemberId()` plus `(gym_id, member_id)` on every statement, so another member's assignment is a 404 whatever the payload says, and no body carries a `member_id`); a member **cannot create a Goal definition** (§4/§6/§16 — nothing writes `personal_goals`, and the picker is a read of the catalogue under its own `(gym_id IS NULL OR gym_id = ?)` rule); the **snapshot is the server's** (§8 — `POST` reads the catalogue row it has already validated and writes its `name`, inheriting the target for a field the request did not mention, which is #1034 §7's own inheritance rather than a second one); and **progress is not the member's to set** (§14 — no route accepts `status`; a member ends a goal by removing it). **Migration 222** adds the one column the Past Goals list needs and `end_date` is it: *when* an assignment stopped, which neither `target_date` (when it was meant to be reached) nor `modified_at` (the last time anything changed) records. It is written only by `endDateTransition()`, asked by **both** routers, so the staff edit, the staff removal and the member's own two writes cannot date the same transition differently: it stamps the day a row stops being live-and-in-progress, keeps the first such date (a goal achieved in March and removed in June ended in March), and clears when the goal is pursued again. Never submitted by a client — a fourth date to validate against the other three is a way to claim an assignment ended on a day it did not — and nullable with **no backfill**, migration 218's shape, since a row that ended before the column existed has no end date to recover. The same migration widens the three `chk_mpgoal_*_by_type` CHECKs to admit **`member`**: #799's actor pairs were written when only staff could touch an assignment, and leaving them NULL would make a member-created row indistinguishable from a pre-column one (`memberActorSnapshot()`; a superadmin impersonating a member still records `superadmin`, which is `actorSnapshot()`'s own rule one router over). Member app: `app/[locale]/goals/page.tsx` plus a dashboard tile, painted by #1038's `personal_goals` slot, which `slotForPathname()` names now that the page it was added for exists. The page decides nothing — `lib/memberGoals.ts` owns the label (a System goal's slug through `goals.goal_<slug>`, the fallback decided *before* `t()` is called, since next-intl prints a missing key verbatim), the target formatting (`0 kg` is a target and not an absence, which is why Maintenance reads as one), which of the two lists a row is in, the two payloads and every client-side rule as a locale **key** — and `components/MemberDialog.tsx` is the app's one dialog shell, promoted out of the shape the Calendar panel and the impersonation dialog had each drawn for themselves: the Add/Edit form and the `Remove goal?` confirmation are two uses of it rather than two overlays (§19). No hex is spelled in either file (#983), and the new `goals` namespace plus `nav.goals` are in en/es/ca. Tests: `api/src/test/me-personal-goals.test.ts` (new, 31 integration — auth and the role guard, the empty answer for a caller with no member profile, the live/past split, the number and date shapes, the name snapshot surviving a rename and its legacy fallback, the selector's three exclusions and its catalogue target, the create including the inheritance, the explicit clear, the 409, the two refusals and a `member_id`/`status` in the body being ignored, the partial `PUT` and the cross-field clear, the immutable goal, the soft delete with its end date and the first-end rule, remove-then-reassign as a new row, and cross-member and cross-tenant isolation on every route), `personal-goal-assignment.unit.test.ts` (+13 — the actor snapshot, the live predicate, all four `endDateTransition` answers, and the date normalisers) and `apps/member/src/test/my-goals.test.ts` (new, 37 — the label resolution and its fallback, the target formatting including zero, the four status words, the form seeding, every validation key, the payloads, and the source scans: no hex, the shared chrome, one dialog component, no `member_id`, no `status`, only `/me/personal-goals` calls, and locale parity in three languages). Two pre-existing gates were rewritten rather than deleted, both of which pinned the *absence* of this page: `members-background.test.ts`'s "does not map a My Goals page yet" and the home page's slot count. Member suite green at 17 files / 330 tests, API unit suites at 67 files / 956 tests, admin untouched and green at 2,338; `tsc --noEmit` clean in all three workspaces. No MySQL and no Docker daemon in this container, so the integration file runs first in CI. **Deliberately out of scope**: a member-facing progress vocabulary (§14 — `status` stays staff-set), and an admin surface for `end_date`, which the column records but no staff screen yet displays.)

- **Done**: #1035 stage 1 of 2 (**The bucket's gym tree is lowercase, and a food's image is keyed by the food.** Gym Bucket Initialization wrote `Nutrition/`, `Nutrition/Images/`, `Exercises/`, `Exercises/Images/`, `Exercises/Videos/` and `themes/` — five mixed-case folders beside the one #829 had already lowercased. `GYM_FOLDERS` now writes `nutrition/`, `exercises/`, `exercises/images/`, `exercises/videos/` and `themes/`, built from constants rather than literals: `NUTRITION_STORAGE_FOLDER`, `EXERCISE_STORAGE_FOLDER` and its two leaves live in `api/src/infra/storage.ts` beside `THEMES_FOLDER` and are re-exported by the domain modules that build keys from them, so a marker cannot diverge from the builder that writes into it — which is the whole reason the names are declared once (a case difference in R2 adds a folder rather than renaming one). `Nutrition/Images/` is gone with its leaf: a food's image was the only thing it ever held, so it sits directly under `nutrition/` now. **No object moved** (§6): an exercise image under `Exercises/Images/…`, a video under `Exercises/Videos/…` and a food image under `Nutrition/Images/<uuid>.<ext>` all keep rendering from the keys their rows hold, and replacing that asset is what lands it on the new name — `docs/go-to-production.md` carries the sweep, as #829's did. The second half is the food image itself: `Q2 named` asked for `nutrition/<food_id>-<food_name>`, which the generic `POST /storage/uploads/nutrition-image` could not produce, because a route that never sees the row has neither the id nor the name — its key was a bare `<uuid>`. That route is **retired**, replaced by the per-row `POST /nutrition-library/:id/image` (`requireModuleWrite('NUTRITION')`, 403 on a System row exactly as the `PUT` is, 404 for another gym's food), and `buildGymNutritionImageKey()` sits beside `buildBaseNutritionImageKey()` in `domain/baseNutritionImages.ts` off the one folder constant and the one sanitizer, so the two roots cannot drift. The platform key moved the same way, from `<image_uuid>-<name>` to `<food_id>-<name>` (§12, and what both of the ticket's trees spell). Three decisions are the rule rather than the implementation. **A deterministic key replaces its own object**, so the per-upload sweep runs only when the key actually moved — a rename, a different format, or a pre-#1035 key — and a surface showing the image cache-busts on the row's `modified_at`, which Cordel's Base library already did. **The gym side keeps its own validation**: the four MIME types and the 5 MB ceiling the retired route accepted, so the JPEGs gyms upload today still work and the extension therefore comes from the server-validated MIME type rather than being a fixed `.png` — the one place the implementation is narrower than the ticket's tree, because naming a JPEG `.png` is the one thing a key must not do. And **`goals/` is not created yet**: nothing writes a Personal Goal image, and a first-level folder no writer populates is exactly what #826 removed, so it arrives with its writer in stage 2. `cordel/` is pinned as uninitialized (§2/§7) — it never was, and a test now says so; the platform routes' own upload-time markers stay, since they are what make a branch visible before the console tree exists. Tests: `base-nutrition-images.unit.test.ts` extended (both key shapes, the four extensions, the shared folder constant, determinism, the gym markers), `storage.test.ts` extended (the six lowercase markers in order, no `goals/`, nothing under `cordel/`, no mixed-case marker, and the first-level folders matching every builder including the new nutrition one), `nutrition-library-images.test.ts` (new, 21 integration — auth, the read-only role, tenant isolation with nothing written, the System 403, the key and the persisted URL, the retired shape's absence, the markers, the extension, replacing with and without a key move, the `cordel/` and other-gym objects left alone, and every refusal leaving the stored image untouched), `storage-uploads.test.ts`'s nutrition block replaced by one asserting the route is gone, and `apps/admin/src/test/nutrition-library-image-path.test.ts` (new, 5). API unit suites green at 68 files / 1,014 tests and the admin suite at 150 files / 2,343 tests; `tsc --noEmit` clean in both. The integration file was not executed here — this container has no MySQL 8 — so it runs first in CI. **Stage 2** is Personal Goal images: the column, the gym and platform routes at `Q3`'s 512×512 PNG without the alpha requirement, the `goals/` folder and its marker, and the control on both Personal Goals screens.)

- **Done**: #1035 stage 2 (**A Personal Goal carries an image, and `goals/` is the folder it lives in.** Stage 2 of the two-stage split agreed on the thread — stage 1 (PR #1119) lowercased the gym tree and keyed a food's image by the food; this is the half whose *subject* did not exist: `personal_goals` had no image column, no key builder, no route and no control, so §4/§5's upload, retrieval and delete paths were new API surface rather than a rename. **Migration 225** adds `personal_goals.image_url VARCHAR(1024) NULL` — the width `exercises.image_url` uses (187) rather than `nutrition_library_items`' 500, because the key carries a gym prefix built from a VARCHAR(255) gym name and the row is written *after* the object reaches R2, so an overflow would leave an orphan and a 500 instead of a clean rejection (the other half of that guarantee is the 80-character cap on the name part, `sanitizeExerciseImageName()`'s own). **One column for both kinds of row**, the shape migration 138 already has: the ownership of the row decides which root its key hangs off, never a second column or a flag. `nutrition_goals` is untouched, which is migration 206/218's divergence rule again, and `IMAGE_GOAL_KINDS` in `api/src/domain/goalLibrary.ts` is the one place that says which kinds have the column — asked once by each router exactly as `MEASURABLE_GOAL_KINDS` is, and gating whether the two routes are **registered at all**, so a Nutrition Goal's `/:id/image` is a 404 rather than a write to a missing column (which would be ER_BAD_FIELD_ERROR and a bare 500, #966). `api/src/domain/personalGoalImages.ts` is the one place that decides where an object goes, what is accepted and what may be deleted, and it is **one module for both roots** rather than the two exercises have (#719 + #716), because a Personal Goal is one table holding both kinds of row: `<gym prefix>/goals/<goal_id>-<name>.png` and `cordel/goals/<goal_id>-<name>.png`, exactly as `docs/cloudflare_structure.md` spells them. `goals/` has **one spelling**, `GOALS_FOLDER` in `api/src/infra/storage.ts`, where the gym's first-level marker is written from the same constant — in R2 a case difference is a different key, so a second spelling would add a folder beside the one uploads populate rather than rename it (#829's rule) — and it joins `GYM_FOLDERS` **appended last**, so existing gyms' markers keep the order they were written in, *with* its writer, which is what #826 requires of a first-level folder at all. It has no leaf: a goal's image is all that branch holds. Routes: `POST`/`DELETE /personal-goals/:id/image` (raw `image/png` ≤ 2 MB; 403 on a System row, which Cordel administers, 404 on another gym's, 409 on a deleted goal or a gym with no `storage_folder_prefix`, 503 unconfigured, 502 on an R2 failure) and the `/platform/personal-goals/:id/image` pair (`requireSuperadmin`, `gym_id IS NULL` rows, **no 409** — a constant root has nothing to initialize first, so it writes `cordel/` + `cordel/goals/` itself, since Gym Bucket Initialization deliberately writes nothing there, the owner's `Q5`). Three of their answers are the rule rather than the implementation. The key is **deterministic**, so a replacement overwrites its own object and the best-effort sweep runs only when the key genuinely moved — a rename, or an image under a pre-#1035 key — which is also why the preview is cache-busted on the row's own `modified_at`, Cordel's Base Nutrition Library's own device. Validation is the **file's** (`domain/pngImage.ts`: signature and dimensions from the IHDR), never the `Content-Type` header or the file name, and a rejection uploads nothing and writes nothing, so an invalid upload cannot disturb the image already there. And what counts as valid is deliberately *not* #715's: **at most** 512×512 rather than an exact square, and **no alpha requirement** (`Q3`: "Same, with a maximum of 512×512 px but no need to alpha"), so an opaque PNG is a legitimate goal image — a non-square one too, since the frames are 1:1 with `object-fit: contain` and refusing it would be a rule the answer did not ask for. Deletion is guarded both ways by `isGymOwnedPersonalGoalImageUrl()` / `isPlatformOwnedPersonalGoalImageUrl()` (#719 §19), the second narrowed to `cordel/goals/` so another platform feature's object is not this one's to delete either; nothing is generated, seeded or backfilled (every row starts NULL — #716's "generating the artwork is out of scope"), and there is no runtime fallback from a gym goal to the System goal's image. Admin: one `components/goalLibrary/GoalImageField.tsx` for both Personal Goals screens, handed `basePath` and `requiresGymStorage` by the page so it names no endpoint and decides no permission (#806) and a Base goal is not blocked by whichever gym a superadmin has selected (#823); the preview is a **value** in the read-only half and the control belongs to Edit mode alone (#797/#799 §17), and the *create* form offers none, because the key needs the row's id (#715's own answer, and #974's rule about never rendering a control the route would ignore). Raw bytes go through `uploadFetch()` rather than a hand-rolled proxy fetch, or `tenantContext` gets no gym and answers a bare 401 (#824); the button is `primaryBtnSmall()` and the error line `formChrome`'s `alertTextColor`, so the file spells no colour. The 1:1 checkerboard frame moved into `components/imagePreviewFrame.ts`, which `ExerciseImageField` now reads too — three upload controls had been about to spell a fourth. Seventeen locale keys in the `goal_library` namespace in en/es/ca, since next-intl prints a missing key verbatim. Tests: `api/src/test/personal-goal-images.unit.test.ts` (new, 18 pure — both keys, the folder constant shared with `infra/storage`, determinism, two names that sanitize alike kept apart by id, the name cap with no trailing separator, both markers, both ownership predicates including a prefix that is a string prefix of another gym's, the ceiling as a maximum, the opaque and non-square acceptances, every refusal including the string/array type-confusion guard, and the kind declaration), `api/src/test/personal-goal-images.test.ts` (new, 40 integration — auth, the read-only role, the System 403, cross-tenant 404s with nothing written, the 409s, the 503, the key and the persisted URL, the markers, a 502 leaving the row alone, a replace with nothing to sweep, a rename sweeping the old key, the platform/other-gym/external objects left alone, the actor, both DELETEs, the platform pair's own key and markers and its 404 for a gym's goal, and the Nutrition Goal 404 on both sides), plus `storage.test.ts` (+1 and three assertions rewritten for the fourth folder, including the one that pins each marker against its key builder) and `apps/admin/src/test/goal-library-ui.test.ts` (+7 — both mirrors against the API's own declarations, the section rendering one control behind the kind, the control naming no endpoint and spelling no colour, the shared frame, and locale parity). Merged with `origin/main` after stage 1 (#1119) landed, which is what the conflicts were: `GYM_FOLDERS` merged by itself (stage 1's lowercase constants plus this one's appended `goals/`), and the three doc bullets and `storage.test.ts`'s first-level-folder assertions were resolved by keeping stage 1's text and adding the fourth folder to it — including stage 1's own "`goals/` is deliberately absent, it arrives with its writer" note, which this stage is that writer and so retires. The **whole** API suite was run here against a real MySQL 8.4, integration files included — **265 files / 5,689 tests** green, the same totals CI reports — because `dockerd` turned out to be startable in this container after all; admin green at 150 files / 2,351, `tsc --noEmit` clean in both and `next build` clean. That is also how CI's one failure was handled rather than guessed at: `returns 403 for a read-only role` answered **409**, and the route was right — this test file mocks the Clerk user as a **superadmin** for its `/platform/*` half, and `tenantContext` gives a superadmin full admin on any gym it is handed, so the `front_desk` membership decided nothing, the request reached the handler and the 409 came from that gym having no `storage_folder_prefix`. The case now mocks an ordinary user, which is what puts the membership's role under test, **and** gives that gym a prefix: with the second explanation removed, a regression in `requireWrite` reads as a 200 rather than as a 409 that looks like a pass. The original failure was reproduced locally before the fix and the suite shown green after it. `db-reviewer` reported no blockers on migration 225 and its three notes are addressed — the `AFTER` prose, the coupling to 218's column, and the two `docs/go-to-production.md` items (a rollback leaves the objects behind; `goals/` reaches an existing gym on its next Initialize bucket, a cosmetic backfill). **Deliberately out of scope**: a member-facing or staff-card rendering of the image beyond the two library screens, and the `members_app/` slot renames the owner's latest `docs/cloudflare_structure.md` edit shows (`goals.png`, `products_services.png`) — a slot name is a stored object key, so those are a migration plus a bucket sweep and need their own ticket.)

- **Done**: #1073 (**Mobile app WP2 — the Members App made ready for the native shell.** Everything `apps/member` needs to run inside Capacitor, with the web build unchanged: `public/manifest.json` plus three icons (the layout linked a manifest that 404ed, and `next build` does not fold `public/` into the standalone output — `apps/member/Dockerfile` copies it explicitly now), safe-area insets, a native Google sign-in button, push registration and link routing. The design is **two modules, not one**: `lib/native.ts` is the decision half — pure, no React, no plugin import — holding `isNative()`, the platform, the `POST /me/devices` body, the `appUrlOpen` rule, the Google configuration and the ID-token extraction, while `lib/nativePlugins.ts` is the access half and the only file in the app that imports a Capacitor package, which is what makes `docs/mobile-app.md` design rule 3 ("everything native sits behind one module; a plain browser never executes native code") an assertion rather than a convention — `native.test.ts` fails the build if any other file under `src` names `@capacitor/` or `@capgo/`. Every plugin import is **dynamic**: the app is server-rendered, so a plugin evaluated at module scope would run during SSR and in every browser, while `await import()` still ships the package (the spike's finding — a remote page gets no `registerPlugin` from the injected bridge, so `@capacitor/core` must be in the web build) and a plugin the shell lacks resolves to `null` rather than throwing, because a web release newer than the shell on a member's phone must not take the page down. Four smaller decisions are the design rather than the mechanics. **Detection and the registered platform are one answer** — `isNative()` is true for exactly the platforms `chk_mdt_platform` accepts, so a future Capacitor target reads as not native instead of registering a value the API would refuse on a fire-and-forget call nobody sees. **A component that renders differently resolves it after mount** (`useIsNative()`, false on the server and on the first client render), since asking during render is a hydration mismatch and React resolves those by discarding the client tree. **Safe areas are not behind `isNative()`** — `env(safe-area-inset-*)` is `0px` wherever there is no inset, so `memberChrome.ts`'s `safeArea`/`withSafeArea()` are correct in both builds and no new surface has to remember a branch; the top inset is `TopBar`'s own padding so the strip under the status bar carries the header's themed background rather than the page behind it (the one layout defect the 2026-10-04 spike reported), and the bottom inset is the layout's, once, for every route. And **the native button does not exist unless it can work**: no bridge, or no Google client ids in the build (`NEXT_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_IOS_CLIENT_ID` / `NEXT_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_WEB_CLIENT_ID`, build args per design rule 1 — the iOS client identifies the app and the *web* client is the audience Clerk verifies, which is the whole of what the spike turned on), renders nothing and leaves the ordinary email-and-password form; Clerk's own Google button is hidden through `appearance` only when native, so the web sign-in screen is unchanged, and a dismissed sheet returns no token and is not an error. Push **registers on sign-in and unregisters before sign-out** — the delete is authenticated as the member whose device it is, so after `signOut()` there is nothing to call it with; `lib/nativePush.ts` keeps the token for exactly that and the app's one sign-out (the invitation page's, #759) calls it. A tapped notification opens `/notifications` and **no per-type route**: the `data` block carries the type and entity for a later ticket, and guessing a destination would answer the wrong screen. `NativeAppState` adds the splash the slow first load needs and an error screen with a retry, native-only, over a new `loadError`/`reload()` on `AppContext` (a thrown `fetch` or a 5xx — never a 401/403, which is an answer about who the caller is, and the app already has screens for those). No migration, no API change.
