Tests: `apps/member/src/test/native.test.ts` (new, 39) and `api/src/test/members-app-native.unit.test.ts` (new, 6 — the two-module rule itself, in the **API** suite for #1009's reason, since CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only and a scan that must hold on every push belongs where every push runs it), plus the `native` locale namespace in en/es/ca; the app's suite is green at 18 files / 366 tests, `tsc --noEmit` clean on both, `next build` passes, and the whole API suite is green at 266 files / 5,695 tests against a real MySQL 8.4 in this container (`dockerd` is startable here), the integration files included. **Still unverified, and WP3's to close:** the first-time Google sign-in by an *invited* member under Clerk's restricted mode, which needs a Clerk instance rather than a code path and stays on the `docs/go-to-production.md` §6 checklist.)

- **Done**: #1074 (**Mobile app WP3 — the Capacitor shell, `apps/mobile`.** The third work package of the mobile app plan (`docs/mobile-app.md`, decision #18): a new npm workspace holding Capacitor 8 with `ios/` and `android/` in the repository, the three plugins `apps/member` already depends on (at the **same** versions — the web half and the native half of a plugin are one plugin), and `www/` holding a single local page, since the UI is the *deployed* Members App loaded through `server.url` and there is no web build step here. The whole of it is parameterised by an **app profile**, which is design rule 1 (*no app identity is hard-coded*) made mechanical: `profiles/<id>.json` is the one place an app id, display name, URL, navigation allow-list or Google client id may be spelled, `MOBILE_*` variables override it **per field**, and `appId`/`appName`/`serverUrl` have no fallback in code at all — a shell with no Bundle ID would install over another app's identity and one with no URL would open a blank WebView. Stage 1 has exactly one profile, `cordel-fitness`. Four decisions beyond the bullet list. **`npm run profile:apply` exists because `cap sync` does not write identity**: `cap add` seeds the Xcode project, `Info.plist`, Gradle and `strings.xml` once and `cap sync` then copies only web assets and plugins, so those four need a writer — `src/nativeProjectFiles.ts`, pure text transforms (assertable with no macOS machine) each of which **throws rather than reporting success** when its anchor is gone, because the failure that matters is an apply that silently leaves the previous gym's Bundle ID in place. **Google's URL scheme is derived, not configured** — the reversed client id, a mechanical transform of the id, so a second field is only something to disagree with; a build with no client id registers none, matching WP2's rule that the native button is absent rather than broken. **A URL opened on a running app reaches the *scene* delegate**, not the app delegate, because `Info.plist` declares a scene manifest — the plugin's README documents the pre-scene template — so `NativeSignInUrl.handle` is one rule asked from both rather than a handler that never runs. And **the iOS token has to be the FCM token**, since delivery is FCM HTTP v1 and FCM does not deliver to an APNs token: `AppDelegate` hands the APNs token to `Messaging` and posts Firebase's token as Capacitor's `registration` value, behind `#if canImport(FirebaseMessaging)` so the project compiles before the Xcode package step — which, until it is taken, is why iOS push cannot be delivered (`docs/mobile-runbook.md` §2). Plus the two entitlements the spike proved are needed (`keychain-access-groups`, written from build settings so a second profile needs no edit, and `aps-environment`), an Android intent filter on `@string/custom_url_scheme` and `POST_NOTIFICATIONS`. No migration, no API change, no route, no locale key, and nothing in `apps/member` touched. Docs: `docs/mobile-runbook.md` (new — the manual simulator, device and profile-switch checks), `docs/mobile-app.md` WP3 marked done with what it decided, `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/go-to-production.md` §6 (the shell's own configuration items: artwork, the Firebase package, the two Firebase config files) and a `CLAUDE.md` constraint. Tests: `apps/mobile/src/test/appProfile.test.ts` (21) and `api/src/test/mobile-shell-profile.unit.test.ts` (15, in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only). **Not verified: anything needing a build** — this container has no macOS, no Xcode and no Android SDK, so neither platform was compiled and no device or simulator ran the app.)

- **Done**: #1037 stage 2 (**An Assigned Personal Goal accumulates readings, and progress is computed from them.** Stage 2 of the agreed plan on the thread — stage 0 (#1034/PR #1097, the Gym Goal's target pair and the `goal_name` snapshot) and stage 1 (#1036, My Goals) are both merged, and the owner answered all four questions: **ECharts** as the app's shared charting layer (`Q1 lib`, stage 3's), **no direction column** (`Q2` — derived from the active initial reading versus the target), the chart and the history on **all three** surfaces (`Q3`: My Goals, the Member card and Assigned Personal Goals) and both `Q4` defaults (progress clamped to 0–100 with the raw value never shown, several readings on one date stored separately). This stage is the data model, the one progress rule and the write routes — the API contract, per CLAUDE.md's backend-first rule — so the §5–§11 header, the Add-reading dialog and the ECharts chart render fields that already exist rather than deciding anything. **Migration 224** adds `member_personal_goal_readings`: `value DECIMAL(10,2)` (the assignment's own unit, the same type and bound as the target it is measured against — a per-reading unit would be the second unit system #1034 §1 forbids), `recorded_at DATETIME`, `is_initial`, and a `created_by` pair admitting `member` as migration 222 already does, with both FKs `ON DELETE CASCADE` and two indexes, one per FK, the gym-leading one shaped for the table's single read — `is_initial` carries none, because nothing narrows it in SQL: the initial-reading history, the active baseline and a reading's period are all derived in memory from that one chronological load (the `db-reviewer` pass dropped the index the first draft had written for them). **One table, not the two §37 sketches**, and that is the stage's one real design decision: `is_initial` *is* the initial-reading period boundary, because §1/§29 put the initial reading **in** the reading history, so a period row carrying its own `value` and `effective_from` would store the same number twice with nothing in SQL able to tie them together, and an `effective_from` differing from the measurement it was taken from would place that measurement in the previous period while the period it opens began earlier. What §37 actually asks for is the capability — *which initial reading was active at any point in time?* — and the flag answers it exactly: the initial-reading history (§22) is `is_initial = 1` ordered by `recorded_at`, the **active** initial reading (§25) is its last row, falling back to the earliest reading when nothing is flagged (§2), and a reading's period (§38) is `assignReadingPeriods()`, an ordering question with no join. **Nothing is stored that can be derived**: `api/src/domain/goalReadings.ts` owns the vocabulary and the arithmetic and `api/src/api/goal-readings.ts` the one loader, so `initial_reading`, `initial_reading_at`, `latest_reading`, `latest_reading_at`, `progress_percent` and `reading_count` ride on **every** assignment-shaped response of both routers — the list, the single read and each mutation — read in one query per page (`newMemberStatusByMember()`'s rule, #927) and recomputed on each read, which is what §11's "must update automatically" means for a field no writer can forget. `progressPercent()` is `(initial - latest) / (initial - target) x 100` for both directions, since the two differences change sign together; `target == initial` is maintenance (100 while the latest reading is still the target, 0 otherwise) rather than a division by zero; the result is clamped to 0–100; and `null` means not computable — no readings or no target — so a card reads `—` and never `0%`, which are different facts. Routes: `GET /:id/readings` plus **two** writers per surface, `POST /:id/readings` (a measurement) and `POST /:id/initial-reading` (a new baseline), deliberately separate so a client cannot re-baseline a member's goal by passing a flag through and the audit rows (`add_reading` / `set_initial_reading`, against the assignment, which is the entity `AUDIT_ENTITY_REGISTRY` already knows) say which happened. Both `POST /` paths may establish the baseline as the goal is assigned, in the **same transaction** as the assignment, taking §4's "reuse the existing timestamp" from `start_date` when no time is named. Append-only (§34): no edit, no delete, and the assignment's soft delete leaves every reading where it is (§28). A future `recorded_at` is refused — §32 allows historical dates only, and a mistyped year would otherwise be the "latest reading" for ever — while a date-only value is accepted at midnight UTC, which is what the Add reading dialog submits. Tests: `api/src/test/goal-readings.unit.test.ts` (new, 34 — both validators including the future and the clock-skew allowance, the two driver shapes of a DATETIME, the total ordering, the active initial reading and its fallback, the period assignment with and without a leading boundary, the ticket's own 50% example in both directions, both clamps, maintenance, every `null` case, the summary, and §4's baseline timestamp including the future `start_date` it declines) and `api/src/test/goal-readings.test.ts` (new, 16 integration — auth and the read-only role, tenant isolation on all three routes with nothing written, scenario 1 end to end, scenarios 3 and 4's periods, a gain goal, the same-date case, every refusal leaving no row, the summary on the list, the member's own three routes with the actor recorded as `member`, another member's assignment 404 on each, and append-only). API unit suites green at 68 files / 991 tests; `tsc --noEmit` clean. (The integration file was not executed in that session; it has since been run against a real MySQL 8.4 as part of stage 3's verification, green.) **Deliberately out of scope**: the structured five-field header (§5–§11's rendering), the Add-reading dialog, the reading-history accordion and the ECharts charting layer with its chart (§13–§24) — the next stages, which read these fields and add no rule.)

- **Done**: #1037 stage 3 (**The readings on screen: the structured header, the Add reading dialog and the reading history.** Stage 3 of the agreed plan — stage 2 (#1111) is merged, so this stage *renders* fields that already exist and adds no rule, no migration and no route. §5–§11's header is the five structured fields `GOAL_READING_FIELDS` declares (Goal · Initial reading · Target · Latest reading · Progress, label above value, an `auto-fit` grid so §41's reflow is one rule rather than a media query), drawn by **one component per app** — `apps/admin/src/components/personalGoals/GoalReadingHeader.tsx` for the gym-wide list's expanded row *and* each card of the Member card's PERSONAL GOALS section, and `GoalHeaderFields` in `apps/member/src/components/GoalReadings.tsx` for My Goals, the two apps sharing no frontend module (`calendarEventPaint.ts`'s rule). The goal's name is one of those five fields rather than a heading above them, because a card that renders it twice is §5's pipe-separated sentence wearing a different shape. **Nothing is computed on screen**: the three reading values and the percentage come off the row (stage 2 derives them on every read), the formatting lives in one module per app (`goalReadings.ts`, `lib/memberGoals.ts`) and a figure that is not computable reads `—` and never `0%` — a test in each app asserts its summary field list is exactly the API interface's, so a rename fails the build rather than rendering `undefined`. §18–§20's **reading history** is one collapsible card per surface, newest first (the chart's order reversed, so the API still answers one ordering), with the **first** boundary marked `Initial` and every later one `New initial reading` (§29) and the earliest reading marked `Initial` when nothing is flagged — exactly the server's own baseline fallback, or the header would quote an initial reading no row explains. It carries no `✕`, no edit and no delete, because §34 has no route for either, and two readings on one date stay two rows (§33 — the time is shown only for a reading that carries one, decided per row rather than by comparing neighbours). §3's **Add reading** is one dialog per app in the app's own shell (`CrudModal`, `MemberDialog`): a number and a date, the assignment's **unit beside the input rather than asked for**, the date defaulting to today (§32) and a future one refused as the server refuses it. Which of stage 2's two writers it calls is the **route** (`READING_ENDPOINTS`) and never a field, so no client reaches the re-baseline through the measurement endpoint. Where the action is *offered* is each surface's own rule: `⋮ → Add reading` / `Change initial reading` on the gym-wide list, whose expanded body stays control-free (#797) and which also gains a **Progress** column; a button inside the Member card's Edit mode, absent outside it (#957); and a button on the member's own card, which has no Edit mode because the goals are theirs. A past goal is read-only and is offered no reading action. 41 new frontend tests (`apps/admin/src/test/assigned-personal-goals.test.ts` +19, `apps/member/src/test/my-goals.test.ts` +18, plus the two row factories) covering the field order and its locale parity in en/es/ca, both summary-field drift gates, every formatter boundary (`0 kg` is a measurement, `99.9%` is not rounded to 100, `—` for null, a time only when recorded), the four history cases, the six validation refusals, the payload carrying no `is_initial`, the endpoints matching the router's own paths, and the gating on all three surfaces. Green: **API 263 files / 5631 tests against a real MySQL 8.4** (all 239 migrations applied), **admin 150 / 2369**, **member 17 / 344**, `tsc --noEmit` clean in all three workspaces and both `next build`s clean. No migration, so `db-reviewer` does not apply; no new router, so `test-writer` does not either. **Deliberately out of scope**: stage 4 — the **ECharts** shared charting layer the owner chose in `Q1 lib` and the chart itself (§13–§17, §23–§24's period segmentation), which reads the `period` field stage 2 already reports on every reading.)

- **Done**: #1037 stage 4 (**One charting layer for both apps, and the goal reading chart drawn with it.** The last stage of the agreed plan, and the owner's `Q1 lib` answer made it two things rather than one: **`shared/charts` (`@gymdesk/charts`) is a new npm workspace** holding Apache ECharts behind one abstraction both apps consume, and the reading chart (§13–§17, §23–§24) is its first consumer on all three surfaces `Q3` names — My Goals, the Member card's PERSONAL GOALS section and the gym-wide Assigned Personal Goals list. The owner's instruction was explicit about the shape: ECharts is the engine, never used directly from a page, with a reusable layer over it so later charts in *either* app reuse the infrastructure rather than adding a second library or an inline SVG — so this is deliberately not "a chart for My Goals". **What the layer owns and what stays the app's** is the whole design: the package holds ECharts itself (`echartsRuntime.ts`, the only file in the repository that imports it, and only ever **dynamically**, because a client component is still evaluated during SSR and the `echarts` barrel pulls in every chart type the library has), the option a chart type is drawn from (`lineChartOption.ts`, pure), the colours (`chartTheme.ts`) and the canvas's lifetime (`EChartCanvas.tsx` — mount, resize, dispose, and a re-read when the Theme is written, since `ThemeProvider` applies its tokens in an effect that may land *after* the chart's); each app owns only the adapter that turns its rows into points, with its own locale and its own labels, and the layer resolves no locale key, formats no date and knows no entity. **Theming is the ticket's §23/§40 taken literally**: ECharts draws onto a canvas and cannot read a CSS variable, so each role is declared as the variable that holds it — the same `--gd-*` names `applyTokens()` writes in both apps — and resolved off the element the chart sits in, so a Center's own Theme reaches it; a value that resolves blank falls back rather than letting ECharts paint its own default, and no chart component spells a colour (the gate fails the build on a quoted hex, on a direct `echarts` import in either app and on a `t()` inside the layer). **Three chart decisions are rules rather than implementation**: the curve is `smooth` *and* `smoothMonotone: 'x'`, because a plain spline overshoots and an overshoot is §14's fake reading; a period boundary is a **colour change, not a gap** — `segmentPoints()` starts a new series per initial-reading period and bridges it with the previous period's own last reading, drawn with no symbol so one measurement is never reported twice; and the value axis is `scale: true`, since a zero-based axis flattens 80 → 75 kg into one line. The target is a dashed reference line in the muted text colour — a target is not a status, and an assignment with no target gets no line and no caption rather than one reading `Target —`. Zoom and pan are ECharts' own `inside` dataZoom (`Q1`'s interaction list) with the **wheel left to the page**, so a chart inside a scrolling card cannot trap the reader; the tooltip triggers on click as well as hover, so a tap works on a phone, and it prints the label and the value the *page* formatted. Nothing computed moved: the header's figures are still stage 2's, and a goal with no readings renders **no chart section at all** rather than an empty canvas, because the history beside it already says there are none. 35 new tests in the API suite (`charts-layer.unit.test.ts`, `goal-reading-chart.unit.test.ts` — there because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only, #1009's reason), covering the theme resolution and its fallbacks, the palette cycling rather than inventing a fifth colour, every option rule above, the tooltip's escaping, and the one thing no type checker says: that **both apps' adapters answer the same chart for the same rows**. No migration, no route and no API change, so `db-reviewer` and `test-writer` do not apply. `tsc --noEmit` clean in all three workspaces — which type-checks the shared package too, since both apps compile it through `transpilePackages` — and both `next build`s clean, with ECharts landing in its own lazy chunk rather than in either app's first load. Both `Dockerfile`s carry the new workspace.)

- **Done**: #1081 (**The payment return page follows the member's language, because the configured URL names none.** `PAYMENT_OK_URL` / `PAYMENT_KO_URL` are one deploy-time value for every member of every gym — the API cannot build them per member — so the fixed `https://members…/es/payment/success` they held landed an English- or Catalan-speaking member on the Spanish result page after paying. The environment variables were pointed at the locale-less `…/payment/success` / `…/payment/error` by hand on 2026-10-04 and the redirect was then exercised against the Members App's own next-intl configuration in isolation: cookie (`NEXT_LOCALE`) first, then `Accept-Language`, then `en`, with the **query string preserved**, which is what carries `purpose=card_update` to the page that polls `GET /me/payment-method` instead of the payment history (#788). So no behaviour needed changing; what this leaves is the half a deployment variable cannot hold. `api/.env.example` pointed at `/en/payment/...`, which is the one place a new environment is copied from, and the rule itself was written down nowhere — nothing at runtime notices a locale creeping back in, since the page renders perfectly well in one language. `api/src/test/payment-return-locale.unit.test.ts` is therefore the gate (in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only, #1009's reason): the two example URLs name no segment from the Members App's **own** language list (read from `lib/memberLocale.ts` rather than restated, #1039) and carry no query of their own, and the middleware keeps next-intl's locale detection on and still routes off that one list — switched off, every locale-less return would render in `defaultLocale` and the defect comes back one layer down. Two facts worth recording rather than re-deriving: a signed-in member whose stored `preferred_locale` (#1039) differs from the detected one is moved onto it by `MemberLocalePreference`, which the locale layout mounts over these two routes like any other, so the **stored** preference has the last word; and both pages stay deliberately outside `isPublicRoute`, because each polls an authenticated route and there is nothing to show a visitor with no session — sign-in-then-back lands on the locale-less path, which redirects again. Docs: `docs/payments.md` §A4 carries the rule, with the env table pointing at it, plus `docs/architecture.md` and `docs/go-to-production.md` §6. **Not verified here, and the ticket's remaining acceptance:** a real return from the hosted page including 3DS (so the cookie is confirmed to arrive on that cross-site top-level navigation), the `purpose=card_update` return still polling after the redirect, and the signed-out return path — all three need a deployed environment, and are a `docs/go-to-production.md` §6 item.)
  _(Issue #1081 is still open: the remaining stages are listed in `docs/roadmap.md`.)_

- **Done**: #1121 stage 1 of 5 (**My Membership becomes My Products and Services, with the gym's own Product catalogue read-only beneath it.** §1–§4 of the ticket; the purchase (§5/§6) is stage 2's and needs the `member_products` table the thread's `Q2` settles, Promotions on a purchase are #1118's stage 3, Membership Plans from the Members App #1122's stage 4 and the Payments redesign #1123's stage 5 — which has already shipped. Unblocked by the owner's `Q1`: which Products a member may be shown is the gym's **own two columns**, `status = 'active'` **and** `enrollment_status = 'public'`, so §7's "do not create a separate product catalogue for Members" is literally true — no new flag, no new column and **no migration**. Four of its answers are the design rather than the mechanics. **The predicate is one place**: `api/src/domain/memberProductCatalogue.ts`, pure, because stage 2 must validate a purchase against the very rule that decided what was offered — a member must not be able to buy what they cannot be shown. **A System item is not excluded**: `is_system` says a Product was seeded from `charge_types`, not that it is internal (a Locker Rental is a System row and a public one), and `mandatory` is a Membership Plan question (#832/#893) — so neither is filtered on, which would be the catalogue overruling what the gym configured. **The price is the server's one gross-up**, `computePriceFields()` with the stored amount as the fallback for a gym with no tax rate (`grossBenefitUnitPrice()`'s own rule), reported as a single `price_incl_tax` plus `tax_included`: a member is quoted what they pay, an unpriced item reads `—` rather than €0.00, and a Sessions package carries its `units` so the card can say *Total price for 10 sessions* — #942's rule, never a denominator. And **the catalogue it could not read is absent, not empty**: the route sits behind `member_web.my_membership` **and** `financials.products` (#1036's reason — a gym that switched Products off did not mean "and offer them to members anyway"), and a 403 renders no subsection rather than claiming the gym offers nothing. The Members App half is the two-module split #1123 already uses: `lib/memberProducts.ts` decides (the frequency **key** from the one `membership.frequency` map, #1128; the package sentence; the tax note) and `components/MemberProductsSection.tsx` draws, so no `t()` in the first and no colour outside `memberChrome.ts` (#983) in the second. A frequency that names no period — `once`, a legacy `per_session` — gets no suffix, because `€50 / Once` describes nothing. `membership.title` and the unused `nav.membership` both read *My Products and Services* / *Mis Productos y Servicios* / *Els Meus Productes i Serveis*; the route `/membership`, the flag `member_web.my_membership` and every identifier are untouched (a label never renames an identifier — #1026/#970), and the dashboard card keeps its own `home.products_services` key (#1116). Tests: `api/src/test/member-products.unit.test.ts` (new, 28 — both pure halves, the copy, and the route's two flags, which a platform-wide `feature_flags` row cannot be toggled for in an integration test) and `api/src/test/me-products.test.ts` (new integration: auth, role, the three exclusions, a public System item, cross-gym isolation, an empty catalogue, the VAT-inclusive price with and without a rate, an unpriced item, a Sessions package, and the absence of any purchase state). Both in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only (#1009's reason). Green: API unit suites 88 files / 1,339 tests, Members App suite 20 files / 435 tests, `tsc --noEmit` clean in `api` and `apps/member`, `next build` clean. The API integration suite was not executed here — this container has no MySQL and no Docker daemon — so CI is its first run. Docs: `CLAUDE.md` (the new rule), `docs/architecture.md`. **Deliberately out of scope**: any Buy action or purchase row, a `member_products` table, Promotions on a purchase, the Admin-side rename of *Additional Products* (#1118 §11), and any change to what is billed.)
  _(Issue #1121 is still open: the remaining stages are listed in `docs/roadmap.md`.)_

- **Done**: #1121 stage 2 of 5 (**A member buys a Product, over one purchase table and the existing payment infrastructure.** §5/§6 of the ticket, unblocked by the owner's `Q2` (a new `member_products` table holding "a snapshot of what has been purchased while maintaining a link against the original product") and `Q3` (self-service, no staff approval; a one-off is prompted to pay). Migration 228 creates that table, widens `chk_payment_requests_source` with **`product_purchase`** (its fifth widening, after 111/165/195) and makes `payment_requests.user_membership_id` **nullable** — a purchase belongs to the member, and under #956 they may hold no plan at all, so hanging the row off "their most recent assignment" the way a card update does would make the shop 400 for exactly the members most likely to use it. Five of its answers are the design rather than the mechanics. **The purchase is read through the catalogue's own predicate**, which is why stage 1 made `memberProductCatalogueSql()` a fragment rather than a query: a `staff_only`, inactive or deleted Product is a 404 on `POST /me/products/:id/purchase` exactly as it is absent from `GET /me/products`, because a member must not be able to buy what they cannot be shown. **Stage 2 buys a one-off**: `Q3`'s recurring half ("create or update the billing event plan for such member") is a second recurring schedule beside `user_memberships.next_billing_date` — a change to what the nightly run charges rather than a shop — so a recurring Product answers `recurring_not_supported` and the Members App renders **no Buy action at all** for it (#1073: a control that cannot work is absent, never broken); `isRecurringFrequency()` (#550) decides, never a second list. **The payment is what grants it**: one transaction writes the `payment_requests` row and the `pending_payment` purchase, the member is handed the hosted-page URL, and the webhook's own branch completes it — so a closed tab loses nothing and a retried delivery completes one row (`UNIQUE (payment_request_id)` plus the `UPDATE`'s own `status = 'pending_payment'`, which is #1118 §10's idempotency without a flag). That branch writes a `payment_recorded` Billing Event with **no** `user_membership_id` (money did arrive, so it belongs in the ledger both the member's Payments card and the staff pages read) and deliberately stores **no card**, stamps **no** `next_billing_date` and clears **none** of #785's dunning counters: a one-off authorises one charge, and a rejected membership cycle is still owed after a member buys a locker. **Only a pending purchase blocks a second one** (`mprod_pending_purchase_key`, UNIQUE while `pending_payment`, migration 183's generated-column device) — an `active` one does not, because nothing in `products` says an item may be bought once and a second session package months later is a real purchase — and `POST /billing/cleanup` gained a third statement (`cancelAbandonedPurchases()`) so a purchase left pending by a missed webhook cannot lock that member out of that Product for ever. And **the hosted page words it as a one-off**: `purpose: 'product_purchase'` is derived from the source rather than from the amount, carrying the snapshot's item name, so its consent sentence does not promise a recurring charge; `GET /members`' `payment_status` and the member's own Pay-now prompt exclude the source, because that column and that prompt are about the **fee**. The Members App half stays stage 1's split — `lib/memberProducts.ts` decides (the Buy action's existence, the pill's status word, the refusal's locale key) and `components/MemberProductsSection.tsx` draws it as one `action` slot — with the confirmation in the app's own `MemberDialog` (#1115) showing the final VAT-inclusive price before the member is handed to `pay.<host>`. Tests: `api/src/test/member-product-purchase.unit.test.ts` (new, 35 — the pure rules, the vocabulary against migration 228's CHECKs, and the handful of money-path lines whose loss would be invisible: no card, no billing date, no dunning clear, the fee-status exclusions, the hosted page's wording) and `api/src/test/me-products.test.ts` (extended: the purchase happy path with its snapshot and its cents-at-the-boundary amount, a frozen snapshot after the Product is renamed and repriced, the 409 duplicate, the recurring and unpriced refusals, 404 for a hidden and for another gym's Product, 401/403, the webhook completing once and writing one Billing Event with no card stored, a failed payment cancelling the purchase and freeing the retry, and the hosted page loading a purchase's token). The migration was reviewed by the `db-reviewer` agent: no BLOCKERs, and its four warnings are applied (`down()` drops the table last and counts the purchases themselves, an index for the sweep's own `status` read, and the three FK-backing keys declared rather than left to InnoDB). Green: API unit suites 90 files / 1,398 tests, the Members App's own 20 files / 435 tests, `tsc --noEmit` clean in `api` and `apps/member`, `next build` clean. The API integration suite was not executed here — this container has no MySQL and no Docker daemon — so CI is its first run. Docs: `CLAUDE.md` (the new rule), `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/payments.md` (§D and the data model). **Deliberately out of scope**: a recurring purchase and its schedule, Promotions on a purchase (#1118 stage 3), the Admin-side view of a member's purchases (#1118 §12), a refund or cancellation path, and any change to what the nightly run charges.)
  _(Issue #1121 is still open: the remaining stages are listed in `docs/roadmap.md`.)_

- **Done**: #1076 (**Mobile app WP4 — an invitation link opens the app.** The fourth work package of the mobile app plan (`docs/mobile-app.md`, decision #18). A universal link works only when **two halves** line up — the domain publishes a file naming the app, and the app declares the domain — so this is one place per half. **The web half** is two route handlers in `apps/member` (`app/api/well-known/apple-app-site-association/route.ts`, `…/well-known/assetlinks/route.ts`), both `force-dynamic`, over the pure `lib/appAssociations.ts` (what the files say) and `lib/wellKnownResponse.ts` (how they are served); `next.config.js` maps the canonical `/.well-known/…` paths onto them with a **rewrite**, because Apple refuses an association file reached through a redirect and a rewrite keeps the `200` on the URL iOS asked for, and the middleware's existing matcher excludes anything containing a dot so neither path meets Clerk or the locale redirect (the gate asserts that *regex*, not the comment claiming it — Apple and Google fetch these with no session and no `Accept-Language`). Five decisions beyond the bullet list. **What a build is for is one variable keyed by app id**: `MOBILE_APP_ASSOCIATIONS` carries each app's Apple Team ID and Android SHA-256 signing fingerprints, in `FCM_SERVICE_ACCOUNTS`' shape and for its reason — a stage-2 per-gym app is a new key, never a code change — as environment rather than a profile field, because the Members App's Docker build copies `apps/member` and `shared` only and cannot see `apps/mobile/profiles/`, and because a Team ID and a release certificate belong to the store account rather than to the shell's source. **A malformed entry is dropped, logged and never published**, and one app's typo costs that app alone: these files are CDN-cached for a day, so a wrong `appID` or a SHA-1 where a SHA-256 belongs claims an association that silently cannot work, which is worse than claiming none. **A file with nothing to say is a `404`**, not a `200` carrying an empty `details` array, which reads as "configured, associates nothing" — WP2's "a native control that cannot work is absent, never broken" one layer down. **The native half is written by `npm run profile:apply`**, still the only writer of a native identity: the `associated-domains` entitlement on iOS and `@string/app_link_host` for an `autoVerify` App Links `intent-filter` on Android, both on the host **derived** from the profile's own `serverUrl`, since that deployment is what serves the files — ticking *Associated Domains* in Xcode instead would carry one gym's host into every other profile's build. And **which paths open the app is one declaration in two syntaxes**: `APP_LINK_PATH_SEGMENT` gives the Apple component (`/*/link`) and the Android `pathPattern` (`/.*/link`), the app claims the invitation path and **not** the whole domain (a filter over `/*` would swallow the OAuth and Clerk redirects a sign-in bounces through), and `api/src/test/mobile-app-links.unit.test.ts` keeps the committed manifest and entitlement equal to it because `apps/mobile` cannot import that module. No migration, no API route, no locale key, and no second link rule — WP2's `appUrlOpenPath()` already reads an `https` URL, which is what a universal link arrives as, and the custom URL scheme stays registered for a link a universal link misses. With the variable unset both files 404, verification fails, and a tapped invitation opens in the browser and completes there, which is today's behaviour exactly. Docs: `docs/mobile-app.md` WP4 marked done with what it decided and the known in-app-browser caveat, `docs/mobile-runbook.md` §2 and four new §5 device checks, `docs/go-to-production.md` §6, `docs/architecture.md` and a `CLAUDE.md` constraint. Tests: `apps/member/src/test/app-associations.test.ts` (23), the WP3 suite extended for the derived host and the two new transforms (27), and `api/src/test/mobile-app-links.unit.test.ts` (11, in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only); both files were also fetched over a real `next start` — `200` + `application/json` with the variable set, `404` without it, no redirect either way. **Not verified: anything needing a device** — no build has been made, so no install has verified an `assetlinks.json` and no link has been tapped in Mail or Notes; the Team ID, the Play App Signing fingerprint and the *Associated Domains* capability on the App ID are `docs/go-to-production.md` §6 items.)

- **Done**: #1083 (**Monei's webhook arrives through the payment app, so the provider needs no public API.** `PAYMENT_NOTIFICATION_URL` already points at `https://pay.…/webhooks/payment` in the GitHub `dev` and `pro` environments, and it reaches the API on its next `deploy.yml` run — so the relay had to exist before the next API deploy or Monei's webhooks would 404 on the payment host and payments would stop being confirmed. The ticket left one thing to decide — what runtime serves a server-side endpoint in a container that is static HTML/JS — and the answer is **nginx, which is already that container's runtime and already proxies `/payment-page/` and `/themes/` to the API for the browser**: one `location = /webhooks/payment` block, no Node process, no dependency, no secret, and nothing that parses and re-serializes the body (which is what would break the HMAC the API verifies over the exact bytes Monei signed). Three of its properties are the design rather than the mechanics. **The relay verifies nothing**: body and signature header are relayed byte for byte, `parseWebhook()` is still the route's first operation and `MONEI_WEBHOOK_SECRET` never leaves the API, so a tampered body is still a 400 *from the API*, relayed as such. **Monei gets the API's own status code** — `proxy_intercept_errors` stays off and the block carries no `return` and no `error_page`, because a blanket 200 loses a confirmation (Monei retries exactly what we report as failed) and an unreachable or slow API is a 502/504, which is also a retry. And **the 60/min budget is keyed on the paying client, not on the relay**: this is the one route sitting behind one more proxy than `trust proxy` accounts for, so `paymentWebhookClientKey()` (`api/src/domain/forwardedClient.ts`, which also becomes the one place `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` is read) adds `PAYMENT_WEBHOOK_RELAY_HOPS` to it and answers the client — keyed on the relay, one gym's payments spend every gym's budget. That hop is declared **per route and defaults to 0** (where the key is `req.ip`, exactly as before) rather than by raising `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` globally, which would make Express trust one more caller-supplied `X-Forwarded-For` entry on every route that is still publicly reachable — and the API is not private yet (#1087 is that open question). Both API origins the image talks to are configuration now (`CORDEL_FITNESS_API_PUBLIC_URL` for the browser-facing proxies, `CORDEL_FITNESS_API_INTERNAL_URL` for the relay) because one image tag serves dev and pro: the config became an `envsubst` template the official nginx image substitutes at start, with the dev values as image defaults so a container started with no environment behaves exactly as before — which also fixes a latent bug, since the hardcoded `api.vdicube.com` would have had a pro payment container proxying token lookups to the dev API. The container's own access log drops query strings (`log_format pay_no_query`), which is what the PCI note on this host already required for `?token=<page_token>`, and `deploy-payment.yml` now probes the relay with the same `GET` Monei's dashboard preflights with, so a wrong internal address fails the deploy instead of losing confirmations quietly. No migration, no new API route and no change to any webhook branch. Tests: `api/src/test/payment-webhook-relay.unit.test.ts` (new, 21 — the hop parsing and its fallbacks, the chain resolution against Express's own rule including a chain shorter than the hop count, the key with and without a relay, and a config gate that scans the template, the Dockerfile and the quadlet: no origin spelled, both defaults present, no status interception, no body-rewriting directive, the timeouts, the forwarded header, and no query string in the log format) and `api/src/test/payment-webhook-relay.test.ts` (new, 3 integration — two clients behind one relay get independent 60/min budgets with the hop declared, and share one without it, which is the collapse the setting exists to undo). `tsc --noEmit` clean. **Not verified here:** nothing in this container runs nginx or Podman, so the relay has not been exercised against a real Monei delivery — the acceptance criterion is a sandbox payment on dev, and the two owner settings are `docs/go-to-production.md` §6. **Not covered:** Clerk's webhook (#1085) and the GitHub Actions nightly runs (#1086) still call the API directly, so this does not yet make the API private.)

- **Done**: #1086 (**The nightly runs arrive through the admin app, so GitHub's runners need no public API.** The third and last of the callers #1083 began on, and the one with no signature to verify: `.github/workflows/billing-run.yml` and `recurring-booking-run.yml` keep calling `$API_BASE_URL/<path>` unchanged, because `API_BASE_URL` in the GitHub `dev` and `pro` environments is the admin app's relay prefix — repointed at `https://admin.<host>/api/internal` on 2026-10-05, which also means **the scheduled runs fail on both environments until an admin app carrying this route is deployed**. `/api/proxy` is deliberately not reusable (its header set is `authorization`/`x-gym-id`/`x-center-id`/`x-impersonate-as`/`x-locale` and **not** `x-internal-secret`, so a run through it would arrive unauthenticated, and widening it would put the internal runners behind a route the browser app also uses), so the relay is its own route over a pure decision module (`apps/admin/src/lib/internalRunRelay.ts`) plus a transport module (`lib/internalRunUpstream.ts`), the WP2/WP3/#1085 split with one extra half for the reason below. Five of its properties are the design rather than the mechanics. **It carries an explicit path allowlist** — `/billing/run`, `/billing/cleanup`, `/promotion-lifecycle/run`, `/recurring-bookings/run`, matched on the whole joined path so neither a prefix nor a traversal nor a case variant resolves to one — because this prefix is public and anything else under it is a `404` from the relay rather than a request the API has to judge (the gate also asserts that set *is* what the two workflows call, so a fifth run cannot be added to one and forgotten in the other). **It authenticates nothing**: neither internal secret is named anywhere in the admin app, `checkInternalSecret()` still answers the `401` — relayed as such, and still spending #783's failed-secret budget. **The workflow gets the API's own status and body, byte for byte**, because both workflows fail on a non-2xx *and* parse the counters out of the body (#778); the three statuses that are the relay's own are a `404` (outside the allowlist), `502`/`504` (unreachable, or outlived its timeout) and `500` (no `CORDEL_FITNESS_API_URL`), and never a 200, which would read as an unreadable body instead of as the API being out of reach. **The upstream call is `node:http`, not `fetch`** — that is the third module: Node's global fetch abandons a response whose headers take more than 300 s and `POST /recurring-bookings/run` is allowed 600 s, so a long night would have been cut off at five minutes, and a run the API completed but the workflow saw as failed is the worst outcome available here (#780's guard then refuses the retry as `already_completed_today`); the relay's own wait is `INTERNAL_RUN_RELAY_TIMEOUT_MS`, default 660 000 ms, deliberately above the longest `--max-time` in the workflows so `curl` is always the first to give up. And **the internal-run budget is keyed on the client, not on the relay**: `internalRunClientKey()` (`api/src/domain/forwardedClient.ts`) adds `INTERNAL_RUN_RELAY_HOPS` to `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` and reads the forwarded chain inwards from the socket, so ten wrong-secret guesses from anywhere cannot answer the nightly run `429` for the rest of the window — only a 401 spends that budget, which is what makes keying it on the relay cheap to attack. The hop is declared per route and defaults to 0 (where the key is `req.ip`, exactly as before) rather than by raising `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` globally, for #1083's reason. The path is exempt from `auth.protect()` **and** from the locale routing, through the same early `return NextResponse.next()` the other two relays use. No migration and no new API route; the workflows changed only in their comments and two error hints, since the one thing the ticket asked of them — the URL — is a GitHub variable. Tests: `api/src/test/internal-run-relay.unit.test.ts` (new, 32 — the cross-app gate, in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only: the route exists on admin and not on member, POST alone, `force-dynamic` and the Node runtime, the allowlist equal to what the workflows call and decided in the module rather than the route, no origin spelled, the forwarded set and none of `/api/proxy`'s, bytes and never `req.text()`, `res.status` relayed with no 200 anywhere, the three failure statuses, neither secret named, the decision half pure, no `fetch` in either I/O half with a timeout above the longest `--max-time`, the middleware exempting the prefix from both guards, the three mounts still behind `internalRunLimiter`, and `internalRunClientKey` itself) and `api/src/test/internal-run-relay.test.ts` (new, 2 integration — one client's budget spent behind a relay leaves another's alone with the hop declared, and both collapse into one bucket without it, which is the collapse the setting exists to undo), plus three admin files: `internal-run-relay.test.ts` (new, 14 — the allowlist including prefixes, traversals and a case variant, the target with and without trailing slashes, `null` for an unset base, the header filter, and the timeout's fallbacks), `internal-run-relay-route.test.ts` (new, 12 — the route against a stubbed transport: the URL, the forwarded set, a body of bytes, the configured timeout, a relayed 200/401/429, the 404 and 500 that never call the API, and the 502/504 split) and `internal-run-upstream.test.ts` (new, 5 — the transport against a real local server: method, path, headers, `content-length`, a non-2xx relayed rather than thrown, a body that is **not** valid UTF-8 arriving byte-identical, the timeout error and a refused connection). `tsc --noEmit` clean in both `api/` and `apps/admin`; the API's 79 unit files (1185 tests) and the admin's 155 (2431) are green. The API's own integration suite was not executed here (no MySQL and no Docker daemon in this environment, so CI is its first run) — including this ticket's own `internal-run-relay.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/decisions.md` #21, `docs/architecture.md` (*Internal run auth*, the tree, the `API_BASE_URL` row), `docs/payments.md` §B1 and its env table, `docs/go-to-production.md` §4 and §4b, `README.md` and both `.env.example`s. **Not verified here, and the ticket's acceptance:** a dispatched `billing-run.yml` against dev through the admin host — that needs the admin app deployed, and with it the two owner decisions in §4b (whether `INTERNAL_RUN_RELAY_HOPS` has to be `1` on that deployment, and Traefik's own timeout on the admin host against the 600 s run). **Not covered:** whether the API's host is then actually closed off is #1087's question, which this ticket does not answer.)

- **Done**: #1114 (**The browser title names the application and the environment, from one module per app.** Both apps titled every tab `Gymdesk` — the same string in both front ends and in both environments — so two tabs open on dev and pro were indistinguishable, and the product's own name was not in it. The titles are now `(Dev) Admin - Cordel.tech Fitness` / `(Dev) Members - Cordel.tech Fitness` on development and `Admin - Cordel.tech` / `Members - Cordel.tech` on production, composed in `apps/admin/src/lib/appTitle.ts` and `apps/member/src/lib/appTitle.ts` (one per app, since the two share no frontend module) as `(<label>) <role> - <brand>`. Three of its properties are the design rather than the mechanics. **Only the role is code**: `APP_ROLE` is each app's one literal, because which front end you are looking at is not configuration, while the environment label and the brand are deployment facts and travel the whole chain — GitHub environment *variables* (`vars.APP_ENV_LABEL`, `vars.APP_BRAND_NAME`, not secrets) → the two deploy workflows' `build-args` → `ARG`/`ENV` in each Dockerfile → the bundle. **A missing value is never invented**: no brand configured answers the role alone (`Admin`, `Members`), never a default brand, because defaulting to the development one would label a production build `Cordel.tech Fitness` and defaulting to the production one would hide that a deployment forgot the variable; no label answers no prefix, which is exactly what production is. So until the two variables are set in the `dev` and `production` environments the titles read `Admin` / `Members` — correct and unbranded, never the retired name. And **the composition is pure** (`appTitle(env)` takes its environment as an argument, with `publicAppTitle()` beside it as the one `process.env` read), which is why the four titles the ticket names are asserted directly rather than inferred from a rendered page. They are `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` for the reason the Google client ids are (#1073): the title is read in a client component too — the admin's signed-out sign-in card and `TopHeader`'s gym-name slot, the two other places the retired name was on screen. The Members App's **installed** name is deliberately untouched: that is `public/manifest.json` for the PWA and the mobile profile for a store build (#1074), neither of which is a browser tab or carries an environment label. No migration, no API change, no locale key (the title names an application and an environment, not anything read in a language) and no functional change. Tests: `api/src/test/app-title.unit.test.ts` (new, 20 — the cross-app gate, in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only: each module's one role literal, the same two variable names in both, the static `process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_*` members Next can substitute, the pure signature, the layout titling from it with no literal left, the build args defaulted to empty rather than to a brand, the workflow passing them from the environment, the `.env.example` development values, and no `gymdesk` spelling left in either app's `src` outside `@gymdesk/*` specifiers) plus `apps/admin/src/test/app-title.test.ts` and `apps/member/src/test/app-title.test.ts` (new, 8 each — the four titles, an arbitrary label, blank-as-unset, the role-alone fallback and a label that already carries its own parentheses). `tsc --noEmit` clean in both apps; the admin's 156 test files (2439 tests) and the member's 20 (422) are green, as are the API's 80 unit files (1205). The API's integration suite was not executed here (no MySQL and no Docker daemon in this environment, so CI is its first run) — nothing in this ticket touches API code. Docs: `docs/architecture.md` (the environment-scoped table) and `docs/go-to-production.md` §1. **Not verified here:** the deployed titles, which need the two variables set per environment and a rebuild — that is the §1 item.)

- **Done**: #1115 (**Personal Goals are cards in the Members App, created and edited inside them.** A Members-App-only change: no migration, no route, no payload field and no business rule — what moved is where the member reads a goal and where they type one. Each live goal is an individual **card**, collapsed by default, whose header carries the name over `Target: 70 kg · 65%` and whose body is what #1037 stages 3 and 4 already render (the five structured fields, the notes, the chart, the reading history). `components/MemberGoalCard.tsx` is the one place that card's shape lives, and like `GoalReadings.tsx` and `NutritionItemRow` it decides **nothing**: the title, the summary and every menu label arrive already resolved and formatted, so it calls no `t()`, reads no row and spells no colour (#983). Four of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **The header is one button and the `⋮` is another**, siblings rather than nested — §4 requires that tapping the menu neither expands nor collapses the card, and a nested button is invalid HTML while `stopPropagation()` is a rule a later edit can forget. **Expanding is presentation and fetches nothing** (#955's rule), so the reading histories still come with the page rather than with the first tap. **Creating and editing are inline**: `+ Add personal goal` (and the empty state's CTA, §6) opens a draft card at the top of the section with today already in **Start date**, `⋮ → Edit` turns that card's body into the same form, Cancel takes the draft away and leaves an edited card exactly as it was, and one `goalForm(mode)` renders both so the two cannot drift apart — which leaves the screen with exactly **two** dialogs, the ones that are not a form over a goal's own fields (#1037's Add reading and the removal confirmation, both still `MemberDialog`'s). And **the unit is read-only** (§1): it belongs to the Gym Goal, so it reads as a value beside the target exactly as the Add reading dialog shows it, and a target the member clears takes it with it — `toGoalUpdatePayload()` drops a unit qualifying nothing, which is what `chk_mpgoal_target_unit` refuses and what the member now has no field to repair; `goalFormError()`'s unit-without-value refusal is gone with it, since that state is no longer a mistake they can make. #1037 stage 3's two reading actions move into the same `⋮` (Add reading · Change initial reading · Edit · Remove), so every write stays reachable with every card closed; its placement rule was always "each surface's own", and this is that surface's. The five-field header stays the one header (#1037's rule) and the history the one history — nothing was re-rendered a second way. Past Goals are untouched: read-only cards with no affordance, as they have been. Locales: `add` becomes *Add personal goal* / *Añadir objetivo personal* / *Afegir objectiu personal*, plus `summary_target` and `menu_label` in en/es/ca; `edit_title` and `error_unit_without_value` are dropped, both now unreachable. Tests: `api/src/test/member-goal-cards.unit.test.ts` (new, 11 — in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only, #1009's reason: both forms inline, exactly the two non-form dialogs, the card's `aria-expanded`/`aria-haspopup`/`role="menuitem"` and the menu as a sibling of the toggle, no `t()` and no hex in it, collapsed by default with no fetch on expand, the draft's date, the picker preserving what was typed, the read-only unit and its payload, the summary joiner and locale parity) and `apps/member/src/test/my-goals.test.ts` (+12, with three pre-existing assertions rewritten to the new shape rather than deleted: the dialog count, the retired unit refusal and the slice that located the old Add/Edit dialog). Green: member suite 20 files / 434 tests, API unit suites 81 files / 1,216 tests, `tsc --noEmit` clean in both workspaces. The API integration tests were not executed here — this container has no MySQL and no Docker daemon — and nothing in this ticket touches the API, so they run first in CI. **Deliberately out of scope**: the progress chart itself and the completion percentage, which §Out-of-scope excludes and #1037 already ships.)

- **Done**: #1116 (**My Membership becomes My Products & Services, and moves into the dashboard's tile grid.** A Members-App-only change: no migration, no route, no payload field, no business rule and no API call — what moved is a card's title and the cell it sits in. The dashboard card is renamed **My Products & Services** / *Mis Productos y Servicios* / *Els Meus Productes i Serveis* (the admin Member card's own `tab_products_services` wording, so the two surfaces name the same thing the same way, #949's Producto/Producte rule included) and it is now the last **cell of the one `tileGrid`** the five navigation tiles already live in, after My Goals — which is what §2's mock asks for: the same width, the same 12px gap, grid `stretch` for the same height, and the row is `1fr 1fr` exactly as it was, so no other tile moved. Three of its properties are the design rather than the mechanics. **The title is a key of its own** (`home.products_services`): the card had been rendering `membership.title`, which is also the `/membership` page's `<h1>`, so renaming that key would have renamed a screen §4 does not put in scope — the page still reads *My membership*, and whether it follows is a product call this ticket leaves open (noted on the thread). **The grid is the layout**: the card declares no width, no `minHeight`, no `gridColumn`, no second grid container and no media query — the Members App has no stylesheet to put one in (#983), and a breakpoint for one cell would be a second layout rule for the dashboard — so §3's stacking is the grid's own behaviour and nothing can introduce horizontal scrolling. And **its body is a column, which is the only thing about its contents that changed**: the title, the plan name, the `· Expires …` / `· Ongoing` tail, the `No active membership.` empty line and the Active badge are the same values from the same `/me/membership` read, but the badge now sits at the bottom right (`marginTop: 'auto'`) as §2's mock draws it, rather than beside a title that wraps to three lines in a half-width cell. The artwork slot (`membership`), the `member_web.my_membership` flag, the `/membership` destination and `statusPillStyle(statusTone(...))` are untouched, and the page still carries exactly six themed slots. Tests: `api/src/test/member-dashboard-products-card.unit.test.ts` (new, 10 — in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only, #1009's reason: the card's own key and its three locale values, `membership.title` gone from the dashboard, the card inside the grid and after My Goals, nothing left between the grid and `</main>`, the one two-column grid with no `@media`, no width/minHeight/gridColumn on its style, the six slots, the flag, the destination, the contents, the shared status tone and no colour literal). Green: API unit suites 82 files / 1,226 tests, the Members App's own 20 files / 434 tests, `tsc --noEmit` clean in `apps/member`. The API integration suite was not executed here — this container has no MySQL and no Docker daemon — and nothing in this ticket touches API code, so CI is its first run. Docs: `CLAUDE.md` (the Members App images bullet, which already owns the home tiles). **Deliberately out of scope**: the `/membership` page's own heading and `nav.membership`, and the contents of the card, which §4 freezes — adding Products & Services to that page is #1121's.)

- **Done**: #1113 (**No alert for a member's own booking, and a 2-hour training reminder instead.** Two halves of one rule: §4's distinction is *who acted*, applied where a notification is **generated** rather than by hiding a row in the Alerts page. §1/§7 — `POST /me/bookings` raised `booking_confirmed` / `waitlist_joined` fire-and-forget for the member who had just tapped Book, and now raises nothing at all; the response is their confirmation. Cancelling and leaving a waiting list are the same route and already behaved that way (`DELETE /me/bookings/:id` alerts only the member it *promotes*, which is not their own action), and nothing on the staff side moves — `POST /bookings` with `waitlist: true`, the staff `DELETE`'s `waitlist_removed`, #979's `event_reactivated` and #980's `waitlist_closed` are all news to the member and all untouched. §2–§6 — the reminder is `booking_reminder_2h` (migration 226, in the `NotificationType` union **and** `chk_member_notifications_type`, the fourth swap of that CHECK), a **third** reminder value rather than a reuse of migration 087's `booking_reminder_24h` / `booking_reminder_1h`, which nothing has ever written and whose copy says *tomorrow* and *starting soon*; those stay declared and unwritten, which the CHECK permits (it may be a superset of the union, never a subset). `api/src/domain/bookingReminders.ts` is the one place that decides what a reminder is — the type, the lead time, who is owed one and what the payload says — and it is pure, with `api/src/api/booking-reminders.ts` as the I/O half. Three of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **Nothing stores that a reminder was sent**: the dedupe is the `member_notifications` row itself (#647 stage 4's device, and why the insert is `recordNotifications()` rather than fire-and-forget), so §5 is a `NOT EXISTS` and §6 falls out of it — a cancelled booking stops being a candidate and the replacement occurrence is owed its own alert, with no `reminder_sent_at` column that could disagree with the row. **The window is "within two hours", not "exactly at two hours"**, because a scheduled runner fires late: 115–120 minutes would silently drop every member whose class fell between two passes, so a late pass costs punctuality rather than the alert. And **the lead time is a declared constant, not configuration** — the member reads *in 2 hours* in their own language, so the copy and the number have to move together. `POST /booking-reminders/run` is mounted beside the other internal runners, with its **own** secret (`BOOKING_REMINDERS_INTERNAL_SECRET`) per the rule `promotion-lifecycle.ts` states, and claims **no** run-log slot: #780's guard is one completed run per UTC date and would silence every pass but the first of the day — this is the one scheduled run that is not nightly. `.github/workflows/booking-reminder-run.yml` fires it every 15 minutes with `concurrency` so two passes cannot overlap, and `/booking-reminders/run` joins #1086's relay allowlist (which is about which paths may be forwarded, never how often). Migration 226 also adds the **two** indexes the run needs, because it executes every quarter of an hour across every gym and neither half may be a scan: `mn_reminder_dedupe_idx` for the `NOT EXISTS` (087's only index narrows to the member and then scans their history; this one's order keeps it off that prefix, since every predicate is equality) and `ce_status_starts_idx (status, starts_at)` for the **candidate** half, which the `db-reviewer` pass is what caught — the query is deliberately cross-gym, so it carries no `gym_id` predicate and every `calendar_events` index is `gym_id`-leading, and without it each pass full-scanned one of the largest tables in the schema; with it the booking side is already covered by `ceb_gym_event_idx`. The first one's write amplification on an append-only log is the stated trade rather than an oversight. Members App: `type_booking_reminder_2h` + `detail_booking_reminder_2h` in en/es/ca and the type in the Alerts page's `DETAIL_TYPES` — the payload carries the occurrence's own title and `starts_at` and no sentence, since the copy is where every other alert's is. Tests: `api/src/test/booking-reminders.unit.test.ts` (new, 34 — in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only, #1009's reason: the type in both places and the CHECK still a superset, the lead-time constant, the pure decision half, every condition of the candidate query including the no-lower-bound window and the absent `reminder_sent_at`, the payload's two keys and its id coercion, the gym grouping, the run's own secret, its mount, the absent run-log/audit/feature-flag, the awaited insert, the workflow's cadence, path, counters and concurrency, both removed alerts asserted on source — they were fire-and-forget, so an HTTP assertion would pass for the wrong reason — the staff alerts left in place, and locale parity) and `api/src/test/booking-reminders.test.ts` (new, 16 integration: the auth matrix including that the billing secret is refused, the alert and its payload, three passes raising one row, an event entering the window, one already started, a cancelled booking, a waitlisted member, every non-`scheduled` and the deleted occurrence, a soft-deleted member, two members on one class, §6's replaced booking, two gyms, and the reported counters). One thing only CI could find, and it is why the gate above it exists: the candidate query bound its cap as `LIMIT ?`, which mysql2's `execute()` refuses, so **every pass answered 500** and the only visible symptom was that no reminders were created — `training-plan-templates.ts` and `gym-training-plans.ts` have carried the reason in a comment since they were written (*"interpolated because mysql2 prepared statements don't accept placeholders in LIMIT reliably"*) and nothing said so to a new query. The cap is written into the statement now, after `limitClause()` asserts it is a positive integer, the interval is `DATE_ADD` like every other one here, and `api/src/test/bound-limit-placeholder.unit.test.ts` is the repo-wide gate (#1009's rule: a defect class gets a gate, not a point fix) — verified by reverting the SQL and watching it fail. Green: API unit suites 84 files / 1,264 tests, admin 156 / 2,439, member 20 / 434, `tsc --noEmit` clean in all three workspaces. The API **integration** suite was not executed here — this container has no MySQL and no Docker daemon — so CI is its first run. `db-reviewer` ran on migration 226. Docs: `CLAUDE.md` (the new constraint), `docs/architecture.md` (the runner's endpoint row, a *Booking alerts* section and the internal-run auth/relay paragraphs), `docs/go-to-production.md` (§1's migration window for 226 and §4b's secret + first-day checklist) and `api/.env.example`. **Deliberately out of scope**: a 24-hour or 1-hour reminder (the two retired types stay unwritten), a reminder for a waitlisted member, the event's location in the alert, and any change to the Alerts page's own UI, which §7 freezes.)

- **Done**: #1128 (**One vocabulary for a billing frequency, across Products and Membership Plans.** A display-only change: no migration, no accepted set, no API change and no stored value touched — what moved is the words. A Product's Billing Frequency was labelled in **five** namespaces and read four ways: the same monthly item said `Month` on the Products page, `Month(s)` in a Membership Plan's benefit row and in a Promotion's, and `Monthly` on an Assigned Plan card, while a Plan billed every four weeks said `4 Weeks` beside Products that spelled that period three other ways, and an Assigned Plan's frozen cadence printed the stored pair itself (`1 / month` on the card, `1 × Month` in its configuration). The labels are now one namespace — `billing_frequency` in en/es/ca — resolved through one module, `apps/admin/src/lib/billingFrequency.ts`: **Once · Every 4 weeks · Monthly · Yearly**, exactly the ticket's table. Four of its answers are the design rather than the mechanics. **It is not an accepted set**: `week` (#821) and `per_session` (#945) are still stored, still billed and still displayed wherever a gym configured them, so they keep a label — one label instead of four — and nothing about them becomes selectable again; `POST`/`PUT /products` still refuses them through `productFrequencyWriteError()`, untouched. **A Membership Plan has no frequency terminology of its own** (§"should not have its own alternative frequency terminology"), so the Plan's Billing frequency dropdown, its read-only row, its Details modal and an Assigned Plan's frozen cadence all read from that same table — which is why the cadence pair, its match and `legacyCadenceText()` moved into that module and `plans/planProfile.ts` re-exports them: a card under `components/assignedPlan/` cannot import a page module, and `cadenceFrequencyLabel()` is what replaced the two pair-printing sites. **A Session Benefit's Frequency is a different question** (#918 — how often an allowance renews, its own set, `Weekly` among them) and is deliberately untouched; so is the **period noun** a duration is counted in, which keeps its own `plans.period_unit_*` keys because `2 × 4 Weeks` (#892 §9) must not become `2 × Every 4 weeks`. And **the Members App says the same words**: the two apps share no frontend module, so it keeps its own map and `One-off` becomes `Once`, with the gate asserting the two agree value by value. Tests: `api/src/test/billing-frequency-labels.unit.test.ts` (new, 12 — in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only, #1009's reason: the module's list equal to the API's stored set, a label for every value in all three locales, the four English words, **no** namespace carrying a second spelling, no surface interpolating a frequency into a key of its own outside that module, the three Assigned Plan surfaces naming the cadence rather than printing the pair, the Session Benefit set intact and the two apps in step) plus nine admin suites re-pointed at the shared namespace rather than deleted. Green: admin 156 files / 2,439 tests, member 20 / 434, API unit suites 83 / 1,238, `tsc --noEmit` clean in both apps. The API integration suite was not executed here — this container has no MySQL and no Docker daemon — and nothing in this ticket touches API code, so CI is its first run. Docs: `CLAUDE.md` (the new one-place rule) and `docs/feature-patterns.md`. **Deliberately out of scope**: adding `Weekly` to Products, removing a retired value, and any change to what may be stored or configured.)

- **Done**: #1135 (**A Periodic Promotion's number is a Duration, and a Product Promotion hides the membership-only sections.** No migration and no new field: `promotion_periodical.quantity` is *already* what the billing engine counts periods with — `buildItemStream()` covers an occurrence while `occurrence < firstCovered + grant.quantity`, and `collectBillableItems()` fixes such a grant's **billed** quantity to 1 ("the grant covers periods, it does not say how many lockers") — so §14's "do not create separate fields unless required by the existing architecture" lands on naming the column for what it counts rather than adding a second one. Four of its answers are the design rather than the mechanics. **One place says which number a section shows**: `BenefitQuantityColumn` + `benefitQuantityLabelKey()` in the shared `ProductBenefits.tsx`, declared on `PRODUCT_BENEFIT_SECTIONS` (`quantityColumn: 'duration'` on the Periodical section and nowhere else) so both halves of the card ask one answer, and still **one column** of #916's shared grid — a per-section meaning is a label and a caption, never a column of its own. A **Membership Plan's** periodical benefit quantity is *units billed each occurrence*, so that section keeps saying Quantity: the two numbers are different questions sharing a column. **The `3 months` sentence is the frequency module's** (#1128): `billingFrequencyDurationLabel()` over new `duration_*` keys in the one `billing_frequency` namespace, pluralised **by the message**, `3 × 4 weeks` for a 4-week period because that is the form #892 §9 already counts them in, and `null` — no caption, the bare number — for a frequency naming no period (`once`, a legacy `per_session`). And **the Duration ends the promotion, never the Product** (§15): the period after the last covered one is `buildItemStream()`'s existing `pay_regular` fall-through, so §15's `Month 4 → €15.00` needed no code at all. §2 is the other half: with `Applies To = Product`, **Billing & Duration** and the **Membership Fee Simulation** join the two sections #926 already hid (both are the span of a fee a Product Promotion does not have), the timeline is not even fetched, the **Billing Event Simulation** stays, and the stored months are untouched — the draft still carries them, so switching the target back shows them again. §13 is the one API change: the three benefit `PUT`s read the number with `Number()` rather than `parseInt()`, so `3.5` is a 400 instead of a silently stored `3`. Tests: `api/src/test/promotion-periodic-duration.unit.test.ts` (new, 14 — in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only, #1009's reason) and `apps/admin/src/test/billing-frequency-duration.test.ts` (new, 8), plus three admin regression guards re-pointed rather than deleted (#926's gate count, the Billing Event Simulation's "Example Timeline beside it", and #814's "the shared editor keeps its Quantity column", which now asserts the helper's answer). Green: API unit suites 86 files / 1,290 tests, admin 157 / 2,447, `tsc --noEmit` clean in `apps/admin`. The API integration suite was not executed here — this container has no MySQL and no Docker daemon — so CI is its first run. Docs: `CLAUDE.md` (the new rule, and #926's bullet extended). **Deliberately out of scope**: a Duration column on an Assigned Plan card's applied-Promotion grant sections (the same stored number, one screen over — noted on the thread), and any change to what a Session or One-off grant's number means.)

- **Done**: #1166 (**Grafana's run-freshness alerts read `/health/runs` through the admin app**, so they need no public API once #1087 closes the host. `GET /api/health/runs` on the admin app (`app/api/health/runs/route.ts` over the pure `lib/healthRunsRelay.ts`) relays one fixed path to the API at `CORDEL_FITNESS_API_URL`: no headers forwarded, the API's own status and bytes relayed with `Cache-Control: no-store`, and 500/502/504 of its own (never a 200, since the rules are Error → Alerting); the exact path is exempt from `auth.protect()` and the locale routing. No migration, no API change. Tests: `api/src/test/health-runs-relay.unit.test.ts` (new, 13 — the CI gate) plus `apps/admin/src/test/health-runs-relay.test.ts` (6) and `health-runs-relay-route.test.ts` (6); verified locally against the dev API (200 with the live body, 502 against an unreachable base, neighbouring paths still behind Clerk). Both Grafana rules were repointed at `https://admin.vdicube.com/api/health/runs` on 2026-10-06 once the relay was live on dev.)

- **Done**: #1149 (**The Products a new gym starts with are configured, not blank.** Gym creation has seeded one `products` row per `is_product` charge type since #543 and the Personal Training package since #371 — with a name and a type and nothing else, so every new gym opened its Products page onto seven rows reading `—` and configured each one by hand. `api/src/domain/defaultGymProducts.ts` declares the seven defaults (Access Key · Insurance Fee · Locker Rental · Parking Fee · Personal Training Class Package (10 Sessions) · Premium Fitness App · Registration Fee) with the price, billing frequency, status, enrollment status, units and `mandatory` flag #1149's table gives them, and `api/src/api/gym-default-products.ts` is the one writer both creation paths call — the duplicate path's own insert had drifted and wrote no `is_system`, so every Product it seeded read as Custom. Four things are the rule rather than the implementation: it is a **default, never a correction** (every statement is an INSERT, so a gym that has repriced its Locker Rental keeps that price (§7) and no existing gym is touched at all (§8) — no migration, no backfill); a System Product's **name and type still come from `charge_types`** (#543), so the declaration carries only what the catalogue cannot; a charge type with **no entry still seeds** with the column defaults, because a seventh `is_product` row must not simply be missing from a gym; and the gym's `Standard VAT` 21% rate is inserted **before** the Products, since their prices are stored inclusive of it. Two visible changes beyond the configuration: the PT package is now **Public** and priced at €500 for the ten sessions (#942 — `amount` prices the whole package), where #371 left it staff-only and unpriced. Tests: `api/src/test/default-gym-products.unit.test.ts` (new, 7 — the declaration against the ticket's table, the Mandatory pair, no retired frequency (#945), the charge type codes against migration 090's own seed list, and the two call sites) plus two new cases and three updated ones in `gyms.test.ts`.)

- **Done**: #1175 (**Gym websites post sign-ups through the admin app, and Website Integration shows them the right endpoint.** Found by the #1087 review: the screen derived the endpoint from `PAYMENT_NOTIFICATION_URL`, which #1083 moved to the payment host, so every dev gym was shown `https://pay.vdicube.com/public/gyms/…/registrations` — a 405 — while the working URL was the API host #1087 means to close. `POST /api/public/gyms/<gymRef>/registrations` on the admin app relays the one route (`lib/publicRegistrationRelay.ts` pure, the route the I/O half): a single raw segment forwarded as received, `x-api-key`/`content-type`/`x-forwarded-for` only, the API's statuses passed through with `Retry-After`, 404/500/502/504 of its own and never a 2xx; the exact shape is exempt from both middleware guards. The API keys the per-IP limiter through `publicRegistrationClientKey()` (`PUBLIC_REGISTRATION_RELAY_HOPS`, default 0) and builds `endpoint_url` from `PUBLIC_REGISTRATION_BASE_URL` alone — no fallback, and `API_PUBLIC_URL` is gone; `deploy.yml` forwards both from GitHub variables. No migration. Tests: `api/src/test/public-registration-relay.unit.test.ts` (new, 21 — the CI gate), `public-registration-relay.test.ts` (new, 2 integration — two websites behind one relay keep separate budgets with the hop declared and share one without it), `website-integration.test.ts` (+2, the URL from the base and `null` without it, whatever `PAYMENT_NOTIFICATION_URL` says), admin `public-registration-relay.test.ts` (10) and `-route.test.ts` (10); verified locally end to end (probe 200, wrong/no key 401, bad body 400 through the relay; an encoded slash 404; other `/api/public` paths still behind Clerk). **Remaining, owner side:** the two GitHub variables per environment, then each gym with a key updates its WordPress endpoint — `docs/go-to-production.md`.)

- **Done**: #1177 (**`deploy.yml` forwards every proxy hop and internal secret the API reads.** It is the only writer of the API's environment — its script deletes every `Environment=` line of the `fitness-api` unit before writing its own block — yet three hop counts (`TRUST_PROXY_HOPS`, `PAYMENT_WEBHOOK_RELAY_HOPS`, `INTERNAL_RUN_RELAY_HOPS`) were documented as "set on the unit", so none could ever take effect, and #1113's `BOOKING_REMINDERS_INTERNAL_SECRET` was never forwarded, so every 15-minute reminder pass answered 401 (also missing as a GitHub secret in both environments). The three hops are now forwarded from GitHub variables and the secret from a GitHub secret, optional with an empty value reading as the default, and a deploy without the secret logs a WARNING rather than failing. Gate: `api/src/test/deploy-env-forwarding.unit.test.ts` (new, 4) derives the hop settings from `forwardedClient.ts` and the internal secrets from every production source, so #1175's `PUBLIC_REGISTRATION_RELAY_HOPS` is covered by the same test. No code path changed. **Owner side:** create `BOOKING_REMINDERS_INTERNAL_SECRET` in each environment, decide the hop values, redeploy the API.)

- **Done**: #1151 (**The Theme editor's `membership` image slot is labelled *My Products*.** #1116 renamed the Members App dashboard card from *My Membership* to *My Products*, and the Theme editor's Members App → Images slot that paints it kept the old name, so a gym owner uploading artwork for *My Membership* could no longer find the card it lands on. A label change and only a label change: six locale values (`members_image_membership` in the `gym_themes` and `themes` namespaces, en/es/ca — *My Products* / *Mis productos* / *Els meus productes*, the admin's own sentence case for es/ca as every other slot label uses, naming the same section as the card's title-case `home.products_services`). No migration, no route, no payload field and **no new slot**: the id stays `membership`, which is what `theme_member_images.slot` holds and what `buildThemeMemberImageKey()` writes into every object key, so every image already uploaded still resolves — renaming an id would strand its object, since R2 has no directories and a stored key is the only way back to one (#1035's reason for lowercasing nothing that was already stored). The editor resolves the label from the slot id (the interpolated `members_image_<slot>` key), so no component spells one.
