Gate: `api/src/test/theme-membership-slot-label.unit.test.ts` (new, 8 — in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only: the label says Products in both namespaces in all three languages and no longer says Membership, the words match the dashboard card's with each app's own casing, the two namespaces agree, the slot id and the seven-slot set are unchanged with no `products` key or slot added, every other slot's label is untouched, and the label is still resolved from the slot rather than spelled in the editor). API unit suites 1,536 across 99 files, admin 2,485 across 161, member 435, `tsc --noEmit` clean in both workspaces.)

- **Done**: #973 stage 1 of the plan agreed on the thread (**Activities name Eligible Professional Services, and there is no plan-based booking gate any more.** Migration 231 drops `activity_type_eligible_plans` — its rows logged, never mapped to services (§4) — and adds `activity_type_eligible_professional_services`; `GET`/`PUT /activity-types/:id/eligible-professional-services` replace the eligible-plans pair, `GET /activity-types/:id` carries the list, and Duplicate copies it. The gate (`domain/activityEligibility.ts` + `api/activity-eligibility.ts`) passes a `public_event` activity, treats one naming no service as **open** (`Q3 open`), and otherwise requires a balance > 0 on a named service read through `resolveMemberProfessionalServices()` — a refusal is `403 professional_service_required` naming the services. Booking spends nothing (`Q2`) except that a member eligible *only* through a purchased package still has it debited, until stage 3's consumption ledger; the Activities page's Booking Access section lists the gym's active Professional Services; `ClassSessionDetailPanel` overrides the new code. Stages 2–4 — the explicit wallet with Membership Plan Session Benefits as a fourth source, consumption on attendance/late cancellation/no-show, and the Members App's route from a refused booking to buying sessions — are follow-up tickets.)

- **Done**: #1026 (**Member is the canonical term on screen, in all three languages.** Spanish said `Socio`/`Socios` and Catalan `Soci`/`Socis` in the navigation, the Members list and 57 other strings per locale, while `miembro`/`membre` already carried the great majority of the copy (87 occurrences in `es.json`, 90 in `ca.json`) — so the two words named one entity on one screen. Spanish is **Miembro/Miembros** and Catalan **Membre/Membres** now, English is untouched, and nothing below the copy moved: no migration, no API change, no route, no permission, no locale **key** (the keys are English identifiers and `members.*` / `MEMBERS` are the domain's own names), no new localization mechanism and no React component holding a string. Two classes of occurrence were decided rather than replaced. The compound names of *other* entities — `cuota de socio` (the **Membership Fee**) and `plan(es) de socio` (the **Membership Plan**) — moved to the wording these same files already used everywhere else (`cuota de membresía` / `plan de membresía`, `quota de membresia` / `pla de membresia`), which removes the retired word without inventing a term or renaming a second entity. And the privacy policy's own prose (`privacyContent.ts`, hardcoded by design — it is the URL on Google's OAuth consent screen) says *sus miembros* / *els seus membres*, because it is user-facing Member terminology that no locale file holds. One occurrence is deliberately **kept**: `api/src/lib/receipt-pdf.ts`'s `'Socio'` fallback, which is the placeholder printed where a person's *name* goes on a Spanish fiscal receipt rather than a label of the Members domain — the ticket's acceptance criteria hold the API unchanged, and the two `plan de socio` strings in `product-terminology.unit.test.ts` are #949's own fixtures of copy that really shipped, which a fixture may not be edited to deny. Tests: `api/src/test/member-terminology.unit.test.ts` (new, 9 — no retired word left in any value of either app's six locale files or in the privacy content, the three canonical `nav.members` values, a vacuity guard, and the patterns catching every wording removed while passing `social`, `sociedad`, `societat`, `associació` and the two new compounds). It sits in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only, the same reason `product-terminology.unit.test.ts` beside it does. API unit suites green; the integration files were not executed here — this environment has no MySQL and no Docker daemon — so CI is their first run.)

- **Done**: #948 stage 1 of 2 (**Nutrition & Goals, and Personal Goals as its own section.** #947 had just shipped the two goal catalogues *inside* the Nutrition Library page, as two of three tabs; this ticket asks the opposite for Personal Goals, and the owner confirmed `Q1 sections` on the thread — *"new sections in the side menu are necessary… personal goals do not depend on nutrition. They are a completely different entity"* — so the route is **not** nested under `/nutrition/`. Stage 1 is the navigation half (§1, §2, §9): the nav group label becomes **Nutrition & Goals** / *Nutrición y Objetivos* / *Nutrició i Objectius* — a label only, with the group id, module, feature key and every existing item's href untouched (§2) — and Personal Goals moves out of the tab strip to `/{locale}/personal-goals`, with **Base Personal Goals** at `/{locale}/cordel/personal-goals` under Cordel (§5). *Nutrition* **Goals** stays exactly where #947 put it, per the owner's *"no need to change anything regarding nutrition goals"*, so `LIBRARY_TABS` is Foods · Nutrition Goals and the strip stays. Both new pages are thin wrappers over the one `GoalLibrarySection` — nothing about the catalogue's list, search, `+ Add`, inline create/edit, `⋮` menu, System/Gym badge or Details modal changed, which is what §3's "reuse the established patterns" means when the component already exists (#806: the page supplies the scope, the permissions and the namespace; the section names no endpoint). One API change follows from the move: `/personal-goals` was mounted behind `nutrition.nutrition_library` because the tab lived on that page, so hiding Foods would have 403'd a section the ticket is explicit is a separate domain — it now has its own `nutrition.personal_goals` flag, seeded by **migration 211** from the Library's current value (migration 160's `financials.taxes` device, so nothing changes on deploy) and still gated by the `nutrition` group key. `/nutrition-goals` keeps the Library's flag, since it is still a tab of that page. Tests: a `#948` block in `api/src/test/goal-library.test.ts` (+5 integration — the seeded key, the default, and the two flags proving independent in **both** directions, plus the group flag still blocking both) and `apps/admin/src/test/goal-library-ui.test.ts` (39, +9 — the two-tab declaration, no `kind="personal"` left on either library page, both new pages rendering the shared section and restating no control of their own, the per-page scope/permission split, the group rename in all three languages, every pre-existing Nutrition href still present, and the route not being under `/nutrition/`). Admin suite green at 1,871; API suite green; `tsc --noEmit` clean on both. **Deliberately out of stage 1**: §4 **Assigned Personal Goals**, which is stage 2 — the owner answered its five open questions on the thread (a row carries a target value + unit, dates, a status, notes and #799's actor snapshot; **many** per member; assignable **directly on a Member**; *Nutrition Plan Templates* meant its chrome rather than its content), so it is unblocked, just not in this PR. One remark on the thread is **not** actioned here and needs a word from the owner: *"the Dietary restrictions sections should have its own tab. Now has disappeared unfortunately."* Dietary Restrictions is a section of the Nutrition Plan tree (`RestrictionsSection` in `NutritionPlanTree.tsx`, rendered by all four plan/template pages) and of the member app's My Nutrition (#932), and all of them still render it on `main` — so what disappeared, and from which screen, is not identifiable from the repository.

- **Done**: #948 stage 2 of 2 (**Assigned Personal Goals — the goals a member actually holds.** Stage 1 moved the Personal Goals *catalogue* into its own section; this stage adds §4, the assignments, whose five open questions the owner answered on the thread: a row carries a target value + unit, a start date and a target date, a status, free-text notes and #799's actor snapshot (“yes” to all); **many** per member; and a goal **is** assigned on a Member directly — the opposite of #931's answer for Promotions — so there are two surfaces rather than one. **Migration 212** creates `member_personal_goals`: `(gym_id, member_id, personal_goal_id)` plus `target_value DECIMAL(10,2)`/`target_unit`, `start_date`/`target_date`, `status`, `notes VARCHAR(1000)` and the three actor pairs. Two design points are load-bearing. **The progress status and the soft delete are separate columns**: `status IN ('in_progress','achieved','abandoned')` (`chk_mpgoal_status`) is what happened to the goal, and deletion is `members`' own `deleted_at` — folding it into the vocabulary the way the catalogue's `status IN ('active','deleted')` does would overwrite the one fact the row records (a goal deleted after being achieved would read back as neither; the catalogue has no second axis to lose). And **one live, in-progress assignment per `(member, goal)`**: “many per member” is many *goals*, not the same goal twice with two different targets, so `mpgoal_live_goal_key` — a VIRTUAL generated column non-NULL only while the row is live and in progress — carries the UNIQUE index (migration 183/206's device), which is what makes the index and the router's 409 agree by construction; an achieved or abandoned goal may be assigned again, and reopening one that has been replaced is the same 409. API: `api/src/api/member-personal-goals.ts` (list with `?member_id=&status=&search=`, single read, assign, edit, soft delete, plus `GET /statuses` serving the accepted set the writes validate against) over `api/src/domain/personalGoalAssignment.ts`, which owns the vocabulary and every field rule. Three of those rules are the ticket's rather than the file's: the goal is checked with the **catalogue's own** gym-facing predicate (`gym_id IS NULL OR gym_id = ?` and not deleted), so a System goal is assignable by every gym, another gym's by nobody and a retired one by nobody; `member_id`/`personal_goal_id` are **immutable**, because re-pointing an assignment is ending one and starting the next and both are what the audit rows are keyed by; and the `PUT` is **partial**, so a `{ status }` body cannot wipe a target the gym typed. The cross-field rules (a unit needs a value, a target date may not precede the start date) are applied to the row the write *produces* rather than to the body — clearing `target_value` while a stored `target_unit` stays behind is exactly the case a per-field check misses — so it is a 400 and not a CHECK violation the global handler would answer as a bare 500 (#966). Mounted behind NUTRITION + `nutrition.personal_goals`, the catalogue's own flag: a gym that hid Personal Goals hid the goals its members hold with them. Audit entity `member_personal_goal`, a composed registry entry resolving `Member — Goal`. Admin: `apps/admin/src/components/personalGoals/` is one declaration and one editor for both surfaces (#806) — `assignedPersonalGoalProfile.ts` (the row shape, the status mirror, the row → form mapping, the two payload builders, the client-side rules as locale *keys*, and the target/period formatters), `AssignedPersonalGoalForm.tsx` (the shared body: on edit the member and the goal render as **values**, never controls, since the `PUT` ignores them — #974 — and the member picker is absent where the surface already knows whose goals these are), `AssignedPersonalGoalsSection.tsx` (the gym-wide list) and `MemberPersonalGoals.tsx` (the Member card section). The page at `/{locale}/assigned-personal-goals` is a thin wrapper like the catalogue's beside it, outside `/nutrition/` for the same §8 reason, and the list is `DataTable` + `FilterBar` + `listChrome` with #797–#800's model (expanding reads, `⋮ → Edit` writes inline, `+ Assign` opens the same body at the top of the list, `⋮ → Details` carries the audit information and the View Audit Log link) — §4's “do not create a new visual pattern” is satisfied by declaring none. On the **Member card** a new **PERSONAL GOALS** section sits between Assigned Training Plans/Nutrition Plans and Session Packages, and every control in it — `+ Assign`, `Edit`, `Unassign`, the form — is gated on `canWrite && editing`, absent rather than disabled outside Edit mode (#797/#957), using the card's existing dashed add affordance and `CardDetailRow` rather than a look of its own. `StatusBadge` gains the three progress tones, each reusing a pair already in its map so no colour is introduced. New `assigned_personal_goals` namespace in en/es/ca plus `nav.assigned_personal_goals` and `members.section_personal_goals`. **Nothing prices, projects or bills off an assignment**, and Nutrition Goals stays unread — assigning one to a plan and defining target values are still a later ticket's. Tests: `api/src/test/member-personal-goals.test.ts` (32 integration — auth, the front_desk role matrix, the happy paths, the DECIMAL-as-number shape, every validation and both cross-field rules including the stored-value cases, the live-uniqueness invariant in all four directions, the partial update, the immutable pair, the soft delete keeping its progress status, the filters and search, and tenant isolation on all five routes) and `personal-goal-assignment.unit.test.ts` (20 pure); `apps/admin/src/test/assigned-personal-goals.test.ts` (21 — the status vocabulary against both the API module and the migration, the payloads, the locale keys for every client-side rule, the formatters, the nav entry, one editor for two screens, and the Member card's Edit-mode gating). Admin suite green at 1,892; API unit suites green (48 files, 723 tests); `tsc --noEmit` clean on both. No MySQL in the session container, so the integration file runs in CI.)

- **Done**: #1118 (**Promotions on a member's Product purchase, from the Promotion's own grant row.** Stage 3 of the #1121 family plan, unblocked by the owner's `Q4` and `Q5`. Migration 229 adds `member_product_promotions`, the immutable snapshot §7 asks for — the sibling of the three `user_membership_promotion_*_snapshot` tables and for the same reason (#635 §16), carrying the Promotion's name, its `(action, value)` pair, its duration in billing cycles and **both** amounts beside `promotion_id`. Five of its answers are the design rather than the mechanics. **`Q4` refuses a second target relation outright** — *"the Product selected inside the Promotion is already the source of truth for applicability"* — so there is no `promotion_products` table: an offer **is** the grant the Promotion already carries for that Product, in the section `classifyProduct()` (#550) puts it in, which also gives `promotions.applies_to` (#926) its **first reader** in the codebase. **An offer is only ever made where it prices the Product lower than its own price and above nothing**: a `no_benefit` grant changes nothing and is the default every grant row carries, a grant that prices it to nothing has no payment to make (§9/§16 — there is no free-grant path here, and a `waive` grant belongs to an Assigned Plan's own application), and a `fixed_price` *above* the price is a configuration the vocabulary permits and a promotion it is not; `chk_mpp_amounts` backstops the last of the three. **The price is `applyLineBenefit()`** at quantity 1 over the VAT-inclusive figure `computePriceFields()` produced — the one place a pair becomes an amount, shared with the billing engine's charge builders and the Promotion card's own *Final Price* — so the member and the gym owner cannot be quoted two numbers and no page does tax arithmetic (#817). **The snapshot is written in the purchase's own transaction, and tapping *Apply promotion* persists nothing**: §7 binds it to "the resulting purchase", so there is nothing for it to hang off before one exists and every member who changed their mind would leave an orphan row — the route takes a `promotion_id` and only that id, re-reads it through the very loader that produced the offer, prices the charge from the answer, and answers `409 promotion_not_applicable` for one that lapsed in between rather than charging a price the gym no longer offers. `member_products.amount` stays what it has always been (the figure actually charged), which is what makes §14 true by construction: the webhook's `payment_recorded` event carries `payment_requests.amount`. And **`duration_cycles` is a Periodic grant's own quantity** (#1135 settled that the column *is* a Duration), `null` for a session or one-off grant — `Q5`: a one-off Product has no billing cycles to express one in — and under stage 2 every purchasable Product is non-recurring, so the caption appears only where there is one. The Admin half is §11–§13: the Member card's section is renamed **Products & Services** / *Productos y Servicios* / *Productes i Serveis* (the `members` namespace only — the Assigned Plan card's own section keeps *Additional Products*), a new read-only **Purchased Products** block over `GET /members/:id/products` shows each purchase and its frozen Promotion, that read joins neither `products` nor `promotions` (§13 forbids resolving the current configuration to describe a historical purchase), and the treatment is worded through `benefitTreatmentLabel()` in the `promotions` namespace so §3's voice is kept with no second vocabulary in `members`. #957's "the section says Product, not Service" gate is widened for that one string and for it alone, which is a widening rather than a relapse: the section now names both halves of what it holds. Tests: `api/src/test/member-product-promotions.unit.test.ts` (new, 34 — the eligibility rule, the arithmetic, the duration, the snapshot, migration 229 against the vocabulary, both apps' copy, and the prohibitions: no second relation, no live join in either read, no control in the Admin block) and `api/src/test/me-products.test.ts` (extended by 14 integration cases: the offers and all five exclusions, cross-gym isolation, the discounted charge and its frozen snapshot, the regular price when none is applied, the 409s, a malformed id reaching no provider, the applied read after the webhook with its one Billing Event at the discounted amount, immutability after the Promotion is edited and the Product repriced, a Periodic grant's cycles, and the Admin read with its gym scope). Both in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only (#1009's reason). Green: API unit suites 92 files / 1,450 tests, the Admin app's own 157 files / 2,453 tests, the Members App's 20 files / 435 tests, `tsc --noEmit` clean in `api`, `apps/admin` and `apps/member`, `next build` clean in both apps. The API integration suite was not executed here — this container has no MySQL and no Docker daemon — so CI is its first run. Docs: `CLAUDE.md` (the new rule), `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/payments.md` (§D and the data model). **Deliberately out of scope**: a recurring purchase and the Promotion duration that only it can consume, more than one Promotion per purchase, a free or price-raising offer, a refund path, and §1–§3 of the ticket, which #1121 stages 1 and 2 already shipped.)

- **Done**: #1123 (**Billing Events become Payments in the Members App, over one shared projection.** §1–§6 of the ticket; the `/membership` → `/products` route move is deliberately **not** here — moving the path drags the page's own title with it and that rename is #1121 §1's, which is blocked on its own `Q1`. One new read and no new engine: `GET /me/billing-event-forecast` is `assignedPlanBillingForecast()` — the Assigned Plan card's own Billing Event Forecast (#924 stage 4) — scoped to the caller through `resolveMemberId()`, so the snapshot rules (#635 §13–§17), each application's own grant snapshot (§16), the Additional Periodic Services and the Personal Membership Fee Benefit (#772) are in the numbers by construction and §3's "the final total must correspond to the Billing Event amount that will actually be charged" is true without a second arithmetic. Four of its answers are the design rather than the mechanics. **Next Payment is that projection's first group** (the thread's "the next billing event in time") and the Forecast subcard is the rest of it, so the two cannot disagree and the next charge is not listed twice; the horizon stays the engine's `SIMULATED_CYCLES = 2` per recurring stream, which is the thread's "2 complete cycles, same as membership plans". **A past event's status is the line's, never the card's**: `GET /me/billing-events` now reports a `status` derived by the one implementation the staff ledger reads (`deriveBillingEventStatus()`, #640/#787), because one date can carry a settled charge and a rejected one and a card-level verdict would have to pick. **Which assignment is "the member's" is one place**: `MEMBER_CURRENT_ASSIGNMENT_ORDER`, shared with `GET /me/membership`, so the card cannot name one plan while the forecast underneath projects another. And **the Payments card decides nothing it draws**: `apps/member/src/lib/memberPayments.ts` owns the grouping, the labels-as-keys and the formatting while `components/MemberPaymentsCard.tsx` owns the chrome — one Billing Event card for both the past and the forecast subcards (§4/§5's "the same card structure"), expanding fetches nothing (#955), and no colour is spelled outside `memberChrome.ts` (#983), so a forecast card is distinguished by the *absence* of the status pill plus an `info`-toned *Forecast* badge rather than by a hue invented for a projection. The page's own *Upcoming payments* and *Payment history* sections are absorbed, and their five locale keys retired with them; `upcoming_payments` stays on `GET /me/membership` for any other caller. Tests: `api/src/test/member-payments.unit.test.ts` (new, 21 — in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only, #1009's reason) and `api/src/test/me-billing-event-forecast.test.ts` (new integration: auth, role, the member's own plan, *equality with the staff route for the same assignment*, a Period Benefit on the fee's date, `available: false` for no plan and for a cancelled one, cross-gym isolation, and the ledger's derived status). Green: API unit suites 87 files / 1,311 tests, `tsc --noEmit` clean in `api` and `apps/member`, `next build` clean. The API integration suite was not executed here — this container has no MySQL and no Docker daemon — so CI is its first run. Docs: `CLAUDE.md` (the new rule), `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/payments.md`. **Deliberately out of scope**: the route and title rename (#1121 §1), a member-facing *Products* catalogue or purchase (#1121/#1118), and any change to what is billed.)

- **Done**: #980 stage 2 (**The Waitlist setting, editable on the calendar event.** The occurrence has carried `calendar_events.waitlist_mode` since migration 154 (#503 stage 2) — nullable, falling back to `activity_types.waitlist_mode` — but no API ever wrote it: the column was read and honoured only, so a gym wanting one session's queue closed had to change the Activity Type and every other occurrence with it. The owner's answers settled the two open questions: the control offers the three states the column actually has (**Open · Closed · Disabled**, not §3's Enabled/Disabled boolean), and `disabled` **removes** every member from the waiting list and alerts them, which is explicitly *not* a booking cancellation (`Q3`). `api/src/domain/waitlistMode.ts` is now the one place the vocabulary and that distinction live (`parseWaitlistModeInput`, `waitlistModeClosesQueue`, `effectiveWaitlistMode`), mirrored for the browser in `apps/admin/src/lib/waitlistModes.ts` and in SQL by migration 154's two CHECKs — so a new mode goes in three places, and `activity-types.ts` and the Activity Types page now import the list instead of each declaring it. The write is the **same `PUT /class-sessions/:id`** stage 1 used: no new route, one request, and since the emptying has to be atomic with it (§9) the route's plain branch is now a transaction too, so a failure leaves the occurrence *and* its queue exactly as they were. `closed` deliberately empties nothing — it stops new joins and keeps every `waitlist_position` — which is the whole reason the column has three states; only `disabled` runs `emptyWaitlistQueue()`, which cancels the `waitlisted` rows under `FOR UPDATE` (the existing `chk_ceb_status` vocabulary §5 asks for), promotes nobody (a closed queue is not a freed place, so it is not `cancelBooking()`) and never touches a `booked` row. Two new `member_notifications` types, in the union **and** the CHECK (migration 217, modelled on 216 — with one deliberate divergence the `db-reviewer` pass asked for: its guard tests **both** new values rather than one standing for the pair, because with a two-value delta a clause holding only one would read as already-correct and leave every insert of the other failing errno 3819 invisibly): `waitlist_closed` for the members a disable removed, and `waitlist_removed` for a member staff take off a list that is still open — two types because they are opposite promises, one saying there is nothing to rejoin and the other that there is. `Q2`'s "every update in the waitlist reaches the Members App" also filled a gap on the staff side: `POST /bookings` with `waitlist: true` raised nothing at all until now (only the member's own `POST /me/bookings` did), so it sends `waitlist_joined`, and the staff `DELETE /bookings/:id` sends `waitlist_removed` when the row it cancelled was a waitlisted one — a member cancelling their own place still gets nothing, since they did it themselves. §11: `waitlist_mode` joins `SESSION_AUDITED_FIELDS`, so a move reads `open → disabled` previous → new (and `null → closed` when the occurrence stops inheriting, which is a real change rather than an absence), with the removed member ids riding that same row as `removed_waitlist_member_ids` rather than a second one. In the panel the setting is a value in the read-only card — the *effective* mode, marked `(inherited from the activity)` while the occurrence's own column is NULL — and a `formChrome` select in Edit mode beside Trainer and Space, with §3's confirmation as a step of Save (it carries the live waiting count and nothing is written until it is confirmed; changing the choice invalidates it). The draft is compared against the **effective** mode, not the stored column: comparing against the column would write the inherited mode onto the occurrence on the first unrelated save and silently end that inheritance. Adding and removing waiting-list members needed no new UI — the panel already had both. Tests: `api/src/test/waitlist-mode.unit.test.ts` (new, 17 — the three modes, the parser's three answers including the refusal rather than a coercion, `closed` not emptying the queue, the effective fallback, and that the module, the browser mirror, the SQL CHECK, the notification union/CHECK and both apps' locale files agree), `api/src/test/class-session-waitlist.test.ts` (new, 22 — the write and the inheritance both ways, the 400, §12's untouched Activity Type and sibling occurrences, tenant isolation, disabling emptying the queue and alerting exactly the removed members while leaving a booked row alone and promoting nobody, its idempotency, `closed` keeping the queue and its order, the four audit shapes, and the three staff add/remove alert cases) and `apps/admin/src/test/class-session-waitlist-edit.test.ts` (new, 11 — one declaration for the modes, the control existing only in Edit mode, the inherited note, the effective-mode comparison, the confirmation gate preceding the request, the ICU count, one request for the whole form, no lilac, locale parity). Four new member locale keys and twelve admin ones in en/es/ca. Stage 3 (Professional Services as a multi-valued relation on the occurrence) still waits on #973's stage 1 so the two relations are modelled once. API suite green at 5,243 across 241 files, admin at 2,028, `tsc --noEmit` clean in all three workspaces.)

- **Done**: #980 stage 1 (**Trainer and Space, editable from the calendar event itself.** `ClassSessionDetailPanel` — the panel that shows an occurrence's enrolled members and waiting list — was read-only: it printed `Trainer: …` / `Space: …` as text (and only the *covering* trainer, so an occurrence with no substitute showed no trainer at all) and offered Change time, Mark as completed and Cancel event and nothing else, so moving one session to another room meant editing the Activity Type and changing every occurrence with it. The owner's answers on the thread settled the staging: `Q1 multi` moves Professional Services out of this stage into stage 3, after #973's stage 1, so the occurrence-level and activity-level relations are modelled once rather than twice in a week; `Q2`/`Q3` are stage 2's waitlist work. So this stage is Trainer + Space, and **no migration and no new route**: both are already the occurrence's own columns (migration 082) and `PUT /class-sessions/:id` already accepted them, already runs one `UPDATE` (§9's "atomic, or unchanged" is the statement) and already applies `validateSessionRefs()` plus `assertSlotAvailable()`'s four `409` codes. What was missing was §11 — the route wrote **no audit row at all**, so a trainer change was invisible in the log a gym reads to find out who moved a class. It records `previous → new` for the fields that moved now, from `diffAuditedFields()` (`api/src/domain/calendarEventChanges.ts`) over `SESSION_AUDITED_FIELDS`, diffing the row before the write against the row after it rather than the request body (the route is a partial write and the screen sends the whole form), writing **nothing** when an edit changed nothing, and reporting values as stored so the registry's FK enrichment can name them — which is why `trainer_membership_id`, `activity_type_id` and `professional_service_id` joined `space_id` in `FK_MAP` and the audit reads `Coach D1 → Coach D2`. Both of the route's write paths end at one `readSessionAndAudit()`, so the transactional shared-slot branch and the plain one audit identically. The panel takes the app's read-then-edit split (#797): values in the info card — Professional Service, Trainer, #193's covering trainer only when it is somebody else, Space — `Edit details` in the Actions block as the one entry point, the two fields as `formChrome` selects in the card they were values in, `primaryBtnSmall()`/`secondaryBtnSmall` and no colour of its own (#912/#954), the slot `409` as its own line rather than a raw toast, and `onMutated()` + a reload on success so §9's refreshed values appear immediately. The lookups are the Calendar page's own `/spaces`/`/trainers` reads passed down rather than a second fetch, a value the occurrence holds that is no longer selectable (a deactivated space, a trainer whose role changed) is offered as a **disabled** option so the stored value still reads correctly, and the save sends only what changed, so an unrelated edit cannot 400 on a value nobody touched. §12 holds by construction: the panel names no activity-type route and no `default_*` column. Tests: `api/src/test/calendar-event-changes.unit.test.ts` (new, 9 — the diff's comparison rules including the `number` vs `string` and two-`Date` cases that would otherwise fabricate a change, an absent key reading as unset rather than as a change to NULL, a cleared field, values reported as stored, and the field list itself), `api/src/test/class-session-details.test.ts` (new, 18 — both fields in one request, the Activity Type's defaults and every sibling occurrence untouched, clearing a field, a field the request never mentioned, the center/inactive-space/foreign-trainer refusals leaving the row exactly as it was, tenant isolation, 401, the shared-slot `409`, and the audit row in five shapes including the save that writes none) and `apps/admin/src/test/class-session-detail-edit.test.ts` (new, 11 — the single entry point, both controls living only inside the edit block, one request to the one route, no activity-type write, only-what-changed, the refresh, the conflict line, the lookups coming from the page, no lilac, the Professional Service staying a value, and locale parity). Four locale keys in en/es/ca. API unit suites green at 764; admin at 2,017; `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` clean. The integration file was written but not executed here — this environment has no MySQL — so CI is its first run.)

Migration 232 adds `calendar_event_eligible_professional_services` and `calendar_events.eligible_services_override` (0 = inherit, 1 = own list, an empty own list meaning any member may book); nothing is copied at materialization or propagated. `PUT /class-sessions/:id` takes `eligible_professional_service_ids` (absent keeps, `null` re-inherits, array owns, new ids validated, stored ones kept), the audit row carries `eligible_professional_service_names` previous → new, the booking hook is handed the occurrence so the gate reads its list, and the PT-slot projection asks per occurrence. The session panel shows the effective list as a value and a checkbox list in Edit mode over the Calendar page's own `/professional-services` read.
