- **Done**: #932 (**My Nutrition shows Dietary Restrictions, and its Goals read as labels.** The member-facing My Nutrition page never showed the plan's **dietary restrictions** at all — `GET /me/nutrition-plan`'s own doc comment claimed it returned them and the query did not — and it rendered a goal's stored slug verbatim (`weight_loss`, `water`) with an unspaced `1.00l` beside it and the goal's `frequency` dropped on the floor. The route now returns `restrictions` (position-ordered, the library item's locale-resolved name and its own `image_url`), and the page renders **Nutrition Goals** and **Dietary Restrictions** as two sections of image-plus-name rows through one shared `components/NutritionItemRow.tsx` — it owns the thumbnail size, 1:1 aspect ratio, radius, alignment, spacing, typography and the same `nutrition.no_image` fallback the #722 food card uses, so the two sections cannot drift apart (§4) — each stating its own empty case in words rather than vanishing (§5), and both strictly read-only (§6). A restriction *is* a Nutrition Library food (`member_nutrition_plan_restrictions.nutrition_library_item_id` is NOT NULL), so its picture is the one image source the nutrition system already has; no second image mapping was added (§3). Goal labels and the `1 l · daily` line come from `goalLabel()`/`goalDetail()` in `lib/nutritionFood.ts`, which decide the fallback **before** calling `t()` (next-intl has no `defaultValue`), and Home's Today's Nutrition Plan card uses the same two helpers so the two screens cannot word the same goal differently. **Deferred, per the issue thread**: a Nutrition Goal has no food to take an image from — `member_nutrition_plan_goals` carries no `nutrition_library_item_id` and only one of the thirteen goal slugs (`water`) even has a same-named food — so a goal row shows the standard fallback until #947/#948's goal catalogues exist, after which the row fills itself with no change to the member app. No migration, no new endpoint. Tests: a `#932` block in `api/src/test/me-nutrition-plan.test.ts` (6 integration: position order, the image and its null, locale resolution in es/ca, the goals beside them, the always-present empty array, and a second gym's restrictions on the same food) and `apps/member/src/test/my-nutrition-sections.test.ts` (34).)

- **Done**: #932 second half (**A Nutrition Goal may be illustrated by a Nutrition Library food.** The Dietary Restrictions half shipped earlier (#1004); the goals half waited on an unanswered question — a goal is a slug from the plan routers' closed vocabulary, not a food, so it had no picture to show — and ships with the recommended answer: migration 233 adds a nullable `nutrition_library_item_id` (`ON DELETE SET NULL`) to both goal tables, `api/src/api/nutrition-goal-illustration.ts` judges the value once and reads the pair back for every goal row, the three goal routers' `POST` (and the template `PUT`) take it, every copy path carries it, the admin goal editor offers a *Picture (optional)* select over the same library the restrictions pick from, and `GET /me/nutrition-plan` carries the food's own `image_url` beside the goal so the Members App's `NutritionItemRow` shows it — or the standard fallback for a goal illustrated by nothing. No backfill, no guessed image.)

- **Done**: #1108 stage 2 of 2 (**Save & Pay → Pending Payment → Active.** Migration 234 widens the status CHECK for `pending_payment`; `POST /user-memberships/:id/save-and-pay` asks the one-plan rule and locks the Draft as pending (or activates it outright when the first cycle owes nothing); `api/src/api/assignment-commit.ts` is the one commit, run by the payment webhook, by the new `POST /:id/record-payment` (a cash first payment, one transaction with its `payment_recorded` event), by a `payment_recorded` appended through `POST /payments`, and by the API-only `/activate`. The supersede runs at activation with the Save & Pay confirmation taken as given; nothing is cancelled while the payment is outstanding; the forecast is not pre-persisted (the real events match it by construction). The Assigned Plan card offers Save & Pay on a Draft and Record cash payment / Send payment link on a pending row; `POST /me/payment-requests` accepts a pending row so the member's own Pay now reaches it. Decisions recorded in `docs/payments.md`.)

- **Done**: #1108 stage 1 (**A newly assigned Membership Plan is a Draft, and one explicit commit makes it the member's.** The first of the ticket's three agreed stages: the `draft` status and the fully editable Draft Assigned Plan. Stage 2 is **Save & Pay** — the commit with the payment raised around it, a *Pending Payment* state and the forecast consolidated into real Billing Events — and stage 3 the remaining Draft/Active UI. Migration 227 widens `user_memberships_status_check` to five values, which is exactly the widening migration 198 (#786) said a payment gate before activation would need; `awaiting_payment` stays retired, because stage 2's *Pending Payment* arrives with the transaction that produces it rather than as a second value nothing can write. All three insert paths write `ASSIGNMENT_CREATION_STATUS` and `POST /user-memberships/:id/activate` is the one `draft → active` transition, with `PUT /:id` refusing that flip and naming it, the way a cancellation is refused and routed to `DELETE`. Five of its answers are decisions rather than mechanics. **A Draft is not the member's plan** (the thread's `Q2`): `draft` is outside `LIVE_ASSIGNMENT_STATUSES`, so one Active plan plus one Draft replacement is a legal state — which is what makes a replacement configurable before it is committed — and #956's check, its `confirm` and `supersedeLiveAssignments()` move off the four insert paths onto that one commit; migration 213's UNIQUE index needs no change, because `active_member_key` is `IF(status = 'active', member_id, NULL)`, and it still backstops the commit since the UPDATE populates that column. **A Draft bills nothing and books nothing** — the nightly run and `activity-eligibility.ts` keep reading `um.status = 'active'`, which makes an unpaid assignment unbookable where #786 left it bookable from creation. **A Draft is fully editable and fully projected** (§2–§5): `draft` joins `SNAPSHOT_EDITABLE_STATUSES`, `ATTACHABLE_STATUSES`, `CLOSEABLE_FROM`, `SIMULATED_STATUSES` and the Member card's own `LIVE_STATUSES`, so the Assigned Plan card's existing **Billing Event Forecast** answers what committing it would bill — `Q3`'s answer is to read it there rather than add a fourth name beside *Membership Fee Simulation*, *Billing Event Forecast* and the **Billing Events** ledger. **A Draft is invisible to every "does this member have a plan" read that is not its own configuration**: `latestEnrollmentStatusSql()` excludes it (it reads the *latest* row, so a Draft would overwrite a currently Active member's enrollment status and drop them out of the Nutrition Dashboard's count), `countsAsRecentMembership()` answers `false` in both directions (#927's window — a Draft's `starts_at` is inside it by definition), the Financials dashboard's assigned-plan join excludes it, and the member's own reads exclude it through `MEMBER_CURRENT_ASSIGNMENT_FILTER` beside `MEMBER_CURRENT_ASSIGNMENT_ORDER`, both halves in both callers, because `FIELD()` answers 0 for an unlisted status and would otherwise sort a Draft *first* and describe it to the member as their membership. And **a Draft nobody commits does not expire** (`Q1a`): staff discard it with `POST /:id/close`, which is warning-free because a Draft has never had a `next_billing_date`. The admin gets `lifecycle_status: 'draft'` (a stored status outranks the date-aware part of `LIFECYCLE_STATUS_SQL`, so a future-dated Draft reads `draft` and not `pending`), the status in the Assigned Plans filter, and `⋮ → Activate` on the Assigned Plan card raising the shared `ReplacePlanDialog` from the 409 — that control is what keeps assignment usable between stages 1 and 2, and stage 2 replaces it with the Member-level **Save & Pay** rather than adding a second commit. Tests: `api/src/test/draft-assignment-status.unit.test.ts` (new, 22 — the CHECK against `STATUSES`, every `INSERT INTO user_memberships` naming the column and passing the constant rather than a literal, the live/recent/enrollment/member-facing exclusions, the editable and simulated lists, and the admin's mirrors and locale keys; in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only) and `api/src/test/draft-membership-assignment.test.ts` (new integration), with `one-active-membership-plan.test.ts` rewritten onto the commit and `user-memberships.test.ts` / `membership-plans.test.ts` updated where they asserted the behaviour that moved. Migration reviewed by the `db-reviewer` agent: no BLOCKERs, and its four warnings are applied — both guards compare the CHECK's literal set exactly (migration 185's device, so a six-value CHECK is not silently accepted), `down`'s row guard is the complement of what it writes rather than a list of its own, and the header states the rollback ordering and that both directions are self-healing on re-run. Green: API unit suites 88 files / 1,332 tests, `tsc --noEmit` clean in `api` and `apps/admin`. The API integration suite was not executed here — this container has no MySQL and no Docker daemon — so CI is its first run. Docs: `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/payments.md`.)

- **Done**: #1130 stage 1 of 3 (**A Membership Plan's Billing & Duration is a cycle, and `auto_renew` is what makes it repeat.** The first of the ticket's three agreed stages: the engine. Stage 2 is the Membership Fee Simulation's `Cycle` column, its two non-touching vertical lines and the `↻ Repeats indefinitely` / `✓ One cycle only — no renewal` markers; stage 3 is the same grouping over the Billing Event Simulation's existing cards. The ticket's premise was not true on `main` — `classifyPlanDurationPeriod()` ran Free → Pre-paid → Paid → Bonus **once** and returned `pay_regular` for ever after, and `billing_policies.auto_renew` was stored, shown on the Plan card and read by **nothing** — so the owner's `Q1 real` makes this a change to what the nightly run charges rather than a preview, which is why it is its own stage and why its three follow-up answers were all about money. Migration 230 adds `user_memberships.auto_renew`. Five of its answers are decisions rather than mechanics. **The flag is the assignment's own** (answer **A** `new-only`): frozen from the Plan by `snapshotAssignedPlan()` at creation and read through a new `ASSIGNMENT_AUTO_RENEW` with deliberately **no** `COALESCE` onto `bp.auto_renew` — that column is `NOT NULL DEFAULT true`, so a live fallback would have put every member already enrolled inside a second Free/Pre-paid/Bonus stretch on the morning after deploy, billed nothing, with no staff action; `NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` *is* the backfill, so there is no second statement to resume and no partially backfilled state. **`materialiseAssignedPlanSnapshot()` deliberately does not capture it** (`captureAutoRenew: false`, its one caller): that helper writes down what an uncaptured assignment resolves *today*, which for this column is the stored 0 — capturing the Plan's current flag there is how a twenty-month-old contract would start renewing because somebody corrected a benefit quantity. For the same reason it is **outside `has_billing_snapshot` / `snapshot_captured`** (#772's rule for a NOT NULL defaulted column, which otherwise reports every row in the table as captured and freezes the duration fallback) and is reported beside `personal_fee_benefit` instead. **`repeats` is a required field of `PlanDuration`**, for the reason `cadence` is one since #892: all five loaders pass it and TypeScript is the gate, because a path that could omit it would bill a renewing contract its regular fee from the second cycle while the card beside it showed the cycle restarting. **The iteration is found by walking boundaries, not by dividing a month count**: `advanceBillingDate()` clamps end-of-month overflow, so `startsAt + n x interval` is not linear in `n` and the only way to know which iteration a date is in is to ask the same helper every other boundary here is measured with — `planDurationCycleLength()` is Free + Paid + Bonus and never plus Pre-paid, which is the first slice *of* Paid. And **`auto_renew` off is today's engine to the letter** (answer **C** `regular`): one cycle, then the regular price, and the membership does **not** expire, so there is no sweep, no `active → expired` transition and no second stopping rule; `prepaidPeriodsDueOn()` collects the lump once per iteration (answer **B**) and once in total for a contract that does not renew, which is #946 unchanged. A Plan's *own* previews read the Plan's **live** flag, because a catalogue preview is about the Plan as it stands now. One thing moved **into** this stage from stage 2, and it is the only part of the ticket's UI half that is here: the projections' **stopping rule**. Both of them stop by *reaching the regular price* (`TRAILING_REGULAR_PERIODS`, and the engine's `promotional` flag for the Billing Event Simulation), and a repeating cycle never reaches one — so without it every renewing Plan's Membership Fee Simulation ran to its 60-row budget. `walkExampleTimeline()` takes an optional `cycle` instead (`timelineCycleFor()`, one helper both adapters call off `PlanDuration.repeats`, `REPEATED_CYCLE_ITERATIONS = 2`), and such a table's last row stays **closed** rather than open-ended: "Bonus, from X onwards" is exactly the lie the walk already refuses to tell when the row budget cuts a duration short, and what says the cycle carries on is stage 2's `↻ Repeats indefinitely` marker beside it. Tests: `api/src/test/plan-duration-auto-renew.unit.test.ts` (new, 27 — the classifier across iterations, the ticket's own `12 pre-paid + 3 bonus` example, the restart at Free rather than into Paid, a 4-weekly iteration, end-of-month non-drift, the zero-length cycle being inert, the money through `resolveMembershipFee()`, and the two-iteration horizon) and `api/src/test/assignment-auto-renew-snapshot.unit.test.ts` (new, 6 — the writer, over an injected `Tx`: creation freezes the flag, materialising does not touch it, and a Plan with no billing policy renews nothing). Both in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only (#1009's reason). Two integration fixtures now pin `auto_renew` **off** where they insert a `billing_policies` row (`membership-plans.test.ts`'s `setBillingPolicy`, `plan-duration-billing-frequency.test.ts`'s `createPlan`) rather than leaning on the column's `DEFAULT true`, because those cases are about what a duration *means* and not about renewal; one new case in the first pins it on and asserts the two iterations. Migration reviewed by the `db-reviewer` agent: no BLOCKERs, and its two migration notes are applied — `down` states the rollback ordering (`loadAssignedPlanSnapshot()` names the column, so dropping it under a deployed API is ER_BAD_FIELD_ERROR → a bare 500, #966) and the header states that `up` → `down` → `up` is lossy, since the second `up` re-defaults a legitimately frozen 1 back to 0. Green: API unit suites 99 files / 1,598 tests (after the sync with `origin/main`), `tsc --noEmit` clean in `api`. The API integration suite was not executed here — this container has no MySQL and no Docker daemon — so CI is its first run. Docs: `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/payments.md` (§B4). **Deliberately out of scope**: §1's and §2's visuals (the `Cycle` column, the two non-touching thin vertical lines, no horizontal divider, the two end markers) and the Billing Event Simulation's own grouping, which are stages 2 and 3; and one interim consequence worth knowing — that second projection still bounds a renewing contract by `MAX_SIMULATION_MONTHS = 36` rather than by iterations, because its horizon is derived per recurring stream and re-expressing it in cycles is stage 3's. Neither surface advertises a charge the run does not make, which is the invariant that matters.)

- **Done**: #1130 stage 2 of 3 (**The Membership Fee Simulation says which iteration a row belongs to.** The second of the ticket's three agreed stages: §1's presentation, over the engine stage 1 shipped. No migration, no pricing change and no new projection — what the table draws is what the run already bills. The **engine now reports** the cycle it bounded the walk by: `timelineCycleFor()` answers for a non-repeating one too (`{ length, repeats: false, iterations: 1 }`), the walk still bounds itself only while `repeats` is set — so stage 1's trailing-regular rule is untouched — and `ExampleTimelineResult.cycle` carries it to both cards. Four of its answers are decisions rather than mechanics. **A non-repeating cycle is reported, not hidden**: `1` beside the configured stretch and `✓ One cycle only — no renewal` under it is exactly what the owner's `Auto Renew = No` asks the table to say, and the trailing regular periods are deliberately **outside** the grouping — they are the contract's regular price, not a second iteration, so they carry neither a number nor a line. **The grouping is arithmetic over the server's own period numbers**, in the one JSX-free module (`apps/admin/src/lib/exampleTimeline.ts`: `exampleTimelineRowCycle()` and `exampleTimelineCycleNote()`), because a card that counted its own iterations could group rows the nightly run bills differently — the drift #635 stage 12 exists to prevent — and nothing at runtime would notice, since a tidy second cycle renders perfectly well. **The number is the iteration a row really is in**, not a relabelled `1`/`2`: for a Plan preview that is 1 and 2, while an Assigned Plan's table starts at the period containing *today*, so a contract on its third cycle reads `3` and `4` rather than claiming to be at its beginning. And **the marker's sentence is each card's locale key**, glyph included (`timeline_cycle_repeats` / `timeline_cycle_once` in the `plans` and `assigned_plans_page` namespaces, en/es/ca): the shared table is handed a rendered note and cannot choose between the two, which is the same split that keeps it free of entity knowledge (#818). The look is the ticket's: the `Cycle` column to the **left** of Period, lighter than every cell beside it (`TIMELINE_CYCLE_LINE` / `TIMELINE_CYCLE_TEXT`, exported beside the table's three tones for stage 3 to reuse rather than re-spell), a 1px line per iteration with a gap at the foot of its last row so the two never touch, and **no** horizontal divider — a caller whose rows carry no cycle (a Promotion's timeline) renders the four columns exactly as before. Both hosts of the shared card get it at once, the Membership Plan card and the Assigned Plan card, since they render one table off one projection. Tests: `api/src/test/plan-cycle-simulation.unit.test.ts` (new, 20 — the engine reporting the cycle in all four shapes, the per-row numbering and the segment ends, the non-repeating trailing rows, a table starting mid-cycle, the marker kinds, the column's position, no iteration arithmetic or glyph in the table or either page, no `borderTop`, and locale parity across both namespaces), in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only (#1009's reason); one assertion in `apps/admin/src/test/plan-example-timeline.test.ts` updated from four headers to five, its intent unchanged. Green: API unit suites 99 files / 1,548 tests, admin suite 161 files / 2,485 tests, `tsc --noEmit` clean in both workspaces and the admin `next build` clean. The API integration suite was not executed here — this container has no MySQL and no Docker daemon — so CI is its first run; nothing in this stage touches a router. Docs: `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/architecture.md`. **Deliberately out of scope**: §2's card grouping and its `MAX_SIMULATION_MONTHS = 36` horizon, which are stage 3's.)

- **Done**: #1130 stage 3 of 3 (**The Billing Event Simulation groups its cards by the same cycle, and a repeating cycle is its horizon.** The last of the ticket's three agreed stages: §2 over the engine stage 1 shipped and the grouping language stage 2 declared. No migration, no pricing change and no new projection. Two halves, and the first is not presentation: a repeating contract **never reaches a regular charge**, so #629 §6's first-regular horizon rule had nothing to find and every renewing Plan's Billing Event Simulation ran to the engine's `MAX_SIMULATION_MONTHS = 36` and reported itself `truncated` — an arbitrary slice of a cycle that might be 15 periods or 40, and 36 months of a 36-period cycle is not even one iteration. `horizonUntil` on `BillingSimulationInput` is that span instead, and it is the **same count** the fee table bounds its rows by: `timelineCyclePeriods()` answers the periods `cycle.iterations` complete iterations span and `timelineCycleHorizon()` turns it into the last billing date they reach, so §3's "the two simulations should tell exactly the same story" is structural — for one Plan the fee table's last period and the card section's last group are the same date by construction, asserted for three duration shapes. Three of its answers are decisions rather than mechanics. It **bounds** #915's per-stream `minimumCycles` floor rather than being bounded by it: a yearly Product inside a 15-period cycle would otherwise add a card from a third iteration that the rows beside it do not list. It **raises the safety cap** to contain a cycle longer than 36 months, so a `24 paid + 12 bonus` renewing Plan is shown rather than cut mid-iteration and no longer claims truncation; both projections clamp at the one `MAX_TIMELINE_PERIODS = 60` budget, so neither can advertise a span the other does not reach. And a contract whose cycle does **not** repeat passes no `horizonUntil` at all, so every horizon rule that existed before the ticket — the Member-level Billing Simulation (#629) and the Promotion preview (#922) included — is untouched. The second half is the grouping, and it decides nothing: which iteration a billing **date** falls in is `planDurationCycleIteration()` (`domain/planDuration.ts`, the date-keyed counterpart of stage 2's period-keyed row rule, over the same `elapsedCycles()` walk — a card section has no period numbers to divide, since a date may be a Product's rather than the fee's), reported per `BillingEventDate`, with the cycle itself reported beside it from the very `timelineCycleFor()` value the fee table carries. `billingEventCycleCells()` (`apps/admin/src/lib/billingEventSimulation.ts`) works out only which card carries the number and which one closes the line, in the fee table's own `ExampleTimelineRowCycle` shape, because it is one grouping language over two layouts — and the number is the **real** iteration, so an Assigned Plan's forecast starting mid-contract reads `3`/`4`. The look is §2's to the letter: a 30px gutter, one `1`/`2` marker and one 1px line per iteration in `TIMELINE_CYCLE_LINE`/`TIMELINE_CYCLE_TEXT` **imported** from `ExampleTimeline.tsx` rather than re-spelled, a gap at the foot of an iteration's last card so the two lines never touch, **no** horizontal divider, no bordered container, no table and no change to the cards themselves (#955's collapsible cards, their expand state and their per-date totals are untouched; expanding still fetches nothing). The marker under the cards is the page's rendered note from the **same** `timeline_cycle_repeats` / `timeline_cycle_once` key the table above resolves, so one card says one thing twice and the component chooses nothing — no new locale key in this stage. A **Promotion** reports `cycle: null`, passes no note and renders exactly as before, because it has no Billing & Duration of its own. One small refactor came with it: `periodContaining()` moved from `assignmentExampleTimeline.ts` into the shared `exampleTimeline.ts`, because both of an Assigned Plan's projections start at the period containing today and must count their displayed iterations from the same place. Tests: `api/src/test/plan-cycle-billing-events.unit.test.ts` (new, 20 — the two-iteration span and the `truncated: false` that goes with it, the last card equalling the last fee period for three duration shapes, the 36-period cycle shown without truncation, the yearly Product's floor bounded, a non-repeating contract's rules unchanged, a Plan with no durations ungrouped, a Promotion with no cycle, the iteration helper in both directions, the per-card numbering including mid-iteration starts, and the source gates: no iteration arithmetic or glyph in the component or either page, no hex and no `border` in the grouping's styles, still one `<table>`, the gutter's accessible name read from `col_cycle`, and both cards passing the marker), in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only (#1009's reason). Green: API unit suites 102 files / 1,578 tests, admin suite 161 files / 2,485 tests, `tsc --noEmit` clean in `api`, `apps/admin` and `apps/member`. The API integration suite was not executed here — this container has no MySQL and no Docker daemon — so CI is its first run; no router, route shape or payload changed beyond the two additive fields (`cycle` on the projection and on each group), and the #1123 gate asserting the member's forecast equals the staff route's still holds because both come from the one adapter. Docs: `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/architecture.md`. `docs/payments.md` is deliberately untouched: stage 3 changes nothing that is charged. **That completes the ticket's three agreed stages.**)

- **Done**: #1122 §1–§6 (**Add Plan with Promotions from the Members App** — stage 4 of the #1121 family, over #1108 stage 2. `GET /me/membership-plans` lists the active public plans with the Promotions compatible with each (`api/src/domain/memberPlanCatalogue.ts` decides what is offerable and the first-cycle preview price), `POST /me/membership-plans/:id/assign` writes the member's own Draft exactly as the staff path does, applies the chosen Promotions with their snapshots and runs Save & Pay, so the member lands in Pending Payment and pays through the same Pay now (`GET /me/membership` reports `pending_membership`); a free first cycle activates straight away; the one-plan rule is the member's too (409 unless confirmed). The Members App's membership page gains *+ Add plan*, the catalogue (`components/MemberPlanCatalogue.tsx` over `lib/memberPlanCatalogue.ts`), the choose dialog and the pending-payment card. §7/§8 shipped earlier (#1163), which completes the ticket.)

- **Done**: #1122 §7/§8 (**Past Membership Plans in the Members App.** The half of the ticket the owner answered `§7 member` on, and the one my staging note said was independent of every other stage — §1–§6 (Add Plan with Promotions from the Members App) stays stage 4 of the #1121 family, after #1108's Draft/*Save & Pay*. No migration, no new route, no new business rule: `GET /me/membership` reports `past_memberships` beside `membership`. Three of its answers are the design rather than the mechanics. **It adds no second "which plan is the member's"**: the history is the *tail* of the one ordering that already decides which assignment the page is about (`MEMBER_CURRENT_ASSIGNMENT_ORDER` + `MEMBER_CURRENT_ASSIGNMENT_FILTER`, #1123/#1108), split by the pure `api/src/domain/memberPlanHistory.ts` — so a `WHERE status = 'cancelled'` of its own is what would have described the same assignment twice the day a member's only plan is cancelled, and a Draft reaches neither half. **A past plan carries no money and no action** (§8: the section exists for historical visibility): a row is a name, a status and the day it ended (`ends_at`, else `closed_at`, and `null` rather than a fall-back to the day it *started*), so there is no fee to resolve per row and no pill to compete with the current plan. And **the card is the app's one collapsible chrome**: `components/MemberCollapsibleCard.tsx` is promoted out of #1123's Payments card (the way the Admin's `Tabs` was promoted out of `LibraryTabs`, #961) and both wear it, so the app keeps one expand affordance; a member with no history gets **no card at all**, since a collapsed card that opens onto nothing is vertical space spent saying nothing. `lib/memberPlans.ts` decides and `components/PastMembershipPlansCard.tsx` draws, the #1123 split. Tests: `api/src/test/member-past-plans.unit.test.ts` (new, 18 — both pure halves, the ordering, the date fallback, the two locale keys, and the two prohibitions: no `t()` or colour in either half, no client-side status filter) and four integration cases appended to `api/src/test/me-membership-snapshot.test.ts` (the split, the `ends_at`/`closed_at` fallback, a Draft in neither half, no price on a past row, and an empty history). Both in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only (#1009's reason). Green: API unit suites 90 files / 1,381 tests, Members App suite 20 files / 435 tests, `tsc --noEmit` clean in `api` and `apps/member`, `next build` clean. The API integration suite was not executed here — this container has no MySQL and no Docker daemon — so CI is its first run. Docs: `CLAUDE.md` (the new rule), `docs/architecture.md`. **Deliberately out of scope**: §1–§6, and any change to which assignment the card above is about — a member whose only plan is cancelled still reads it there, which is the existing rule `GET /me/membership` and the Payments forecast already share.)

- **Done**: #1184 stages 1–2 (`% Discount` offered on a Membership Plan benefit again, reversing #997; stage 2 adds the per-benefit **Mandatory** Yes/No, migration 237 — see CLAUDE.md; stage 3, declining optional benefits at assignment, follows); earlier, #997 (**A Membership Plan benefit is `No benefit` or `Waive`; `% Discount` is retired, not deleted.** #896 §16 gave a Plan three of the five treatments a Product can carry inside a relationship, and this ticket drops the third: a Plan benefit describes what the membership *includes*, so a percentage off one line of it was a third price beside the Product's own and the Promotion's. **No migration, no backfill, no coercion** — which is the whole of §6. Migration 203's `chk_<table>_action` keeps permitting all three on the six Plan-side tables, because a row holding a percentage has to stay valid, and the route rather than the CHECK is what refuses a new one; the two prohibitions §6 states are what forced that shape, since converting a stored 20% to `Waive` makes a €20 item free and reading it as `No benefit` charges €20 for a line agreed at €16. So `api/src/domain/productBenefitActions.ts` now draws two sets per context instead of one — `PLAN_BENEFIT_ACTIONS` (the offered pair), `LEGACY_PLAN_BENEFIT_ACTIONS` (`percentage_discount`) and `STORED_PLAN_BENEFIT_ACTIONS`, with `isBenefitActionAllowed()` answering the write question and `isStoredBenefitAction()` the read one, which `toProductBenefit()` asks. It is the split `domain/productFrequency.ts` already draws for #945/#821's retired Billing Frequencies, down to the `(next, current)` write rule: `keepsRetiredBenefit()` accepts a retired action only when the action *and* its value are exactly what the line stores, so a legacy line's quantity stays editable, a new percentage is a 400 naming the two that remain, and moving a stored 20% to 50% is a 400 too — the section `PUT` reads those pairs with `loadStoredPlanBenefitPairs()` before validating, while the `FOR UPDATE` read inside the transaction stays the only thing that decides what a *kept* line is written with. A legacy line therefore keeps pricing, displaying, snapshotting and billing exactly as stored, in the sections, the Billing Event Simulation, the Assigned Plan snapshot and the nightly run. In the editor the stored value is a **disabled** option of the shared `ProductBenefits.tsx` select with its number beside it as read-only text rather than the percentage input §2 forbids — dropping the option would make the row read *No benefit* and the first unrelated save would make that true — and the read-only card still reports it through `benefitTreatmentLabel()`, because a card says what a line costs. §6's identification step is `npm run plans:percentage-benefits` (`api/src/scripts/report-plan-percentage-benefits.ts`), a read-only report listing every Plan line still carrying one, correctable in the Plan's Benefit section, and separately every Assigned Plan snapshot line, which is what those members were *agreed* at and is left alone. §8 leaves **Promotions** untouched: all five actions, `isRetiredBenefitAction()` false in that context, one stored vocabulary still. Tests: a `#997` block in `api/src/test/product-benefit-actions.unit.test.ts` (+9 — the two sets and the retired one, the read/write gates diverging, the keep rule in both directions and on a line that stores nothing, `current` not laundering a §16 action or loosening the ordinary value rules, and a legacy line still pricing at its discount) and 7 integration cases in `api/src/test/membership-plan-benefits.test.ts` — a new `#997` describe (a stored percentage read back as itself, the 400 on a fresh line with the section left untouched, the 400 on a different percentage, the quantity-only save keeping the pair, the stored pair carried back unchanged, and the correction to `Waive` with no way back) plus the refusal in the existing benefit-actions block, with the four #916 price cases that configured a percentage through the `PUT` now seeding it directly, which is the only way such a line can exist from now on and a better test of §6 than the old one. `apps/admin/src/test/product-benefit-actions-ui.test.ts` gains the mirror and editor assertions (+5). API unit suites green at 814 across 56 files, admin at 2,048 across 137 files, `tsc --noEmit` clean in both. The integration file was not executed here — this environment has no MySQL and no Docker daemon — so CI is its first run.)
  _(Issue #1184 is still open: the remaining stages are listed in `docs/roadmap.md`.)_

- **Done**: #1152 (**Members App typography and Section Card text settings.** The `Text (Members App)` section reads *Typography (Members App)* (six locale values, the `group_members_text` id untouched); each Title carries a Font Family beside its Color, sourced from the same typography level's `fontFamily` (a typography source names its `field` now) and written into the level's own `--gd-font-h1..h3`, which the Members App's headings read for the first time (`fontFamily: memberTheme.titleNFont` beside every Title colour); and the Section Cards section holds the text's Color (from `colors.textColor`), Size, Font Family (from `typography.body.fontFamily`), Vertical Position and Horizontal Position. Size and the two positions are the model's one amendment: `source: null` plus a `default` that is today's rendering (13px, centred both ways), since the Admin app has no Section Cards and nothing honest to inherit from — the thread's `Q1 members-only`, taken with its recommendation. Three value types (`font-size` 8–48, `align-v`, `align-h`) are validated by the API and mapped to CSS by both resolvers (`alignmentCssValue()`), so the variable carries the `justify-content`/`text-align` value and `memberChrome.ts` maps nothing; `sectionCardText` there is spread by `MembersSectionCard` under the caller's style, the navigation tile spells none of the five, and the dashboard's products card keeps its left-aligned structured body (#1116) while taking the colour and font through its title. No migration, no API surface, no second font catalogue. Gate: `api/src/test/members-app-typography.unit.test.ts`, plus the three apps' own parity tests.)

- **Done**: #1158 (**My Next Bookings has a background image of its own.** The eighth Members App image slot, `next_bookings` (migration 235 widens `chk_theme_member_images_slot`; `MEMBER_IMAGE_SLOTS` and the Admin and Members App mirrors gain it; *My Next Bookings* in en/es/ca). The dashboard's Next Booking card renders through `MembersSectionCard slot="next_bookings"` in all three states, so an uploaded image is its background at full opacity with the contents untouched, and with no image it is the Section Card surface every other card already has — no background colour setting of its own. The one deviation from the ticket is forced: it asked for `members_app/bookings.png`, which is the My Bookings tile's key since #725, so a second card stored under it would share that tile's artwork — exactly what §6 forbids — and the slot is named for the card it paints instead (`next_bookings.png` beside `bookings.png`, each overwritten in place under its deterministic key, no UUID, no orphan). The Members App heading reads *My Next Bookings* too, so the slot label names the section the app does (#1151's rule).)

- **Done**: #1162 (**Booking cancellation grace period, and `booked_on` in both apps.** `api/src/domain/bookingCancellation.ts` decides a member's own cancellation — allowed while the event is 24 hours away or the booking is under 2 hours old, refused otherwise — from seconds computed in SQL (`api/src/api/booking-cancellation.ts`), enforced on `DELETE /me/bookings/:id` (`409 cancellation_window_closed`) and reported as `can_cancel` / `cancellation_block` by `GET /me/schedule` and `GET /me/bookings` beside `booked_on`, which is the existing `calendar_event_bookings.booked_at` (written at booking and at waitlist promotion) and nothing new; the staff `GET /bookings` reports it too and the Admin calendar's enrolled rows read *Booked on:*. The Members App shows *Booked on* in the booking details and, where the Cancel action was, the explanation (`lib/bookingCancellation.ts` picks the key; the Professional-Service wording follows the API's flag). A waiting-list place stays leavable until the event starts. No migration. Note for the record: no 24-hour rule existed before — the ticket's §2 is the rule now.)

- **Done**: #1181 (**Personal Goals — Duplicate, per-gym activation and the standard table header.** Migration 236 adds `gym_personal_goals` (`gym_professional_services`' shape: UNIQUE `(gym_id, personal_goal_id)`, `active | inactive`, #799 actor pairs), backfilled with an active row per gym × visible goal and seeded at gym creation; every reader resolves `COALESCE(gpg.status, 'active')` through `gymGoalStatusSql()`, so no row means active. `POST /personal-goals/:id/activate` / `/deactivate` upsert that row (idempotent, audited as a gym-level change, the System row untouched, another gym's state independent); an inactive goal is refused on `POST /member-personal-goals` and `POST /me/personal-goals` (`409 goal_inactive`) and absent from `/available`, existing assignments untouched. `POST /personal-goals/:id/duplicate` copies any visible goal — System ones included — into a new active gym-owned `<name> - Copy`, image object copied into the gym's own key. The admin list reads Name · Description · Target · Created At · Created By · Status · Actions, the `⋮` offers Duplicate and Deactivate (danger) / Activate, and the pickers offer active goals only. Nutrition Goals and the platform library are untouched; `GYM_CONFIGURABLE_GOAL_KINDS` is the declaration, mirrored in `goalProfile.ts`.)

- **Done**: #1085 (**Clerk's webhook arrives through the admin app, so Clerk needs no public API.** The second of the three callers #1083 began on: Clerk's dashboard endpoint moves from `https://api.<host>/webhooks/clerk` to the admin app's `POST /api/webhooks/clerk`, which relays the request to the API's existing route at `CORDEL_FITNESS_API_URL`. `/api/proxy` is deliberately not reusable — it forwards `authorization`/`x-gym-id`/`x-center-id`/`x-impersonate-as`/`x-locale` and **none** of the `svix-*` headers, so a webhook through it would arrive unsigned — so the relay is its own route over a pure decision module (`apps/admin/src/lib/clerkWebhookRelay.ts`: what is forwarded, where, and the two failure statuses), the WP2/WP3 two-module split for the same reason, with the route as the I/O half deciding nothing. Four of its properties are the design rather than the mechanics. **The relay verifies nothing**: body and the three `svix-*` headers cross byte for byte (`req.arrayBuffer()` in, the same buffer out — the signature is over `svix-id.svix-timestamp.<body>`, so a parse-and-re-serialize would break an HMAC this app cannot recompute, #830's rule applied to a request), `verifyWebhook()` is still the API route's first operation over `express.raw`, and `CLERK_WEBHOOK_SIGNING_SECRET` never leaves the API — a tampered body is still a 400 *from the API*, relayed as such. **Clerk gets the API's own status code**, never a blanket 200, because Clerk retries exactly what we report as failed and a swallowed `user.deleted` leaves an orphaned Clerk account linked to rows that should have been cleaned up (#709); an unreachable API is a **502** and an unset `CORDEL_FITNESS_API_URL` a **500**, both retried and both replayable from the endpoint's *Replay → Replay missing messages*. **Nothing of the caller's crosses the boundary**: the forwarded set is the three Svix headers plus the content type, declared once, with no cookie, Authorization or `host`. And the path is exempt from `auth.protect()` **and** from the locale routing, through the same early `return NextResponse.next()` `/api/proxy` uses — one without the other answers Clerk with a 401 or a redirect to `/en/…`. The **admin** app rather than the members app, as the ticket chose: the two have identical middleware and proxy, so there is no technical difference and one endpoint is one thing to keep in step with the Clerk dashboard. No migration, no API change and no change to either webhook branch — the trade-off the ticket names is real and unchanged: the webhook now depends on the admin app being up, with Clerk's retries, the replay and `POST /staff/link`'s sign-in fallback as the three things that make a missed delivery a delay rather than a lost link. Tests: `api/src/test/clerk-webhook-relay.unit.test.ts` (new, 23 — a gate scanning both admin files and the middleware, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only: the route exists on admin and not on member, answers POST alone, names no API origin, forwards bytes and never `req.text()`, relays `res.status` and spells no 200, names neither the signing secret nor a verifier, keeps the decision half pure, and the middleware exempts the path from both guards with the early return still ahead of the i18n call) plus two admin files: `apps/admin/src/test/clerk-webhook-relay.test.ts` (new, 11 — the header filter including the case-insensitive and drop-the-caller's-credentials cases, the target with and without a trailing slash, `null` for an unset base, and the two failure statuses) and `clerk-webhook-relay-route.test.ts` (new, 8 — the route itself against a stubbed `fetch`: the URL and method, the forwarded header set, a body of bytes that are **not** valid UTF-8 arriving unchanged, a relayed 200/400/500, the 502 on a rejected fetch and the 500 that calls out at all). `tsc --noEmit` clean in both `api/` and `apps/admin`, both unit suites green (API 1153 tests across 78 files; admin 2392 across 151) and `npm run build` clean in `apps/admin`, where the route appears as a dynamic `/api/webhooks/clerk`. The API's own integration suite was not executed here (no MySQL and no Docker daemon in this environment, so CI is its first run) — nothing in this ticket touches API code. Docs: `docs/decisions.md` #20, `docs/architecture.md` (a Clerk webhooks row of its own, the tree, the `CLERK_WEBHOOK_SIGNING_SECRET` row) and `docs/go-to-production.md` §2. **Not verified here, and the ticket's remaining acceptance:** pointing the Clerk dashboard endpoint at the new URL per environment and seeing a `user.created`/`user.deleted` land through it — that is an owner-side setting plus a deployed admin app, and it is the §2 item. **Not covered:** the GitHub Actions nightly runs (#1086) still call the API directly, so this does not yet make the API private — #1087 is that open question.)

- **Done**: #1077 (**Mobile builds** — Cordel → Mobile builds, a superadmin-only page listing the test builds `mobile-build.yml` publishes, newest first, each with a Download button.) Read-only and **no table**: the workflow uploads `cordel/mobile-builds/<app id>/<platform>/<file>` plus a `<file>.json` sidecar to the environment's bucket and prunes to the newest 20 per app and platform; `GET /platform/mobile-builds` lists the bucket and `…/:id/download` streams one file. Builds are versioned (`apps/mobile/package.json` version + the workflow run number) and `dev` builds the separate `com.cordel.fitness.dev` app. Only Android is published; an iPhone gets an *Open in TestFlight* button (`MOBILE_TESTFLIGHT_URL`, disabled until the Apple account and app exist). Not verified end to end until a workflow run has published to a real bucket. See `docs/architecture.md` ("Mobile builds") and `docs/mobile-runbook.md` §5b.)
  _(Issue #1077 is still open: the remaining stages are listed in `docs/roadmap.md`.)_

- **Done**: #964 (**The Free Exercise DB is the Base Exercise catalogue.** 876 exercises are imported from the public [free-exercise-db](https://github.com/yuhonas/free-exercise-db) dataset by `npm run exercises:import-free-db`, reading the repository's structured `dist/exercises.json` rather than a scraped page (§2), into the **existing** Base Exercises (§11) — not one statement touches a row with a `gym_id`, so Custom Gym Exercises, training plans and assigned exercises are out of it by construction (§1, §14). Migration 209 makes it repeatable: `source` + `source_id` carry the dataset's identifier, `slug` is the application handle generated once from the name (§5's own `alternate-incline-dumbbell-curl`), and `equipment`, `category`, `level`, `mechanic` and `force_type` preserve the five metadata values verbatim (§6; `force` is a MySQL reserved word). Uniqueness is two VIRTUAL generated columns in migration 183's shape — `base_source_key` scoped to base rows and deliberately *including* deleted ones, so an import somebody soft-deleted is skipped rather than resurrected (§13), and `base_slug_key` among live base rows. Matching follows §12's order (provenance → slug → exact case-folded name) and the two fallbacks only adopt a row with no provenance of its own; a name or slug already claimed by another source id is **reported as a potential duplicate and left alone**, never merged. One pure module, `domain/freeExerciseDb.ts`, decides every mapping — the script is only I/O — including the muscle rule §8 asks for: `quadriceps`/`abdominals` reuse the existing `quads`/`core`, every other source muscle becomes a key of its own (`MUSCLE_KEYS` gained `lats`, `middle_back`, `lower_back`, `traps`, `forearms`, `adductors`, `abductors`, `neck`, with labels in en/es/ca so the picker offers them), a muscle outside the list is still stored on the link and reported rather than failing the run, and `lats` / `middle back` / `lower back` deliberately do not collapse into `back`. **§7's Exercise Type taxonomy does not exist in this product** — the list it gives (Bodyweight, Machine, Dumbbell, …) is the dataset's *equipment* axis, and the only column ever named after an exercise type is migration 071's unread `reps`/`time`/`distance` — so the ticket's own closing rule decided it: the equipment axis is preserved verbatim in a column, and **no exercise-level type is written at all** — migration 074 (#154) dropped `exercises.exercise_type`, so there is nothing to write it to, and the dataset's `category` is preserved verbatim and mapped onto nothing rather than deriving a value for a column the schema does not have. Instructions become one numbered-per-line `description` with the wording untouched (§9). **No image is imported** (§10): the dataset's `images` field is not carried into the parsed shape, so no media column and no raw GitHub URL can be written, and a test greps both files for them. The importer also never overwrites what an administrator edited — an update fills an empty name or description, keeps the source's own facts in step and adds missing muscle links without removing any — so a second run over the real dataset reports 876 *unchanged* (§2, §17), and one bad record never aborts the run: every exercise is its own transaction and the report prints Created / Updated / Unchanged / Skipped / Failed, the new muscles, the unmapped and ambiguous types, the potential duplicates and the deleted-locally skips, with a non-zero exit on any failure (§16, §21). §18 is answered at the API level — `GET /platform/exercises` now filters on `q` (name **or** slug), `muscle`/`primary_muscle`/`secondary_muscle` (comma-separated or repeated), `equipment`, `category`, `level`, `mechanic` and `source` — while the inline filter UI stays #969's and the expanded view's new fields #965's, since §20 forbids redesigning the page here. Exercise names are English-only, as §4/§19 require: there is no exercise translation junction table to write to (that is #967), so the base `name` column is the single value and no translation is invented. Tests: `api/src/test/free-exercise-db.unit.test.ts` (40 unit — the images guard, validation, the slug and its stable disambiguation, every source muscle mapping to an offered key, the primary/secondary split, the type mapping including cardio and the equipment refusal, instruction order, the matching precedence, the adopt/never-overwrite/idempotent/skip-deleted plans, the locale labels and migration 209's own shape) and `api/src/test/base-exercise-source-filters.test.ts` (10 integration — every filter, the multi-value shapes, the 400, auth and a gym row that may never appear). Verified against the real dataset offline: 876 parsed, 876 created, 0 failed, 0 unknown muscles, 0 unmapped types, 14 ambiguous (cardio), 876 unique slugs, second pass all unchanged.)

- **Done**: #964 follow-up (**The importer could not be run.** #964 was reopened with *"Exercises have not been imported"*, and the catalogue was empty for two reasons, neither of them in the import logic. First, a real defect: every script under `api/src/scripts/` wrote `import { config } from 'dotenv'` above `import { db } from '../infra/db'` and called `config()` in the body — but imports run first, and `infra/db.ts` builds its pool at module scope, so `npm run exercises:import-free-db` with a correct `api/.env` died on *"CORDEL_FITNESS_DB_HOST, _USER, _PASSWORD and _NAME environment variables are required"* before reading a row. All five scripts now use the bare `import 'dotenv/config';` that `src/app.ts` and `src/infra/seed.ts` already used, placed before any `../` import, and `api/src/test/script-env-loading.unit.test.ts` fails the build if the deferred form comes back. Second, there was no way to run it against the deployed database at all: it is VCN-private (the reason `deploy.yml` runs migrations from the VPS), and the runtime image carries neither `tsx` nor `src/`, so even an SSH session had to know the compiled path. `.github/workflows/exercises-import.yml` is that path — `workflow_dispatch` only (a catalogue import is an operator action, not a nightly job), the environment picked per run, `dry_run` **true** by default so a dispatch nobody thought about reports rather than writes, no `podman pull` so it runs the image actually deployed, and `node dist/scripts/import-free-exercise-db.js` as the entry point, which the same test keeps in step with `package.json`. Verified end to end against a real MySQL 8.4 and the live dataset: 876 parsed, 876 created, 0 failed, 0 new muscles, 0 potential duplicates; the second pass 876 *unchanged*; the compiled entry point and `--dry-run` exercised exactly as the workflow invokes them.)

- **Done**: #369 (4-Week Billing/Duration Option: investigation found Membership Plans already support an arbitrary 4-week period today via the existing generic `billing_policies.recurring_billing_interval`/`recurring_billing_unit` fields — `advanceBillingDate()` in `billing.ts` already advances `unit='week'` by `interval * 7` days, so interval=4/unit='week' correctly yields 28 days and is never conflated with a calendar month; no Plan-side schema or code change was needed. Products' `billing_frequency` had no such interval multiplier — it's a fixed single-value enum used only as a descriptive label (actual entitlement length is tracked separately via `validity_days`) — so migration 123 widens the `gym_charges_billing_frequency_check` CHECK constraint to add `'four_weeks'` alongside the existing `once/per_session/week/month/year` (renumbered from 122 to 123 after merging #370, which independently claimed migration 122 for `enrollment_status`); `VALID_FREQUENCIES` in `products.ts` and `FREQUENCIES` in the admin Products page updated to match; `frequency_four_weeks` i18n key added to en/es/ca under the `products` namespace; `advanceBillingDate` exported and covered by a new unit-test file `billing-date-math.test.ts` (5 tests, no DB) confirming 4×week = 28 days and that it never equals a 1-month advance; `gym-charges.test.ts` extended with POST/PUT `billing_frequency=four_weeks` acceptance tests and an invalid-value rejection test).

- **Done**: #371 (System Product — Personal Training Class Package (10 Sessions): data/setup-only migration 124 backfills one system Product per existing gym into `gym_charges` — `type='sessions'`, `units=10`, `is_system=1`, `status='active'`, `enrollment_status='staff_only'` (maps the ticket's "Active — Only Internal" onto the split introduced by #370: staff can sell it internally, Members cannot purchase it directly), `validity_days=182` (≈6 calendar months — `gym_charges` has no month-based validity field, only a fixed day count), `tax_rate_id` set to each gym's own system default tax rate (same lookup migration 113 used); `amount` left `NULL` so price stays configurable via the normal Product admin flow; no API, UI, or model changes — `gym_charges.name` has no localization mechanism today so no translated-name column was added; idempotent via `NOT EXISTS` guard, `down` deletes the seeded rows; migration renumbered from 123 to 124 after merging #369, which independently claimed migration 123 for the four-week billing frequency).
