Source labels in the list become **System sourced** (`gym_id IS NULL` or `cloned_from_id` set) and **Custom** — a label change only, the ownership model is untouched. `POST /exercises/import-defaults` and its `DEFAULT_EXERCISES` list are removed.)

- **Done**: #720 (Workout exercise rows show the media the exercise already carries: every endpoint that returns a workout exercise — the Workout Template and Training Plan JSON trees, the Training Plan Template tree, the block-exercise list and the create/update/move/duplicate responses — now projects `exercise_image_url` and `exercise_video_url` straight off `exercises`, so no workout view looks media up per row. One shared `apps/admin/src/components/ExerciseMediaThumbnails.tsx` renders them at the right of the row in all four admin views (Workouts/Training Plan builder, Workout Templates tree, Training Plan Templates tree, member Training Plan block exercises): a lazy-loaded image thumbnail, and a video tile with a play indicator that links out — never a `<video>` element, so no MP4 is downloaded to draw a row. A YouTube video URL gets its poster from `img.youtube.com`; anything else falls back to a plain play tile. No schema change and no source/ownership logic in the workout layer — the 512×512 image thumbnail and the stored video poster arrive with #719, and the component prefers them as soon as the API returns them.)

- **Done**: #722 (Member app Nutrition foods are horizontal, swipeable image cards: `GET /me/nutrition-plan` now projects the Nutrition Library item's own `image_url` on every meal item and attaches its nutritional qualities (#644) — read once for the whole plan, not per item, so no third level of N+1 and no extra request per food. Two new Member components, `NutritionFoodCard` (square image with a placeholder fallback, name, quantity+unit, role, quality chips — every optional line omitted when absent) and `NutritionFoodCarousel` (one carousel per meal, the meal's foods being the alternatives a member chooses between). The swipe is the browser's own: a flex row with CSS scroll snapping, no gesture library and no `touchmove`/`preventDefault`, so vertical page scrolling is untouched; the buttons, dots and the `aria-live` counter all read the real scroll position. Images lazy-load except the first card. No schema change, no new endpoint, and the Nutrition data model is untouched.)

- **Done**: #723 (Members → My Training Plan shows each exercise's image and video, inside the exercise's own row: no API or schema change was needed — `GET /me/training-plans` reads the same `PLAN_TREE_SELECT` that already projects `exercise_image_url`/`exercise_video_url` off `exercises` (#720), so the media arrives with the plan tree and costs no request per exercise. Three new Member files: `lib/exerciseMedia.ts` (pure helpers — thumbnail-over-master, YouTube id/poster/embed, and the video *kind* that decides how a URL can be played), `components/ExerciseMedia.tsx` (a lazy-loaded image tile and a video tile with a play indicator, rendered next to the exercise's sets and reps; nothing at all when the exercise has no media, and a broken thumbnail hides itself without touching the other) and `components/ExerciseMediaViewer.tsx` (the larger views — an overlay, never a route, so the member keeps their position, the selected weekday and their scroll: Escape and a click outside close it, the close button takes focus and hands it back to the tile). A YouTube video plays in a `youtube-nocookie` embed, an object in R2 in a `<video controls preload="metadata">`, and a URL the app cannot embed opens in a new tab; nothing autoplays and no `<video>` is mounted to draw a row. The hierarchy, ordering and weekday navigation of the plan are untouched, and the Member app resolves nothing about where the media came from — no System/Custom, Base/Gym, import or inheritance logic.)

- **Done**: #724 (Training Plans' filters and list header adopt the Assigned Plans pattern: the five filters (Name, Member, Source, Author, Status) move out of the page header into a labelled filter bar — each with its label above its control — and the naked row of small sort buttons becomes the list's own column-header band. Two shared pieces came out of Assigned Plans to make that reuse rather than a copy: `components/FilterBar.tsx` (`FilterBar`/`FilterField` + `filterControlStyle`, now used by both pages) and `components/listChrome.ts`, the surface/header band/cell insets/row dividers `DataTable` itself is built from, so a card list wears the same chrome without restating a grey or a padding. Header and rows share one `LIST_COLUMNS`-derived grid and one `overflow-x: auto` wrapper (#637's rule), so titles line up with their values and a narrow viewport scrolls the list, not the page. Presentation only: the same five filters with the same behaviour, the same sort keys, the same columns, the same values (member and description on a second line under the plan name, the end date under the start date) and the same expand/edit/context-menu behaviour; the page title and `+ New Training Plan` are untouched.)

- **Done**: #757 (Website registration joins the **gym** only: `center_id` removed from `POST /public/gyms/:gymRef/registrations` (one sent by an older site is dropped, not rejected), no center resolution or center-related 400s, `gym_signup` metadata is `{ gym_id, name }`; first sign-in (`/me/link`) creates the member with no `member_centers` row. Single-active-center gyms keep working through `centerContext`'s sole-center fallback; in multi-center gyms staff assign the center. No migration.)

- **Done**: #759 (Member app `/link`: an invitation link opened while a *different* account is signed in no longer links that account (404 → "Algo salió mal", invitation left unused). When the URL carries `__clerk_ticket` and someone is signed in, the page signs them out — `signOut` callback, so no Clerk redirect — and reloads the same URL, which redeems the ticket. No API change.)

- **Done**: #715 (Base Nutrition Library foods carry an image: a base food is a `gym_id IS NULL` row and belongs to no gym, so its image lives in the platform's own R2 folder at `cordel/Nutrition/<food_id>-<sanitized name>.png` — `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT` (#732), never a gym prefix. No migration: the existing `nutrition_library_items.image_url` column (migration 138) holds the Cloudflare URL for a base food exactly as it already does for a gym-owned one, and the row's ownership decides the folder. `POST /platform/nutrition-library/:id/image` (superadmin) validates the bytes rather than the request — PNG signature, 512×512 from the IHDR, an alpha channel — writes the two folder markers, uploads, and only then updates the row, so a refused upload leaves the existing image untouched; a food renamed since its last upload has its old object swept best-effort afterwards. The expanded food card on Cordel → Base Nutrition Library shows the image over a checkerboard (so transparency reads as transparency) with an **Upload Image** button that states the required 512×512 transparent PNG and checks both before sending. `npm run nutrition:base-images` is the one-time backfill: it discovers the foods from the database, takes artwork from `--from <dir>` when supplied and otherwise renders a consistent stylized illustration offline (`domain/nutritionImageArt.ts` — no AI integration, per `CLAUDE.md`), uploads and persists each one, skips foods that already have an image, keeps going after a failure and reports discovered/present/generated/uploaded/failed with each failure's id and name. **The run itself is a production step** — it needs the real base library and `CLOUDFLARE_R2_*` credentials, neither of which a PR session has — and is tracked in `docs/go-to-production.md`.)

- **Done**: #716 (Base Exercises carry the same image pair: a base exercise is a `gym_id IS NULL` row and belongs to no gym, so its master and thumbnail live in the platform's own R2 folder at `cordel/Exercises/Images/<id>-<Name>[-thumbnail].png` — `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT` (#732), never a gym prefix — built by the new `domain/baseExerciseImages.ts` off the same `Exercises/Images` leaf and the same name sanitizer the gym side uses. No migration: `exercises.image_url` + `image_thumbnail_url` (migration 187) hold the resulting Cloudflare URLs for a base row exactly as they do for a gym-owned one, and no object-key column was added (§7). `POST`/`DELETE /platform/exercises/:id/image` (superadmin) are the only writer and clearer; the pair is prepared in the browser by #719's own `lib/exerciseImageUpload.ts` and re-validated by #719's own `validateExerciseImagePair()` — #716 §2/§3 are #719 §5 word for word, so there is one implementation of "a valid exercise image", not two. Each router answers for its own rows: the platform route is 404 for a gym's exercise, the gym route 403 for a base one. Stale objects are swept with the mirror of the gym rule — `isPlatformOwnedExerciseImageUrl()` refuses a gym object and an external URL — plus a reference check that spans **every** tenant, because `POST /exercises/import` copies media *references* and each gym that imported the exercise points at the platform's own object; removal clears both references with no fallback. Cordel → Base Exercises becomes an expandable list like Base Nutrition Library: the image, `Upload Image`/`Replace`, `Remove` and `View full size` (the only thing that fetches the 2048px master) live on the expanded card, name/description edit **inline** and the `CrudModal`s are gone (the ticket's Q4 answer), and the card offers the shared View Audit Log deep link. **Generating the artwork is out of scope** (the thread's answer to Q1/Q2): no generator, no backfill script, so an existing base exercise stays image-less until an administrator uploads a master — tracked in `docs/go-to-production.md`. 35 integration cases in `base-exercise-images.test.ts`, 15 unit cases for the platform keys and ownership test, 12 structural cases in the admin app's `base-exercise-images.test.ts`.)

- **Done**: #767 (Standing decision on the CodeQL `js/xss-through-dom` alert the browser media-upload helpers raise, written down rather than re-derived per PR: `docs/architecture.md` gains a *Code scanning (CodeQL)* section — the two checks a PR carries (`Analyze` is the scan, `CodeQL` is the results check), the four routes an alert can take in order (fix the code; a human dismissal in Security → Code scanning, which needs `security_events: write` an agent does not have; an owner-authorized merge that records the assessment, which is how #764 landed; narrowing the query suite, for a systematically wrong rule only), and why the detached-media-element flow is a false positive — with the tell that the identical `img.src = url` in `exerciseImageUpload.ts` is unflagged because `new Image()` resolves to a known element type. No suppression comment is ever part of that: `// codeql[rule]` and `// lgtm[rule]` were both tried on the same alert and GitHub honoured neither, so the two inert `// lgtm[js/missing-rate-limiting]` comments on `/sellable-items` and `/taxes` are removed and `api/src/test/codeql-suppressions.unit.test.ts` fails the build if one returns. The rate-limiting claim those comments made is now a go-to-production item: `app.ts` applies a global `apiLimiter` (500 req / 15 min per IP) ahead of every route, so `/sellable-items` and `/taxes` were never the two unthrottled routes the comments implied. No API, schema or UI change.)

- **Done**: #719 part 1 (Gym Exercise images are a 2048×2048 master plus a 512×512 thumbnail: migration 187 adds `exercises.image_thumbnail_url` next to `image_url` — no backfill, and a NULL thumbnail is simply "no thumbnail", never "ask the library". `POST /exercises/:id/image` is the only writer of the pair: the **browser** draws the thumbnail from the master (`apps/admin/src/lib/exerciseImageUpload.ts`, per the ticket's Q2 answer — no `sharp` or `ffmpeg` in the API image) and uploads both in one JSON request, and the server re-validates each file from its own bytes (PNG signature, exact square, alpha channel) rather than from the `Content-Type` header or the file name. Both objects land under the gym's own prefix at `<prefix>/Exercises/Images/<id>-<Name>[-thumbnail].png`, never in `cordel/`; nothing is uploaded and nothing is written until both files pass, so a refused upload leaves the existing image exactly as it was. Replacement and removal are ownership-aware: the old objects go only after the row points at the new ones, only when they sit under this gym's prefix (a System `cordel/…` object, another gym's and an external link are never deleted) and only when no other exercise still references them — `duplicate`/`clone`/`import` copy media *references*, so two rows can share one object. `DELETE /exercises/:id/image` clears both references and does **not** fall back to the Base Exercise's media; re-importing is the supported way back. Uploading or removing never changes **System sourced** to **Custom**. The Exercises page manages the image through a dedicated control rather than a form field (so cancelling an edit can neither undo an upload nor re-apply a removed image), the Add modal stages the pair and uploads it the moment the exercise exists, and every workout projection now also returns `exercise_image_thumbnail_url` — which #720/#723 already preferred — so no list row downloads a 2048px master. **Part 2 landed next**; part 3 (import/re-import media copying) landed after #741.)

- **Done**: #719 part 2 (A Gym Exercise video is an MP4 plus the 512×512 poster the UI draws instead of downloading it: migration 188 adds `exercises.video_thumbnail_url` next to `video_url` — no backfill, so a YouTube or hand-typed link simply has no stored poster and the apps keep deriving one. `POST /exercises/:id/video` is the only writer of the pair: the **browser** captures one frame of the clip into a square canvas (`apps/admin/src/lib/exerciseVideoUpload.ts`, the same #719 Q2 answer — no `sharp`/`ffmpeg` in the API image) and uploads both files in one JSON request, and the server validates each from its own bytes — the MP4's `ftyp` brand, `moov` and `stsd` video sample entries (`api/src/domain/mp4Video.ts`, no `ffprobe`), the poster's PNG signature and exact 512×512 — never from the `Content-Type` header or the file name, so a QuickTime `.mov` renamed to `.mp4` and an audio-only file are both refused. `EXERCISE_VIDEO_MAX_MB` (default 50) bounds the upload, which the route parses with its own `express.json` limit. Both objects land under the gym's own prefix at `<prefix>/Exercises/Videos/<id>-<Name>.mp4` and `…-thumbnail.png`, never in `cordel/`; nothing is uploaded and nothing is written until both files pass. Replacement and removal reuse part 1's ownership rules unchanged — the old objects go only after the row points at the new ones, only when they are this gym's own, and only when no other exercise still references them — and `DELETE /exercises/:id/video` clears both references without falling back to the Base Exercise's media. Uploading or removing never changes **System sourced** to **Custom**. The Exercises page manages the video through a dedicated control beside the image one, the Add modal stages the pair, and every workout projection now also returns `exercise_video_thumbnail_url`, which #720/#723 already preferred. **Part 3 landed next**: import/re-import media copying, once #741 had merged.)

- **Done**: #719 part 3 (Import and **re-import** carry the media references: `POST /exercises/import` already copied a Base Exercise's four media references into the gym's new copy (§2 — references only, no System object is duplicated into a gym's folder) and the copy owns them from then on (§3). What part 3 adds is the way *back*: §12 makes re-importing the only supported restore path — there is deliberately no "restore System media" action and no runtime fallback — so an id the gym already has no longer just reports `skipped`. Its copy's media references are refreshed from the Base Exercise's current ones and come back under `refreshed`, making the response `{ imported, refreshed, skipped }`. One pure rule decides it (`api/src/domain/exerciseMediaImport.ts`): the image pair and the video pair move independently, and a pair the Base Exercise does not have restores nothing — a base row with no image leaves the gym's own upload exactly where it is, because a routine re-import must not delete work the gym did. A refresh touches media only: the name, description, defaults and `cloned_from_id` of a copy the gym may have edited are its own, so a row matched by name alone keeps reading as **Custom**. Objects the copy stopped pointing at are swept after the transaction commits, through part 1's ownership check — a `cordel/…` System object, another gym's object, an external link and an object another exercise still references are never deleted — and each refresh is audited as an `update`. `GET /exercises/base` now carries all four references plus `media_refreshable`, the SQL mirror of the same rule, so the Import modal enables an already-imported row only when a re-import would move something, badges it **Media update**, and resolves nothing itself; *Select all matching* stays away from those rows. 12 unit cases for the rule, 23 integration cases in `exercise-media-reimport.test.ts` (which mocks R2), and 5 more structural cases in the admin app. No migration — #719's own columns already hold everything.)

- **Done**: #635 (stage 1 — Membership Plans aligned with the Promotion benefit structure: migration 173 adds `free_months`/`paid_months`/`bonus_months` to `membership_plans` and the three Product-keyed benefit tables `membership_plan_session`/`_oneoff`/`_periodical`, the same shape migration 155 gave Promotions; new `GET`/`PUT /membership-plans/:id/{session,oneoff,periodical}-benefits` with the Promotion endpoints' exact contract (replace-all `{items:[{gym_charge_id,quantity}]}`, category checked via `classifyProduct()`, an already-attached item survives going inactive); `PUT /membership-plans/:id` accepts the three duration fields, clearing one back to NULL rather than 0; `/:id/duplicate` carries both; new `BILLING & DURATION`, `ONE-OFF`, `SESSION` and `PERIOD BENEFITS` sections on the Plans page, each with its own Edit/Save/Cancel and no modal, sharing the new `components/ProductBenefits.tsx` editor; no Membership Fee Benefits (Promotion-only) and no billing change — Included Services and Charge Benefits stay load-bearing until stage 4; 60 integration cases in `membership-plan-benefits.test.ts`, 17 structural cases in the admin app's `plans-benefit-sections.test.ts`)

- **Done**: #635 (stage 2 — the Assigned Plan keeps its own snapshot of the commercial configuration it was assigned with: migration 174 adds `free_months`/`paid_months`/`bonus_months` + `recurring_billing_interval`/`recurring_billing_unit` + `membership_fee_price` to `user_memberships`, the three benefit tables `user_membership_session`/`_oneoff`/`_periodical` (quantity plus the item's frozen `item_name`/`item_type`/`item_billing_frequency`/`unit_price`/`currency`, `gym_charge_id` FK deliberately without CASCADE so the record outlives a retired item), pricing columns on migration 156's `user_membership_promotion_*_snapshot` tables and on `user_membership_services`, and a non-destructive backfill of existing rows from the live catalogue (the #635 Q3 answer allowed hard-deleting them instead); `snapshotAssignedPlan()` in the new `api/src/api/assigned-plan-snapshot.ts` runs inside the assignment transaction of all three entry points (`POST /user-memberships`, `/:id/assign-new-plan`, `POST /membership-plans/:id/assign`), `snapshotPromotionGrants()` finally writes the migration-156 tables when a Promotion is applied, and attaching an Additional Periodic Service captures the item's price; `GET /user-memberships/:id` serves it as `snapshot` (with `snapshot_captured` marking a pre-migration row) and each service row carries a `snapshot` object; billing and the Billing Simulation still read the live catalogue — that cutover, with the fallback for pre-snapshot rows, is stage 3; 16 integration cases in `assigned-plan-snapshot.test.ts`)

- **Done**: #635 (stage 3 — billing reads the Assigned Plan snapshot: the Billing Simulation now takes the regular Membership Fee, the billing cadence, the Plan's One-off/Session/Period Benefits, each applied Promotion's grants and each attached service's price from the assignment's own rows, falling back to the live catalogue only for an assignment that captured none; the Plan's benefit sections become billable lines for the first time — charged, not free — and a Product carried by both the Plan and a Promotion is merged into one stream so it is never billed twice; the shared `ASSIGNMENT_CADENCE` SQL fragment (`COALESCE(um.recurring_billing_*, bp.recurring_billing_*)`) puts the nightly run, the Billing Events projection, the Billing Events page's scheduled rows, the Payments dashboard, the post-payment schedule advance and the first-payment webhook on the frozen cadence, each LEFT JOINing `billing_policies` so a deleted policy no longer strands assignments; no migration; fixes a pre-existing defect in `POST /billing/run` where `insertId` was read off `rows`, so every settled charge threw, was logged as a provider error, rolled back and left `next_billing_date` unmoved; 15 integration cases in `assigned-plan-snapshot-billing.test.ts`, 7 engine unit cases and 2 billing-run regression cases)

- **Done**: #635 (stage 4, part 1 — **Charge Benefits retired**: migration 176 drops `plan_charge_benefits` (089/091/145) and its assignment-time snapshot `user_membership_charge_benefits` (130), answering Q4's "clean up completely these legacy structure"; `GET`/`PUT /membership-plans/:id/charge-benefits` are gone, `enrichPlan` no longer serves `charge_benefits` and no longer copies them on assign or duplicate, `GET /user-memberships/:id` drops the field, and the #485 Plan Billing Forecast is the plan fee and its cadence alone (`benefitLines: []`) — nothing else could move its total. Admin: the `CHARGE BENEFITS` section, its state/handlers and every `plans.cb_*`/`forecast_benefit_*` key in en/es/ca are removed; the Details modal's Benefits count is now the three Product-keyed sections. Nothing billed off these tables after stage 3, so no assignment changes what it charges. 10 integration cases in `charge-benefits-retired.test.ts`, 6 structural cases in `plans-charge-benefits-removed.test.ts`)

- **Done**: #635 (stage 4, part 2 — **Included Services retired**: migration 177 drops `plan_allowances` (061), after the issue answered where the relation belongs — *"Activity type should flag [which] membership plans are enabled to attend [a] certain activity type"* — which is `activity_type_eligible_plans` (#481/migration 139), already editable from the Activity Types page. `GET`/`POST`/`PUT`/`DELETE /membership-plans/:id/allowances` are gone, `enrichPlan` no longer serves `allowances` or copies them on duplicate, `GET /user-memberships/:id` and the Member membership configuration drop `activity_allowances`, and the Close action's unused-value check is back to the pending billing event alone. `plan-allowances.ts` is deleted: its activity-type gate is now `activity-eligibility.ts`'s alone, its **center coverage** check moved unchanged to the new `plan-center-access.ts`, and the **class-package claim** it delegated to `package-credits.ts` is made by the eligibility gate itself via the new `tryClaimPackageCredit()` — so a member whose plan does not cover an activity still pays for it out of prepaid sessions, and the two hooks no longer depend on registration order. Admin: the `INCLUDED SERVICES` section with its state, handlers and `plans.*` allowance keys in en/es/ca is removed; the Assigned Plan card's `BENEFITS` section now renders the assignment's own #635 snapshot (One-off / Session / Period) instead of allowance usage, and `allowance_exhausted` leaves the staff-override list since nothing emits it. The one behaviour genuinely dropped is the per-window `session_count` cap, which eligibility has no equivalent for and §1 forbade relocating. 18 integration cases in `included-services-retired.test.ts`, 9 structural cases in the admin app's `included-services-removed.test.ts`)

- **Done**: #635 (stage 5 — **the Membership Fee Benefit gets its own table**, answering the issue thread's *"information under promotion_session_benefits, promotion_period_benefits, promotion_membership_fee_benefits and promotion_one_off_benefits is no longer reading from promotion_charge_benefits"* and *"I'd prefer to migrate it to a new and clean structure"*: migration 178 creates `promotion_membership_fee_benefits` — one row per Promotion, no `charge_type_id` and no Product, since the item was never a choice — migrates into it the `promotion_period_benefits` row whose charge type was `membership_fee` (#551) and, for a Promotion configured before #626 removed the editor, its `promotion_charge_benefits` row on a membership-fee item (an unbounded benefit, which is how it always priced), then drops all three legacy `charge_types`-keyed tables: `promotion_charge_benefits` (020/092/102/145), `promotion_period_benefits` (021/070/093/102/144/162) and the long-unused `promotion_included_benefits` (102). `GET`/`PUT /promotions/:id/charge-benefits` are gone (the section had no UI since #626); `/membership-fee-benefit` reads and upserts the new table and no longer returns a `charge_type_*` triplet; `computeFinalPrice()` applies one benefit per Promotion instead of a Charge Benefit and a Period Benefit in turn; the apply-time snapshot carries `membership_fee_benefits` instead of the three `charge_types` arrays, with `membershipFeeBenefitsFromSnapshot()` reading the pre-stage-5 shape so an existing application still prices identically; `MembershipFeeBenefit` collapses from a two-variant union to one duration-gated shape. Assigned Plans are untouched — since stage 3 billing reads their own snapshot. 6 unit cases in `membership-fee-benefit-snapshot.test.ts`, plus the Promotion/apply suites updated to the new table)

- **Done**: #635 (stage 6 — **the Assigned Plan's snapshot is shown and edited section by section** (§9/§10/§15): the Assigned Plans expanded row gains the Membership Plan's own structure — **Billing & Duration** (Free Period / Paid Duration / Bonus Duration, plus the frozen cadence and regular Membership Fee) above **One-off / Session / Period Benefits** — each with its own Edit/Save/Cancel and no modal, reusing the shared `ProductBenefits` editor the Plans and Promotions pages already use. New `PUT /user-memberships/:id/billing-duration` and `GET`/`PUT /user-memberships/:id/{session,oneoff,periodical}-benefits` take the same replace-all `{ items: [{ gym_charge_id, quantity }] }` contract as the Plan-side sections but write the assignment's own rows: the source Plan, its other assignments and the Products are untouched (§15), and since stage 3 that is exactly what the assignment bills, so the Billing Simulation moves with the edit. A line already in a section keeps the price it was agreed at and only changes quantity — a newly added line freezes the item as it is now (§17) — and a retired item stays saveable, while a new one must be active and of the section's category. An assignment that captured no snapshot (a pre-migration-174 row the backfill could not reach) is materialised from what it resolves live *before* the edit applies (`materialiseAssignedPlanSnapshot`), so replacing one section cannot blank the other two; a cancelled or expired assignment is read-only. No migration — the tables are stage 2's. 22 integration cases in `assigned-plan-snapshot-editing.test.ts`, 12 structural cases in the admin app's `assigned-plan-configuration.test.ts`)

- **Done**: #635 (stage 7 — **the Assigned Plan's applied Promotions are read and priced from their own snapshot** (§16): the Promotions section of the expanded row becomes one expandable card per application — checkbox, name, applied at, applied by, StatusBadge — opening onto the agreed window, Billing & Duration, the Membership Fee Benefit and the Products it granted, every line at its frozen price; a revoked application reads as `inactive`, its checkbox cleared and its card not expandable, and clearing a standing one's checkbox revokes it (the existing DELETE, behind a confirmation), which recomputes the price and moves the Billing Simulation with it. `fetchAppliedPromotions()` gains `applied_by_name`, `display_status` (new pure `domain/promotionApplicationStatus.ts`: inactive / expired against the *snapshot's* window / active — "incompatible" cannot persist, since a Promotion that does not stack is refused at apply time) and the three grant arrays, loaded through the same `loadPromotionGrantSnapshots()` the Billing Simulation uses, falling back to the Promotion's live benefits only for an application that predates the snapshot flow. `computeFinalPrice()` reads each application's Membership Fee Benefit out of its snapshot instead of joining `promotion_membership_fee_benefits` live — the last path by which editing a Promotion could still move an existing assignment's price, since `final_price` is recomputed at every apply/revoke (§13); the `duration_months` gate stays in SQL so month arithmetic clamps as before. No migration — everything read was already captured by stages 2/5. 15 integration cases in `assigned-plan-promotions.test.ts`, 7 unit cases in `promotion-application-status.test.ts`, 13 structural cases in the admin app's `assigned-plan-promotions.test.ts`)

- **Done**: #635 (stage 8 — **the Assigned Plan's Billing & Duration finally bills** (§7 + the thread's Q2 answer): `free_months`/`paid_months`/`bonus_months` have been stored on a Plan since stage 1 and frozen onto every assignment since stage 2, but nothing read them — a Plan sold with a free first month billed its full fee on day one. New pure `api/src/domain/planDuration.ts` classifies a date as `free_plan`/`pay_plan`/`bonus_plan`/`pay_regular`, counted from the assignment's own `starts_at` (not snapped to the first of its month, the way a Promotion's illustrative timeline is) with every boundary measured from that anchor in one step so `advanceBillingDate`'s end-of-month clamping can't reorder them; the Billing Simulation waives the Membership Fee in the Free Period and the Bonus Duration, reporting a `source: 'membership_plan'` benefit line carrying the period, and charges the regular price in the Paid Duration — where the projection stops, since that *is* the regular charge, unless a Bonus Duration behind it still has to be shown. **The Promotion wins any overlap** (Q2: "in case of conflict, prioritize the promotion"): where an applied Promotion governs the date — inside its own timeline or applying its Membership Fee Benefit — it decides the fee alone, and the Plan's own period applies only where no Promotion does (including after one is revoked). The durations are read from the assignment's own columns, falling back to the Plan's live ones only for an assignment that captured no snapshot at all — the same all-or-nothing rule as `loadPlanBenefitsForSimulation`, because the columns are nullable and a per-column COALESCE would let a Free Period *added to the Plan later* reach an existing assignment (§13). Only the Membership Fee is waived: the Plan's Period Benefits are still charged, as a Promotion's free months leave them charged too. No migration, and the nightly run is unchanged — free/paid/bonus shape the projection, exactly as a Promotion's always have. 11 unit cases in `plan-duration.test.ts`, 14 engine unit cases in `billing-simulation.test.ts`, 12 integration cases in `assigned-plan-duration-billing.test.ts`, 2 structural cases in the admin app's `member-billing-simulation.test.ts`)

- **Done**: #635 (stage 9 — **a Promotion can be deselected and selected again** (the thread's Q2 answer: Promotions are *"selectable and deselectable adjusting automatically the simulation"*): stage 7 shipped only the deselect half, because `ump_unique_pair` (migration 022) reserved `(user_membership_id, promotion_id)` across every status, so a revoked application held the pair for good and a second apply answered 409. Migration 183 narrows the uniqueness to the applications still *standing* — a `standing_promotion_key` VIRTUAL generated column, non-NULL only while `status = 'applied'`, under `ump_one_standing_per_promotion`, the same shape migration 164 gives Additional Periodic Services — after creating the plain index the dropped unique key was also serving for the `user_membership_id` foreign key. A re-apply writes a **new** application rather than resurrecting the revoked one: the row owns the snapshot it was agreed with (§16) and its `[applied_at, revoked_at]` window tags the Billing Events ledger, so the spent one stays as history and the new one snapshots the Promotion as it stands today — a Promotion repriced in between moves the new application only (§13). Every eligibility rule stays on the unchanged apply path; the one addition is an explicit "a standing application already holds this pair" check, so the database's key surfaces as a 409 with a message. New pure `canReapplyPromotion()` (`domain/promotionApplicationStatus.ts`) decides the per-application `can_reapply` the card reads — inactive, nothing of the same Promotion standing, and the Promotion active and inside its own window today — and `AssignedPlanPromotions.tsx` ticks a spent checkbox back through the apply endpoint behind its own confirmation, which says the Promotion is agreed as it stands today. The migration also moves `applied_at`'s default from migration 022's `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` (the server's session time zone) to `UTC_TIMESTAMP()`, the clock the revoke path stamps `revoked_at` with: the two bound the same window, and since stage 9 a replacement application's `applied_at` is compared against the `revoked_at` of the one it replaced. 7 unit cases in `promotion-application-status.test.ts`, 17 integration cases in `promotion-reapply.test.ts`, 4 structural cases in the admin app's `assigned-plan-promotions.test.ts`)

- **Done**: #635 (stage 10 — **the Member sees the snapshot too**: `GET /me/membership`, the Member app's My Membership page, was the last surface reading an existing assignment's commercial configuration live. Its `BENEFITS` list came from `membership_plan_benefits` — migration 006's plan-keyed benefit vocabulary, joined to `benefit_types`, keyed to the *Plan* and written by no code in the project's lifetime — and its billing cadence straight from `billing_policies`, so a Plan re-cadenced after enrollment moved the dates the member was shown (§13/§14). Both now read the assignment's own rows: the cadence through `ASSIGNMENT_CADENCE`, like every other billing query, and the benefits through `loadPlanBenefitsForSimulation()`, so the Member reads the same Products the Billing Simulation and the nightly run bill, at the quantity, frequency and price frozen with them — with the Plan's live sections as the fallback only for an assignment that captured no snapshot at all (the same all-or-nothing rule as stage 3). Migration 184 drops `membership_plan_benefits` now that its last reader is gone; `benefit_types` stays, a global vocabulary with its own read-only router, so retiring it is a separate decision. What is *charged* is unchanged: `upcoming_payments` still projects `final_price`, which is what the nightly run charges — only its dates now come from the assignment. Member app: the lines are grouped one-off → session → period (`membership.benefit_group.*`) and carry their frozen price and frequency (`membership.frequency.*`), replacing the fixed `membership.benefit.<code>` / `membership.recurrence.*` label sets in en/es/ca. 9 integration cases in `me-membership-snapshot.test.ts`, 11 structural cases in the member app's `membership-benefits-snapshot.test.ts`)

- **Done**: #635 (stage 11 — **the nightly run stops charging a waived cycle**: stages 8 and 10 made the Billing Simulation and the Member's My Membership page bill from the assignment's own Billing & Duration, but `POST /billing/run` still charged `user_memberships.final_price` flat — a Plan sold with a free first month was shown €0 and charged for it that night, and an applied Promotion's own free or bonus month never reached the charge either, `final_price` being a single number with no date attached. New `api/src/api/billing-run-pricing.ts` decides, for the date being billed (`next_billing_date`, not "today"), whether the fee is waived, by calling the simulation's own `resolveMembershipFee()` — exported for the purpose, so the run and what the member was shown cannot disagree, precedence rule included (a Promotion that governs the date decides alone). A waived cycle calls no payment provider and writes no `payment_requests` row: it records a `waived_billing` billing event (amount 0, `source = 'system'`, `notes` naming the period — `free_plan` / `bonus_plan` / `free_promotion` / `bonus_promotion`, migration 185 widens the `event_type` CHECK) and advances `next_billing_date` exactly as a charged cycle would, leaving `last_billed_at` untouched; the run answers with a fourth counter, `waived`. A cycle that is *not* waived is charged `final_price` unchanged — the remaining §16 gap (a Promotion's Membership Fee Benefit that has run out keeps discounting) needs `computeFinalPrice`, the simulation engine and `computeMembershipFeePriceAt` reconciled first, and charging a member *more* on the strength of their disagreement is not a billing run's call. An assignment that captured no snapshot reads its Plan's live durations, all-or-nothing (§13); a €0 `final_price` is treated as waived rather than sent to the provider as a zero-amount MIT. `advanceBillingDate` moved to its own leaf module `domain/billingDate.ts` (the run importing the engine would otherwise close an import cycle), re-exported from `api/billing.ts` so no caller changed. 9 integration cases in `billing-run-waived.test.ts`, 11 unit cases in `membership-fee-resolution.test.ts`, 9 structural cases in the admin app's `billing-event-waived-label.test.ts` and 4 in the member app's)

- **Done**: #635 (stage 12 — **one date-aware Membership Fee rule, everywhere**: the thread's stage 12 answer (a) — "a Promotion's own Free/Paid/Bonus months determine when its Membership Fee Benefit ends" — settled the last §16 gap. Three paths priced a fee and only one was right: `final_price` (what the run charged) and `computeMembershipFeePriceAt()` (the Billing Events projection) applied a Promotion's benefit for as long as the application stood, gated only by its own `duration_months`, while the Billing Simulation and My Membership stopped it at the end of the Promotion's timeline, so the same cycle was *shown* one price and *charged* another, in both directions. `resolveMembershipFee()` is now the only implementation: `computeMembershipFeePriceAt()` delegates to it (and gained the assignment's own Billing & Duration, so a Free Period shows €0 there too), `computeFinalPrice()` resolves the cycle `next_billing_date` names rather than discounting a date-less number, `GET /me/membership` prices each of its two `upcoming_payments` on its own date instead of repeating `final_price`, and `POST /billing/run` charges what that resolver returns. `MembershipFeeBenefit` / `AppliedPromotionForBilling` / `promotionCoversDate` moved to a new leaf module `domain/promotionApplication.ts` (carrying the Promotion's months now that every caller needs them) to invert the import edge that had kept the two rules apart, re-exported from `domain/assignedPlanBillingEvents.ts`; `regularMembershipFee()` moved to `api/user-memberships.ts` so the number every path discounts *from* is resolved by one chain too (snapshot fee → Plan price window → non-zero `base_price` → `final_price`). Because the correction *raises* the charge of a member whose promotional months already elapsed, the two money-moving writers — `computeFinalPrice()` and the run — are gated on `billing.date_aware_membership_fee`, which migration 186 seeds **disabled** (its `down()` deliberately keeps the row: a missing key reads as enabled). While it is off nothing about a charge changes and the impact is reported instead: the run logs every assignment whose resolved price differs and counts them in a fifth response counter (`drift`), and the new `GET /user-memberships/reports/membership-fee-drift` (`api/src/api/membership-fee-drift.ts`, read-only, tenant-scoped) lists each affected assignment with its stored price, regular fee, promotion timelines, benefit end date (`promotionTimelineEndsOn()`), newly resolved price and the difference. A manual discount still in force (`discount_reason`) outranks the catalogue fee, so this can never undo a discount a human agreed. 15 integration cases in `billing-run-date-aware-fee.test.ts`, 3 in `me-membership-snapshot.test.ts`, 7 unit cases in `membership-fee-resolution.test.ts` and 8 rewritten in `assignedPlanBillingEvents.test.ts`. **Remaining before the epic closes**: review the drift report on real data and switch the flag on)

- **Done**: #635 (stage 13 — **Billing & Duration is the Plan's only billing section, and gains a Pre-paid Duration**: the thread's final-model comment asked for the Initial Billing / Initial Service / Recurring Service pairs to go and for all consumers to move off them. Migration 189 drops the three from `billing_policies` — nothing billed off them, the Plans editor was their only reader — and keeps `recurring_billing_*`, which is the only thing in the schema that says how *often* a contract charges (Free/Paid/Bonus say how long it runs): it is now presented as the single **Billing frequency** inside BILLING & DURATION, beside Auto-renew, exactly as the Assigned Plan's own section already did, so nothing any existing assignment is charged moves. The same migration adds the fourth duration field the thread asked for — "the pre-paid duration which will flag in the simulation as pre-paid - no charge" — as `membership_plans.pay_beforehand_months` and its assignment snapshot `user_memberships.pay_beforehand_months`, the Promotion's own column (migration 141) with the same 0..paid_months bound, validated on both the Plan (`PUT /membership-plans/:id`) and the assignment (`PUT /user-memberships/:id/billing-duration`, rejected inside the transaction so a materialised snapshot rolls back with it). `domain/planDuration.ts` gained a `prepaid_plan` period — the first N months of the Paid Duration, charging nothing because they are already paid — so the Billing Simulation, the Billing Events projection, My Membership and the nightly run all treat it as a no-charge cycle through the one `resolveMembershipFee()` rule, labelled pre-paid rather than waived. 15 integration cases in `plan-prepaid-duration.test.ts`, 2 added to `billing-run-waived.test.ts`, unit cases in `plan-duration.test.ts`, structural ones in the admin app's `plans-benefit-sections.test.ts`, `assigned-plan-configuration.test.ts` and `member-billing-simulation.test.ts`)

- **Done**: #635 (stage 14 — **the stage 12 drift report gets the page it is reviewed on**: the epic's last open item is "review the drift report, then switch `billing.date_aware_membership_fee` on", and the thread made the review the precondition for the nightly run acting on the correction — but the report existed only as `GET /user-memberships/reports/membership-fee-drift`, which a gym admin cannot read. **Payments → Membership Fee Drift** is that page: read-only (one GET, no write control, no flag switch), the shared `FilterBar`/`DataTable` chrome of #724, a banner naming the rule the run is currently following, four counters — assignments examined, affected, members who would pay more, total difference — and one expandable row per affected assignment with its stored `final_price`, regular fee, cycle priced, resolved price, difference and every applied Promotion's Free/Paid/Bonus timeline plus the date its Membership Fee Benefit ends, keyed per *application* (stage 9). Migration 190 gives it its own `payments.membership_fee_drift` key, seeded from `payments.transactions`' current value (migration 160's rule for splitting a key out from under another, so a gym with Transactions off finds nothing newly reachable), and the endpoint is re-mounted **before** `/user-memberships` so the new key is the one that actually decides. 19 structural cases in the admin app's `membership-fee-drift-page.test.ts` and 4 integration cases in `billing-run-date-aware-fee.test.ts`)

- **Done**: #635 (stage 15 — **date-aware Membership Fee pricing is standard behaviour and nothing stores a price**: the thread's answer to stage 14's review was *"remove the billing.date_aware_membership_fee feature flag entirely as well as the stored final_price approach. Date-aware Membership Fee pricing should be standard system behaviour"*. Migration 191 deletes both flag rows and drops `user_memberships.final_price` the flag helper and the legacy branch of the run's pricing go with them and **Payments → Membership Fee Drift** — endpoint (`membership-fee-drift.ts`) page nav entry locale keys and the run's `drift` counter — is deleted empty by construction once nothing is stored to drift from. `billing-run-pricing.ts` becomes the shared **`membership-fee-pricing.ts`**: `priceMembershipFeeOn(row, date)` a batch `priceMembershipFeesFor()` `currentCycleDate()` (what "now" means: `max(next_billing_date, today)` never before `starts_at`) `currentMembershipFee(s)()` and one `FEE_ASSIGNMENT_COLUMNS` fragment so the nightly run the Assigned Plans list and card the create/renew/lifecycle responses apply/revoke `GET /members/:id/membership-configuration` `GET /me/membership` and the Payments dashboard's projected rows all resolve the same number and report it as a computed `membership_fee`. Projected rows are now priced **per date** (a Free Period reads €0) a payment request (staff- or member-raised) asks for the resolved fee and is refused 400 when the cycle owes nothing and apply/revoke price the same cycle either side of the write for their `adjustment` ledger entry instead of diffing a stored column. A **negotiated** fee now lives in `membership_fee_price` (`POST /user-memberships` and `/:id/assign-new-plan` take that field with `discount_reason`; `PUT /:id` takes no price at all — editing one is a snapshot edit so it goes through `PUT /:id/billing-duration`) and `POST /membership-plans/:id/pricing/apply-to-assigned-plans` writes that column too — it wrote only `base_price`/`plan_price_id`/`final_price` before none of which the resolver reads first so under date-aware pricing it would have changed nothing an assignment bills — materialising each affected assignment's snapshot first. The migration's backfill is narrow (it writes the fee only where the old chain would have returned `final_price` anyway) and materialises an uncaptured candidate in full before writing it because `membership_fee_price` is one of the disjuncts that define `has_billing_snapshot` and the fallback it turns off is all-or-nothing)

- **Done**: #717 (Base Exercises carry a demonstration **video** too: the image pair of #716 one folder over, at `cordel/Exercises/Videos/<id>-<Name>.mp4` and `…-thumbnail.png` — `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT`, never a gym prefix — built by the new `domain/baseExerciseVideos.ts` off the same `Exercises/Videos` leaf and the same name sanitizer the gym side uses. No migration: `exercises.video_url` + `video_thumbnail_url` (migration 188) hold the resulting Cloudflare URLs for a base row exactly as they do for a gym-owned one, and no `video_object_key` column was added (the ticket's Q5 answer). `POST`/`DELETE /platform/exercises/:id/video` (superadmin) are the only writer and clearer; the **browser** captures the 512×512 poster with #719's own `lib/exerciseVideoUpload.ts` and the server re-validates the pair with #719's own `validateExerciseVideoPair()` + `domain/mp4Video.ts` — Q3 and Q4 confirmed reusing both, so there is one implementation of "a valid exercise video" and no `ffprobe` in the API image. `EXERCISE_VIDEO_MAX_MB` (default 50, clamped at 200) and the buffered `PutObject` path are reused unchanged (Q2), with the platform route mounting its own `express.json` limit in `app.ts`. Each router answers for its own rows: the platform route is 404 for a gym's exercise, the gym route 403 for a base one. Stale objects are swept with `isPlatformOwnedExerciseVideoUrl()` (a gym object and an external YouTube link are never deleted by a platform operation) plus a reference check that spans **every** tenant and **all four** media columns, because `POST /exercises/import` copies media *references*; removal clears both references with no fallback, and a `PUT` that repoints `video_url` still drops the poster that belonged to the MP4. The expanded card on Cordel → Base Exercises gains a **Media** section holding the image block and the new video block: the stored poster with a play affordance, `Upload Video`/`Replace`, `Remove` behind the page's confirmation, and no `<video>` mounted until the player is asked for — then with `controls` and `preload="metadata"` and nothing else, because §8 forbids autoplay. Per the ticket's Q6 answer the gym Exercises card's media section is renamed **Media** and no longer offers `video_url` as an editable text box (the edit PUT stops sending it, so a video uploaded while the editor was open can no longer be repointed by saving the form); nothing else on either card is hidden or moved. Base videos are also what makes #719 part 3's re-import able to restore a System poster. 42 integration cases in `base-exercise-videos.test.ts`, 15 unit cases for the platform keys and ownership test, 12 structural cases in the admin app.)

- **Done**: #772 (Personal Membership Fee Benefit on an Assigned Membership Plan: `No benefit` / `% discount` stored as `user_memberships.personal_fee_benefit_action` + `personal_fee_benefit_value` (migration 192, no CHECK on that table — `toPersonalFeeBenefit()` clamps 0..100 instead), edited through `PUT /user-memberships/:id/fee-benefit` and its own MEMBERSHIP FEE BENEFIT section on the Assigned Plan card. Applied last and on every cycle by `resolveMembershipFee()` via a required `personalFeeBenefit` on `MembershipFeeContext`, so it stacks with an applied Promotion's own benefit and survives it — the one fee benefit bounded by nothing. Deliberately outside the Assigned Plan snapshot: no materialisation, no effect on `snapshot_captured`, and not carried onto a superseding assignment)

- **Done**: #777 (Migration 191 carries a staff-negotiated price that lived only in `final_price`: a second backfill pass, after the last-resort one and before the `DROP COLUMN`, moves `final_price` into `membership_fee_price` for an assignment created with a price override before stage 15 — the override went to the dropped column while `snapshotAssignedPlan()` (or migration 174's backfill, for an older row) froze the catalogue price. Guards: non-empty `discount_reason`, `membership_fee_price <> final_price`, non-terminal status, no Promotion application in any status (a standing one has its discount baked into the total, a revoked one had `final_price` recomputed from scratch — typically to 0 under the legacy rule — so the agreed number is in neither column; those rows are logged by the migration and are a go-to-production checklist item); `discount_expires_at` is not a guard because the column means what was agreed, and a lapsed agreement resolves the catalogue price through `ignoreFrozenFee` anyway)

- **Done**: #778 (the nightly Billing Run workflow reports honestly: `.github/workflows/billing-run.yml` reads the run's response instead of discarding it and goes red on `failed > 0`, on a body with no counters, or on a non-2xx including the guard's 429 — a red run is the notification; `/billing/cleanup` still runs when the charge step failed, and `recurring-booking-run.yml` gets the same rule with `skipped` staying green)

- **Done**: #780 (the nightly run guard is a calendar rule: migration 193 turns `billing_run_log` and `recurring_booking_run_log` from `last_run_at` singletons into histories — one row per run with `run_date`, `status`, `started_at`/`finished_at` and the run's own counters — and both `POST /billing/run` and an unscoped `POST /recurring-bookings/run` now refuse only when a run for today's UTC date has already **completed**. That fixes the two defects of the 23-hour window: a cron GitHub fired late no longer skips a day, and a run that crashed no longer locks one (its row is closed as `failed` from the route's `catch`, and a row left `in_progress` past `STALE_RUN_MINUTES`=30 is taken over by the next claim). Overlap is still prevented — a fresh `in_progress` row answers `429` — while "today's run already completed" answers `200 { skipped_reason: 'already_completed_today', run_date, …zeroed counters }`, deliberately not a 429, so the second daily attempt #781 adds is a green no-op and #778's body parse keeps working; both workflows learned to report that as the no-op it is. The decision is pure in `domain/runGuard.ts` (unit-tested: a late cron, a crashed run, a stale lock) behind `infra/run-log.ts`, whose `claimRun()` reads and inserts under one short `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` transaction that never spans the run itself)

- **Done**: #781 (the nightly Billing Run gets a second scheduled attempt: `.github/workflows/billing-run.yml` fires `POST /billing/run` at 06:00 **and** 10:00 UTC, so a first attempt GitHub delayed or dropped is still charged the same day. Nothing else changed — #780's guard makes the second attempt a no-op (`skipped_reason: 'already_completed_today'`, reported green by the step #778 wrote), so a normal day still produces exactly one set of charges, and `POST /billing/cleanup` running twice is idempotent. §3 decided: `recurring-booking-run.yml` keeps its single 03:00 schedule, because a dropped booking night is recovered by the next one re-projecting the rolling window from *now*)

- **Done**: #785 (nightly Billing Run dunning: a rejected recurring charge now escalates instead of repeating every night for ever. The first provider rejection bumps `user_memberships.failed_attempts`/`last_failed_at` (migration 194) and is retried on the **next run day**, since `next_billing_date` never moved; the second consecutive rejection of the same due cycle pauses the assignment (`recordStatusChange`, `active → paused`, `source = 'system'`), which drops it out of the run's `WHERE status = 'active'` — no third provider fee, no third `failed_billing` row. The count advances per run *day*, so a second rejection on a date the assignment was already rejected on (#781's 10:00 UTC attempt after a crashed 06:00 one) records itself without escalating. A settled or waived cycle, the payment webhook's `completed` branch, a staff Retry that succeeds, a Manual payment and any transition back to `active` all clear the count; a provider exception (`provider_error`) and a missing stored card (`no_payment_method`) never count towards the pause, because neither is a decline. The rule is pure in `domain/billingDunning.ts` (unit-tested) and the run reports a new `paused` counter. Per the ticket the member is **not** notified and reactivation stays explicit (`POST /user-memberships/:id/reactivate`))

- **Done**: #787 (Receipts for recurring payments. `POST /payments/:id/receipt` no longer refuses everything that is not a `payment_recorded`: `isReceiptableEvent()` (`domain/billingEventStatus.ts`, unit-tested) accepts any event that represents money actually received — event type in `payment_recorded`/`recurring_payment`/`failed_billing` **and** derived status `paid` — so the nightly run's settled charges and a `failed_billing` later settled by Retry or Manual payment (#640) both qualify, the latter through the predicate rather than a special case. A `waived_billing`, an `adjustment` and anything failed, expired or pending are refused with a `400` naming the reason. Sequence allocation moved into `issueReceiptNumber()` (`domain/receiptNumbers.ts`), idempotent under `FOR UPDATE`, so one payment can never burn two numbers. `POST /billing/run` now issues automatically for each charge it settles — in a transaction of its own **after** the charge's has committed, failure logged and swallowed, because that transaction records money the provider already took — and reports `receipts_issued`. UI: `can_issue_receipt` on the Billing Event Details response drives an Issue/Download receipt button in Payments → Billing Events (en/es/ca), and the member app's billing history offers the download on any row carrying a number, not only `payment_recorded`. `pdfFetch` moved into `useApiClient` so the two screens share one implementation. No migration. Tests: `receipt-eligibility.unit.test.ts` (15), `receipts.test.ts` (18), plus 4 run cases in `billing-run.test.ts`.)

- **Done**: #788 (Let a member replace their stored card without being charged: a **card verification** — `PaymentProvider.createCardVerificationRequest()`, which takes no amount at all, sent to Monei as `amount: 0` + `transactionType: 'VERIF'` + `generatePaymentToken: true` — replaces the card on file without charging, where the only way before was the Start payment button's full membership fee, and only on a cycle that owed one. The verification needs a `payment_requests` row for its `page_token`/`provider_order`, so migration 195 widens `chk_payment_requests_source` with `card_update`, makes `charge_type_id` nullable (a verification bills nothing, and a `card_verification` charge type would put a non-charge in the cash-payment picker) and adds `payment_methods.updated_at`, which is what "card stored since" now reads. The webhook's card-update branch upserts the new token and does nothing else — no `payment_recorded` event, no `next_billing_date`, none of #785's dunning counters cleared, since no money arrived; a rejected or token-less verification leaves the previous card in place. `GET /members`' `payment_status`, `GET /payment-requests` and `GET /me/payment-requests` all exclude the new source, so a verification can never read as an unpaid fee. New staff router `payment-methods.ts` (read-only card + **Send replace-card link**) and `/me/payment-method` (GET / POST replace-requests / DELETE) share `api/card-updates.ts`; the rules are pure in `domain/storedCards.ts`. Removal is refused (`409 billable_membership`) while any assignment is `active`/`paused` with a `next_billing_date` — the nightly run skips a card-less assignment *silently* and #779 does not exist yet — while replacing is never blocked. Member app: a Payment method section on My Membership; the hosted page renders the verification with no amount and a "save card" button, and `purpose=card_update` on the return URL is how the payment return page knows what came back)

- **Done**: #789 (Payment webhook — cleanup could expire a request the member had already paid. `POST /billing/cleanup` expired every `pending` row past `page_token_expires`, which is the deadline for *opening* the checkout link (creation + 10 min), not for paying through it: `GET /payment-page/token/:token` consumes the token as the page loads, and the member then spends minutes in the Card Input and a 3DS redirect, after which Monei may retry its webhook. A row expired inside that window was skipped by the webhook's `pr.status !== 'pending'` guard as already-processed — money taken, no completed request, no `payment_methods` row, no `next_billing_date`, and the member app still offering *Start payment*. Cleanup now expires against **two** deadlines (`domain/paymentRequestExpiry.ts`): a request whose page was **never opened** (`page_token IS NOT NULL` — the page load is the only writer that clears it) still goes with its token, while one that **was** opened keeps the long `PAYMENT_REQUEST_ABANDONED_HOURS` window (default 24, floored at 1) so a terminal webhook can still land on it; `expired` stays the total the workflow parses (#778), with `expired_unopened`/`expired_abandoned` beside it. Belt and braces, as the ticket asked: the webhook also accepts a `completed` payload on an **`expired`** row, because the provider is the source of truth about money — narrow on purpose, so a `failed`/`expired` webhook never revives one and an already-`completed` row is still skipped. §2's coverage gap closed too: `webhooks.test.ts` now asserts the `payment_methods` upsert and its second-payment update, `next_billing_date` as `starts_at` + the assignment's own `ASSIGNMENT_CADENCE` (and left alone when already set), the `billing_event_id` back-link, idempotency under a retried event, and the race end to end through the real page load and the real cleanup; new `me-payment-requests.test.ts` covers `POST /me/payment-requests` for the first time (consent stamp, minor units at the provider boundary, the 409 for two active memberships, the 400 for a cycle that owes nothing, the 3/hour limiter). No migration. New `payment-request-expiry.unit.test.ts`.)

- **Done**: #791 (Payments documentation — one reliable source for the two processes. New `docs/payments.md`, written from the code on `main` and structured as the two flows the system actually runs: the member's first payment (CIT — member + Clerk link, the three `status = 'active'` insert paths, the staff and member payment-request routes, the hosted page's single-use token, and every branch of the Monei webhook) and the nightly recurring charge (MIT — the workflow and its secret, #780's calendar run guard, the selection query and what it does *not* check, date-aware pricing through the one `resolveMembershipFee()`, all five outcomes in a table, #785's dunning rule, #640's Retry / Manual payment, and cleanup). Plus reference sections: the five tables with their current CHECK sets and the migration holding each, where the four non-persisted projections live, the Assigned Plan status model and which statuses are unreachable (#786), the provider boundary and its env vars, the money rules linked rather than copied, what observability exists and what does not, a step-by-step **manual test runbook** against Monei test keys, and an Open gaps table naming #778–#790 so the document says what is not built. Alignment: the six payments rows of `docs/architecture.md`'s routers table and its `payments/` tree now keep a one-line contract and link to the process document, its TL;DR gains a Payments line, and the `001_ … 052_` migration range was stale; `docs/decisions.md` records the three settled choices of 2026-09-26; `docs/go-to-production.md` §5's items carry their ticket and point at the runbook. `PAYMENT_ENV` and `MONEI_ACCOUNT_ID` were read by the code but missing from `api/.env.example`. Docs only — no API, schema or UI change)

- **Done**: #797 (Members — the expanded card shows the complete Member Profile, read-only: a new `PROFILE` section opens the expanded row with every field the Edit form submits plus the Member's Assigned Centers and Default Center, and it carries no input, no toggle and no Edit affordance — `⋮ → Edit` stays the only way in. One definition drives both sides (`apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/members/memberProfile.ts`: the field set, their read-only labels, the persisted-row → form-values mapping and the date formatting), so the read-only view and the form cannot drift apart, and the Profile renders the same list row the form is seeded from rather than re-reading the Member. The one read it adds is `GET /members/:memberId/centers`, which now answers the sole-active-center fallback itself (`soleActiveCenterId()`) instead of leaving each caller to guess that an empty `member_centers` list means "no center" — and 404s for a member outside the caller's gym, so that fallback cannot leak the caller's own center)

- **Done**: #802 (Professional Services context menu: **Duplicate** first, the **Deactivate**·Activate slot second with Deactivate carrying `ContextMenu`'s shared `danger` style, **Details** always last — Edit and Delete keep their behaviour in the slots between, and a system row (which hides both) renders exactly Duplicate/Deactivate/Details. - **Done**: #804 (Exercises: **import is the visibility boundary** — `GET /exercises` returns the gym's own rows (`gym_id = ?`) and no longer `OR e.gym_id IS NULL`, so a Base Exercise appears on the Exercises page only once it has been imported, as the gym's copy carrying `cloned_from_id` (still badged **System sourced**). No new state: the copy *is* the import state (#718), so no column, migration or flag was added. Filtering in the list query rather than per-control is what keeps `?q=`, `?status=` and ordering from reaching a non-imported row. `GET /exercises/base` (the Import modal), `GET /exercises/:id`, `POST /exercises/import` and its duplicate protection are untouched, and the write paths already agreed with the new rule — `workout-templates.ts` and `training-plans.ts` validate an `exercise_id` with `gym_id = ?`, so the base rows the exercise pickers used to offer were rejected on save)

- **Done**: #803 (Exercises: the header button that opens the Import Exercises modal is labelled **Import System Exercises** — its own locale key `exercises.import_system_exercises`, because the modal's primary action keeps reading `Import`; and the modal's Cancel, which read as disabled at `#aaa` behind white text, takes the `#444` of ExerciseDetailModal's own Cancel and now dims only while an import is in flight. Label and styling only: the modal title, the import flow, its API, validation, permissions and the Add Exercise flow are unchanged)

- **Done**: #786 (retired the Assigned Plan's unreachable `draft`/`awaiting_payment` statuses, the Submit action and `POST /user-memberships/:id/submit` — an assignment is `active` from creation and its first payment is collected afterwards; migration 198 narrows the status CHECK and refuses to run while a row holds a retired status)

- **Done**: #779 (staff alert for failed payments awaiting action: `GET /payments/billing-events/attention` → `{ count, oldest_created_at }` over the Billing Events whose derived status is `failed` (`isAwaitingAction()`), a red count badge on Payments / Billing Events in the sidebar polled every 60 s, and a Payments Dashboard card; both open `billing-events?status=failed&order=asc`. Counts events, recurring charges only, no extra role gate beyond PAYMENTS read — the thread's proposed answers)

- **Done**: #790 (First payment on a back-dated assignment: the payment webhook stamps the first `next_billing_date` as the first `starts_at`-anchored cycle boundary strictly after UTC today, and any transition back to `active` walks a past `next_billing_date` forward the same way — `firstBillingDateAfter()` in `domain/billingDate.ts`, `domain/nextBillingDateStamp.ts`. Elapsed back-dated cycles and cycles missed while paused are written off (decisions (a1), no confirm flag, a pause is not a debt — `docs/payments.md` A5). No migration)

- **Done**: #854 (removed the `draft` Billing Events projection #786 left without callers — `projectDraftBillingEvents()`, `computeMembershipFeePriceAt()`, the admin's Projected tag and its `billing_event_projected` label, and the always-false `projected` field of the Billing Events response (view and events); three `resolveMembershipFee()` rules only those tests pinned moved to `membership-fee-resolution.test.ts`)

- **Done**: #805 (Exercises: **+ Add Exercise** opens an inline creation card at the top of the list — the `CrudModal` is gone, and Cancel discards the staged form without calling the API. `exercises/exerciseForm.ts` declares the section order (**General** with Name / Description / Status, Configuration, Allowed Result Types, Muscles, **Media last**), the row → form mapping and both payloads once, so the creation card and the `⋮ → Edit` editor render one form body; the creation card is still the only half that submits `video_url` (#717 Q6 keeps it out of the `PUT`). Allowed Result Types is a reflowing checkbox grid reading `exercises.result_type_<slug>` labels (added for migration 073's nine slugs in en/es/ca, falling back to the catalogue's own `name` for a slug added later) instead of showing the raw key, and Media renders Image and Video side by side, stacking on a narrow card. Presentation only: same `POST`/`PUT /exercises`, same staged media uploads, same validation, permissions, import, duplicate, clone and status toggle; no API or schema change)

- **Done**: #806 (Unify Exercise and Base Exercise editing: one editor, `components/exercises/ExerciseEditor.tsx`, rendered by both the gym Exercises page and the platform Base Exercises page, with `useExerciseEditorState()` beside it owning the form values, muscle roles, selected result types, validation, error line and saving flag, and `exerciseForm.ts` moved up out of `app/[locale]/exercises/` to sit with them. The Base Exercises editor gained the whole field set it never had — Status, the five Configuration defaults, Allowed Result Types, Muscles and Media — and its media upload/removal controls moved off the read-only expanded card into the editor behind `⋮ → Edit` (#797's rule); `ExerciseImageField`/`ExerciseVideoField` take the route root from the context (`basePath`, defaulting to `/exercises`) plus `requiresGymStorage`, since a Base Exercise's objects live in `cordel/Exercises/…` and no gym's bucket gates them. Removing media now asks first on **both** screens (#717 §7 generalised). New `GET /platform/exercises/lookups` (superadmin, registered before `/:id`) serves the muscle and result-type catalogues, because the gym-facing `/muscles` + `/result-types` sit behind a gym's module access and feature flags. No schema change, no endpoint merged: each page keeps its own routes and permissions)

- **Done**: #808 (Language selector styling: the native `<select>` in the top header now gives every `<option>` the header's own colour pair, so the dropdown reads as a continuation of the band instead of the UA's white popup under the header's white text. The pair lives in the new `apps/admin/src/components/headerChrome.ts` — `HEADER_BG` / `HEADER_TEXT`, the exact token chains `TopHeader` paints with, plus `headerOptionStyle` — so a header control never restates them. Presentation only: the trigger, the three languages, the locale routing and every other header control are untouched; `GymSelector`/`CenterSelector` keep their pre-token `--chrome`/`#fff` options per the ticket's §6)

- **Done**: #810 (Assigned Nutrition Plans: the sidebar entry and the page heading are both **Assigned Nutrition Plans** — one wording, via `nav.nutrition_plans` and `nutrition_plans.title`, which keep their keys — and the collapsed card header now carries the assignment plus the record's standard metadata: the assigned member reads at the plan name's own size and weight (one `headerTitleStyle` both cells spread), and `Created By` joins the `Created At` and the shared `StatusBadge` that were already there, rendered as `created_by_name ?? '—'` like every other list. Frontend only — `GET /member-nutrition-plans` already returned `created_by_name`, so no API, schema or behaviour change: creation, assignment, inline editing, the ⋮ menu, expansion and the member filter are untouched (§6))

- **Done**: #812 (Food Type selector fixed: the Add/Edit food form showed `nutrition_plan_templates.tree_component_type_undefined`, because `NutritionPlanTree` still read `nutrition_library_items.category` — the scalar column migration 142 (#501) replaced with the `nutrition_library_categories` catalogue and an M2M junction, so every food's type was `undefined`. It now reads the `categories: [{id, slug}]` the API has returned all along, and the options come from the backend: the accepted `component_type` set lives once in `api/src/domain/nutritionComponentTypes.ts` (the three routers' duplicated `COMPONENT_TYPES` consts are gone) and is served by `GET /nutrition-plan-templates|/member-nutrition-plans|/platform/nutrition-plan-templates/component-types`, so the selector can never offer a value the CHECK refuses — an assigned plan's meal items take four types (`chk_mnpmi_comp`, migration 105) where a template's take seven (`chk_nptmi_component_type`, migration 111, #294). Reopening an item now shows the Food Type it was saved with rather than the food's own first category, and every label goes through one `foodTypeLabel()` helper: the `{ defaultValue }` that was supposed to catch this does nothing, since next-intl's `t()` has no such option and prints a missing key verbatim. Frontend + one read endpoint per router; no migration, and no translation was added for `undefined`)

- **Done**: #813 (Dietary Restrictions item picker shows the food's name alone: the parenthetical after each option is gone. It carried the food's own Food Type — empty `()` for every food until #812, since it read the scalar `category` migration 142 dropped, and the type once #812 fixed the read. A restriction picker is choosing a food to forbid, not classifying it, so the suffix is noise either way. One JSX expression in `NutritionPlanTree`'s restrictions section, shared by the gym and Base template pages and the Assigned Nutrition Plans page; the option's value, the POST to `/restrictions` and the configured-restriction rows are untouched)

- **Done**: #814 (Membership Fee Promotions lose **Quantity**, **Every** and **Unit**: the section now edits Duration, Action, Value and Enabled — the four fields `resolveMembershipFee()` actually reads. The three came from the `promotion_period_benefits` shape the editor was built on ("2 sessions every 3 months") and said nothing about a membership fee, whose cadence is the Assigned Plan's own Billing frequency (`ASSIGNMENT_CADENCE`), never a Promotion's; nothing ever priced on them. Migration 199 drops the columns from `promotion_membership_fee_benefits` plus `chk_pmfb_positive` and `chk_pmfb_frequency_unit` (`chk_pmfb_action` stays), the singleton `PUT /promotions/:id/membership-fee-benefit` stops requiring and persisting them, `POST /promotions/:id/duplicate` stops copying them and `fetchLiveBenefits()` stops freezing them onto the application snapshot. No assignment re-prices: the surviving columns are untouched and snapshots written before #814 keep their old keys, which no caller reads. Deploy the API before the migration, as for 176/177/179/197)

- **Done**: #815 (Promotion sections renamed after the Promotion: **Session Promotion**, **One-off Promotion**, **Periodical Promotion**, **Membership Fee Promotion**, in the Promotion editor and in the applied-Promotion card of an Assigned Plan, with their empty states and `+ Add …` buttons. Label-only — the translation keys, the `session-benefits`/`oneoff-benefits`/`periodical-benefits` endpoint slugs, the `action` enum values, the snapshot and every pricing rule are untouched. A Membership Plan's own benefit sections keep the Plan's terminology (#816 requires it in as many words), and so do the Assigned Plan's own benefit sections, which render the assignment's snapshot of the Plan — which is why the promotion card got its own `promo_benefits_*` keys instead of sharing `AssignedPlanConfiguration`'s)

- **Done**: #816 (Membership Plan expanded card is read-only, and PRICING moves up: expanding a Plan now renders the complete plan — GENERAL, PRICING, BILLING & DURATION, ONE-OFF BENEFITS, SESSION BENEFITS, PERIOD BENEFITS, CENTERS, BILLING EVENTS FORECAST, then PRICE HISTORY — with no section-level `Edit` button and no control that writes, and `⋮ → Edit` is the single entry point into the General form *and* into the five section-level editors, which are now gated on `isEditing` inside the one expanded body rather than living in the view half. Two defects that shape fixed: PRICING and PRICE HISTORY were invisible to anyone editing (Edit mode replaced the whole card with the General form, which is also why §4 reads as "currently only visible in the editing view"), and "Apply new price to assigned plans" — which repriced every Assigned Plan — sat in the read-only card. The Plan's own General columns and the section order are declared once in `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/plans/planProfile.ts` (`PLAN_GENERAL_SECTION`, `PLAN_SECTION_ORDER`, `toPlanGeneralFormValues()`, `toPlanGeneralUpdatePayload()`), with `interface Plan extends PlanGeneralRow`. Presentation only: no migration, no API change, and each section still submits exactly what it did — the `plans.section_status` header becomes `plans.section_general` and the Plan's benefit sections keep the Plan's terminology (§14), never the Promotion names #815 introduced)

- **Done**: #817 (Price History collapses, Current price shows gross + net: the PRICE HISTORY section of the expanded Membership Plan card is now a collapsible card that starts **collapsed** every time the plan is expanded (`CollapsibleSectionHeader` in `plans/page.tsx` — the whole header is a `<button>` carrying `aria-expanded`, the treatment #632 gave the Theme Colors groups; `priceHistoryOpen` is a per-plan id set that nothing seeds, and `toggleExpand` drops the id so re-expanding starts collapsed again). Its rows, dates, amounts and status badges are untouched. `Current price` reads as the customer price *and* its net — `€60.00 VAT included (net €49.59 + tax = €60.00)` — through `formatPlanCurrentPrice()` in `planProfile.ts`, which prints the server's own `amount_incl_tax`/`amount_excl_tax` and does no arithmetic of its own, so the card cannot drift from the Pricing editor's live preview. That exposed a real defect behind the row: a Plan on the **Default** tax rate (`membership_plans.tax_rate_id IS NULL`) had no rate for `computePriceFields()` to split at, so `enrichPlan` returned nulls and the Current price rendered `—` for a Plan that has a price — and the apply-to-assigned confirmation quoted an empty amount. `api/src/domain/planTaxRate.ts` (`selectPlanTaxRates()`) now decides that once, server-side: the Plan's **own** rate is what the Tax rate row displays (`null` ⇒ "Default", unchanged since #413), the **effective** rate is the Plan's own else the gym's system rate (`is_system = 1`, non-deleted — the row `seedSystemPtPackage()` already picks), and that is what the split and `applied_tax_rate` come from. One query serves both, so the list endpoint's per-plan round trips do not grow. No migration, no new endpoint, no change to any stored price. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/plans-price-history-collapsible.test.ts` (12), `api/src/test/plan-tax-rate.unit.test.ts` (8) and a new describe block in `membership-plans.test.ts` (system-rate fallback, own rate wins, no price ⇒ null split))

- **Done**: #820 (Membership Plan Billing Frequency is a single dropdown — *Month* or *4 Weeks*. The Billing & Duration editor's number box plus the whole `recurring_billing_unit` ENUM offered "every 3 days" and "every 2 years", combinations nobody sells; the section now offers the two cadences a gym bills on. **No migration and no new column**: the choice maps onto the same `billing_policies.recurring_billing_interval` + `_unit` pair every assignment snapshots and `advanceBillingDate()` steps, so nothing downstream moves — `api/src/domain/planBillingFrequency.ts` (pure) names the two pairs and `PUT /membership-plans/:id/billing-policy` rejects anything else with a 400, which is what keeps the single dropdown from being a frontend-only rule. A Plan configured before the rule is left alone: `planBillingFrequencyOf()` answers `null` for its cadence, so the card, the Details modal and the editor keep saying "Every 2 months" instead of relabelling it, and the editor warns that saving the section moves it to the selected frequency. Products' own billing frequency is untouched.)
