- **Done**: #821 (Product Billing Frequency is five choices — *Once*, *Per Session*, *4 Weeks*, *Month*, *Year*. **Week** left the dropdown; nothing left the database. `gym_charges_billing_frequency_check` (migration 123) still permits all six values and there is no backfill and no re-mapping, because a weekly item is a real price whose period is neither a calendar month nor 28 days — so "what may be stored" and "what may be configured" became two different answers, held once in `api/src/domain/productFrequency.ts`. `productFrequencyWriteError(next, current)` is the one rule both routes apply: `POST /sellable-items` refuses `week` outright, `PUT /sellable-items/:id` accepts it **only** when it is the value the row already stores — which is what keeps a legacy weekly item editable (its form submits the value back untouched) without a 400 and without silently rewriting its frequency — and nothing may be moved onto it. A legacy item keeps classifying as a `periodical` benefit (`isRecurringFrequency()` still counts `week`) and keeps billing; `POST /:id/duplicate` copies it verbatim, since Duplicate is a copy, not the dropdown. Admin: the inline create card and the inline editor build their options from one `frequencyOptions()` (#805), which renders the retired value as a **disabled** option only while the form holds it, under a one-line notice (en/es/ca) saying that choosing another replaces it. No migration. Tests: `product-frequency.unit.test.ts` (13), six `#821` integration cases in `gym-charges.test.ts` (create refused, each offered frequency created, legacy read/classified/edited/moved-and-not-back, duplicate faithful) and `apps/admin/src/test/product-frequency.test.ts` (12). Membership Plan billing frequency (#820) is untouched.)

- **Done**: #823 (A Theme upload control says why it is unavailable. The **Upload logo** and Members App **Upload image** buttons were live even when the gym's Cloudflare bucket could not be written to, so an admin could pick a file, press Save and watch the section come back unchanged — the API's 503 (deployment has no `CLOUDFLARE_R2_*`) and 409 (this gym was never initialized, so there is no `storage_folder_prefix` to build a key from, and none of its folder tree — the gym-level `Themes/` root included, #735) reached them only as a toast, after the fact. Both controls, and their hidden file inputs, are now disabled while either holds, with the reason stated in the section; picking is refused in the page's own handlers too, so nothing can be staged for an upload Save would then attempt. The rule is one pure function — `gymStorageBlock()` (`apps/admin/src/lib/gymStorageReadiness.ts`, `'not_configured' | 'not_initialized' | null`) — and the three controls that had their own inline copy of it (`ImageUploadField`, `ExerciseImageField`, `ExerciseVideoField`) now read it from there, so `activeGym.storage_configured`/`storage_folder_prefix` are named in exactly one place. **Base** Themes are not gated: their logo is a blob on the row and their slots live under `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT` (`cordel/Themes/…`), which no gym's bucket state reaches — the same reason a Base Exercise passes `requiresGymStorage={false}`. Frontend only: no route, no payload and no schema changed, and the logo display, the *Logo contains name of the gym?* option, the accepted formats and the size limits are untouched.)

- **Done**: #824 (A Theme logo is stored in the **theme's own** Cloudflare folder, and a failed save says what broke. Two defects in one ticket. (1) The key was #713's gym-wide `<storage_folder_prefix>/Branding/Logo/logo.<ext>`, so a gym held one branding logo and every upload handed the slot over — the uploading theme took the key and every sibling dropped its reference. `buildThemeLogoKey()`/`themeLogoFolderKeys()` (`domain/themeLogo.ts`, over the new shared `domain/themeFolders.ts`) make it `<storage_folder_prefix>/Themes/<theme_id>-<sanitized name>/Logo/logo.<ext>` — the sibling of the `Members/` leaf #725 writes — so each theme carries its own logo, the upload deletes only what *its own* row pointed at, the hand-over is gone with the key that required it, and `Branding/` is written by nothing (removing it from Gym Bucket Initialization is #826's). The theme folder and its `Logo/` leaf are created on demand; the gym-level `Themes/` root deliberately is not — it is #735's, so a gym without it stays a 409 and a disabled control (#823). No migration: a row written before this keeps its `Branding/Logo/` key, still renders (the URL is derived from the stored key), and moves the next time its logo is replaced. `infra/storage.ts`' `buildGymLogoKey()`/`BRANDING_LOGO_FOLDER` are deleted. (2) The generic **Unauthorized** the ticket reports was the frontend's: the theme logo and Members background uploads were hand-rolled `fetch('/api/proxy/…')` calls sending only the bearer token, and the Next proxy forwards `x-gym-id` but cannot invent it — so every tenant-scoped upload reached `tenantContext` with no gym. New `uploadFetch()` in `lib/apiClient.ts` sends the same headers `apiFetch` does with the file's own `Content-Type`, and both Themes pages use it. On top of that the storage routes now answer with `stage` (`domain/storageFailureStage.ts` — resolve the path, create the theme folder, create the leaf, upload, remove, save settings) and the `path` they were working on, beside `describeStorageError()`'s existing `details`; `lib/storageErrorMessage.ts`'s pure `formatStorageError()` renders the ticket's Operation / Path / Error / Details block, with the page naming the step only as the fallback for a failure that never reached storage. One locale key per stage in en/es/ca, since next-intl prints a missing key verbatim. Tests: 10 unit cases for the new keys and folder markers in `storage.test.ts`, 7 in `storage-failure-stage.unit.test.ts`, the rewritten `theme-logo-storage.test.ts` (per-theme keys, the folder markers, the legacy `Branding/` row moving on replace, siblings untouched, `stage`/`path` on 409/502/503) and 13 admin cases in `theme-upload-diagnostics.test.ts`.)

- **#782 / #872 (done)**: freshness alert for the nightly runs. New unauthenticated `GET /health/runs` (outside `/billing/`) answers `{ billing, recurring_bookings }` with `{ last_completed_at, age_hours, stale }` each, from the latest `completed` row of each run log; threshold `RUN_FRESHNESS_THRESHOLD_HOURS` (default 26, `domain/runFreshness.ts`). No migration. The Grafana half (#872) was configured by hand on 2026-10-06: two alert rules (billing `critical`, recurring bookings `warning`) reading the endpoint through the Infinity data source, No data/Error → Alerting, routed to `gymdesk-dev`; proved with a temporary inverted rule that fired and resolved. Setup in `docs/payments.md` → Observability today; the alert email was delivered on a contact-point test the same day; routing the alerts through the admin app (#1166) stays open in `docs/go-to-production.md` §4b.

- **Done**: #826 (Gym Bucket Initialization creates exactly three first-level folders. `GYM_FOLDERS` in `api/src/infra/storage.ts` still wrote `Branding/`, `Branding/Logo/`, `Branding/Images/` and `Members/` under every gym root, and nothing has written a byte into any of them since #824 moved the theme logo to `Themes/<theme_id>-<name>/Logo/` and #725 put a theme's six Members App slots under `Themes/<theme_id>-<name>/Members/` — so a freshly initialized bucket showed an admin five empty folders that will never fill. The gym root is now `Nutrition/`, `Exercises/` and `Themes/` and nothing else; the leaves below them (`Nutrition/Images/`, `Exercises/Images/`, `Exercises/Videos/`) are untouched, since the ticket is scoped to what sits directly under the root (§6). The markers keep the spelling the key builders use — `domain/exerciseImages.ts`, `domain/exerciseVideos.ts` and `domain/themeFolders.ts` write `Exercises/Images`, `Exercises/Videos` and `Themes/`, and a marker differing in case alone would add a *fourth* first-level folder beside the one uploads populate rather than rename it; a unit test asserts the two agree. Initialization stays idempotent and write-only, so a gym provisioned earlier keeps the five obsolete markers until someone deletes them — a `docs/go-to-production.md` sweep, which notes that a `themes.logo_object_key` written before #824 may still point into `Branding/Logo/`. No migration, no API change, no frontend change.)

- **Done**: #827 (A Custom Theme is created only when the gym's Cloudflare bucket is, and creating one initializes the theme's own folders. Cloning was the one way a Custom Theme came into existence and it wrote a `themes` row with no regard for storage, so a gym whose bucket had never been initialized could accumulate themes whose every upload control was disabled (#823) and whose every upload route answered 409 — a theme with nowhere to keep its assets. `POST /system/themes/clone/:sourceId` now resolves the gym's `storage_folder_prefix` **before** the INSERT (503 when the deployment has no `CLOUDFLARE_R2_*`, 409 when this gym was never initialized, each with the `stage`/`path` diagnostic #824 added) and then writes the new theme's three folder markers — `Themes/<theme_id>-<sanitized name>/` with its `Logo/` and `Members/` leaves — through the new `themeStorageFolderKeys()` (`domain/themeFolders.ts`) and `ensureStorageFolders()`. Storage first, row second, which is what makes a partially created theme impossible: a 502 from R2 leaves no row, and the markers are zero-byte keys ending in `/`, so re-writing one is idempotent and can never touch a real object. The clone gets its own folder (the key carries the new id), the gym-level `Themes/` root is still Gym Bucket Initialization's (#735) and is never created here, and a failed marker is named by position — `themeFolderStageForKey()` maps the three keys onto `create_theme_folder`/`create_logo_folder`/`create_members_folder` rather than pattern-matching a key, so a theme *named* "Logo" is not mistaken for its own leaf. The two leaf names moved to `domain/themeFolders.ts` (re-exported by `themeLogo.ts` and `themeMemberImages.ts`) because creation now writes them before either upload feature is involved; their case is load-bearing, since a `logo/` marker would add a folder beside `Logo/` rather than rename it (#826's rule). Admin: `⋮ → Clone` is disabled with the reason as its tooltip while `gymStorageBlock(activeGym)` holds — #823's one rule, no second copy — the submit refuses too, and a failed creation renders the same Operation/Path/Error/Details block every other storage-backed save does. **Base** Themes are untouched: their objects live under `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT` (`cordel/Themes/…`), which no gym's bucket governs. No migration, no schema change. Tests: 18 unit cases (`theme-storage-folders.unit.test.ts`, `storage-failure-stage.unit.test.ts`), the new `theme-storage-init.test.ts` (409/503 create nothing, the three markers, the roots untouched, the clone's own path, 502 per leaf, plus the clone cases moved out of `gym-themes.test.ts`, which mocks no R2 client) and 8 admin cases in `theme-create-storage-gating.test.ts`.)

- **Done**: #828 (**Initialize bucket** on both Theme context menus — the manual, repeatable counterpart to the initialization #827 does at creation time. A Theme created before #827, or one renamed since (a rename moves its folder and nothing re-creates it until the next upload), had no way to get its folder tree back short of uploading something. Two routes, one per storage root: `POST /system/themes/:id/storage/initialize` (gym admin, the Theme's folder under the gym's `storage_folder_prefix`) and `POST /platform/themes/:id/storage/initialize` (superadmin, under `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT`), each answering only for its own rows — a Base Theme is 404 on the gym route and a Custom Theme 404 on the platform one, so neither reaches the other's objects by changing the Theme id. Both write exactly `themeStorageFolderKeys()` (#827): the theme's own folder plus its `Logo/` and `Members/` leaves, zero-byte markers whose keys end in `/`, so a re-run ensures the structure and can never delete or overwrite an object — and neither route touches the `themes` row, so the Theme's id, name and configuration are untouched (§3). The gym root and its `Themes/` branch stay Gym Bucket Initialization's (#735): a gym with no prefix is a 409 rather than having its tree papered over, which is §5's distinction, and the gym-side menu item is disabled with the reason through `gymStorageBlock()` exactly as Clone is (#823). The platform side is gated by no gym's bucket, so only the deployment-wide 503 applies there. Failures report the marker that broke, reusing #824's `stage`/`path`/`details` — new `formatStorageErrorLine()` in `lib/storageErrorMessage.ts` renders the same diagnostic on one line, because `Toast` renders its message as a single text node. The clone route's inline folder-writing block became `ensureThemeStorage()` in `gym-themes.ts`, shared with the new route so the keys, the stage and the 502 shape have one definition. No migration, no schema change. Tests: 21 integration cases in `theme-bucket-initialize.test.ts` (idempotence, markers only, the roots untouched, the row untouched, cross-router 404s, 401/403, 409/503/502 per leaf) and 14 admin cases in `theme-bucket-initialize.test.ts`.)

- **Done**: #830 (A failed Theme asset upload says what broke, and one asset failing no longer takes the others with it. Three defects. (1) The **broken `logo preview`** the ticket is filed with was the Next proxy, not the Theme editor: `app/api/proxy/[...path]/route.ts` read every backend response with `res.text()`, and a UTF-8 decode of binary replaces each invalid byte with U+FFFD. A Base Theme's logo is a `logo_bytes` blob served as raw bytes by `GET /themes/:id/logo` (it has no R2 `logo_url`), so the `<img>` received a corrupted PNG carrying the right `Content-Type` — nothing errored, the browser simply could not decode it. Both proxies now forward `await res.arrayBuffer()`, byte-exact for JSON too; the Admin one also carries the receipt PDFs `pdfFetch()` reads with `res.blob()`, which had the same corruption. (2) Each Theme screen carried **its own copy** of the Save sequence, and the copies had drifted: the Base Themes page removed a logo and a Members slot through a bare `apiFetch` with no diagnostic, so a failure there was the `Unauthorized` #824 existed to replace, and its settings `PUT` had none either. The sequence is now declared once in `apps/admin/src/components/themes/themeAssetSave.ts` — `planThemeAssetOps()` (the logo first, then the six slots in editor order, a picked file always beating a queued removal), `themeAssetOpRequest()` (the per-asset path under the router root the page passes in, `/system/themes` or `/platform/themes`, so #806's rule holds and no permission decision moves into shared code), `runThemeAssetOps()` and the failure formatting. (3) The first failure **aborted every asset after it**: the loop threw, so a rejected `training` upload meant `nutrition`, `calendar` and the rest were never attempted. `runThemeAssetOps()` attempts all of them and throws nothing, the page renders every failure as its own Operation/Path/Error/Details block (`formatThemeAssetFailures()`), and `pendingAfterFailures()` keeps exactly the failed assets queued with their files and previews, so pressing Save again retries those and re-uploads none of the successes. A Members failure names its slot (`storage_error_title_members_image_slot`, `{slot}` interpolated), each failing control is marked next to itself (`logoError`, `slotErrors`), an error with nothing usable in it gets `storage_error_fallback` instead of an empty `Error:` line, and a preview whose URL will not load renders `logo_preview_unavailable` rather than the browser's broken-image icon. The only step that still aborts the Save is the settings `PUT`: the asset keys are built from the theme's persisted name, so there is nothing to gain from uploading against a rename that did not happen. Storage locations, validation rules, Remove semantics and the successful path are unchanged (§Existing Successful Behavior, §Remove Behavior). No migration, no API change. Tests: 37 admin cases in `theme-upload-error-handling.test.ts` (the plan and its ordering, the per-asset paths under both roots, every asset attempted despite a failure, the API error carried through, the diagnostic and its fallback, what stays queued, both proxies reading bytes, both pages on the shared module, and every interpolated key present in `gym_themes` and `themes` across en/es/ca); `theme-members-images.test.ts` and `base-theme-members-images.test.ts` follow the sequence to its new home.)

- **Done**: #832 (**Mandatory** on Products. A new `gym_charges.mandatory` flag (migration 200, `tinyint(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`), rendered as a single checkbox in the inline create card and the inline editor and as a Yes/No row on the read-only expanded card and in the Details modal. One column and one control serve both kinds of row the page holds — the per-gym System items seeded from `charge_types` (`is_system=1`, the ticket's "Base Products", the Insurance Fee among them) and the gym's own (`is_system=0`, "Custom") — because both live in this one table, so there are no two implementations to keep consistent. The flag is **editable on a System item**, which is why `PUT /sellable-items/:id` writes it outside the `IF(? = 0, …)` `is_system` guard that still freezes a System row's name/type/units, and as `mandatory = IF(?, ?, mandatory)` keyed on whether the request supplied the field (the shape `PUT /promotions/:id` uses for `only_applicable_for_new_members`) — a `COALESCE` on the value itself could never persist an unchecked box. `validateMandatory()` accepts a boolean or 0/1 and 400s on anything else, so the truthy string `"false"` cannot silently mark an item mandatory. `POST /sellable-items` defaults it to `0`, existing items are non-mandatory by the column default (no backfill statement), and `POST /:id/duplicate` copies it verbatim. No CHECK: the router coerces to `? 1 : 0` and adding one would rebuild `gym_charges` under `ALGORITHM=COPY`. **Nothing reads the flag** — automatic inclusion in Billing Plans, preventing removal from one, Billing Plan pricing, Membership Plan Benefits and any Insurance Fee special case are explicitly out of scope and belong to the later ticket that consumes the attribute. Tests: eleven `#832` integration cases in `gym-charges.test.ts` (default, create both ways, read-back on `GET /:id` and the list, PUT toggling both ways, PUT leaving it alone, editable on a System item whose name/type still are not, 0/1 accepted and a non-boolean 400d, an explicit null treated as absent, duplicate) and nine admin cases in `products-mandatory.test.ts`.)

- **Done**: #833 (Members App theme settings with per-setting Admin inheritance. The shared Theme editor gains a **Members App** section holding five collapsible sub-sections — `Header (Members App)`, `Background (Members App)`, `Section Cards (Members App)`, `Text (Members App)`, `Calendar (Members App)` — and the 18 settings of §8's mapping. Both Theme screens render one component (`ThemeMembersAppEditor`) over one declaration (`apps/admin/src/lib/membersAppTokens.ts`), so Base Themes and Custom Themes get the same settings, sources and UI with no second editor (§15). **No migration and no schema change**: `tokens.membersApp` is an overrides-only map, so an inherited setting is never stored (§13, §19) and `Restore inherited value` deletes the key — dropping the map when it empties — rather than writing the Admin value into it. That is what keeps inheritance dynamic: an Admin colour edited later moves every setting still inherited from it and none that was overridden, decided per setting (§12, §18). Each control carries the `Inherited`/`Custom` badge the `advanced` attributes already use plus a secondary line naming its source — `inherited from Header Background` — and `validateMembersApp()` (`api/src/domain/membersAppTokens.ts`, reached through `validateTokens()`, so both routers apply it) refuses an unknown key, a non-hex colour, a font outside `FONT_STACKS`, a separator width outside 0–20 and a blank CSS length; `HEX_RE`/`FONT_STACKS` moved to `domain/themeTokenFormats.ts` so the two validators need not import each other. Two Admin sources genuinely did not exist and were added as `advanced` attributes beside the settings they belong to, each wired to a CSS variable a real surface reads — `headerTextFont` → `--gd-header-font` (`TopHeader`) and `cardBorderWidth` → `--gd-card-border-width` (`cardSurfaceStyle`) — because an attribute the editor persists that nothing reads is the #677 defect. The two §14 flagged as possibly missing were **not** added: `Application Surface` is the existing `advanced.modalBackground` and `Input Background` the existing `colors.inputBackgroundColor`, reused per §7/§14 rather than duplicated, and the modal settings deliberately never read `calendarBackground`. On the Members App side `ThemeProvider` calls `applyMembersAppTokens()` after `applyTokens()`, so a setting sharing a variable with its source (`--gd-app-bg`, `--gd-calendar-*`, `--gd-color-h1..h3`, `--gd-header-sep-*`) wins, and the rest get `--gd-members-*` variables read by `TopBar` (which had been borrowing the Admin **sidebar** colour for the header), `MembersSectionCard` (the border, on the one component every navigation card renders through) and the member Calendar page (the event window and its dropdowns); the member pages' heading colours now read `--gd-color-h1..h3` with their current values as the fallback, so Title 1/2/3 reach a surface. No Members App preview (§16), and the editor holds no save state — it hands the page a token draft, so the existing Save/Cancel and dirty state cover it (§17). Tests: 24 admin cases in `members-app-theme-settings.test.ts` (the mapping against §8 transcribed from the ticket, every source resolving to a setting that exists, override/restore/null semantics, the editor's structure, the locale keys in en/es/ca across both namespaces, and the two mirrors), 16 member cases in `members-app-theme-vars.test.ts` (mirror agreement, resolution, overriding a shared variable, no setting left unpainted), 11 API unit cases in `members-app-tokens.unit.test.ts`, plus integration cases on both Theme routers.)

- **Done**: #809 (Nutrition → Dashboard — Nutrition Plan overview cards. The `Nutrition → Dashboard` page (`apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/nutrition/page.tsx`, a placeholder until now) renders one card per **Nutrition Plan Template** — its name, the number of **active members** holding a Nutrition Plan created from it, and the Template's own status badge — plus one bucket card, *No base nutrition template plan*, for the plans created from scratch (`member_nutrition_plans.template_id IS NULL`), which the issue owner asked for by name on the thread. `GET /nutrition/dashboard/nutrition-plans` (`api/src/api/nutrition-dashboard.ts`, new router mounted on the **`nutrition` group flag** so switching the Nutrition Plans page off leaves the Dashboard readable — #638's Finance Dashboard choice) answers it in one aggregate query, driven by the assignments rather than by the Templates: a card therefore exists only where a counted member exists (no empty `0` cards, §4), the Template's status is never the filter (an `inactive` or `draft` Template shows as long as an active member is on it, §7) and a plan whose Template row belongs to another gym resolves to no Template and falls into the bucket, so no other gym's Template name can be read off the endpoint. A Template soft-deleted while members still hold plans from it keeps its card with `deleted` as the status — dropping it would silently remove those members from the overview. **Active member** is the Members page's `enrollment_status = 'active'` (the member's latest `user_memberships` row) plus `members.deleted_at IS NULL`, and that derivation now lives once in `api/src/domain/memberEnrollment.ts` (`latestEnrollmentStatusSql()`), read by both `GET /members` and this endpoint — a gym owner reading `12 active members` off a card and then filtering the Members page by `Enrollment status: Active` gets the same set. Only `active` Nutrition Plans count (a `completed` one is no longer currently assigned), and the count is of **members**, so a member holding two plans from the same Template counts once. No migration, no write path, no change to Nutrition Plan creation, editing, assignment or Template behaviour. Tests: `api/src/test/nutrition-dashboard.test.ts` (auth, the two feature flags, tenant isolation, which cards show, the bucket and its ordering, and the counting rules) and `api/src/test/member-enrollment.unit.test.ts`)

- **Done**: #818 (Membership Plan **Example Timeline** — the Plan card's simulation is now the Promotion card's. The #485 *Billing Events Forecast* listed the next ten charges from price × cadence and never looked at Billing & Duration, so a Plan with a two-month Free Period forecast a charge for each of those months; the section is replaced (same slot, `PLAN_SECTION_ORDER`'s `section_example_timeline`) by a Period / Dates / Status / Billing table, **one row per billing period of the Plan's own cadence**. `api/src/domain/planExampleTimeline.ts` (pure) is the projection: rows step with `advanceBillingDate()` over the stored `billing_policies` pair — a legacy cadence included, stepped exactly as stored — and each row's Status is `classifyPlanDurationPeriod()` evaluated at its own start date, i.e. the one rule the nightly run, `resolveMembershipFee()` and `GET /me/membership` already price a cycle with. That is the thread's Q1 answer, "only the simulation": the durations stay calendar months, so a 4-weekly Plan with Free Period = 2 shows **three** free rows rather than advertising a charge the run does not make. Rows = Free + Paid + Bonus + 2 trailing regular ones (Q5), capped at `MAX_TIMELINE_PERIODS`; the last row is open-ended only when it is regular. The Billing column quotes the Plan's current price **VAT-inclusive** as the server computed it (`amount_incl_tax`, gross alone for a gym with no rate — no arithmetic in the frontend, #817) and `No charge` for a Free / Bonus / Pre-paid period; a Plan with no price reads `—`, never €0.00. Statuses read *Free (benefit)* / *Prepaid (benefit)* / *Pay (benefit)* / *Bonus (benefit)* / *Pay (regular)* (Q3) — the Plan's vocabulary, never the Promotion's. `enrichPlan` embeds it as `example_timeline` and `GET /membership-plans/:id/example-timeline` replaces `…/billing-forecast`; `domain/billingForecast.ts` and its unit test are deleted. No migration, nothing persisted, no billing rule changed — Auto-renew and assignment stayed out of scope per the thread. The table itself is the shared `apps/admin/src/components/ExampleTimeline.tsx`, rendered by **both** the Plan and Promotion cards (the ticket's "do not introduce a second simulation design"), presentational only: labels, classification and the Billing cell stay with each page. Tests: `plan-example-timeline.test.ts` (13 unit), reshaped `#818` cases in `membership-plans.test.ts`, `charge-benefits-retired.test.ts`, `membership-plan-benefits.test.ts` and `apps/admin/src/test/plan-example-timeline.test.ts` (13).)

- **Done**: #829 (Theme assets land in the folders the ticket names, and a Base Theme logo is an object like every other asset. Two halves. (1) **The spelling.** The theme tree was `Themes/<theme_id>-<name>/` with `Logo/` and `Members/` leaves; it is now `themes/<theme_id>-<name>/` with `logo/` and `members_app/`, which also lowercases the gym root's third first-level folder (`THEMES_FOLDER` in `api/src/infra/storage.ts`, `THEME_LOGO_FOLDER`/`THEME_MEMBERS_FOLDER` in `domain/themeFolders.ts` — one constant each, so every key builder, folder marker and initialization route moved together). R2 has no directories, so nothing was renamed and nothing was rewritten: a row's stored key is the only way back to its object, so assets already in the bucket keep rendering under the old names and move on their next upload — the owner sweeps the old tree by hand, which is the thread's answer ("I'll delete the old structure manually so no migration is needed"), and `docs/go-to-production.md` carries the item. (2) **The Base Theme logo.** `POST /platform/themes/:id/logo` wrote a `themes.logo_bytes` MEDIUMBLOB, the one asset still in the database, because #824 had no platform folder to upload into; `cordel/` has been a real root since #732, so it now stores `cordel/themes/<theme_id>-<sanitized name>/logo/logo.<ext>` and keeps the key in `themes.logo_object_key` with `logo_bytes` cleared — the Custom Theme's own columns, not a second store (the thread's "No logo_bytes. Just reuse the same fields"), and the URL stays derived (`themeLogoUrl()`), so `shapeTheme()` on that router returns `logo_url` too. Same 512 KB / PNG-SVG-JPEG-WebP validation, extension from the validated MIME (the uploaded file name reaches no key), folders created on demand (`themeRootFolderKeys()` + `themeLogoFolderKeys()` — `cordel/` and `cordel/themes/` included, since no Gym Bucket Initialization owns the platform root), best-effort sweep of the key it replaced, `DELETE` removing the object first and reporting a 502 rather than dropping the reference, and `stage`/`path` diagnostics throughout — minus the gym route's 409, which a constant root cannot have. 404 for a gym's Custom Theme, as the gym route is 404 for a Base Theme (§8). `GET /themes/:id/logo` is untouched and still serves either mode, so a logo uploaded before this keeps rendering from its blob. No migration and no schema change. Tests: `base-theme-logo-storage.test.ts` (25, the new platform route end to end) and `theme-asset-paths.unit.test.ts` (8, the ticket's §1–§4 tree pinned for both roots — logo plus all six slots), plus the renamed paths across the nine existing theme-storage suites and the admin/member source guards.)

- **Done**: #879 (Membership Plan **Billing & Duration** adopts the Promotions look — a presentation-only alignment. The Plan card's read-only section rendered a two-column `DetailRow` list with a long explanatory sentence under *Billing Frequency* and under *Auto-renew*; it now renders the compact `Label: Value` summary the Promotion card has shown since #550, from **one shared component** (`apps/admin/src/components/BillingDurationSummary.tsx`) both pages import — the style literal that used to sit inline in `promotions/page.tsx` lives there and in no page, so the next change to that look cannot reach one screen and miss the other. All six values stay (Free Period, Paid Duration, Pre-paid Duration, Bonus Duration, Billing Frequency, Auto-renew), durations first, wrapping responsively; an unconfigured duration still reads *Not configured* and a Plan with no `billing_policy` still says so. Which items appear remains each page's own decision — the Promotion keeps omitting a zero month count. The two descriptions are gone from the summary only: they are still rendered by the editor `⋮ → Edit` → *Edit* opens, which is untouched, as are every label key, the API, the durations, the cadence and Auto-renew. No migration, no API change, no new locale key. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/plans-billing-duration-summary.test.ts` (16), and `plan-billing-frequency.test.ts`'s read-only assertion now pins the `billingFrequencyText()` call rather than the JSX around it.)

- **Done**: #881 (Membership Plan **Price History** moves inside **Pricing**. #817 made the history collapsible but left it trailing the card as a section of its own, two sections below the price it is the history of. It is now a collapsible card rendered *within* PRICING, after the Tax Rate / Current price rows and before Billing & Duration, still collapsed every time the plan is expanded. The framing is the only change: the rows, their newest-first ordering, the VAT hint, the active/inactive badge and the empty-state line are untouched, no pricing or price-history logic is involved, and the card holds no control but its own expander, so the expanded card stays read-only outside Edit mode (#797/#816). `CollapsibleSectionHeader` became `CollapsibleCard`: it now renders its body as a child behind `open` — so "only while open" is the component's, not a caller's — and wears the plan card's own themed chrome (`cardSurfaceStyle`) instead of the `subSectionSt` divider, which inside a section would have read as the start of a new one. `PLAN_SECTION_ORDER` drops `section_prices` and the new `PLAN_PRICING_SUBSECTIONS` declares it as PRICING's, so "section or sub-section?" is answered once and a key in both lists — which would render the history twice — is a failing test. Frontend only: no migration, no API change, no new locale key.)

- **Done**: #882 (Members **PROFILE** reads in the layout it is edited in — a presentation-only change to the expanded Member row. The read-only section rendered a compact `Label: value` list while `⋮ → Edit` rendered a responsive grid, so opening the form moved every field on screen and showed the Profile twice on one page. Both halves now render the same `MemberProfileLayout` (`apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/members/MemberProfileLayout.tsx`): one grid, one field order, one set of labels, Notes full-width and Assigned Centers / Default Center in one position, at the same breakpoints. Each half still owns only the contents of a cell — an `<input>`/`<select>`/`<textarea>` on one side, the persisted value on the other — and the read-only side carries no control, no help text and no Edit affordance (#797 unchanged). The declaration in `memberProfile.ts` grew each field's `editLabelKey`, `placeholderKey` and `helpKey`, so the Edit form no longer hand-writes an input per field and Name keeps its required marker in the form alone. While the inline form is open the read-only section stands down (`editing` prop), so the Profile renders once. No API, migration, validation, save behaviour, centers logic or locale key changed. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/member-profile-layout.test.ts` (13), with #797's `member-expanded-profile.test.ts` re-pointed at the shared layout.)

- **Done**: #883 (the **mobile navigation drawer scrolls**. The drawer was bounded to the viewport below the top bar, but the panel inside it carried `min-height: 100vh` and nothing was allowed to overflow, so with several groups expanded the lower navigation items sat past the bottom of the screen and no swipe could reach them. The wrapper now bounds (`100dvh` with the `100vh` fallback, `overflow: hidden`), the panel fills it (`flex: 1; min-height: 0`, no viewport height of its own) and the `<nav>` is the single scroll container, with `overscroll-behavior: contain` and `-webkit-overflow-scrolling: touch`; the page behind is frozen by a `sidebar-drawer-open` class on `<body>` scoped to the mobile media query. The nav became a block container rather than a column flex box, so an expanded group overflows instead of squeezing its neighbours. Navigation items, hierarchy, permissions, routes, active-route detection, expand/collapse and the drawer's own open/close behaviour are untouched, and the desktop sidebar takes no height or overflow rule. Frontend only: no migration, no API change, no new locale key. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/mobile-sidebar-scroll.test.ts` (10).)

- **Done**: #884 (Icons on the sidebar's first-level navigation. Each of the nine collapsible groups now carries an icon beside its expand/collapse chevron — Membership (people), Calendar, Organization (building), Training (dumbbell), Nutrition (apple), Payments (card), Financials (banknote), Configuration (sliders) and Cordel (shield). The icon is **named in the nav config** (`icon: NavIconName`, required, so a group added later cannot reach the sidebar without one) and drawn by one registry, `apps/admin/src/components/icons/NavIcons.tsx`; `NavGroup` renders `<NavIcon name={group.icon} />` and branches on nothing. The nine are one set, not nine drawings: a single `IconShell` declares the 24×24 grid, the 1.75 stroke, the round joins and the 16px box, they stroke in `currentColor` and fill nothing — so they follow the header's existing colour rather than adding a state — and each `<svg>` is `aria-hidden`/`focusable="false"`, leaving the label as the accessible name. No icon library was added: nine icons do not pay for a dependency, and the components follow the app's inline-style shape. The chevron is untouched and keeps its own job; the only spacing that moved is the group header's padding (20px → 16px) and gap (8px → 6px), which is what buys the icon its room — measured across en/es/ca, the longest label (`ENTRENAMIENTO`, 196px) still fits the 220px sidebar on one line, and every label starts at the same x since neither the chevron nor the `<svg>` may shrink. Second-level items stay text-only. Frontend only: no migration, no API change, no locale key, no route, role, feature-key gate, hierarchy or expand/collapse behaviour touched. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/nav-icons.test.ts` (13).)

- **Done**: #893 (**Mandatory Products are always part of a Membership Plan.** #832 added `gym_charges.mandatory` and deliberately left it unread; this is the ticket that consumes it, for the Membership Plan Benefit sections and nothing else (§9 scopes out Promotion benefits, billing logic and Product pricing). The rule lives once, in `api/src/domain/mandatoryPlanBenefits.ts` (pure): `mandatoryItemsForCategory()` picks the gym's **active, non-deleted** mandatory items that classify into one section through `classifyProduct()` (#550, never a second mapping), `mergeMandatoryBenefits()` reports the ones a Plan has no stored row for as part of the section (`implicit: true`, `quantity: 1` — §5's "appropriate default configuration"), and `withMandatoryBenefits()` is what a replace-all `PUT` writes. Two decisions: **the server decides, not the editor** — §7 is explicit that the frontend must not be able to bypass the rule, so `GET /membership-plans/:id/{session,oneoff,periodical}-benefits` and `enrichPlan`'s three embedded sections merge, and the `PUT` writes a mandatory item whether or not the client sent it; and **a `PUT` missing one preserves it rather than 400s** (§7 allows either), which is what makes §5 work on its own — an existing Plan picks up a newly mandatory item the first time any of its sections is saved. A submitted mandatory item passes through untouched, quantity included (§4: mandatory says the item must exist, never what its quantity is), and §8's no-duplicates rule is the merge's own `has` check. An **inactive or soft-deleted** mandatory item is ignored: the benefit `PUT` already refuses a newly selected inactive item, and forcing one in would fail every Plan save on a catalogue change nobody asked for. Frontend: the shared `ProductBenefits.tsx` gains an explicit `enforceMandatory` opt-in the Plans page passes to both halves of each section — a mandatory row renders its item as a labelled value with a `Mandatory` pill instead of a picker and has no `✕` control, so the item can be neither dropped nor swapped away, and the form (never the read-only view, #797) carries the sentence that says why. Promotions pass nothing and are unchanged. The benefit `SELECT` joins `gc.mandatory` as `gym_charge_mandatory`; no migration, no new endpoint, and nothing in billing or the Assigned Plan snapshot changes — a mandatory item reaches an assignment's snapshot the ordinary way, as a stored Plan benefit row. Tests: `mandatory-plan-benefits.unit.test.ts` (13 pure cases) plus thirteen `#893` integration cases in `membership-plan-benefits.test.ts` and twelve admin cases in `plan-mandatory-benefits.test.ts`.)

- **Done**: #892 (**Membership Plan durations are counted in the Plan's Billing Frequency periods.** A Plan billed every 4 Weeks showed `Paid Duration: 2 month(s)`, and meant it: the Example timeline stepped rows by the cadence while `classifyPlanDurationPeriod()` stepped statuses by `advanceBillingDate(startsAt, n, 'month')` with the unit hard-coded, so the rows and their statuses were measured differently — #818's Q1 answer, which this ticket reverses in the nightly run as well as in the preview. The four durations are now counts of the Plan's own Billing Frequency periods: `PlanDuration` carries the cadence it is counted in and `toPlanDuration()` takes it as a **required** argument (the reason `personalFeeBenefit` is required, #772), resolved through `ASSIGNMENT_CADENCE` for an assignment and `billing_policies` for a Plan, falling back to `1 month` — what every duration meant before — when there is none. There is still exactly one classifier, and `computePlanExampleTimeline()` re-binds the duration to the cadence it steps, so a 4-weekly Plan with Free Period = 2 shows exactly **two** free rows. Migration 201 renames the columns the owner asked for on `membership_plans` **and** on every assignment's frozen snapshot (`free_periods` / `paid_periods` / `pay_beforehand_periods` / `bonus_periods`), dropping and re-adding migration 189's CHECK around the rename; `RENAME COLUMN` is in-place in MySQL 8, no value is converted and no data is reinterpreted at rest (§6/§7 — changing the frequency changes the unit, never the numbers), and a Plan on the Month cadence bills exactly as it did. `FEE_ASSIGNMENT_COLUMNS` gained the cadence pair and `FEE_ASSIGNMENT_FROM` a LEFT JOIN on `billing_policies`, so every path that prices a fee — the nightly run, the Billing Simulation, `GET /me/membership`, the Payments dashboard, the Promotion apply/revoke adjustment — reads the same unit. Admin: each number box in the Billing & Duration editor is suffixed with the **selected** frequency (`× 4 Weeks`, so the dropdown changes the unit and never the values), and the card summary, the Details modal and the Assigned Plan snapshot render through one `formatPlanDurationPeriods()` — `2 × 4 Weeks`, `2 month(s)` for a monthly Plan, `2 period(s)` for a pre-#820 cadence or a Plan with no billing policy. **Promotions are untouched**, per the ticket's closing note: `promotions.free_months`/`paid_months`/`bonus_months` and `promotionTimeline.ts` keep their calendar months, so a Plan period and a Promotion month can appear in the same simulation. `docs/go-to-production.md` gains the deploy-order item (migrate before deploying the API) and the announcement that a 4-weekly Plan's free and bonus windows get shorter, which raises real charges. Tests: `plan-duration.test.ts` and `plan-example-timeline.test.ts` extended with Month / 4 Weeks / legacy-cadence cases asserting the period **dates**, a new integration file `plan-duration-billing-frequency.test.ts` (the nightly run charging the third 4-week cycle a calendar-month reading would have waived, the timeline, the editor's payload, and Promotions staying on months), and `apps/admin/src/test/plan-duration-periods.test.ts` (20).)

- **Done**: #894 (**Mandatory shows in the Product card header.** #832 gave `gym_charges.mandatory` a checkbox in both forms and a row in the two read-only surfaces, so learning whether an item was mandatory meant expanding the card or opening its Edit form. The collapsed header's name cell now carries a compact `Mandatory` pill beside the existing `System` one, in that order, for an item whose `mandatory` column is set — read from the column and never from the item's name or type (§4), so it works identically for a per-gym System item (the Insurance Fee) and a Custom one. The badge is read-only (§5): no checkbox, no handler, and the Status and Enrollment `StatusBadge` columns, the two forms' checkboxes and the payloads are untouched. The pill look moved into `listChrome.ts` as `listNameBadgeStyle` rather than being restated a fourth time — the three pages that draw a name-cell badge (Products, Taxes, Professional Services) now read it from there, which is the #724 rule applied to this one piece of chrome. Frontend only: no migration, no API change (`GET /sellable-items` already projects `gc.mandatory`), one new locale key per language (`products.mandatory_badge`, kept separate from the form's `label_mandatory`). Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/products-mandatory-badge.test.ts` (10).)

- **Done**: #897 (**Promotion component Edit actions move inside Edit mode.** #627 gave each part of an expanded Promotion its own section-level `Edit` button, and they were reachable straight from the read-only card: a staff member reading a Promotion could open the Session, One-off, Periodical or Membership Fee editor without ever passing through `⋮ → Edit`. The card now has one Edit mode (`editingId`), entered only from the context menu, and every editor in the expanded body is gated on it — the main configuration renders as a form while it lasts, and each Benefit section shows its own `Edit` button, unchanged in look, label and placement (§3/§6: the button is not redesigned, only its availability changes). `editingSection` became `openSection` and lost its `'main'` member, since the main configuration is no longer one of the mutually exclusive sections; the per-section saves, their endpoints, `enterSectionEdit`'s re-read and #625's duration clamp are untouched. Cancelling or saving a Benefit section closes that section alone and stays in Edit mode (its error line is its own, so a failed section save no longer prints under the main form); cancelling or saving Edit mode itself returns the Promotion to its **read-only expanded** view rather than collapsing the row — #816's rule, now applied to Promotions — with every section editor closed. Creation is unchanged: the new-Promotion row is still one form with one Save, its section headers carry no Edit button, and cancelling it still discards the row. Frontend only: no migration, no API change, no new locale key. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/promotions-expanded-read-only.test.ts` (8), with #627's `promotions-section-editing.test.ts` updated to the new state names.)

- **Done**: #900 (**Promotions expire automatically after their End Date.** A new `promotions.lifecycle_status` value, `expired`, and a scheduled sweep that writes it. The two states stay distinct on purpose (§1, §4): `inactive` is an administrative decision, `expired` is the calendar catching up with a Promotion left switched on — before this ticket a Promotion whose window closed in July still read *Active* in the list while every apply path already refused it (`validatePromotionSelection` / `applyPromotionToMembership`: *outside its active window*), so the status column and the behaviour disagreed. `expired` is exactly "no longer applicable", which is also why §7 needed no new code: both paths require `lifecycle_status = 'active'`. The rule lives once in `api/src/domain/promotionLifecycle.ts` — `promotionExpiryWhereSql(alias)` (only `active` expires, `ends_at IS NOT NULL` for §6, `ends_at < UTC_TIMESTAMP()` so the comparison stays in the database's clock, the same end-date semantics the apply paths already use) plus `PROMOTION_LIFECYCLE_STATUSES`, which `GET /promotions?lifecycle_status=` and the write validation now both read. Excluding `expired` from the clause is what makes the sweep idempotent (§2): its second run matches nothing. The trigger is `POST /promotion-lifecycle/run` (`api/src/api/promotion-lifecycle.ts`), mounted beside the other internal runners — system-wide, so it cannot sit on the tenant-scoped `/promotions` router — and added as the **third step of `billing-run.yml`**, whose `BILLING_INTERNAL_SECRET` it shares rather than introducing a third secret to provision and rotate (#783 documents the two that exist); a job of its own would get a secret of its own. No run-log claim and no audit row, both deliberate: `claimRun()`/`finishRun()` (#780) exist to stop two *charging* runs overlapping, and `recordAudit()` needs a `tenantCtx` actor a scheduled job does not have (the nightly billing run records `billing_events` for the same reason). Only `lifecycle_status` is written (§8) — the dates, the Billing & Duration months, the benefit sections, the targeted Plans and every `user_membership_promotions` application are untouched, and an application prices from its own snapshot (#635 §16), so a Promotion expiring re-prices nobody. Migration 202 only widens `chk_promotions_lifecycle_status`; **nothing is backfilled** (a statement that expired every past-dated Promotion at deploy time is a data decision taken invisibly — the sweep does it on its first run, counted in the workflow log), and its `down` clears the `expired` rows back to **`active`** before narrowing the CHECK — MySQL validates existing rows and DDL is not transactional, so they have to go, and `active` is both where they were before #900 and the only value that survives the round trip, since the sweep's expirable set is `active` alone and `inactive` would strand every one of them in the status that means a human switched it off. Admin: the page now has a **filter** list (Active / Inactive / Expired, §10 — never lumped together) and an **edit** list (Active / Inactive), because `Expired` is something the sweep writes and not a second way of saying Inactive; an already-expired row renders its status as a *disabled* option so saving its other fields cannot quietly reactivate it, and choosing Active is how a gym revives one (the sweep expires it again unless the End Date moved). `StatusBadge` already had an `expired` colour and `status.expired` already existed in en/es/ca, so §9 needed no new key. `POST /:id/duplicate` still copies the source's status and dates verbatim — Duplicate is a copy. Tests: `promotion-lifecycle.unit.test.ts` (9 pure cases, including that migration 202 permits every status the union declares), `promotion-expiry.test.ts` (15 integration cases — the six §13 scenarios, auth, cross-gym, §8's untouched columns and the API surface) and `apps/admin/src/test/promotions-expired-status.test.ts` (10).)

- **Done**: #896 (**A Product configured in a Promotion or a Membership Plan carries a pricing treatment, not just a quantity.** Stage 1 of the plan agreed on the issue thread: schema + vocabulary, nothing reads it yet. Migration 203 puts an `(action, value)` pair on twelve relationship tables — `promotion_{session,oneoff,periodical}`, `membership_plan_{session,oneoff,periodical}` and the six assignment-side snapshots that have to carry it for the Assigned Plan rule — and on nothing else (§12: the global Product is untouched). Two option sets, declared once in `api/src/domain/productBenefitActions.ts` and mirrored by the `chk_<table>_action` CHECK beside each table: five actions for a Promotion, three for a Plan, since §16 says Plans cannot configure *Fixed discount* or *Fixed Price*. The vocabulary is `PromotionBenefitAction`, not a new enum, and `applyLineBenefit()` delegates to `applyPeriodBenefit()` — §11's "do not introduce a second independent pricing system". New rows default to `no_benefit` (§13); rows that already exist on the **Promotion** side are backfilled to `waive`, because a Promotion grant is an implicit waive today and leaving them neutral would, at stage 3, start charging members for items their Promotion gives them free (the thread's Q1 answer). Stage 2 = API validation, persistence and the snapshot writers; stage 3 = the pricing cutover in `domain/billingSimulation.ts`; stage 4 = the dropdown and its conditional value input in the shared `ProductBenefits.tsx`, plus the labels-only terminology sweep of §3. **Stage 2** made the pair writable and readable: the six replace-all `PUT`s (`/promotions/:id/{session,oneoff,periodical}-benefits` and the Membership Plan equivalents) validate the line's `(action, value)` against its own context through `parseProductBenefitInput()` and persist it, every read reports it normalized (`value` a number, not mysql2's `DECIMAL` string, and an action the context may not configure read back as the neutral default), and both Duplicate paths copy it verbatim. The load-bearing rule is that a `PUT` which does **not** name the pair keeps what the line is stored with rather than resetting it — a replace-all from a client that only knows `gym_charge_id` + `quantity`, which is every client until stage 4, would otherwise wipe migration 203's `waive` backfill and start charging at stage 3 for what a Promotion gives away. Clearing a treatment stays possible and stays explicit (`action: 'no_benefit'`). #893's mandatory rule carries through unchanged: an implicit row reads `no_benefit`, and a mandatory item the client dropped is re-added naming no treatment, so preserving it can never reprice it. **Stage 3** is the pricing cutover: `domain/billingSimulation.ts` stops hard-coding "a Plan benefit is charged and a Promotion grant is free" and reads each row's own pair instead. A Product line is now priced in two steps — the item's own treatment applies to the whole line (`applyLineBenefit()`: the **line**, `unit × quantity`, per the thread's Q2 answer, never the unit) for the life of the assignment, and each covering Promotion grant's treatment is folded on top through `applyPeriodBenefit()` for the periods or units that grant covers, so two Promotions covering the same period both count and a `waive` over anything still gives 0. Nothing an existing member holds moves by a cent: migration 203 backfilled every promotion-side row to `waive` (which is what the hard-coded free coverage meant) and every plan-side row to `no_benefit` (the normal price), and the whole pre-ticket test suite passes unchanged apart from the label a line reports — a grant now says `waive` rather than the pre-#896 `included`, which meant the same thing when a grant could carry no pair. Two rules are load-bearing: a Plan's own treatment never makes a charge **promotional**, because it never ends and the projection's horizon (#629 §6) would otherwise run every discounted item to the 36-month safety cap — it is the same reasoning that keeps the Personal Membership Fee Benefit (#772) out of that flag; and a grant configured `no_benefit` covers the period without changing its price or its horizon, since it discounts nothing. On a one-off or session line the units are allocated grant by grant in order, capped at the quantity actually billed, so two Promotions granting 4 sessions each on a 6-session Plan treat 4 and 2 rather than 8 of 6; whatever is left over is priced by the Plan's own treatment. The three loaders that feed the engine (`loadPlanBenefitsForSimulation`, `loadPromotionGrantSnapshots` and the live-Promotion fallback in `api/billing-simulation.ts`) project the pair through `toProductBenefit()`, each in its own context, so a `fixed_price` that reached a Plan row cannot price there. The pair is a **required** field of `SimulationGrant`/`SimulationPlanBenefit` for the reason `personalFeeBenefit` is: a loader that forgot it would silently charge for what a Promotion gives away. No migration, no API change, no frontend change — the dropdown that configures any of this is stage 4, so until it lands a **newly** created grant defaults to `no_benefit` (§13) and is charged, which is the ticket's intended default and harmless while there is no production environment. Tests: nine new cases in `billing-simulation.test.ts` (57 total) covering waive/percentage/fixed-price/fixed-discount on both shapes, stacking, the horizon rules and the per-grant unit allocation. **Stage 4** is the UI, and it is where the two terminologies finally differ on screen: the shared `ProductBenefits.tsx` editor gains an action dropdown and, beside it, a value input that appears only for the three actions that take one (§6) and names itself after the action — *Promotion (%)*, *Promotion amount*, *Promotion price* — because one column header could not say all three. The option list is per context: `benefitContext="promotion"` offers all five, `benefitContext="plan"` the three §16 allows, and omitting the prop renders no column at all, which is what leaves the Assigned Plan snapshot editor exactly as it was (its `PUT` takes quantity alone, so a dropdown there would change nothing). The lists are mirrored for the browser in `apps/admin/src/lib/productBenefitActions.ts` — a **third** place a new action goes, beside the API declaration and the CHECK, which `product-benefit-actions-ui.test.ts` asserts by reading the API's source. Two rules from §16 are implemented where they cannot drift: a typed value is **kept** in the draft while the action changes, so switching away and back restores it, and `toBenefitItems()` submits only the value the selected action takes; and a line whose draft carries no action at all still sends a quantity-only payload, because stage 2's `PUT`s read that as *keep what is stored*. The Promotions page stopped carrying its own copy of the grid with this stage — `renderProductBenefitEditor` / `…View` are now thin wrappers over the shared component, which is what makes the new field exist once rather than twice. §3's sweep is labels-only, as agreed on the thread: the three remaining `promotions` strings that said "Benefit" now read *Item* and *No promotion* in en/es/ca, and a test fails if any `promotions` label says it again — route paths, table names and locale *key* names are untouched, so the same stored `no_benefit` reads as *No promotion* on one screen and *No benefit* on the other.)

- **Done**: #899 (**The Membership Fee Benefit's Duration is picked from a list of the durations the Promotion can carry.** #625 already bounded the benefit at the Promotion's own timeline (Free Period + Paid Duration + Bonus Duration — Pay Beforehand reclassifies paid periods, it never lengthens the Promotion) and enforced it server-side with a 400 on `PUT /promotions/:id/membership-fee-benefit`, so every backend acceptance line of this ticket was already true and no API, migration or validation changed. What was open is §"Existing Membership Fee Promotion duration"'s "no silent truncation": the editor's Duration was a free-text number input that clamped what you typed with `Math.min(raw, max)`, so entering 12 on a 5-period Promotion silently became 5. The owner's answer on the thread was to remove the problem rather than add a validation message — *the selector only displays the valid options* — so the control is now a `<select>` over `1..max` plus the existing `—` (the whole Promotion, which is what a null duration means and what the API stores), and a value the Promotion cannot carry is simply never offered. A Promotion with no periods at all offers `—` alone, which is how a zero-period Promotion keeps a positive Duration off it. The list and the ceiling moved into one pure module beside the page (`apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/promotions/membershipFeeDuration.ts`), which both `promotionDurationFromForm()` and `promotionDurationFromPromo()` now compute through, so the maximum is derived once and still recomputed live from the unsaved edit form. The one clamp that stays is `clampSavedMembershipFeeDuration()`: it is not truncation of user input but the case where the value was valid when it was picked and the Promotion was shortened underneath it, which would otherwise leave a benefit the backend refuses to save. Durations stay **calendar months** (the thread's Q2 answer: "in promotions we assume 1 month") — #892 moved *Membership Plan* durations onto the Plan's Billing frequency and left `promotionTimeline.ts` alone on purpose, so the column keeps its `Duration (months)` label and no locale key changed. Frontend only: no migration, no API change, no new locale key. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/promotion-membership-fee-duration.test.ts` (13).)

- **Done**: #901 (**One themed subsection `Edit` button for Membership Plans and Promotions.** The same action — the `Edit` beside a subsection header inside a card's Edit mode — was styled twice and differently: Promotions rendered a filled `btnSmall('#6c63ff')`, Membership Plans a bare `linkBtn` text link, on two cards that are otherwise deliberately identical (#879's shared Billing & Duration summary, #816 §14's shared section set). Both now render one component, `apps/admin/src/components/SectionEditButton.tsx`, which owns the geometry (the Promotions filled shape, per §1) and the colours and nothing else. The colours are the Theme's **existing** `primaryButton`/`primaryButtonText` — §4 asks that an existing setting be reused rather than a new one added, and those two were already in the editor's Buttons group and already written by `applyTokens()` as `--gd-primary-btn`/`--gd-primary-btn-text` while being consumed by nothing, the same inert-setting gap #677 closed for `cardBorderRadius`. `primaryButton`'s default *is* `#6c63ff`, so nothing looks different until a gym themes it, and the two literals in the module are CSS `var()` fallbacks for the frames before `applyTokens()` has run, never a second source of truth (§3's "no hardcoded lilac"). Deliberately **not** `--brand`, which is `sidebarSelectedItemBackground` — the sidebar's selected-item colour, not an action colour — and which is what the Plans link read. The shared button resolves no locale key and makes no permission decision (#806's split, and #879's reason: the two pages namespace their keys differently and a Plan says *Edit pricing* where a Promotion says *Edit*), so `label`, `disabled` and `title` arrive decided; §6's availability is untouched, which is the load-bearing half — the button is still **absent** rather than disabled on a read-only expanded card (#897 for Promotions' `onEdit: null`, #816 for the Plan sections' `isEditing &&` gate) and still disabled with `readOnlyTitle` for a role that may not write, with Promotions keeping `edit_busy_hint` while another section is open. Four Plan sections change (Pricing, Billing & Duration, the three Product benefit sections through one JSX site, Centers) and the orphaned `linkBtn` constant is deleted with them. Frontend only: no migration, no API change, no new locale key, no new Theme setting, and no editing behaviour moved. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/section-edit-button.test.ts` (11), with #897's `promotions-expanded-read-only.test.ts` updated — it pinned the lilac literal it was this ticket's job to remove.)

- **Done**: #912 (**The Theme editor's own primary actions read the Theme's Primary Button colours.** #901 made the subsection `Edit` button the first consumer of the Buttons group's `primaryButton`/`primaryButtonText`, but the two Theme screens still painted their own primary actions in the legacy hardcoded lilac — so a gym could set *Primary Button = red* in the very editor whose `+ Assign Centers…` and `Save changes` buttons stayed `#6c63ff`. Four buttons move: `+ Assign Centers…` and `Save changes` on Custom Themes (`[locale]/themes`), `Save changes` and `+ Add` on Base Themes (`[locale]/system/themes`). The pair of `var()` fallbacks is now declared **once**, as `primaryActionColors` in `apps/admin/src/components/ui.tsx`, with `primaryBtnStyle()`/`primaryBtnSmall()` spreading it over `btnStyle()`/`btnSmall()`'s geometry; `SectionEditButton` derives from `primaryBtnSmall()` and carries no colour literal of its own, since a second spelling of the same fallbacks is the drift #901 existed to remove. Spreading the pair explicitly is **not** the same as dropping `btnStyle()`/`btnSmall()`'s argument: their own default is `--brand`, which `applyTokens` maps to `sidebarSelectedItemBackground` — a different Theme setting, and not an action colour. No new Theme setting (§5), no token semantics changed (Out of scope), and each button keeps the `opacity`/`cursor` pair it already rode on, so a Save with nothing to save and a picker on a non-active Theme look exactly as before (§6). Deliberately left: the Cancel (`#888`), upload (`#444`) and remove (`#c0392b`) buttons, which are secondary or destructive (§4), and the *show all centers* disclosure **text link**, which is not a primary action — its natural home is the Links group's `--gd-link`, a different Theme setting and a different ticket. `CrudModal`'s own Save is likewise untouched: it is shared app-wide chrome, not a Theme-editor button. Frontend only: no migration, no API change, no new locale key. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/theme-primary-buttons.test.ts` (10), with #901's `section-edit-button.test.ts` updated — it pinned the literals this ticket moved into `ui.tsx`.)

- **Done**: #913 (**The `Mandatory` pill stands out from the `System` one in Products.** #894 put both name-cell badges on the shared neutral `listNameBadgeStyle`, which is the right voice for `System` ("what kind of row is this") but left `Mandatory` reading as part of the row's chrome — grey on light grey beside a grey pill. `listChrome.ts` now also exports `listNameBadgeAccentStyle`, the accent variant a row's attention-worthy metadata wears, and the Products header renders the `Mandatory` badge through it. It is declared as a spread of the neutral style overriding `color` and `background` and nothing else, so the ticket's "existing chip sizing, typography, padding and border radius are preserved" is structural rather than a matching pair of literals: a value changed on the neutral pill still reaches this one. The pair is amber on light amber (`#92400e` on `#fde68a`, ~8:1), distinct from the row surface, the card and the grey `System` pill. No new Theme setting or CSS variable — the neutral pill's own colours are literals too (#894), so the existing token architecture does not require one — and `System`, the two `StatusBadge` columns, the badge's placement, label, meaning and read-only-ness, the two forms' checkboxes and every payload are untouched; Taxes and Professional Services keep the neutral pill. Frontend only: no migration, no API change, no new locale key. Tests: six new cases in `apps/admin/src/test/products-mandatory-badge.test.ts` (16 total), pinning that the accent style is derived rather than restated, that it overrides exactly the two colour properties, that it shares no colour with the `System` pill, and that the other two badge pages stay neutral.)

- **Done**: #915 (**Billing Event Simulation on Membership Plans.** A third read-only projection on the expanded Plan card, answering the question the other two do not: not "what does each billing *period* do to the Membership Fee" (#818's Example Timeline) but **"what is billed, in full, on each *date*"** — one group per billing date, listing the Membership Fee and every Product the Plan carries, each at the price the Plan's own `(action, value)` treatment gives it (#896), mandatory items included (#893), with a per-date total and tax-inclusive amounts. The ticket's load-bearing constraint was "do not implement a second billing engine", so the new pure module `api/src/domain/planBillingEventSimulation.ts` is a thin adapter over the existing `domain/billingSimulation.ts`: it expresses the Plan as the hypothetical assignment a member enrolling today would hold (no Promotions, no Additional Periodic Services, no Personal Membership Fee Benefit — none of those is a property of the Plan), hands it to `computeBillingSimulation()` and does exactly two things of its own — supply the horizon floor and re-group the engine's cadence sections by date. Every date, every amount and every Free / Pre-paid / Pay / Bonus decision stays the engine's and, through it, `classifyPlanDurationPeriod()`'s — the same classifier the nightly run prices a cycle with, so the section cannot advertise a charge the run does not make. The horizon is the ticket's own rule rather than a month count: a new `minimumCycles` option on the engine raises the projection to at least `start + N x cadence` for **every** recurring stream (`SIMULATED_CYCLES = 2`), so a Plan carrying a yearly item runs two years and drags the 4-weekly ones with it, and a lone 4-weekly EUR 70 fee reads Sep 30 / Oct 28 / Nov 25 — the ticket's own example. It only ever *raises* the horizon, so #629 section 6's first-regular-charge rule still carries a six-period Free Period through to its first paid period. Frequencies stay independent (each item is its own stream at its own frequency — nothing is converted to a common cadence), a `Once` item is billed once, and a `Waive`d line is still shown, at EUR 0, beside the regular price it would otherwise have cost. Tax is handled by running the whole projection in VAT-inclusive euros — the router grosses the Plan's price and each item's price up first, which is exact because a Plan may only configure `no_benefit` / `waive` / `percentage_discount` (#896 section 16) and its durations only waive outright — so no tax arithmetic happens in the projection or in the page (#817), and an item with no tax rate contributes its stored amount exactly as `formatPlanCurrentPrice()` falls back. `enrichPlan` embeds the result as `billing_event_simulation` and `GET /membership-plans/:id/billing-event-simulation` serves the same calculation, mirroring #818's pair; the benefit and catalogue `SELECT`s gained the price/tax columns so no per-plan round trip was added. No migration, nothing persisted, nothing charged, and the Example Timeline is untouched. Admin: `section_billing_event_simulation` is declared last in `PLAN_SECTION_ORDER` and rendered by `plans/PlanBillingEventSimulation.tsx`, which formats and never recomputes. Tests: `api/src/test/plan-billing-event-simulation.test.ts` (11 unit), the engine's own `minimumCycles` block in `billing-simulation.test.ts` (4), `apps/admin/src/test/plan-billing-event-simulation.test.ts` (7) and integration coverage of the route in `membership-plans.test.ts` (6).)

## August 2026

- **Done**: #192 (Calendar Event Bookings & Waiting List: new `event_bookings` table + FIFO waitlist with auto-promote on cancel and on capacity increase; admin roster UI with expandable rows, confirmed/waitlist split, Add Member, Remove, Promote actions, FULL badge; member schedule page shows Calendar Events inline with Book/Join Waitlist/Cancel/Leave Waitlist buttons; cancellation policy: allowed if event starts >24h from now OR booking made <4h ago; waitlisted always cancel freely; GET /me/events, POST /me/event-bookings, DELETE /me/event-bookings/:id; event-bookings.test.ts with 23 tests)

- **Done**: #190 (Calendar Foundation & Calendar Events: new `calendar_events` table with full audit/soft-delete; `activity_types` extended with `default_space_id`, `default_trainer_membership_id`, `color`; `/calendar-events` router with CRUD, space/trainer conflict detection (409), drag-and-drop and resize via PUT; FullCalendar v6 admin UI with day/week/month views, four filter modes (all/space/activity_type/trainer), CalendarEventModal with activity-type auto-populate; `/events` admin page removed; calendar-events.test.ts with 15 tests)

- **Done**: #193 (Attendance Management for Calendar Events / Class Sessions: new `attendance_status VARCHAR(20) DEFAULT 'pending'` CHECK ('pending','present','absent') column on `bookings` — separate from the booking lifecycle `status` column; backfill migrated old 'attended'→'present' and 'no_show'→'absent' then reset those rows' status to 'booked'; `bookings.status` CHECK reduced to ('booked','waitlisted','cancelled'); `attendance_recorded_at DATETIME` + `attendance_recorded_by_membership_id FK→gym_memberships` audit fields on `bookings`; `effective_trainer_membership_id FK→gym_memberships` + `effective_trainer_confirmed_at DATETIME` on `class_sessions` to record who actually delivered the session; new endpoints: `POST /bookings/:id/attendance` (requireRole admin/front_desk/trainer), `POST /class-sessions/:id/bulk-present` (marks all pending booked→present), `PUT /class-sessions/:id/effective-trainer`, `POST /class-sessions/:id/complete` (hard-blocked until all booked bookings have non-pending attendance_status AND at least one trainer field is set — returns 400 with {pending_count, missing_trainer}); `POST /bookings` gains `force=true` to add walk-ins over capacity (returns over_capacity:true flag); admin SessionRosterPanel rewritten with attendance progress header (present/pending/absent pills), per-booking attendance toggle buttons, bulk-present, effective trainer selector, complete session flow with ConfirmDialog and error feedback; attendance.test.ts with 21 tests)

- **Done**: #194 (Calendar Notifications & Member Experience: new `member_notifications` table (migration 087) with fire-and-forget `sendNotification`/`sendBulkNotification` helpers; 7 notification types: booking_confirmed, waitlist_joined, promoted_from_waitlist, event_cancelled, event_updated, booking_reminder_24h, booking_reminder_1h; notifications written on book/cancel/session-cancel flows; GET /me/notifications (paginated + unread count), GET /me/notifications/count, PUT /me/notifications/:id/read, PUT /me/notifications/read-all; GET /me/upcoming (next 30-day confirmed bookings, sessions + events combined), GET /me/activity-history (past bookings paginated); GET /me/schedule fixed to join activity_types + spaces instead of class_types + rooms, adds trainer_name + can_cancel; member app: Notifications page with mark-all-read and per-item read tracking, bell tab in BottomNav with unread badge, unreadNotifications count in AppContext)

- **Done**: #210 (Theme Token Mapping + Editor Organization: `--gd-card-bg` applied to all expandable card containers across Themes, Spaces, Specialities, Centers, Promotions, Staff, Plans, Training Plans, Training Plan Templates, Workout Template blocks; sidebar hover background wired via `onMouseEnter`/`onMouseLeave` in `NavGroup` using `--gd-sidebar-hover-bg`; selected item text and background use `--gd-sidebar-selected-text`/`--gd-sidebar-selected-bg` CSS vars instead of hardcoded values; `Separator Height (px)` moved into the Header section of the Colors editor in both gym themes and base themes pages)

## July 2026

- **Done**: Phase M (#45–#49, MySQL cutover 2026-07-04)

- **Training module redesign (#60–#63, done)**: dynamic Workout Block form driven by
  `blockFieldConfig.ts` (#60); tree-grid Training Plan Template editor (#61); dependency
  awareness for shared catalog entities (#62); tree-grid Workout Template editor with
  in-place block/exercise CRUD, paginated/filtered list (`created_by_membership_id`,
  migration 053) and cross-template block drag-and-drop via `PUT …/blocks/:id/move` (#63).
  Patterns documented in `docs/feature-patterns.md` (Tree-Grid Editor, Config-Driven Fields,
  Dependency Awareness).

- **Phase 9 — Centers (#59, supersedes Phase 7)**: `Center` (migration 043) as the single
  location concept going forward — `gym_locations` was never built. `member_centers`
  junction (044) gives each Member one or more Centers with a default; `center_id` +
  audit-column backfill added to `rooms`/`class_sessions`/`bookings` (045–047); every
  existing Gym auto-got one default Center named after the gym, every existing Member
  defaulted onto it (046), and new gyms get the same default Center at creation. New
  `resources`/`trainer_availability`/`events` tables (048–050), each `center_id`-scoped
  from creation. `centerContext` middleware (`x-center-id` header) mirrors `tenantContext`;
  `resolveCenterId()` falls back to a gym's sole active Center so single-center gyms need
  no UI changes. Admin: **Organization → Centers/Resources/Events** pages, Trainer
  Availability inline on the Trainers page, Member-edit Assigned Centers/Default Center
  (hidden until a gym has >1 center). Member app: `GET /me/centers` + an optional
  center switcher, hidden for the single-center case. **Phase 7 (#39–#41) is superseded
  and closed** — its `gym_locations`/nullable-FK design was never implemented.

- **Cordel platform menu + two-scope audit log (#66, done)**: superadmin-only **Cordel**
  sidebar group (Gyms, Users, platform Audit Log at `/cordel/audit`); **System** reduced to
  gym-scoped Audit Log + Customize and opened to gym admins (`requiredRole: 'admin'`).
  `GET /audit-logs?scope=all` (platform superadmins only, `tenantCtx.isSuperadmin`) lifts
  the tenant gym filter and joins `gyms.name`; shared `AuditLogView` component renders both
  scopes, adding a Gym column in platform mode.

- **Personalized Training Plans module (#67, done)**: gym-level **Training → Training
  Plans** page (list, filters by member, source template, status, created by, and date
  ranges) with a two-step "New Training Plan" dialog (From Template / From Scratch) that
  posts to `POST /training-plans`. Existing-active-plan collisions return `409`; the UI
  swaps to a keep-vs-expire prompt whose "Expire" branch closes prior actives
  (`status='expired'`, `end_date` = new plan's start) and their `member_training_plans`
  rows atomically before the new plan is created. Editor page at
  `/training-plans/[id]` with a header form (name/desc/dates/status), drag-reorder for
  workouts, cross-parent move + duplicate for blocks and exercises via new endpoints on
  `training-plans.ts` (`…/blocks/:id/move`, `…/exercises/:id/move`,
  `…/duplicate` at each level). Templates page gains an **Assign Plan to Member** action
  on active templates that opens the same dialog with the template preselected.
  Migration 054 adds `training_plans.start_date` (backfilled from the latest
  `member_training_plans.valid_from`) and `end_date`, and updates the status CHECK to
  `draft/active/expired/deleted` (existing `inactive` rows remapped to `expired`).
  `training-plan-creation.ts` extracts the clone/write-history transaction so both
  `POST /training-plans` and the legacy `POST /members/:id/member-training-plans` share
  one implementation.

- **Audit Log UX Improvements (#69, done)**: each audit row is now a self-contained
  historical snapshot. `actor_name` and `entity_name` are captured at write time and never
  recalculated (migration 055; existing rows truncated). Actor name comes from the Clerk user
  object already fetched in `tenantContext` (zero extra queries). Entity name is resolved by
  `infra/audit-registry.ts`: named entities use a `name`-column lookup; M-N/link entities
  get a composed label from their FK parents (e.g. `user_membership` → member name + plan
  name). FK fields in `previous_values`/`new_values` are enriched at write time from plain
  `foo_id: value` to `foo: { id, name }` objects. `GET /audit-logs/meta` returns the full
  entity-type list + action list for dropdown population. New filter params: `entity_name`
  (LIKE), `actor` (name LIKE or Clerk ID exact match), `action`, `source`. `AuditLogView`
  gains dropdowns for entity type, action and source; actor now searched by stored name;
  entity column shows `type#id` + name beneath it; Clerk uid surfaced only in the expanded
  row. `class-types.ts` and `promotions.ts` gain `recordAudit` coverage.

- **Training Plans UX Redesign — Inline Editing (#112, done)**: replaced the
  `/training-plans/[id]` editor page with an inline expandable card list matching the
  Training Plan Templates UX. Each card expands to show workouts/blocks/exercises with
  the existing drag-reorder and block/exercise CRUD modals. New context menu actions:
  **Edit** (inline metadata), **Details** (read-only modal with full audit info),
  **Duplicate** (clones the full plan tree to a draft), and **Complete** (transitions
  status to `completed`, stamps `end_date`, makes plan read-only). Migration 066 adds
  `completed` to the status CHECK constraint. The old `/training-plans/[id]` route now
  redirects to the list. All write endpoints on `training-plans.ts` reject completed plans
  with 403.

- **Done**: #114

- **Billing ledger member history endpoint (#114, done)**: `GET /billing-events/member/:memberId`
  convenience alias added to `billing-events.ts` (paginated, tenant-isolated, 404 on unknown
  member). First test suite for the billing ledger (`billing-events.test.ts`): 14 tests
  covering auth, happy paths, new endpoint, and cross-gym tenant isolation.

- **Training Plan Workout Builder Parity (#164, done)**: Training Plan workout cards now have
  full UX parity with the Workout Template builder. Extracted `WorkoutBlockBuilder.tsx`
  (`apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/workout-templates/`) — a self-contained component with its own
  dnd-kit `DndContext` that handles block reorder, exercise reorder within a block, and
  cross-block exercise moves via `PUT …/blocks/:srcBlockId/exercises/:exId/move`. Props:
  `workoutKey` (namespaces drag IDs to avoid collision across multiple open workout cards),
  `blocksUrl`, `blocks: HierBlock[]`, `canWrite`, `onChanged`. Training Plans page updated:
  block/exercise CRUD modals removed from the workout tree; each workout card now renders
  `WorkoutBlockBuilder` inline with a searchable `ExerciseCombobox`, always-editable fields
  (inline block name + type + config, inline exercise rows, result_type/target_value/unit),
  block Duplicate/Delete context menus, and drag-and-drop at both levels. Workout name
  always-editable inline (on-blur PUT, reverts on error). `+ Add Workout` replaces
  `window.prompt()` with an inline form. `canWrite` now uses `canWriteModule(role, 'TRAINING')`
  (matches Training Plan Templates pattern). No API or DB changes — backend already supported
  all required endpoints (`/blocks`, `/blocks/:id/exercises`, cross-block `/move`, etc.).
